(b) Verified by diff, not by eye. Sliced `requestPanelSharedStyles.ts:381-394` and `FollowUpInput.styles.ts:93-106`, stripped leading indentation, `diff` → IDENTICAL, 14 lines. Host blocks: `#followUpInput` (:70-79) vs `:is(${FEEDBACK_INPUTS})` (:361-372) differ only in `--textarea-max-height` (`clamp(min, 32vh, 240px)` vs `clamp(min, 24vh, 10rem)`), as claimed. The wrapper blocks are NOT identical (FollowUpInput's `::part(textarea-wrapper)` adds max-height/overflow/border/background/box-shadow; requestPanel's adds min-height and also styles `::part(base)`) — correctly left out of scope. Self-declared duplication confirmed verbatim at `requestPanelSharedStyles.ts:355-360`: "Mirror the follow-up textarea rules so the two prose boxes stay in lockstep."

Deliberate-justification check (kill rule 4). Traced the mirror to `dbdee4d62d` (#9773, composer sizing) and `1d4ac9e847` (#9944, "feedback field sizing"). Read #9944's body: it says the rejection textarea should "match the follow-up composer … and shared CSS in requestPanelSharedStyles". Nothing rules against extracting a shared fragment; the mirror comment is an admission of manual maintenance, i.e. an invitation. No doc in docs/proposals/ or docs/architecture/ addresses it. No AGENTS.md or review-checklist rule forbids sharing a CSS declaration fragment across the shared-styles boundary.

Settled-surface check (kill rule 5). Touches none of: @agent/* SDK, PocketFlow engine, platform ports, the six browser-reachable utils, AgentEvent/SessionFact, vscode-free zones (both files are already vscode-free and stay so). No ratchet mentions `shared/styles`; knip-baseline has no row for any of the three files. `packages/extension/src/progressView/frontend/` already imports `@shared/styles/controlStyles` (progressAppStyles.ts:6), so the (b) import direction is an established edge, not a new one.

Masking check (kill rule 7). No catch, no fallback, no `??`, no Zod `.catch`. N/A.

Net-adds check (kill rule 6). This is where I diverge from the claim. Part (a): 5 blocks collapse to one line each — 10→1 and 4×(9→1) = -41 source lines; the const with a doc comment and a blank line is +5 (prettier will wrap `unsafeCSS(` over three lines since the 108-char literal cannot be broken) → about -36. Part (b): two 16-line rules become 3 lines each = -26; the shared fragment (selector-less, 14 declarations + `css\`` + backtick-semicolon + doc comment + blank) is about +18; trimming the six-line mirror comment to a pointer is about -2; one new import line +1 → about -9. Total about -45, not -55. Elements: +1 file-local const in controlStyles.ts and +1 exported CSSResult = +2, not +1. Still a genuine reduction, and both edits are semantically CSS-preserving.

Thinness check (kill rule 8). Part (b) alone (-9 LoC, +1 export) would be too thin to stand. Batched with part (a) (-36, five verbatim repetitions of a nine-line block in a 578-line file) it clears the bar, and both halves apply a pattern the same directory already established — `selectorGroup`/`unsafeCSS` at requestPanelSharedStyles.ts:93-95 for (a), and `truncateTextRule` at :29-33 (a selector-less declaration fragment interpolated into a rule body at :317) for (b). Two independent in-file precedents means neither half invents an abstraction.

Risk. Low. Zero behavior change, no exported symbol removed or renamed, no persisted data, no wire contract. The only real trap is that the three vitest suites assert on the SOURCE TEXT of controlStyles.ts, so the const must hold the class names as one literal string; a `.map()`-based construction would break DesktopControlSystem.vitest.ts:46-49. Second-order trap: `packages/desktop/src/renderer/styles.css` mirrors the same list seven times and the same suite asserts parity by `toContain` on individual class names, which still holds.

</details>

#### Let the catalog own settings-view display copy: delete the label/description restated at every `renderStateSettingToggleRow` / select / number call site

- **Lens**: `settings-surface` · **Kind**: dual-representation · **Risk**: medium
- **Net**: -42 LoC, -3 elements (claimed -45 LoC, -3 elements; verifier figure governs)

**Evidence**

`StateSettingEntry.description` is documented as 'Human-readable description, shared across every host that renders it' (src/shared/schemas/stateSettings.ts:226) and is mandatory on every surfaced row (`SurfacedSettingEntry`, :262-266). It is shared with nobody. Reader census over every file that imports a catalog accessor (`rg -l "stateSettingByKey|settingByKey|ALL_SETTINGS|settingsViewSnapshotEntries|CLI_STATE_SETTINGS|modelsTabSettings|settingsViewSettingByKey" src packages | grep -v test-kernel` → 11 files, each then grepped for `.description`/`.title`/`.category`): PRODUCTION readers of `entry.description` = 0. `entry.title` is read only for CLI `/config` row labels (packages/cli/src/chat/tui/forms/ConfigForm.tsx:115, CliConfigForm.tsx:172) and host error toasts (packages/extension/src/settingsView/SettingsViewMessageHandler.ts:555,561,566; packages/desktop/src/main/desktopSettingsIpc.ts:263). The `/config` list line is `description: `${valueText} · ${store}${suffix}`` (ConfigForm.tsx:133) — the row's prose is never rendered anywhere.

Meanwhile the shared catalog-bound renderer takes the copy as caller-supplied props: `StateSettingToggleRowOptions.label` / `.description` (packages/extension/src/settingsView/frontend/components/shared/stateSettingRows.ts:47-55), even though the same file already imports `stateSettingByKey` (:16) to derive enum options for the same key. Every call site therefore restates copy the row already owns, and it has drifted:

- ToolsTab.ts:211-217 `texra.toolUse.requireEditApproval` — label matches the row title (stateSettings.ts:676) but description 'When policy is Ask, review a diff before an agent changes files…' ≠ row :677-678 'When approval policy is Ask, show a diff before an agent changes workspace files…'. Same drift at ToolsTab.ts:218-224 vs row :684-685.
- ToolsTab.ts:239-244 `texra.skills.enabled` — label 'Make skills available to tool-use agents' + description 'Includes built-in TeXRA skills and imported skills.' vs row title 'Agent skills' (:662) and description 'Discover TeXRA and imported skills and expose them to tool-use agent prompts.' (:663-664). Full drift.
- ToolsTab.ts:291-297 `TOOL_PATH_PROTECTION_ENABLED` — 'Restrict tool paths to the working directory' / short blurb vs row title 'Restrict tool paths' (:1498) and its longer description (:1499-1500). Drift.
- AccountTab.ts:170-176 `texra.telemetry.enabled` — 'Share usage telemetry' + a 3-line description vs row title 'Usage telemetry' (:650) + description (:651-652). Drift.
- MemoryTab.ts:77-82 (row :1084), GitTab.ts:388-395 (row :990), MultiAgentTab.ts:346-352/:353-359/:360-366 (rows :1052, :1066, :1026) each restate label+description.
- MultiAgentTab.ts:367-377 and AgentsTab.ts:309-324/:325-338 restate the number-row `label` ('Child-run concurrency budget', 'Compaction threshold', 'Automatic retries') while already sourcing `description`/`min`/`max` from the `*_SETTING` constants — proof the pattern is half-migrated.
- AIAgentsTab.ts:156-195 hard-codes six labels ('Sandbox mode', 'Reasoning effort', 'Approval policy', 'Model', 'Reasoning effort', 'Permission mode') in a `readonly [string, WorkspaceStateKey, string]` tuple, and AIAgentsTab.ts:133-137 renders one generic help string 'Applied whenever this integration runs.' for all six — while the rows carry real per-setting titles and descriptions (stateSettings.ts:1098-1099, :1109-1110, :1120-1121, :1137-1138, :1153-1154, :1170-1171).

Cross-host asymmetry confirms the seam is the bug: for the same rows the CLI `/config` reads `entry.title` while the webview reads its own literal, so the two hosts already show different names for the same setting.

Dedupe: `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "settings view label"` and `--search "settings catalog description"` → only unrelated CLOSED items (#11308, #11357, #9449, #9597). Closed #9412 ('finish the settings-write migration onto the generic settingsAccess.writeSetting path') and closed #6606 ('promote remaining state-backed domains') both cover the WRITE path and catalog membership; neither mentions display copy. `git log --oneline -6 -- .../components/shared/stateSettingRows.ts .../tabs/AIAgentsTab.ts` → #10875, #10609, #9990, #9942, #9829, #9803, all generic sweeps. Round-1 index: #11417 folded the memory toggle onto a catalog row (membership, not copy) and one REFUTED item touched AIAgentsTab's `@property` default initializers — a different mechanism in a different part of the file. Not a re-file.

Settled surfaces untouched: no ratchet, no @agent/* deep import (all edits are in `packages/extension/src/settingsView/**` and `src/shared/schemas`), no PocketFlow engine, no host/platform composition root, no browser-safe @utils module, no AgentEvent/SessionFact change, no wire-schema change (snapshots carry values only, `snapshotMessage` at src/shared/schemas/settingsViewMessages.ts:221-236). Nothing removed is a catch/`??` masking site, so checklist §15 is N/A. R5: this shrinks required call-site input rather than relocating it — the drift class disappears with the second copy.

**Proposal**

1. In stateSettingRows.ts, drop `label` and `description` from `StateSettingToggleRowOptions` (:47-55) and have `renderStateSettingToggleRow` (:61-77) resolve them from `stateSettingByKey(options.key)` (the import is already there at :16), throwing on an unknown key the same way `settingSignal` does (settingsState.ts:102-106). Add sibling `renderStateSettingNumberRow` and `renderStateSettingSelectRow` helpers that do the same over `renderSettingsNumberRow` and the AIAgentsTab select markup, so all three catalog-bound row shapes read one owner.
2. Delete the restated `label:`/`description:` lines at the 10 toggle sites (ToolsTab.ts:211-217, :218-224, :239-244, :291-297; MultiAgentTab.ts:346-352, :353-359, :360-366; AccountTab.ts:170-176; MemoryTab.ts:77-82; GitTab.ts:388-395), the 3 number-row labels (MultiAgentTab.ts:368, AgentsTab.ts:310, :326), and the 6 select labels plus the generic help string in AIAgentsTab.ts:133-137,156-195 — the `rows` tuple collapses from `[string, WorkspaceStateKey, string]` to `[WorkspaceStateKey, string]` and `renderSelectRow` loses its `label` parameter.
3. For each of those rows, pick the better of the two wordings and store it once on the catalog row in src/shared/schemas/stateSettings.ts (the tab wording usually wins: e.g. keep 'Share usage telemetry' and the fuller privacy sentence on `telemetry.enabled` :649-656; keep 'Make skills available to tool-use agents' on `skills.enabled` :661-672). The chosen text then also reaches `/config`, which is the point.
4. Verify with `npm run typecheck`, `npm test -- stateSettings`, `npm run lint`, and a manual pass over the Tools / Multi-Agent / AI Agents / Account / Memory / Git / Agents tabs.
5. Out of scope on purpose: LaTeXTab's rows, which go through the `LATEX_CONFIG_FIELD_TO_KEY` field vocabulary that round 1 already refuted touching.

**What we give up**

Per-surface wording — the webview and `/config` must show the same label and description for a given setting. That is the intent of `StateSettingEntry.description`'s own doc comment; where a tab genuinely needs extra context it can still render its own section heading (`renderSettingsSectionHeading`, already used in ToolsTab.ts:231-236 and AgentsTab.ts:301-306), which this change does not touch.

**Verifier corrections to the evidence above**

1. WRONG LINE NUMBERS (minor, all verified at HEAD d8c6dfc071):

- The `description` doc comment + field is stateSettings.ts:227-228, not :226.
- `StateSettingToggleRowOptions` is stateSettingRows.ts:48-54 (`label` :51, `description` :52), not :47-55. (`stateSettingByKey` import :16 ✓, `renderStateSettingToggleRow` :61 ✓, `SurfacedSettingEntry` :262-266 ✓.)
- `snapshotMessage` is settingsViewMessages.ts:220-232, not :221-236 (conclusion unchanged: values only, no display copy on the wire).
- AI-agents rows are SWAPPED in the evidence: `CLAUDE_AGENT_PERMISSION_MODE` is :1149-1166 (title :1152) and `CLAUDE_AGENT_EFFORT` is :1167-1180 (title :1173). The evidence assigns "Reasoning effort" to :1153-1154 and "Permission mode" to :1170-1171, i.e. exactly backwards. Codex rows :1096/:1106/:1117 with titles at :1098/:1109/:1120 ✓; `CLAUDE_AGENT_MODEL` title :1137 ✓.

2. CENSUS IS INTERNALLY INCONSISTENT. The stated grep (`rg -l "stateSettingByKey|settingByKey|ALL_SETTINGS|settingsViewSnapshotEntries|CLI_STATE_SETTINGS|modelsTabSettings|settingsViewSettingByKey"`) returns 11 files and does NOT include `packages/cli/src/chat/tui/forms/ConfigForm.tsx`, yet ConfigForm.tsx is where the cited `entry.title` read actually lives (`settingDisplayName`, :114-116, used at :133 and :212/:327). The real production reader set is 12 files. The conclusion (`entry.title` readers = ConfigForm.tsx:115, CliConfigForm.tsx:172, SettingsViewMessageHandler.ts:555/561/566, desktopSettingsIpc.ts:263) reproduces exactly; only the census method was wrong.

3. "It is shared with nobody" OVER-CLAIMS, and misses the one place the catalog's own display copy IS rendered. `ModelsTabSurface` (stateSettings.ts:170-178) carries its own `label` + `description`, projected by `SettingsProfileController.getProviderSettings` (src/controllers/settingsView/SettingsProfileController.ts:132-136) onto `ProviderSetting` and rendered at `components/profile/ProviderKeyList.ts:188`. So the catalog already has a _second, per-surface_ copy field that is read in production. That is a counterexample to "display copy has one owner" the proposal should acknowledge (it does not refute the candidate — the Models rows are provider-scoped, and `entry.description` itself is still read by zero production sites — but the framing is wrong).

4. NEAR-MISS READERS THE EVIDENCE NEVER DISAMBIGUATES (I checked; none is a catalog reader, so "entry.description readers = 0" holds): `SettingsApp.ts:533` renders `activeEntry.description` — that is a `SETTINGS_NAV_GROUPS` nav entry, not a catalog row. `ToolsTab.ts:205` renders `option.description` — that is `TEXRA_APPROVAL_POLICY_OPTIONS` from src/shared/approvalPolicy.ts, not the catalog. `AgentsTab.ts:311/:327` and `MultiAgentTab.ts:369` read `*_SETTING.description` from coreSettings.ts, which is the same string the catalog derives via `.describe()` — so those three are already single-sourced and are NOT drift.

5. TWO PROPOSED HELPERS ARE WRONG AND MUST BE CUT FROM SCOPE (this is the load-bearing correction):

- `renderStateSettingSelectRow` would have exactly ONE caller. LaTeXTab is excluded by the candidate's own step 5; ToolsTab's `<wa-select>` (`:197-209`) is bound to `TEXRA_APPROVAL_POLICY_OPTIONS`/the approval-policy authority (pinned by `src/test-kernel/architecture/approvalPolicyAuthorityRatchet.vitest.ts`), not the catalog; `catalogEnumChoices` has only 2 consumers repo-wide (AIAgentsTab.ts:125, LaTeXTab.ts:1010). A single-caller shared extraction is explicitly banned by CLAUDE.md "Design guardrails". Do it as a change to AIAgentsTab's existing private `renderSelectRow` instead.
- `renderStateSettingNumberRow` saves exactly 3 `label:` lines (MultiAgentTab.ts:368, AgentsTab.ts:310, :326) while adding a ~20-line exported helper: net POSITIVE LoC, i.e. checklist-13 churn. Cut it. The number rows' `description`/`min`/`max` already come from the `*_SETTING` SSOT (correction 4), so the only duplication left there is one literal per site.

6. `title` IS OPTIONAL ON `SurfacedSettingEntry` — the proposal does not account for this. stateSettings.ts:262-266 requires `description`, `category`, `surfaces` but NOT `title`. I ran the catalog: 57 surfaced rows, 6 with no title (`texra.workflow.autoOpenPdf` plus the five LaTeX rows, all out of scope). So "resolve them from `stateSettingByKey(options.key)`" needs either a `entry.title ?? stripPrefix(entry.key)` fallback (which already exists as `ConfigForm.settingDisplayName` — promoting it gives it a legitimate 2nd caller) or a type tightening plus 6 added titles. Either way it is +lines the estimate omits.

7. THE AIAgentsTab LABEL FOLD CONFLICTS WITH AN EXISTING PRECEDENT AND SHOULD BE SECOND-STAGE. Closed issue #6608 ("re-point remaining tabs to the shared catalog", which is exactly the AIAgentsTab enum-option work already done) rules: "**Preserve the displayed wording exactly** and lock it with a byte-for-byte test". The six catalog titles are provider-prefixed ('Codex sandbox mode', 'Codex reasoning effort', 'Claude Code model', 'Claude Code reasoning effort', …) because they must disambiguate in the FLAT `/config` list; the tab labels are bare ('Sandbox mode', 'Reasoning effort') because they sit inside a per-integration card. "Pick the better of the two wordings" is not available here: moving the bare wording to the catalog makes `/config` show two rows named 'Reasoning effort', and moving the prefixed wording into the card changes displayed wording against #6608. Only the generic help string ('Applied whenever this integration runs.' AIAgentsTab.ts:135-137) is an unambiguous win — the six real descriptions exist (:1099, :1110, :1121, :1138, :1153, :1174).

8. UNDER-CITED SUPPORTING EVIDENCE: the MemoryTab drift is brand new — #11417 (16d66fd077, round 1) added the catalog row with title 'Memory for chat agents' (:1086) while the same PR's call site passes 'Enable memory for chat agents' (MemoryTab.ts:79). Round 1 minted a fresh instance of exactly this drift class, which is the strongest single argument for the fold. Also uncited: `src/test-kernel/shared/stateSettings.vitest.ts:315` and :348 already pin _option_ labels to the catalog for LaTeXTab and AIAgentsTab — the precedent the row-level fold extends. Also uncited: 3d35da9a45 (#9729, "apply the interface-writing review") word-smithed the tab-side copy but not the catalog titles, which explains the drift's origin and confirms step 3 (keep the tab wording) is the right direction.

9. `git log` counts: `stateSettingRows.ts` has 4 commits, not 6 (#10609, #9990, #9829, #9803); the evidence's list mixes in AIAgentsTab's history. Conclusion (all generic sweeps, none touched row copy) holds.

<details><summary>Verifier reasoning</summary>

Verified at HEAD d8c6dfc071 (branch moved past the #11416-#11422 round-1 merges; MemoryTab's catalog row from #11417 is present).

DUPLICATE (kill test 1): No. round1-index.md contains only two settings-adjacent titles — "Delete StreamSnapshot.description" and "Delete four zero-consumer surfaces left behind in src/controllers/settingsView" — neither is this. In the 49-candidate survey doc, the only overlapping items are the memory-toggle catalog fold (line 384-400, SHIPPED as #11417, membership not copy) and the REFUTED AIAgentsTab item (line 509/525), which is about six `CLAUDE_AGENT_DEFAULT_*`/`CODEX_*_DEFAULT` `@property` initializers at AIAgentsTab.ts:22-27/:101-113 — a different mechanism in a different part of the file from the `rows` tuple at :156-195. Not a re-file. Not already done: the label/description props are still on `StateSettingToggleRowOptions` at HEAD.

ALREADY FILED (2): No. `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search` on "settings label description catalog", "settings copy duplication", "settingsView in:title" returns nothing matching. #6608 (CLOSED) is the closest prior art and covers enum OPTION arrays only, explicitly listing the AIAgentsTab option arrays — already done; it says nothing about row titles/descriptions. `git log --all --grep=renderStateSettingToggleRow` → only #9803, which created the helper.

CONSUMER COUNT (3): I re-grepped everything myself.

- `rg -n "renderStateSettingToggleRow"` → exactly 10 production call sites in 6 tabs (ToolsTab :211/:218/:239/:291, MultiAgentTab :346/:353/:360, AccountTab :170, MemoryTab :77, GitTab :388) plus the definition. Matches the claim.
- `entry.description` catalog readers: I enumerated every `.description` hit in src+packages and ran down each candidate. `SettingsApp.ts:533` = nav entry. `ProviderKeyList.ts:188` = `ModelsTabSurface.description` (a different field, and it IS read — see corrections). `ToolsTab.ts:205` = approvalPolicy options. `AgentsTab.ts:311/:327`, `MultiAgentTab.ts:369` = coreSettings `*_SETTING` constants. CLI `/config` (`buildConfigListItems`, ConfigForm.tsx:118-136) overwrites the list `description` with `${valueText} · ${store}${suffix}`. Confirmed: zero production readers of `StateSettingEntry.description`, while `isSurfaced` (:1529-1536) makes it mandatory for every surfaced row. The dual-representation is real.
- No hits in packages/extension/package.json contributions, commands.ts, resources/, prompts/, or supabase/functions — this is webview render copy only, so those surfaces are correctly irrelevant.
- Drift claims spot-checked against source: `toolUse.requireEditApproval` (row title :674 identical, description :676-678 vs ToolsTab :213-215 — drift, real), `skills.enabled` (row 'Agent skills'/:663-664 vs ToolsTab 'Make skills available…' — full drift, and note ToolsTab.ts:231-236 already renders a section heading titled 'Agent skills', so the tab wording exists precisely to avoid a duplicated heading), `TOOL_PATH_PROTECTION_ENABLED` (row title :1498 'Restrict tool paths' vs :293 — drift), `telemetry.enabled` (:649-652 vs AccountTab :172-174 — drift), MEMORY_ENABLED (:1086 vs :79 — drift), GIT_MARK_COMMITS/ALLOW_ORCHESTRATOR_KILL/DETACH_SUBAGENTS_ON_STOP/GIT_WORKTREE_SUPPORT (rows :990/:1052/:1066/:1026, all restated). The three number-row labels ('Child-run concurrency budget' :392, 'Compaction threshold' :530, 'Automatic retries' :541) are byte-identical restatements of the catalog title.

DELIBERATELY JUSTIFIED (4): I searched docs/proposals + docs/architecture + docs/dev for a ruling. docs/proposals/2026-06-26-config-catalog-unification.md:105 rules the OPPOSITE way — "Delete the hardcoded enum/label arrays and read labels/descriptions from the catalog: AIAgentsTab.ts …". No ratchet or AGENTS.md rule protects per-tab copy. The one real constraint I found is #6608's "preserve the displayed wording exactly", which the candidate's step 3 satisfies for the toggles but cannot satisfy for the six AI-agents select labels (correction 7).

SETTLED SURFACE (5): Untouched. Edits sit in packages/extension/src/settingsView/** and src/shared/schemas/stateSettings.ts. No @agent/* deep import (host-agent-import-baseline unaffected), no shared-schemas deep import added, no PocketFlow engine, no platform port, no browser-safe @utils module, no AgentEvent/SessionFact change. The wire contract is untouched: `snapshotMessage` (settingsViewMessages.ts:220-232) carries `{key: value}` only. `approvalPolicyAuthorityRatchet` is adjacent but the ToolsTab approval select is deliberately excluded.

NET-ADDS (6): As proposed, borderline — the two new exported helpers would eat most of the win (see correction 5). With those cut, the fold is a clean deletion: ~28 lines of restated toggle copy, ~19 net in AIAgentsTab, against ~7 added in the resolver. It shrinks required call-site input rather than relocating it, so R5 is satisfied.

MASKING SITE (7): N/A. Nothing removed is a catch, `??` over a failed read, or a Zod `.catch`. The resolver's throw-on-unknown-key is loud by construction and mirrors `settingSignal` (settingsState.ts:102-106).

TOO THIN (8): No. 13 call sites across 7 files plus a live cross-host inconsistency (CLI `/config` and the webview show different names for the same setting today), and round 1 minted a fresh instance of the drift.

Recommended scope for the implementing PR: (a) fold `label`/`description` out of `StateSettingToggleRowOptions` at all 10 toggle sites, moving the reviewed tab wording onto the catalog row; (b) replace AIAgentsTab's generic 'Applied whenever this integration runs.' with the row description; (c) DEFER the six select labels and the three number-row labels — the first collides with `/config` disambiguation and #6608's preserve-wording rule, the second is net-positive churn.

</details>

#### Retire the channel-first `logger` value contract threaded through the three webview trees

- **Lens**: `webview-tree-parity` · **Kind**: dual-representation · **Risk**: low
- **Net**: -38 LoC, -3 elements (claimed -45 LoC, -4 elements; verifier figure governs)

**Evidence**

THE DUAL SURFACE. `BaseViewMessageHandler` binds the SAME channel three ways in its constructor: `this.logger = logger` (the whole `@logger/logUtils` namespace, narrowed by the file-local `ViewMessageLogger` type at :14-17), `this.channel = `${viewName}MessageHandler`` (:53), and `this.log = logger.createLog(this.channel)` (:54, `private`). `createLog(channel).error(msg, opts)` is literally `loggerSelf.error(channel, msg, opts)` — src/logger/logUtils.ts:243-255 binds each level to exactly that delegation — so `this.logger.X(this.channel, …)` and `this.log.X(…)` are the same call with two extra tokens. The base already uses `this.log` for its own four sites (:82, :127, :134, :139, :151); every subclass uses the other surface because `log` is private.

PRODUCTION CONSUMERS OF THE `logger` VALUE (22 call sites, all production, zero non-production): `packages/extension/src/progressView/ProgressViewMessageHandler.ts` 15 (:133, :272, :301, :320, :472, :501, :508, :514, :519, :562, :569, :581, :876, :908, :926); `packages/extension/src/settingsView/SettingsViewMessageHandler.ts` 1 (:405); `packages/extension/src/settingsView/handlers/agentHandlers.ts` 1; `packages/extension/src/settingsView/handlers/latexSettingsHandlers.ts` 2; `packages/extension/src/webview/MainViewMessageHandler.ts` 1 (:169); `packages/extension/src/webview/slices/commonSlice.ts` 1 (:21); `packages/extension/src/webview/slices/bannerSlice.ts` 1. (`rg -c "this\\.logger\\.|ctx\\.logger\\.|host\\.logger\\." packages/extension/src` = 25 total; the 3 in `ProgressViewProvider.ts:108,123,208` are a different field — `createChannelTrace` — and are out of scope.)

THE CONTRACT IS HAND-RESTATED IN TWO CONTEXT TYPES, one per tree: `mainViewInboundContext.ts:19-25` declares `readonly logger: { debug: (channel, message, options?) => void }`; `settingsView/handlers/SettingsHandlerContext.ts:14-18` declares `readonly logger: { warn/error/debug: (channel, msg, options?) => void }`. Both are filled from the base at `MainViewMessageHandler.ts:100-101` and `SettingsViewMessageHandler.ts:128-129` (`{ channel: this.channel, logger: this.logger }`). Both structural types exist only because `Log` (the already-bound type `createLog` returns) was not passed instead. Non-production consumers of either interface: 0 — `rg "MainViewInboundHost|SettingsHandlerContext"` over `src/` and `packages/` returns only the 14 production sites listed above plus the two declarations; no `src/test-kernel/` mock implements either, so there is no test churn.

WHY THIS IS RESIDUE, NOT DESIGN. `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "logger channel webview"` → #10013 "Migrate remaining 78 modules from `import * as logger` to `createLog`", CLOSED. Its final comments record the deliberate stopping point: #10708 "the two retained `import * as logger` sites (`BaseViewMessageHandler.ts`, `SupabaseAuthProvider.ts`) stay only as channel-first value sources for injectable/passed contracts, narrowed to the methods actually used", and #10710 closed the census at "0 non-test `src/` namespace-logger sites". `rg "import \\* as logger"` on HEAD confirms exactly two production survivors: `packages/extension/src/common/webview/BaseViewMessageHandler.ts:6` and `packages/extension/src/frontend/auth/SupabaseAuthProvider.ts:25`. This candidate is the residual delta the campaign explicitly deferred, for the one of the two that lives in the webview seam. `git log -10 -- packages/extension/src/common/webview/BaseViewMessageHandler.ts` shows the last functional touch is 6337f454b5 (#10708) itself; nothing since. Not in the round-1 index and `rg -i "ViewMessageLogger|BaseViewMessageHandler|SettingsHandlerContext|MainViewInboundHost" docs/proposals/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md` → 0 hits.

NOT AFFECTED: `this.channel`/`ctx.channel`/`host.channel` as a _string_ stays — it is still needed by `showLoggedErrorMessage(channel, …)` (`packages/extension/src/frontend/ui/errorHandlingUtils.ts:23`), `showLoggedMessage`, `signInWithSubscription`, and `safeExecuteCommand(cmd, args, channel)`. This candidate removes only the redundant second _logging_ surface. `SupabaseAuthProvider.ts` is deliberately out of scope (auth, not a webview tree).

**Proposal**

1. In `BaseViewMessageHandler.ts`: delete the `ViewMessageLogger` type (:14-17), the `logger` field and its `this.logger = logger` assignment (:51, :52), and change `private readonly log` to `protected readonly log` (:49, :54). The `import * as logger` at :6 becomes `import { createLog, type Log } from '@logger/logUtils'`. Keep `channel` and `viewName`.
2. Rewrite the 22 call sites from `this.logger.X(this.channel, msg, opts)` to `this.log.X(msg, opts)`. Most are 4-6 line wrapped calls that collapse to one line (e.g. ProgressViewMessageHandler.ts:501-504, :508-511, :519-526, :561-566, :568-574, :580-584).
3. In `mainViewInboundContext.ts` replace the `logger` member (:19-25) with `readonly log: Log`; in `SettingsHandlerContext.ts` replace it (:14-18) the same way. Update the two literals that build them (`MainViewMessageHandler.ts:100-101`, `SettingsViewMessageHandler.ts:128-129`) to pass `log: this.log`, and the 3 slice/handler call sites (`commonSlice.ts:21`, `bannerSlice.ts`, `agentHandlers.ts`, `latexSettingsHandlers.ts`) to `host.log.debug(msg)` / `this.ctx.log.warn(msg, opts)`.
4. Verify with `rg "import \\* as logger" packages/extension` — should leave only `SupabaseAuthProvider.ts`.
   Behavior is byte-identical: the emitted line is `LEVEL [ts] [channel] message` either way (`src/logger/logUtils.ts:139-142`), and `createLog` delegates through `loggerSelf` so existing test spies still intercept (the documented requirement in #10013).

**What we give up**

The ability to hand a _narrowed subset_ of log levels into a context (today `MainViewInboundHost` exposes only `debug`, `SettingsHandlerContext` only `warn`/`error`/`debug`). After the change both receive the full four-level `Log`. That narrowing has never prevented anything — nothing in either tree is constrained by it — and it costs two hand-written structural types that must be kept in sync with `logUtils`'s signature by hand. Restoring it is a one-line `Pick<Log, 'debug'>` if it is ever wanted.

**Verifier corrections to the evidence above**

Nine factual errors / gaps in the evidence, none fatal:

1. WRONG LINE NUMBERS IN THE BASE FILE. `BaseViewMessageHandler.ts` at HEAD declares `protected readonly logger: ViewMessageLogger;` at **:41**, not :51; `private readonly log` at **:43**, not :49. The proposal's ":51, :52" and ":49, :54" pairs mix the declaration lines up with the constructor (:51) and its two assignments (:52, :54). `ViewMessageLogger` at :13-17 and the constructor assignments at :52/:54 are correct.

2. "FOUR SITES" IS FIVE. "the base already uses `this.log` for its own four sites (:82, :127, :134, :139, :151)" — the enumerated list has five entries, and all five are real (`:82` reportNotificationFailure, `:127` Zod debug, `:134` unsupported debug, `:139` error, `:151` unhandled warn).

3. THE PROVENANCE CLAIM IS WRONG. "`git log -10 -- .../BaseViewMessageHandler.ts` shows the last functional touch is 6337f454b5 (#10708) itself; nothing since" is false. Two later commits touched the file: `ddf7301599` (#10782, "fix(extension): surface unhandled host failures" — functional, it added the error branch and `reportNotificationFailure`), and `1d17d1b29a` (#10875, 2026-08-18, "sweep local simplifications", `4 insertions / 22 deletions`). #10875 is precisely what rewrote `ViewMessageLogger` from four hand-written 5-line method signatures into today's `Pick<typeof logger, 'debug'|'info'|'warn'|'error'>`. So the 5-line type the candidate proposes to delete is one week old, already the product of one simplification pass, not untouched #10708 residue. This does not kill the candidate (the _contract_ is still dual), but it does mean ~18 of the LoC a naive reading might credit to this change were already harvested by #10875.

4. `Log` IS NOT EXPORTED. `src/logger/logUtils.ts:221` declares a **file-local** `interface Log`; the only export is `export function createLog(channel: string): Log` at :243. Proposal steps 1 and 3 (`import { createLog, type Log } from '@logger/logUtils'`, `readonly log: Log`) do not compile as written. The PR must either add `export` to `interface Log` — a new export, which needs its consumers in the same PR to clear `npm run check:dead-code-ratchet` (it would have 3: the base class and the two context interfaces, so it clears) — or use `ReturnType<typeof createLog>`, which is what the base already does today. Either is fine; the evidence just asserts an export that does not exist.

5. WRONG OUT-OF-SCOPE LINE NUMBERS. The three `ProgressViewProvider.ts` sites the candidate excludes are at **:208, :236, :361**, not ":108, :123, :208". (:108 is `this.logger = createChannelTrace('ProgressViewProvider')` and :123 is passing it into a sub-object — neither is a call site.) The _kind_ claim is right: the field is `private readonly logger: AgentTrace` (:97), a genuinely different surface, correctly out of scope.

6. THE 22-SITE COUNT IS CORRECT — I re-ran it. ProgressViewMessageHandler 15 (:133, :272, :301, :320, :472, :501, :508, :514, :519, :562, :569, :581, :876, :908, :926), SettingsViewMessageHandler 1 (:405), agentHandlers 1 (:326), latexSettingsHandlers 2 (:142, :204), MainViewMessageHandler 1 (:169), commonSlice 1 (:21), bannerSlice 1 (:63) = 22. The "25 total, 3 out of scope" arithmetic also holds. Every one of the 22 passes its own `this.channel`/`ctx.channel`/`host.channel` — no consumer ever varies the channel, which is the load-bearing fact and it is true.

7. A TEST CONSUMER OF THE CLASS WAS MISSED. "no `src/test-kernel/` mock implements either, so there is no test churn" is true _of the two interfaces_, but the candidate never names `src/test-kernel/webview/BaseViewMessageHandler.vitest.ts` (56 lines), which subclasses the class under change and `vi.mock`s `@logger/logUtils` with a factory exposing **only** `createLog`. It survives unchanged (it never reads `this.logger`), and it actually _helps_ the candidate — the mock already proves the base's own diagnostics run entirely through `createLog`. But a verifier should have found it before claiming zero test surface.

8. THE EQUIVALENCE CLAIM HAS A LIVE WITNESS THE EVIDENCE OMITS. `src/test-kernel/progressView/ProgressViewOnboardingRefresh.vitest.ts:558-575` does `vi.spyOn(logger, 'error')` and asserts `toHaveBeenCalledWith('ProgressViewMessageHandler', 'Error handling message', …)` on a real `ProgressViewMessageHandler`. That message originates from the base's already-migrated `this.log.error` (:139), so it is direct running proof that `createLog`'s per-call `loggerSelf[level]` lookup keeps namespace spies intercepting. It belongs in the evidence as the strongest support for "byte-identical", and it does not appear.

9. LoC IS OVERSTATED. Measured site by site against Prettier's 80-column wrapping: base file −7, `mainViewInboundContext.ts` −6 (the 7-line `logger` member → one `readonly log: Log`), `SettingsHandlerContext.ts` −4, call sites ≈ −21 (most of the wins are :508-511 → 1 line, :519-526 → ~4 lines, :514-516 → 1 line; :133, :301, :320, :472, :876, :908, :926, :169, :21 are all 0 because they are already single-line or their `data:` expression forces the wrap regardless). Total ≈ **−38**, not −45, and +1 if `Log` is exported.

TWO THINGS THE EVIDENCE GOT RIGHT THAT I EXPECTED TO KILL IT, AND DID NOT:

- The #10013 stopping-point quote is accurate — I read the closing comments. But it is a _deferral_, not a ruling: it justifies the two survivors as "channel-first value sources for injectable/passed contracts, narrowed to the methods actually used", and passing a `Log` satisfies both halves (`Log` is equally injectable; `Pick<Log, 'debug'>` restores the narrowing). Guardrail 4 does not fire.
- Direction of travel is documented the candidate's way, which the evidence never cites: `docs/prds/2026-05-06-prd-logger-v2.md:209` states the target call site is `ctx.log.info(message, fields)` with "channel becomes a field on the per-`child()` logger", and `docs/prds/2026-05-10-prd-cli-runcontext-logger-orchestration.md:218` explicitly wants new hosts not to "inherit the old channel-first API". This is stronger support than anything in the submitted evidence.

<details><summary>Verifier reasoning</summary>

DUPLICATE (guardrail 1): clear. `rg -i "logger|BaseViewMessageHandler|channel-first"` over the round-1 index returns nothing. `rg -i "ViewMessageLogger|BaseViewMessageHandler|SettingsHandlerContext|MainViewInboundHost|createLog"` over `docs/proposals/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md` returns 3 hits, all at :681/:685/:699 and all about `AgentDirectoryServiceLogger` in `src/agent/index/AgentDirectoryService.ts` — a different candidate in a different tree. Round-1 PRs #11416-#11422: #11417 touched `ProgressViewMessageHandler.ts` but left the logger contract alone; the base class is untouched since #10875 (2026-08-18). Not already done at HEAD — `rg "import \* as logger"` still returns exactly two production survivors, `BaseViewMessageHandler.ts:6` and `SupabaseAuthProvider.ts:25` (everything else is `src/test-kernel/` plus the `logUtils.ts` self-import).

ALREADY FILED (guardrail 2): clear. `gh issue list --state all --search` on "BaseViewMessageHandler", "channel-first logger", "ViewMessageLogger OR MainViewInboundHost", and "logger channel webview" surfaces only #10013 (CLOSED, the completed migration campaign) and unrelated tracking issues. No open item covers the residual.

CONSUMER COUNT (guardrail 3): re-grepped from scratch and the count holds at 22 (breakdown in corrections item 6). I also checked the surrounding surfaces this repo hides consumers in: no command IDs, no `packages/extension/package.json` contributions, no `commands.ts` registration, no Zod settings key, no `resources/`/`prompts/`/`supabase/` reference — this is a pure in-process typed field. The four other `SettingsHandlerContext` consumers (`githubSubscriptionHandlers`, `subscriptionHandlers`, `memoryHandlers`) and four other `MainViewInboundHost` consumers (`onboardingSlice`, `chatSlice`, `sessionSlice`, `documentSlice`) exist but none reads `.logger`, so the member swap does not reach them. Three subclasses of `BaseViewMessageHandler` in production (Progress, Settings, MainView) plus one test subclass — all four enumerated and checked. The barrel `packages/extension/src/common/webview/index.ts` re-exports only the class, never `ViewMessageLogger`, so the type has zero cross-file consumers (confirmed: `rg ViewMessageLogger` = 2 hits, both in the defining file).

DELIBERATELY JUSTIFIED (guardrail 4): this is where I expected it to die, and it does not. #10013's closing comments do record a deliberate stop, and I read them rather than trusting the quote — they say the two retained sites "stay only as channel-first value sources for injectable/passed contracts, narrowed to the methods actually used." But that reasoning is fully answered by handing over a `Log`, which is itself an injectable value and narrowable with `Pick`. More decisively, the two logger PRDs (`2026-05-06-prd-logger-v2.md:209`, `2026-05-10-prd-cli-runcontext-logger-orchestration.md:218`) rule the _other_ way: the stated target is `ctx.log.info(message, fields)` with channel bound into the logger, and new hosts are explicitly not to inherit "the old channel-first API." No ratchet touches `logUtils`/`createLog` (only `shared-schemas-deep-import-baseline.json` mentions them, incidentally). No AGENTS.md or review-checklist rule applies.

SETTLED SURFACE (guardrail 5): does not apply. This is extension-host code inside `packages/extension/src/`, not the frozen `@agent/*` SDK, the PocketFlow engine, a platform port, a browser-reachable util, the AgentEvent/SessionFact split, or a vscode-free zone (the base class imports `vscode` legitimately).

NET-ADDS (guardrail 6): it removes strictly more than it adds. The only addition is possibly one `export` keyword on `interface Log`, which arrives with three consumers. No new file, no new indirection, no new lint rule. The `channel` string field survives on all three contexts for `showLoggedErrorMessage(channel, …)` / `safeExecuteCommand(cmd, args, channel)` — I confirmed those still take a channel — so nothing downstream churns.

MASKING SITE (guardrail 7): does not apply. Not one `catch`, `??`, `.catch()`, or `default:` arm is deleted. The five base-class log sites at :82/:127/:134/:139/:151 are all preserved verbatim, and #10782 deliberately made them loud — this change does not touch that.

TOO THIN (guardrail 8): no. ~-38 LoC over 10 files eliminating one whole redundant logging surface is exactly the size this repo's own logger campaign shipped as standalone PRs (#10707, #10708, #10710 were each one wave of the same migration). It is the last webview-tree piece of a campaign that otherwise reached zero.

BEHAVIORAL EQUIVALENCE: verified at the source, not assumed. `createLog` (`src/logger/logUtils.ts:243-256`) binds each level to `loggerSelf[level](channel, message[, options])`, read fresh per call, and omits `options` when undefined so arity matches a direct call — the doc comment at :229-242 says this is deliberate to keep `vi.spyOn(logger, 'warn')` working. `ProgressViewOnboardingRefresh.vitest.ts:558-575` is a live witness: it spies the namespace and matches an assertion on a message the base already routes through `this.log`. So the 22 rewrites are observationally identical, including for the ~20 test files that spy on the namespace.

RISK: low. Every change is mechanical and type-checked; there is no dynamic dispatch, no string-keyed access, no `...spread` of the `logger` field (checked), and no serialization boundary. The one thing to get right is the `Log` export mechanics (corrections item 4), which `npm run typecheck` catches immediately.

VERDICT: survives. The mechanism is real (two spellings of one delegation, one of them only because `log` is `private`), the consumer count is right, the campaign that created it explicitly deferred rather than blessed it, and the repo's own PRDs point the same direction. The evidence's errors are provenance and arithmetic, not substance.

</details>

#### Delete twelve stale vi.mock factory keys that stub production exports which no longer exist

- **Lens**: `test-only-exports` · **Kind**: expired-compat · **Risk**: low
- **Net**: -34 LoC, 0 elements (claimed -17 LoC, 0 elements; verifier figure governs)

**Evidence**

A `vi.mock(spec, () => ({...}))` factory silently accepts keys for names the real module does not export, so every production deletion can leave inert stubs behind. I parsed every `vi.mock` factory under `src/test-kernel/` (834 test files), resolved each specifier through the tsconfig alias map, and compared the factory's top-level keys against the target module's source. After discarding four false positives that were nested object keys (`src/test-kernel/tools/ExecutionsToolWorkspaceFiles.vitest.ts:49` inside `getExecutionStore`, `src/test-kernel/tools/setup/CredentialReporting.vitest.ts:82`, `src/test-kernel/progressView/ProgressBackendStreamLifecycle.vitest.ts:84`), twelve keys survive verification.

Lease family (deleted 2026-08-23 by the single-owner-sessions campaign, `git show -s 8eaf242095` = "refactor(runtime): one held state, no reclaim, no durability retention", PRs #11303-#11329):

- `src/agent/storage/executionLease.ts` exports (grep `^export` on the file): `ExecutionLeaseSchema`, `LeaseReapPolicy`, `executionHeldMessage`, `ExecutionLeaseActiveError`, `ExecutionLeaseLostError`, `ownsExecutionLease`, `assertOwnedExecutionLease`, `validateOwnedExecutionLease`, `runWithExecutionLeaseWriteFence`, `acquireFreshExecutionLease`, `acquireResumedExecutionLease`, `releaseOwnedExecutionLease`, `releaseOwnedExecutionLeaseAfterFailure`, `runWithOwnedExecutionLeaseLaunchGuard`, `inspectExecutionLease`, `runWithInactiveExecutionLease`. Neither `abandonOwnedExecutionLease`, `completeOwnedExecutionLease`, nor `markOwnedExecutionLeaseUndurable` is among them; `rg -n "abandonOwnedExecutionLease|completeOwnedExecutionLease|markOwnedExecutionLeaseUndurable" src packages --glob '!node_modules'` returns ONLY test files.
- `src/test-kernel/agent/runtime/AgentLaunchActivation.vitest.ts:40` (`abandonOwnedExecutionLease`), `:43` + hoisted bag entry `:7` (`completeOwnedExecutionLease`), and `:143` `mocks.completeOwnedExecutionLease.mockResolvedValue({ status: 'released' })` — a test arranging behavior on a stub nothing can ever call. The real consumer, `src/agent/runtime/executeAgent.ts:19-23`, imports exactly `acquireResumedExecutionLease`, `assertOwnedExecutionLease`, `releaseOwnedExecutionLeaseAfterFailure`.
- `src/test-kernel/tools/NativeSubagentStrategy.vitest.ts:70` and `:71-73`.
- `src/test-kernel/agent/runtime/ChildRunLoop.vitest.ts:42` (`markOwnedExecutionLeaseUndurable`, zero occurrences anywhere in production).

Other stale keys:

- `src/test-kernel/settings/SettingsNavigation.vitest.ts:5-6` stub top-level `getState`/`setState` on `@shared/hostBridge`; `src/shared/hostBridge.ts` exports only `hostBridge` (`:25`) and `postMessage` (`:30`). Lines `:9-10` (the same names inside the `hostBridge` object) ARE correct — `HostBridgeApi` at `src/shared/hostBridgeTypes.ts:5-9` declares `postMessage`/`getState`/`setState` — so only `:5-6` go.
- `initialize` on `@logger/logUtils` at `src/test-kernel/frontend/RecordingManager.vitest.ts:35`, `src/test-kernel/cli/CliInitPlatform.vitest.ts:105`, `src/test-kernel/commands/setup/SetupAssistantRouting.vitest.ts:184`. `src/logger/logUtils.ts` `^export` list: `LogUtilsOptions`, `disposeAgentChannel`, `isDebugModeEnabled`, `ChannelWriter`, `createChannelWriter`, `setOutputChannelFactory`, `debug`, `info`, `warn`, `error`, `createLog` — no `initialize`, and `rg -n "\binitialize\b" src/logger/logUtils.ts` returns nothing.
- `src/test-kernel/cli/History.vitest.ts:65-67` is a whole `vi.mock('@utils/files/taskRunStorage', () => ({ findExistingRunStoragePath: ... }))`. `src/utils/files/taskRunStorage.ts` has exactly one export, `TaskRunFileService` (`:34`); `findExistingRunStoragePath` does not exist. Worse, the factory shape means the module is replaced by an object carrying NONE of its real exports — `rg -n "taskRunStorage|TaskRunFileService" src/test-kernel/cli/History.vitest.ts` returns only line 65, so nothing in that graph needs it today, but any future import of `TaskRunFileService` under this suite silently resolves to undefined.

Production consumers: zero by construction (all sites are `src/test-kernel/`). Non-production: the 8 files above. Ambiguous (`scripts/`, `docs/scripts/`): none. Dedupe: `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "mock" --limit 15` shows this class is an accepted, previously-filed find in this repo — #10971 "Remove dead getApiKeySecretName member from SecretManager mock" and #10986 "Remove the stale codexSubscriptionSignIn mock from MainViewMessageHandler.vitest", both CLOSED and both about different symbols. Searches for `completeOwnedExecutionLease` and `vi.mock stale` return no matching issue. `rg` over docs/proposals/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md finds none of these symbols, and none of the 90 round-1 titles covers stale mock keys. `git log --oneline -15` on each test path shows all eight files were last touched by the refactors that removed the symbols, not by a deliberate decision to keep the stubs.

**Proposal**

1. `src/test-kernel/agent/runtime/AgentLaunchActivation.vitest.ts`: delete the `completeOwnedExecutionLease` entry from the `vi.hoisted` bag (`:7`), the `abandonOwnedExecutionLease` and `completeOwnedExecutionLease` keys from the `@agent/storage/executionLease` factory (`:40`, `:43`), and the now-orphaned arrangement at `:143`.
2. `src/test-kernel/tools/NativeSubagentStrategy.vitest.ts`: delete `:70` and the `:71-73` block from the `@agent/storage/executionLease` factory.
3. `src/test-kernel/agent/runtime/ChildRunLoop.vitest.ts`: delete `:42`.
4. `src/test-kernel/settings/SettingsNavigation.vitest.ts`: delete `:5-6` only; keep `:9-10` inside the `hostBridge` object.
5. Delete the `initialize:` key at `src/test-kernel/frontend/RecordingManager.vitest.ts:35`, `src/test-kernel/cli/CliInitPlatform.vitest.ts:105`, `src/test-kernel/commands/setup/SetupAssistantRouting.vitest.ts:184`.
6. Delete the entire `vi.mock('@utils/files/taskRunStorage', ...)` block at `src/test-kernel/cli/History.vitest.ts:65-67`.
7. Run `npm test` for the eight suites; no production file changes, so no ratchet or typecheck movement is expected.

**What we give up**

Nothing. Every deleted key names a function that no longer exists, so no code path loses a stub. The only behavioral change is that `src/test-kernel/cli/History.vitest.ts` stops shadowing `@utils/files/taskRunStorage` with an object that lacks its real export — which restores, rather than removes, a capability.

**Verifier corrections to the evidence above**

1. "Twelve keys" is wrong — there are ELEVEN stale factory keys in the eight listed files. Counting: AgentLaunchActivation :40 + :43 (2), NativeSubagentStrategy :70 + :71 (2), ChildRunLoop :42 (1), SettingsNavigation :5 + :6 (2), logUtils `initialize` x3 (3), History :66 (1) = 11. The twelfth item the evidence counts is the `vi.hoisted` bag entry at AgentLaunchActivation:7, which is not a mock-factory key.

2. The scan is NOT exhaustive, despite the "parsed every vi.mock factory under src/test-kernel (834 files)" claim. I re-ran the same analysis myself (alias-resolved specifier, brace-depth top-level key extraction, compared against `^export` + `export {…} from` + `export *` closure) and reproduced all 11 claimed keys exactly, plus THREE more real stale sites of the identical class that the candidate missed:
   - src/test-kernel/tools/CodexResumeFallback.vitest.ts — hoisted bag entry :19, the whole `vi.mock('@utils/files/taskRunStorage', () => ({ ensureRunDir: mocks.ensureRunDir }))` at :69-71, and the orphan arrangement `mocks.ensureRunDir.mockResolvedValue(undefined)` at :147.
   - src/test-kernel/tools/ClaudeAgentResumeFallback.vitest.ts — same shape at :25, :75-77, :161.
   - src/test-kernel/progressView/FollowUpEventHandlers.vitest.ts:13-15 — `vi.mock('@progressView/frontend/components/ExternalInquiryPanel', () => ({ clearInquiryDraft: vi.fn() }))`. That module's ONLY export is `class ExternalInquiryPanel` (packages/extension/src/progressView/frontend/components/ExternalInquiryPanel.ts:92); `clearInquiryDraft` is IMPORTED there from `../slices/inquiryDraftState` (:44-51) and called at :534.
     The PR should be 11 files, not 8.

3. "`findExistingRunStoragePath` does not exist" is factually wrong and it is the most load-bearing error in the write-up. It exists at src/utils/files/runStorageFs.ts:44 with three production consumers (src/tools/ExecutionsTool.ts:810, src/tools/executions/runGeneratedFiles.ts:53, src/tools/AcceptRunFilesTool.ts:115). The same is true of `ensureRunDir` (src/utils/files/runStorageFs.ts:141, consumed by src/controllers/progressView/ProgressWorkflowFileActionsController.ts:66 and src/tools/delegation/subagentResults.ts:461). The defect is a WRONG-MODULE mock — four `vi.mock` blocks aim at `@utils/files/taskRunStorage` (single export `TaskRunFileService`, :34) / at ExternalInquiryPanel, and have therefore silently intercepted nothing while shadowing a module the graph may need. That reframing matters for the PR body and for the honesty of "What we give up: Nothing."

4. "What we give up: Nothing" is slightly overstated for the four whole-block deletions. Removing them un-shadows real modules: History and the two ResumeFallback suites start seeing the real `@utils/files/taskRunStorage`, and FollowUpEventHandlers starts really importing the Lit component module `ExternalInquiryPanel`. I verified all four are safe — see reasoning — but this is a real (if benign) module-graph change, not a no-op, and the PR should say so.

5. Minor line-number slip: in SettingsNavigation.vitest.ts the correct keys inside the `hostBridge` object are :8-10 (`postMessage` :8, `getState` :9, `setState` :10); the object opens at :7. Evidence cites ":9-10". The keys to delete (:5, :6) are cited correctly.

6. LoC is understated. For the 8-file version I measured exactly -17 raw, but deleting the History block leaves a double blank line that `prettier` collapses, so the formatted result is -18. With the three missed files folded in, the formatted batch measures -34 lines across 11 files (`git diff --stat` after `npx prettier --write`).

7. Everything else in the evidence checks out verbatim: `git show -s 8eaf242095` = "refactor(runtime): one held state, no reclaim, no durability retention"; the executionLease export list (16 names, src/agent/storage/executionLease.ts:45-799) is exact and contains none of the three lease symbols; `src/logger/logUtils.ts` has no `initialize`; every cited line number in the 8 files is correct at HEAD (d8c6dfc071); `rg` for the three lease symbols across src/packages hits only test files (plus four docs/proposals + docs/prds prose references, which the evidence omitted but which are historical design docs, not consumers).

The three executionLease `vi.mock` calls survive (only keys are dropped), so no row disappears; the deleted whole blocks target `@utils/files/taskRunStorage` and `@progressView/...`, which the baseline does not track (its History.vitest.ts row is `@agent/storage`, untouched). Zero ratchet movement, as claimed. knip and typecheck are unaffected: `vi.mock`'s factory parameter is typed `() => unknown`, so extra keys were never type-checked, and no production file changes.
- Touches no frozen surface: not the @agent SDK, PocketFlow engine, platform ports, browser-safe utils, AgentEvent/SessionFact split, or vscode-free zones.

MASKING (checklist §15)

- Nothing removed is a catch, a `??`, a `.catch()`, or a `default: return`. The eleven inert keys mask nothing — they cannot be reached. The four wrong-module blocks are themselves an M-class hazard (a factory mock replaces the whole module, so any future `import { TaskRunFileService }` under those suites silently binds `undefined`); deleting them is the loud fix, and I verified empirically that it does not paper over a failure.

NET / THINNESS

- Net -34 formatted lines, 0 elements, 0 production files, 11 test files, all green. It is mechanical and test-only, but it is a coherent batch of 15 sites in one class with repo precedent (#10971, #10986 shipped the single-symbol version), and four of the sites are latent traps rather than cosmetics. Thin, but above the batched-standalone bar.

</details>
