(2) `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "lineChanges"` → #10580 and #6705, both CLOSED; #10580's closing ruling preserves `edits[].lineChanges`, not the roll-up, so it does not bar this. Nothing filed for `recordEdits`. (3) Consumer count re-grepped myself and corrected above. (4) No dated proposal, ratchet, AGENTS.md rule, or review-checklist section covers this; `grep -rn lineChanges docs AGENTS.md .claude` returns one incidental line. The nearest prior ruling is the Dec-2025 `fallbackLineChanges` commit, which created the fill but never a reader. (5) Settled surfaces untouched — no `@agent/*` deep import changes, no PocketFlow engine, no platform port, no browser-safe utils, no AgentEvent/SessionFact change, no ratchet baseline change (no export is added or removed, `LineChangesSchema` keeps its `EditRecordSchema` consumer, so `knip-baseline.json` is unaffected). (6) Net negative, no new element, no unrelated churn. (7) Not a masking site: the deleted `if (!result.lineChanges && trackedEdits.lineChanges)` is a fill-if-absent for a write-only field, not a swallowed error — checklist §15 M1-M6 does not apply, and deleting it hides no failure because nothing downstream ever observed the value. (8) Thin-ness is the only real weakness: −28 LoC across five files. It is a genuine end-to-end write-only chain (schema field + producer + accumulator + sanitizer entry), the same shape as things #11421 shipped, but it should ride in a batched schema-cleanup PR rather than stand alone.

**Behavioral give-up, verified rather than asserted.** The persisted `ToolUseLog.output` stops carrying a per-call `+N/-M`. No renderer, no model-facing serializer, and no archive formatter reads it today. `formatToolResultAsText` — the only thing that turns a persisted `ToolResult` back into text — ignores it. So the change is invisible in all four hosts and in the exported transcript.

</details>

#### Erase the phantom `C` client-type parameter from the flow layer — every production instantiation is `unknown`

- **Lens**: `speculative-generality` · **Kind**: speculative-generality · **Risk**: low
- **Net**: -20 LoC, 0 elements (claimed -65 LoC, 0 elements; verifier figure governs)

**Evidence**

THE GENERIC. `AgentCore<C = unknown>` (src/agent/core/flows/BaseFlowServices.ts:47) carries `modelCell: ModelCell<C>` (:54). `C` then threads down through `BaseFlowContextInit<C>` (:66), `CycleRunServices<C>` (CycleServices.ts:19), `ToolUseRoundServices<C>` (CycleServices.ts:40), `ReflectionServices<C>` (ReflectionServices.ts:20-22), `ToolUseServices<C>` (ToolUseServices.ts:10), `FollowUpMessageServices<C>`/`FollowUpBatchServices<C>` (followUpMessages.ts:24,118), the five reflection node classes (`TeXCountNode<C = unknown>`:10, `MediaExtractionNode<C = unknown>`:18, `PrepareContextNode<C = unknown>`:19, `OutputNode<C = unknown>`:48, `ResponseCycleNode<C = unknown>`:37), the four ResponseCycleFlow node classes plus `createResponseCycleFlow<C>` and `responseCycleToolsForModel<C>` (ResponseCycleFlow.ts:108,207,231,435,463,612-636), `createToolUseRoundFlow<C>` (ToolUseRoundFlow.ts:43) and the tooluse nodes, and finally `runReflectionFlow<C = unknown>` (runReflectionFlow.ts:88) and `runToolUseFlow<C = unknown>` (runToolUseFlow.ts:167).

ARITY PROOF — production always binds `unknown`. (1) The single production construction site is `const modelCell = new ModelCell(modelHandler, config.model)` (src/agent/runtime/AgentLaunchContext.ts:360), where `modelHandler` comes from `createModelHandler(...)`/`createModelHandlerForCompatibilityKey(...)` (:347-357). `createModelHandler` is declared `Promise<ModelHandler>` (src/agent/runtime/ModelFactory.ts:390-394) — the erased base with all five defaults, so `C` = `unknown` by construction. (2) `rg -n "runToolUseFlow<|runReflectionFlow<|new ModelCell<|ModelCell<[A-Z]|RunModelHandler<[A-Z]" src packages --glob '*.ts'` returns ZERO production explicit type arguments; the only non-`unknown` bindings anywhere are three test files (src/test-kernel/agent/modelHandlers/ModelClientRouteSnapshot.vitest.ts:22-23 `TestClient`, src/test-kernel/agent/modelHandlers/GoogleClientRefresh.vitest.ts:63 `GoogleGenAI`, src/test-kernel/agent/runtime/ModelCell.vitest.ts:14) — all of which construct `ModelCell` directly and none of which touch the flow layer. (3) The smoking gun that the parameter carries no information: inside `runToolUseFlow<C>` the model-switch path must cast the freshly built handler back into the generic — `(await createModelHandler(nextConfig, ...)) as RunModelHandler<C>` (src/agent/implementations/flows/tooluse/runToolUseFlow.ts:304-307). A type parameter you can only satisfy with a cast is not typing anything. (4) The two flow-layer readers of the client value pass it straight back into the same handler — `const client = await services.modelCell.getClient(); modelHandler.getWireRouteKey(client); ... createResponse({ client, ... })` (src/agent/core/flows/ModelInvocationNode.ts:683-706) and ToolUseDispatchNode.ts:592 — so with `C = unknown` the value stays `unknown` end to end and no assignability is lost.

WHERE THE GENERIC IS REAL (and stays): `ModelHandler<M, U, T, C, Resp, Media>` is genuinely instantiated per provider — `Anthropic` (modelHandlerAnthropic.ts:229-236), `GoogleGenAI` (modelHandlerGoogleInteractions.ts:341-348), `OpenRouter` (modelHandlerOpenRouterNative.ts:97-104), `LanguageModelPort` (modelHandlerVscodeLm.ts:188-195), `OpenAI` (OpenAICompatibleModelHandler.ts:34). The proposal keeps `ModelCell<C = unknown>` and `RunModelHandler<C = unknown>` (src/agent/runtime/ModelCell.ts:10,38) exactly as they are — the erasure stops at `AgentCore`, which is where `createModelHandler`'s erased return type already erases it in fact.

CONSUMER CLASSIFICATION. Production: 20 files listed above, all under src/agent (VS Code-free zone, unchanged). Non-production: one test annotation pair, src/test-kernel/agent/followUp/MediaExtractionNodeTranscriptLog.vitest.ts:15-16,23 (`Partial<ReflectionServices<unknown>>` → `Partial<ReflectionServices>`); the three ModelCell tests are untouched because ModelCell keeps its parameter. Ambiguous (scripts/, docs/scripts/): zero hits. `rg -n "<C>" packages/cli packages/desktop` hits only unrelated generics (`ExecuteAgentResultForCategory<C>` where C extends AgentCategory, runExecution.ts:93-129; `AgentMessage<C extends AgentCommand>`, desktopAgentSettingsController.ts:46) — no host touches the flow-layer `C`.

DEDUPE. Not in the round-1 index (49 shipped / 41 refuted) — the nearest neighbour is "Delete AgentRosterSnapshot.agents", whose write-up notes its `Entry` parameter collapses as a side effect; different symbol, different file, ~20x smaller. `grep -i "ModelCell\|type parameter\|generic\|AgentCore" docs/proposals/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md` finds no claim about `C`. `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "ModelCell generic"` and `--search "type parameter"` return nothing on this (nearest: #9255, about declaring runScope on AgentCore — closed, unrelated). `git log --all --grep=ModelCell` and `git log --oneline -12 -- src/agent/runtime/ModelCell.ts` show the cell landed in #9547 and was ruled on for OWNERSHIP only: docs/proposals/2026-08-01-architecture-rulings-ledger.md:97-149 rules construction/swap/dispose ownership authoritative and says nothing about parameterization; docs/prds/2026-06-29-prd-runtime-gold-standard.md §2 likewise. `git log -L 47,48:src/agent/core/flows/BaseFlowServices.ts` shows `C` predates ModelCell entirely (b713d4fef8 "unify type hierarchy with AgentCore base interface" simply renamed `BaseFlowContextInit<C>` to `AgentCore<C>`), so it is inherited scaffolding, not a decision. No ratchet lists it (`rg "AgentCore|ModelCell" config/ratchets/*.json` → nothing).

**Proposal**

1. In `src/agent/core/flows/BaseFlowServices.ts`, drop the parameter from `AgentCore<C = unknown>` and `BaseFlowContextInit<C = unknown>`; type the field as `readonly modelCell: ModelCell` (default `unknown`).
2. Drop `<C>` from `CycleRunServices`, `ToolUseRoundServices` and the third declaration in `src/agent/core/flows/CycleServices.ts`.
3. Drop `<C>` from `ReflectionServices` and `ToolUseServices`, then from every node class and flow factory that only forwarded it: the five reflection nodes, `ResponseCycleFlow`'s four node classes plus `createResponseCycleFlow` and `responseCycleToolsForModel`, `createToolUseRoundFlow`, and the six tooluse nodes.
4. Drop `<C>` from `runReflectionFlow` and `runToolUseFlow`, and simplify the model-switch cast at runToolUseFlow.ts:304-307 from `as RunModelHandler<C>` to `as RunModelHandler` (or drop it if inference now suffices).
5. Drop `<C>` from `FollowUpMessageServices`/`FollowUpBatchServices` and from `appendFollowUpAsUserMessage`/`applyFollowUpBatch` in src/agent/followUp/followUpMessages.ts; the embedded `IModelHandler<ProviderMessage, unknown, SdkToolCall, C>` becomes `IModelHandler<ProviderMessage, unknown, SdkToolCall>`.
6. Leave `ModelCell<C = unknown>`, `RunModelHandler<C = unknown>`, `IModelHandler`'s five parameters and every concrete handler's instantiation untouched.
7. Update the two annotations in src/test-kernel/agent/followUp/MediaExtractionNodeTranscriptLog.vitest.ts. No other test changes; no behavior changes.
8. Verify with `npm run typecheck` — this is a type-only change, so the compiler is the proof. Let prettier re-collapse the multi-line generic argument lists (that is where most of the LoC comes from).

**What we give up**

The theoretical ability to run a flow against a handler whose provider client type is statically known — e.g. a future `runToolUseFlow<Anthropic>` where a node reads `getClient()` as a typed `Anthropic`. Nothing does this today and nothing can, because `createModelHandler` returns the erased `ModelHandler`. Restoring it means re-adding one type parameter to the same declaration chain (mechanical, compiler-guided) AND narrowing `createModelHandler`'s return type, which is the actual blocker either way.

**Verifier corrections to the evidence above**

1. **CONSUMER COUNT WRONG BY ~19x (tests).** "Update the two annotations in `src/test-kernel/agent/followUp/MediaExtractionNodeTranscriptLog.vitest.ts`. No other test changes" is false. Nine test files carry explicit type arguments on the erased declarations and every one becomes a TS2315 ("Type ... is not generic") error the moment the parameter is dropped. My grep (`rg -n "AgentCore<|BaseFlowContextInit<|CycleRunServices<|ResponseCycleServices<|ToolUseRoundServices<|ReflectionServices<|ToolUseServices<|...Node<|create...Flow<|run...Flow<" src packages | grep -v flows dirs`) returns, outside the 20 production files, exactly:

- `src/test-kernel/agent/core/ToolUseProcessNodeResponseFinalized.vitest.ts:15,38,50,51` (4 sites, incl. `ToolUseProcessNode<unknown>`)
- `src/test-kernel/agent/followUp/ToolUsePrepareNodeTranscriptLog.vitest.ts:14,15,37` (3)
- `src/test-kernel/agent/followUp/MediaExtractionNodeTranscriptLog.vitest.ts:15,16,23` (**3**, not 2)
- `src/test-kernel/agent/followUp/ToolUseWaitNodeFollowUpTranscriptLog.vitest.ts:15,16,33,50,59,70` (6)
- `src/test-kernel/agent/followUp/ToolUseRoundPrepNodeFollowUpTranscriptLog.vitest.ts:10,11,25,38` (4)
- `src/test-kernel/agent/followUp/ToolUseProgressEvents.vitest.ts:99,108,150,151` (4)
- `src/test-kernel/agent/ToolUseDispatchParallel.vitest.ts:106,107,137,142,152` (5)
- `src/test-kernel/agent/ResponseCycleContinuation.vitest.ts:75,84,102,140` (4)
- `src/test-kernel/tools/BashTool.vitest.ts:160,162,164,566,965` (5)
  ≈ 38 annotation sites across 9 files, not 2 sites in 1 file. All mechanical, none behavioral, but the PR is 29 files, not 21.

2. **THE ARITY PROOF'S "ZERO NON-`unknown` BINDINGS OUTSIDE THREE UNRELATED TESTS" IS FALSE, AND UNPROVABLE BY THE COMMAND QUOTED.** `src/test-kernel/tools/BashTool.vitest.ts` binds the flow layer at a concrete client type in five places: `ToolUseRoundServices<OpenAI>` (:160, :162, :164) and `new ToolUseDispatchNode<OpenAI>()` (:566, :965). The quoted proof command (`rg -n "runToolUseFlow<|runReflectionFlow<|new ModelCell<|ModelCell<[A-Z]|RunModelHandler<[A-Z]"`) never searches the flow-service or flow-node names, so it structurally could not have found them. The _production_ arity claim still holds (see reasoning), and the `<OpenAI>` binding is inert because `testModelCell<C>(handler: unknown)` (`src/test-kernel/agent/modelCellTestUtils.ts:8-13`) casts its argument anyway — but the evidence's blanket statement is wrong as written and should not be repeated in the PR body.

3. **-65 LoC IS ~3-4x TOO HIGH; THE "PRETTIER RE-COLLAPSES" MECHANISM MOSTLY DOES NOT FIRE.** Nearly every multi-line declaration stays multi-line after erasure because the _remaining_ type arguments still blow 80 columns:

- `export class TeXCountNode extends BaseNode<ReflectionFlowShared, ReflectionServices> {` = 86 chars
- `class ResponsePrepNode extends BaseNode<ResponseCycleShared, ResponseCycleServices> {` = 85 chars
- `export function createResponseCycleFlow(): Flow<ResponseCycleShared, ResponseCycleServices> {` ≈ 92 chars
- `export class MediaExtractionNode extends BaseNode<...>` ≈ 93 chars
  Only four declarations in the whole flow layer actually put `C` on its own line and can therefore collapse: `CycleServices.ts:28`, `ReflectionServices.ts:21`, `runReflectionFlow.ts:69`, `runToolUseFlow.ts:63` (verified with `rg -n '^\s*C(\s*=\s*unknown)?,\s*$'`; the other four hits of that pattern are in `IModelHandler.ts`, `ModelHandlerContracts.ts`, `ModelHandler.ts`, `ModelCell.ts` — all deliberately untouched). Every other ~95 occurrence is an in-line deletion with zero line delta. The nearest precedent confirms the scale: **#8563 `62200489ad` "remove model handler type indirection"** removed one type parameter from `IModelHandler` and the same handler chain across 25 files for **60 insertions / 85 deletions = net -25 LoC**. Realistic here: -15 to -25.

4. **THE STRONGEST PIECE OF EVIDENCE IS MISSING FROM THE WRITE-UP.** `ModelInvocationNode` — the one kernel node that actually consumes the client value (`services.modelCell.getClient()` → `handler.createResponse({ client })`) — **is not parameterized by `C` at all**; it is declared over `<Shared, Services>` and constrains against the _bare_ `AgentCore` (`src/agent/core/flows/ModelInvocationNode.ts:15, :166`). Two more production sites already use `AgentCore` at arity 0: `AgentLaunchContext extends AgentCore` (`src/agent/runtime/AgentLaunchContext.ts:82`) and `type WorkspaceScopedCore = AgentCore & { workspace: AgentWorkspaceState }` (`src/agent/core/flows/CommonCycleTypes.ts:85`). So the erased form of `AgentCore` is already the form the client-reading kernel node compiles against — that, not the `as RunModelHandler<C>` cast, is the decisive proof the parameter carries no information.

5. **PRIOR ART EXISTS AND SHOULD BE CITED (it supports the change, it does not rule against it).** Besides #8563, `155c733c6b` / #6798 "remove dead R type parameter from IModelHandler chain" did the identical class of change on a sibling parameter of the same declaration. Neither commit message nor `docs/proposals/2026-08-01-architecture-rulings-ledger.md:97-149` nor `docs/prds/2026-06-29-prd-runtime-gold-standard.md` says anything about `C`'s parameterization — the candidate's "ownership only" reading of the ledger is correct. The right framing for the PR is "the third parameter in the same sweep", not "novel erasure".

6. **Minor line-reference fixes.** `createModelHandlerForCompatibilityKey` also returns the erased `Promise<ModelHandler>` and sits at `src/agent/runtime/ModelFactory.ts:420-424`, not inside the cited 390-394 range (which is `createModelHandler`); both matter to the arity proof and only one was cited. `FollowUpMessageServices`/`FollowUpBatchServices` are declared `<C>` with **no default** (`followUpMessages.ts:24, 118` — refs correct), unlike every other member of the chain, so their erasure is the one that cannot be a no-op for a caller that omitted the argument; in fact no caller supplies it (`ToolUseProcessNode.ts:219,257`, `ToolUseRoundPrepNode.ts:75`, `ToolUseWaitNode.ts:200` all infer), so it is still safe.

7. **Path list is otherwise exactly right for production.** I independently reproduced the 20-file production set (`rg -ln "\bC\b"` over `core/flows`, `implementations/flows`, `followUp` minus false positives) and it matches the candidate's list member-for-member, with no omission and no extra. Nothing in `packages/agent/src/` mentions any of these symbols (not on the frozen SDK surface), `rg "AgentCore|ModelCell|CycleServices|BaseFlowServices" config/ratchets/*.json` returns nothing, and no host package binds the flow-layer `C`.

8. **"0 elements" is defensible but should be stated honestly.** No exported symbol is added or removed; what disappears is one type parameter at ~133 textual occurrences. The value of this PR is conceptual (one fewer cross-cutting generic threaded through 20 files), not LoC — and the PR body should say so rather than leading with a -65 number it cannot deliver.

<details><summary>Verifier reasoning</summary>

**Dedupe (kill-check 1 & 2): clean.**

- Not in the round-1 index: I read all 90 titles. The nearest neighbour is indeed "Delete AgentRosterSnapshot.agents" (shipped), whose write-up at survey line 1012 notes its `Entry` parameter collapses as a side effect — different symbol, different file, and notably that candidate shipped at "~5 LoC" partly _because_ "it also collapses a generic parameter to nothing". That is a useful precedent for the too-thin question here.
- `grep -in "ModelCell\|AgentCore\|type parameter\|generic\|phantom\|ToolUseRoundServices" docs/proposals/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md`: hits are all unrelated (compactRows "phantom", `uploadGoogleMediaEntries<T>`, `AgentRosterSnapshot<Entry>`, a `P` generic in ProgressBackend, and two entries that merely _mention_ `AgentCore` while refuting other claims — survey line 2149 quotes `InvocationServices = Pick<AgentCore, ...>` in the course of refuting the `getPostCompactionContext` fold, which is a different finding and does not rule on parameterization).
- `git log --oneline -8 -- src/agent/core/flows/BaseFlowServices.ts` shows the last touches are #10875, #10594/#10647, #9569, #9547, #9519, `b2c95bcd65` (declare runScope on AgentCore), #8781, #8563 — none removes `C`. Round-1 PR #11419 (`a5fdd1b504`) did touch `ReflectionServices.ts`, `MediaExtractionNode.ts`, `OutputNode.ts`, `ResponseCycleNode.ts`, `followUpMessages.ts`, but for un-exporting types; `C = unknown` is still at `ReflectionServices.ts:21` and `BaseFlowServices.ts:47` at HEAD (`d8c6dfc071`). NOT already done.
- `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "generic type parameter ModelCell"` → empty.

**Kill-check 3 (consumer count) — production side holds, test side does not.** I re-grepped every affected symbol across `src` and all four packages. Outside the 20 production files there is **zero** production consumer: no host, no `packages/agent/src`, no `packages/cli`, no `packages/desktop`, no `packages/extension`. The only cross-package `<C>` hits are unrelated generics (`ExecuteAgentResultForCategory<C extends AgentCategory>`, `MessageFor<C extends ...Message['command']>`, `AgentMessage<C extends AgentCommand>`). This is a pure type-level change, so `packages/extension/package.json` contributions, `commands.ts`, `*Settings.ts` schemas, `resources/`, `prompts/`, `supabase/functions/` are all structurally out of scope — none can reference a TS type parameter. Nine _test_ files do break; see corrections.

**Production arity independently re-proved (three ways, one of which the candidate missed).**
(a) `createModelHandler` → `Promise<ModelHandler>` (`ModelFactory.ts:390-394`) and `createModelHandlerForCompatibilityKey` → `Promise<ModelHandler>` (`:420-424`); `ModelHandler` is declared `<M=..., U=unknown, T=..., C=unknown, Resp=unknown, Media=unknown>` (`modelHandlers/ModelHandler.ts:195-202`), so the bare reference pins `C=unknown`. Both factory results feed the single `new ModelCell(modelHandler, config.model)` at `AgentLaunchContext.ts:360` → `ModelCell<unknown>`.
(b) `AgentLaunchContext extends AgentCore` (bare, `:82`) — the run's services bag is spread from this, so every flow infers `C = unknown` from the caller down.
(c) `ModelInvocationNode` constrains on bare `AgentCore` (`:166`) and is the only site that reads `getClient()` and passes the value back to `createResponse({ client })`. Erasing therefore cannot change assignability at the one place the client value is actually typed. `ToolUseDispatchNode.ts` reaches the same handler through `ToolUseRoundServices<C>` and would go `unknown → unknown` — identical to today.
The `as RunModelHandler<C>` cast at `runToolUseFlow.ts:304-307` is real and reads exactly as claimed; it is a fair (if weaker) smoking gun.

**Kill-check 4 (deliberately justified): searched, nothing found.** The rulings ledger `docs/proposals/2026-08-01-architecture-rulings-ledger.md:97-149` and `docs/prds/2026-06-29-prd-runtime-gold-standard.md` govern ModelCell _ownership_ (construct/swap/dispose), not parameterization — the candidate's reading is correct and I confirmed it. AGENTS.md / the review checklist have no rule protecting provider-client typing in the flow layer. To the contrary, the repo has shipped this exact class of change twice: #8563 (`62200489ad`, "remove model handler type indirection") and #6798 (`155c733c6b`, "remove dead R type parameter from IModelHandler chain"). Precedent runs _for_.

**Kill-check 5 (settled surface): not touched.** `packages/agent/src/{index,node,schemas}.ts` never mention `BaseFlowServices`, `CycleServices`, `ToolUseServices`, `ReflectionServices`, `runToolUseFlow`, `runReflectionFlow` or `ModelCell`, so nothing on the frozen `@texra-ai/agent` surface changes arity. `rg "AgentCore|ModelCell|CycleServices|BaseFlowServices" config/ratchets/*.json` → nothing, so no ratchet moves. No import specifier changes at all, so `host-agent-import-baseline` cannot widen. No `bus.emit`, no AgentEvent/SessionFact change, no PocketFlow engine change (`BaseNode`/`Flow` signatures untouched — only the type arguments passed to them), no vscode-free-zone edge, no browser-safe utils. Provider-level generality is explicitly preserved: `ModelHandlerAnthropic extends ModelHandler<MessageParam, BetaUsage, AnthropicToolCall, Anthropic, BetaMessage, ContentBlockParam>` (`modelHandlerAnthropic.ts:229-236`) and siblings stay exactly as they are, as does `ModelCell<C = unknown>` / `RunModelHandler<C = unknown>` (`ModelCell.ts:10, 38`). The erasure stops precisely where `createModelHandler`'s return type already erased it in fact.

**Kill-check 7 (masking site): N/A.** No catch, no fallback, no `??`, no Zod `.catch` is deleted. The only deleted defensive construct is a type assertion (`as RunModelHandler<C>`), which is the opposite of a masking site — it is a hole being closed, and §15's M1-M6 taxonomy does not apply to type-level assertions with no runtime effect.

**Kill-check 6 (net-adds) and 8 (too thin): the real weak point, and it survives narrowly.** The claimed -65 does not hold (see correction 3); realistic is -15 to -25 across 29 files with ~133 mechanical edit sites and zero exported-element change. That is close to the too-thin line. It clears it on three grounds: (i) direct in-repo precedent — #8563 merged at exactly this shape and scale (25 files, net -25) and the round-1 survey itself shipped a ~5-LoC generic collapse on the grounds that "it also collapses a generic parameter to nothing"; (ii) it is compiler-verified end to end, so the risk of the "reduction PR nets positive" failure mode from checklist §13 is bounded — there is no new abstraction, no relocation, no added helper, only deletions; (iii) it removes a genuine cross-cutting concept from the codebase's most-read subsystem — 20 files in the flow layer currently carry a type parameter that no production code can bind to anything but `unknown`, and every new node added to either flow family has to keep re-threading it. The PR body must lead with that, not with a LoC number.

**Risk: low, not medium.** Type-only; `npm run typecheck` is a total proof; zero runtime bytes change (TS type parameters emit nothing). The only real cost is 38 mechanical test annotation edits the candidate did not budget for, and one of them (`BashTool.vitest.ts`'s `<OpenAI>`) is safe only because `testModelCell` already casts — worth checking explicitly during implementation rather than assuming. Recommend implementing as a single PR with the corrected file list (20 prod + 9 test), and dropping the `as RunModelHandler<C>` cast at `runToolUseFlow.ts:304-307` only if inference genuinely suffices after erasure (`ModelHandler` → `RunModelHandler` may still need the assertion; verify, do not assume).

</details>

#### Fold ReviewIssueReport into the Zod schema of the tool that produces it, and delete the seven-field re-projection

- **Lens**: `dual-representation` · **Kind**: dual-representation · **Risk**: low
- **Net**: -14 LoC, -1 elements (claimed -16 LoC, -1 elements; verifier figure governs)

**Evidence**

A Zod schema and a hand-written interface for the same payload, one field-for-field copy of the other.

Schema: `ReportReviewIssueInputSchema` at src/tools/ReportReviewIssueTool.ts:18-46 — file, startLine, endLine, severity, title, description, suggestion.
Interface: `ReviewIssueReport` at src/agent/review/reviewIssues.ts:35-43 — the same seven names, same order, same optionality (`endLine?`, `suggestion?` vs the schema's `.nullish()`), same `ReviewSeverity` for `severity`. The tool already imports the vocabulary from the interface's file (`import { REVIEW_SEVERITIES } from '@agent/review/reviewIssues'`, ReportReviewIssueTool.ts:5), so the two are already coupled — just in the wrong direction to eliminate the copy.

The tax is a third writing of the same seven fields: ReportReviewIssueTool.ts:69-78 builds `sink({ file: input.file, startLine: input.startLine, endLine: input.endLine ?? undefined, severity: input.severity, title: input.title, description: input.description, suggestion: input.suggestion ?? undefined })` — a pure `null`→`undefined` re-projection that exists only because the two shapes disagree on how they spell 'absent'.

Consumers, production (`rg "ReviewIssueReport|reportReviewIssue"` over src and packages, minus test-kernel): src/agent/review/reviewIssues.ts:62 (`createReviewIssue(report: ReviewIssueReport)`), src/agent/runtime/HostInteractions.ts:1 and :73 (`ReportReviewIssueSink`), src/agent/review/index.ts:32 (re-export, documented public surface), packages/extension/src/frontend/review/AgentReviewService.ts:29 and :390 (`addIssueReport`), packages/extension/src/progressView/extensionHostInteractions.ts:39 (wiring). Five files, one host. Non-production: no test-kernel file names `ReviewIssueReport`.

Null-tolerance is already there: `createReviewIssue` (reviewIssues.ts:63-73) reads `report.endLine ?? startLine` and `report.suggestion?.trim() || undefined`, both of which behave identically for `null` and `undefined`. AgentReviewService.addIssueReport spreads the report into `createReviewIssue` (:409-410), so it never branches on absence either.

Direction is legal and creates no new edge: today src/tools imports from @agent/review; after the move src/tools still imports from @agent/review, and @agent/review gains only a `zod` import. It does NOT create an @agent → @tools edge (which is what an inverted fix would have needed).

Dedupe: `git log --oneline -5` on both paths shows b0bfa1f41e, 56d649559f, b78963e722, 9960863306, c256bc8ef0 — none touches the pair. `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "ReviewIssueReport"` returns zero rows. `grep -i 'reviewIssue|report_review_issue'` over docs/proposals/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md: zero hits; no round-1 index title matches.

**Proposal**

1. Move `ReportReviewIssueInputSchema` (with its `.describe()` strings, which are the tool's model-facing docs) from src/tools/ReportReviewIssueTool.ts:18-46 into src/agent/review/reviewIssues.ts, beside `REVIEW_SEVERITIES`, and export it.
2. Replace the `ReviewIssueReport` interface (reviewIssues.ts:35-43) with `export type ReviewIssueReport = z.infer<typeof ReportReviewIssueInputSchema>;`. Keep the one-line doc comment.
3. In ReportReviewIssueTool.ts, import the schema instead of declaring it; keep `normalizeStructuredOutputSchema(...)` and the class unchanged. Replace the seven-field re-projection at :69-78 with `const result = sink(input);`.
4. Update src/agent/review/index.ts's re-export list if the schema should join the documented surface (it need not — the tool imports the file directly, and CLAUDE.md's barrel rule says a barrel only carries a documented public surface).
5. No ratchet change: no export is deleted, and `ReviewIssueReport` keeps its name and its importers.

**What we give up**

Downstream types widen from `T | undefined` to `T | null | undefined` for `endLine` and `suggestion`, matching what the wire actually carries. The two current readers already handle null identically, but any future reader must too. Also gives up the freedom to state the host-facing report shape independently of the tool's input shape — which is only useful if a second producer of review findings ever appears, and there is none today.

**Verifier corrections to the evidence above**

1. **Test coverage claim is wrong and materially so.** The evidence says "Non-production: no test-kernel file names `ReviewIssueReport`." Literally true, but it hides the fact that `src/test-kernel/tools/ReportReviewIssueTool.vitest.ts` exists and pins the exact projection this candidate deletes. Two of its six tests break:
   - `it('normalizes null optional fields to undefined for the sink')` calls `tool.call({ ...REPORT, endLine: null, suggestion: null })` and asserts `expect.objectContaining({ endLine: undefined, suggestion: undefined })`. With `sink(input)` the sink receives `endLine: null`, and `toEqual` semantics do NOT treat `null` as equal to `undefined` for a present key, so this fails. The test must be **deleted** (it becomes a tautology once the shapes agree), not "fixed".
   - `it('streams each finding to the sink immediately and unchanged')` asserts `toHaveBeenLastCalledWith({ ...second, endLine: undefined, suggestion: undefined })`. This one survives (missing `.nullish()` keys are absent from the strictObject parse output, and `toEqual` ignores keys whose expected value is `undefined`), but it is worth re-running rather than assuming.
     The PR is not test-free: it deletes ~10 lines of test. That is a bonus, but it must be stated, and it means the `?? undefined` was a _deliberately tested_ behavior (test predates `3db89eada6`, "refactor(review): align issue tool with output spine", 2026-07-19), not incidental glue.

2. **Line numbers off by one on every range.**
   - `ReportReviewIssueInputSchema` is **18-47**, not 18-46 (line 47 is `});`).
   - `ReviewIssueReport` is **35-44**, not 35-43 (line 44 is `}`).
   - The re-projection is **70-78**, not 69-78 (line 69 is `try {`).
   - `createReviewIssue` body is 63-74, not 63-73. The substance of each claim holds.

3. **Dedupe git log is incomplete.** `git log --oneline -5` on the three listed paths (the candidate lists `src/agent/review/index.ts` as a path) has `68bc4fefec` ("refactor(agent): seed export/review/templates Tier-1 barrels", 2026-08-21) as its newest entry; the evidence's list starts at `b0bfa1f41e` and omits it. That commit created `src/agent/review/index.ts` and shrank `config/ratchets/host-agent-import-baseline.json` (extension 12→11), so it is exactly the kind of commit the dedupe pass is supposed to see. It does not touch the schema/interface pair, so the verdict is unaffected — but the dedupe as performed did not cover one of the candidate's own paths.

4. **Consumer count phrasing.** `ReviewIssueReport` is _named_ in **4** files (`src/agent/review/reviewIssues.ts`, `src/agent/runtime/HostInteractions.ts`, `src/agent/review/index.ts`, `packages/extension/src/frontend/review/AgentReviewService.ts`). `packages/extension/src/progressView/extensionHostInteractions.ts:39` wires the sink but never names the type. I independently confirmed there is exactly one sink implementation, one `createReviewIssue` call site, and zero CLI/desktop consumers (`rg -ln "agent/review" packages/cli packages/desktop` is empty).

5. **LoC is optimistic.** Claimed -16. My recount: reviewIssues.ts nets **+24** (−10 interface, +29 moved schema, +2 type alias, +3 for a new `// Third-party imports` group with `zod`); ReportReviewIssueTool.ts nets **−38** (−29 schema, −3 zod import group, −1 `ReportReviewIssueInput` alias, −8 projection, +3 for the widened `@agent/review/reviewIssues` import that prettier will wrap to four lines). Production net ≈ **−14**, plus ≈ **−10** in the vitest file.

6. **Element accounting is one-sided.** `ReportReviewIssueInput` is deleted (−1 declaration), but `ReportReviewIssueInputSchema` goes from module-private const to an **exported** symbol (+1 export). Net declarations −1; net _exported_ surface +1. The new export has a same-PR consumer (the tool) so `npm run check:dead-code-ratchet` / knip stays green, but the "exports are contracts" rule applies and the PR body should say so.

7. **One argument the evidence missed that strengthens it.** The stated cost of moving the model-facing `.describe()` strings into a domain module is smaller than it sounds: `src/agent/review/reviewIssues.ts` already owns `buildReviewInstruction` and `buildFixInstruction`, i.e. it already carries LLM-facing prompt text, and its own header comment says it "owns the canonical issue shape" plus "the instruction builders for the review and fix agent sessions". The schema belongs there by the module's own charter.

8. **One argument against that the evidence missed.** AGENTS.md:307-315 documents `input.x ?? undefined` as the house pattern for "passing nullish tool values to functions expecting `T | undefined`". A reviewer will cite it. It is not a ruling against this change (it prescribes what to do when the callee's type is fixed, not that domain types must stay narrow), and CLAUDE.md's "Schemas are the single source of truth: define the schema, derive types with `z.infer`" points the other way — but the PR should name the tension rather than let review discover it.

<details><summary>Verifier reasoning</summary>

**Re-read both files at HEAD.** The duplication is real and exactly as described: `ReportReviewIssueInputSchema` (ReportReviewIssueTool.ts:18-47) and `interface ReviewIssueReport` (reviewIssues.ts:35-44) carry the same seven fields, same order, same optionality intent, same `ReviewSeverity` enum, and the tool already imports `REVIEW_SEVERITIES` from the interface's file — so the coupling exists, just backwards.

**Rule 1 (DUPLICATE).** `grep -in "reviewIssue" docs/proposals/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md` hits exactly one line (1054), inside an unrelated un-export candidate's justification, which merely lists what `src/agent/review/index.ts` exports. No candidate targets this pair. Round-1 index: `grep -in "ReviewIssue\|report_review"` returns nothing. The nearest round-1 title, "Un-export 10 types with zero cross-file references in agent trace/roster/review/followUp", explicitly excludes `ReviewIssueReport` (it has cross-file references, four of them). Not a duplicate. Not already done: I read the current files, the interface and the seven-field projection are both still there.

**Rule 2 (ALREADY FILED).** `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "ReviewIssueReport"` → zero rows. `gh issue list --state all --search "report_review_issue in:body,title"` → #8834 and #8829, both CLOSED and both about the structured-output spine, not this pair. `git log --all --grep="ReviewIssueReport"` → nothing.

**Rule 3 (CONSUMER COUNT).** I re-grepped myself with `rg -n "ReviewIssueReport" src packages` and `rg -n "reportReviewIssue" src packages`. Full production set: `reviewIssues.ts:36,62`; `HostInteractions.ts:1,73` (`ReportReviewIssueSink`), plus `:354` and `:526-527` for the capability accessor; `src/agent/review/index.ts:9,32` (barrel re-export); `AgentReviewService.ts:29,390`; `extensionHostInteractions.ts:39` (the only sink wiring). One test file. I also checked the surfaces the brief names: no command ID, no setting key, no `packages/extension/package.json` contribution, no `resources/`, `prompts/`, or `supabase/functions/` reference — the tool name `report_review_issue` is a `defineTool` literal, and the agent YAML references tools by name but nothing here renames the tool. `packages/agent/src` has **zero** "review" hits, so the frozen SDK surface is untouched.

**Null-tolerance actually holds.** `createReviewIssue` uses `report.endLine ?? startLine` (null → startLine) and `report.suggestion?.trim() || undefined` (optional chaining short-circuits on null). `AgentReviewService.addIssueReport` spreads `{ ...report, file: ... }` straight into `createReviewIssue` at :409-410 and only touches `report.file` (a required, non-nullish field) via `path.isAbsolute`. Nothing else reads `endLine` or `suggestion` off the report. So the widening from `T | undefined` to `T | null | undefined` is runtime-safe and typecheck-safe at both readers.

**Rule 4 (DELIBERATELY JUSTIFIED).** I searched for a ruling. `grep -rn "ReviewIssueReport" docs/ .claude/ AGENTS.md` finds one hit: `docs/proposals/2026-08-21-agent-sdk-readiness-reverify.md:194`, which merely records the barrel's export list — not a decision about the shape duplication. The projection's origin is `#5818` (the original agent-review feature commit); `3db89eada6` later added the "streams each finding unchanged" test but did not defend the two-shape split. The closest thing to a contrary rule is AGENTS.md:307-315's `?? undefined` coalescing pattern, which is a how-to for a fixed callee type, not a ruling that the callee type must stay narrow. CLAUDE.md's schema-SSOT mandate cuts the other way. No ratchet, no checklist section, no dated doc decided this.

**Rule 5 (SETTLED SURFACE).** Not touched. `config/ratchets/host-agent-import-baseline.json` records `@agent/review` (one door, extension) — unchanged, since `ReviewIssueReport` keeps its name and its barrel export and the extension's specifier does not move. `src/tools` is not a host, so its existing `@agent/review/reviewIssues` deep import is outside the ratchet and stays as-is. Direction check confirmed: the fix keeps the `@tools → @agent/review` edge and adds only `zod` to `@agent/review`; it does **not** create the illegal `@agent → @tools` edge an inverted fix would need. No import cycle: `reviewIssues.ts` imports only `node:crypto` and `@utils/core`, and nothing in `src/agent/review` imports `src/tools`. `reviewIssues.ts` is not browser-reachable (it already imports `node:crypto`), so the six-browser-safe-utils rule is not in play.

**Rule 6 (NET-ADDS / CHURNS).** Net negative, but smaller than claimed — see corrections. The churn is confined to three production files plus one vitest file, all of which the change genuinely simplifies. No unrelated churn.

**Rule 7 (MASKING SITE).** The `?? undefined` is not a masking site under §15's M1-M6 taxonomy: it is not a `catch {}`, not a `??` over a failed read, not a Zod `.catch` on persisted data, and not a `default: return` that drops an unknown case. It is a pure shape normalization between two spellings of "absent". Deleting it hides no failure. Cleared.

**Rule 8 (TOO THIN).** At −14 production LoC it is thin on its own, but it is a textbook instance of the lens (one interface field-for-field mirroring one schema, plus a hand-written re-projection existing only because the mirrors disagree), it is zero-risk, and it is exactly the size the survey is batching. It stands as a batch member, not as a solo PR.

**Verdict: survives**, with the test-coverage correction being mandatory for the implementing PR — the "no test coverage" framing is the one error that would have caused a red CI run.

</details>
