#### Delete wrapProviderOAuthClient: the subscription-OAuth client wrapper is a provable no-op round-trip since #9922

- **Lens**: `error-paths` · **Kind**: dual-representation · **Risk**: low
- **Net**: -42 LoC, -1 elements (claimed -40 LoC, -1 elements; verifier figure governs)

**Evidence**

WHAT IT DOES TODAY. `src/auth/oauth/providerAuthBridge.ts:42-75` `wrapProviderOAuthClient(client, ErrorType)` wraps both `SubscriptionOAuthClient` methods; its `toShared` (`:46-58`) catches `error instanceof ErrorType` and rethrows `new SubscriptionOAuthError(error.message, error.kind, error.status, { cause: error })`. The opposite conversion, `rethrowAsProviderAuthError` (`:25-36`), runs on the way out of every mutating/refreshing coordinator method via `SubscriptionOAuthCoordinator.mapErrors` (`src/auth/oauth/SubscriptionOAuthCoordinator.ts:134-142`), turning the base class back into the provider subclass. One failure is downgraded and then re-upgraded.

WHY THE DOWNGRADE IS DEAD. PR #9922 made both provider classes SUBCLASSES of the shared one — `src/auth/codex/codexSessionTypes.ts:82` `export class CodexAuthError extends SubscriptionOAuthError` and `src/auth/xai/xaiSessionTypes.ts:60` `export class XaiAuthError extends SubscriptionOAuthError`. Its own PR body (`gh pr view 9922`) says they are now "thin named subclasses used only for `instanceof` narrowing at provider boundaries". Every consumer inside the coordinator therefore already accepts the subclass unchanged: the only internal read is `src/auth/oauth/SubscriptionOAuthCoordinator.ts:308` `if (error instanceof SubscriptionOAuthError && error.kind === 'fatal')`, true for a `CodexAuthError`/`XaiAuthError`. On the way out, `providerAuthBridge.ts:29` `if (error instanceof ErrorType) throw error` rethrows the original instance. Same `message`, `kind`, `status`, `name`, `needsReauth` — one fewer wrapper, original stack preserved.

CALL SITES (production, 2): `src/auth/codex/CodexSessionCoordinator.ts:103` `client: wrapProviderOAuthClient(client, CodexAuthError)` (import at `:8`) and `src/auth/xai/XaiSessionCoordinator.ts:108` `client: wrapProviderOAuthClient(client, XaiAuthError)` (import at `:7`). Both also pass `errorType: CodexAuthError`/`XaiAuthError` on the next line (`:105`, `:110`), which is what actually restores the subclass. Non-production consumers: 0 — `rg wrapProviderOAuthClient src/test-kernel` returns nothing. Ambiguous (scripts/): 0.

EVERY REACHABLE CLIENT THROWS THE SUBCLASS. `src/auth/codex/codexOAuthClient.ts:45` and `src/auth/xai/xaiOAuthClient.ts:40` declare `ErrorType: CodexAuthError` / `XaiAuthError` on their `OAuthFormEndpoint`, and the shared form client throws only through that field (`src/auth/oauth/formTokenClient.ts:74`, `:88`, `:105`). The remaining direct throws are `codexOAuthClient.ts:81,100,142` and `xaiOAuthClient.ts:110,134,141,148,155`. Anything that is NOT the ErrorType is already rethrown untouched by `toShared` (`providerAuthBridge.ts:57`), so no path changes.

NO TEST PINS THE DOUBLE WRAP. The injected-client suites assert only on classified fields: `src/test-kernel/auth/CodexSessionCoordinator.vitest.ts:415-424` (`rejects.toMatchObject({ kind: 'fatal', needsReauth: true })`), `:429-437` (`kind: 'transient'`), `:227-245` (fatal-refresh delete gating), and `src/test-kernel/auth/XaiSessionCoordinator.vitest.ts:153-167` (`kind: 'fatal'`). The one test that asserts a cause chain, `src/test-kernel/agent/modelHandlers/ModelFactoryRouting.vitest.ts:661` (`cause: { name: 'CodexAuthError', kind: 'transient', cause: rawError }`), mocks `getFreshAccessToken` directly and never reaches the client wrapper; that shape is minted by `src/auth/codex/codexAuthAccess.ts:56-79`.

DOWNSTREAM `instanceof` READERS UNAFFECTED (all still see the subclass, now the original instance): `src/agent/runtime/ModelFactory.ts:517`, `src/agent/modelHandlers/openai/modelHandlerCodex.ts:323`, `src/agent/modelHandlers/openai/modelHandlerXAI.ts:178`, `src/controllers/modelAccess/subscriptionUsage/SubscriptionUsageService.ts:281`, `src/auth/codex/codexDeviceLogin.ts:86`, `src/auth/xai/xaiDeviceLogin.ts:78`.

DEDUPE CHECKS RUN. (a) `grep -rln wrapProviderOAuthClient docs/` → only `docs/proposals/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md`, and there it appears ONLY inside the round-1 verifier note for a different candidate (line 1581), which explicitly says the redundancy it was analysing is "NOT by `wrapProviderOAuthClient` at `:102`, which only wraps the HTTP client" — round 1 never assessed the wrapper itself. That round-1 item touched `src/auth/codex/codexAuthAccess.ts` and is already merged (`codexAuthAccess.ts:56` now reads the single `instanceof SubscriptionOAuthError`); this proposal touches different files. (b) `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "SubscriptionOAuthError"` → 0; `--search "auth error"` → 20 rows, none on this seam; `gh issue list --state all --search "wrapProviderOAuthClient"` → 0. Closed #9930 covers RFC-8628 device-polling dedupe, not this. (c) `git log --all --grep=wrapProviderOAuthClient` → 0. (d) `git log --oneline -8 -- src/auth/codex/CodexSessionCoordinator.ts src/auth/xai/XaiSessionCoordinator.ts` → last touch 8b71e6149d (#9990), a broad sweep, nothing about this seam. (e) No settled surface: `providerAuthBridge.ts` appears in none of the five files under `config/ratchets/`; `grep -n 'providerAuthBridge' config/ratchets/knip-baseline.json` → no rows.

**Proposal**

1. Delete `wrapProviderOAuthClient` and its docstring from `src/auth/oauth/providerAuthBridge.ts` (lines 38-75). Keep `ProviderAuthErrorCtor` (`:17-22`, still consumed by `SubscriptionOAuthCoordinator.ts:112,120` and `formTokenClient.ts:41`) and `rethrowAsProviderAuthError` (`:25-36`, still load-bearing: `getFreshSession` throws a bare `SubscriptionOAuthError` at `SubscriptionOAuthCoordinator.ts:284`, and the not-signed-in / session-changed throws at `:207`/`:218` must reach callers as the provider subclass). The `ProviderAuthError` interface at `:11-15` stays as `ProviderAuthErrorCtor`'s return type.
2. In `src/auth/codex/CodexSessionCoordinator.ts`, change `:103` to `client,` and drop the `wrapProviderOAuthClient` import at `:8`. Leave `errorType: CodexAuthError` at `:105` untouched.
3. In `src/auth/xai/XaiSessionCoordinator.ts`, change `:108` to `client,` and drop the import at `:7`.
4. No test edits. Re-run `src/test-kernel/auth/CodexSessionCoordinator.vitest.ts`, `XaiSessionCoordinator.vitest.ts`, `CodexDeviceLogin.vitest.ts`, `src/test-kernel/agent/modelHandlers/ModelFactoryRouting.vitest.ts`, plus `npm run typecheck`.
5. PR note for reviewers: the only observable delta is that a client-thrown `CodexAuthError`/`XaiAuthError` now propagates as the ORIGINAL instance rather than a re-minted copy — one fewer `cause` hop and the original stack. No production or test reader walks that hop.

**What we give up**

Nothing. The provider error class, its `kind`/`status`/`needsReauth` contract, and the `errorType` restoration on the way out all stay exactly as they are. If a future coordinator ever consumed a client that throws a foreign error type needing normalization into `SubscriptionOAuthError`, that wrapper would have to be reintroduced — but no such client exists, and the shared `formTokenClient` is structurally incapable of throwing anything other than the endpoint's declared `ErrorType`.

**Verifier corrections to the evidence above**

Errors and omissions in the candidate's evidence (all re-checked at HEAD d8c6dfc071):

1. NET LoC IS -42, NOT -40. I applied the exact proposed edit; `git diff --stat` reports `2 insertions(+), 44 deletions(-)` across the three files (providerAuthBridge -40, CodexSessionCoordinator -3/+1, XaiSessionCoordinator -3/+1). Net -42.

2. MISSED EDIT IN THE PLAN: the now-unused `import type { SubscriptionOAuthClient } from './SubscriptionOAuthCoordinator';` at `src/auth/oauth/providerAuthBridge.ts:9` must also be deleted. The proposal's step 1 only names lines 38-75 and the docstring; leaving line 9 in place fails lint/typecheck. (This unused import is why the file's deletion count is 40 rather than 38.)

3. MISSED EDIT IN THE PLAN: the module header at `providerAuthBridge.ts:1-4` reads "Bridge between provider-specific auth errors AND the shared SubscriptionOAuthError used inside the coordinator machine" — it documents the bidirectional bridge. After the deletion only one direction remains, so the header needs rewording. Minor, but the plan claims "no other edits".

4. MISCHARACTERIZED THROW SITES (in the candidate's favor). The candidate lists `codexOAuthClient.ts:81,100,142` and `xaiOAuthClient.ts:110,134,141,148,155` as "remaining direct throws". None of them is on the coordinator's client path at all: `codexOAuthClient.ts` line 81 is inside `postJson` (65-88), 100 inside `requestDeviceUserCode` (89-125), 142 inside `pollDeviceToken` (126+); the xAI ones are all inside `requestDeviceCode`/`pollDeviceToken`. The coordinator only ever calls `exchangeAuthorizationCode` (`codexOAuthClient.ts:50`) and `refreshTokens` (`:58`), both of which delegate to `formTokenClient`, which throws exclusively through `endpoint.ErrorType`. So the "no path changes" claim is stronger than stated, not weaker.

5. TEST LINE NUMBERS SLIGHTLY OFF. In `src/test-kernel/auth/CodexSessionCoordinator.vitest.ts` the fatal-refresh test is 412-424 (throw at 415, assertion 419-422), not "415-424"; the transient test is 426-437 (throw at 428), not "429-437". The gating test at :227 and the xAI test at 153-167 check out. `ModelFactoryRouting.vitest.ts:661` is exact. Also worth stating plainly: `rg SubscriptionOAuthError src/test-kernel` returns ZERO hits — no test anywhere references the base class, which is a cleaner proof than the per-test citations.

6. UNVERIFIED-BUT-TRUE claims I confirmed: `ProviderAuthErrorCtor` consumers at `SubscriptionOAuthCoordinator.ts:112,120` and `formTokenClient.ts:41` — exact. `mapErrors` at `:134`, bare `SubscriptionOAuthError` throws at `:207`, `:218`, `:284`, fatal gate at `:308` — all exact. Call sites `CodexSessionCoordinator.ts:8/103/105` and `XaiSessionCoordinator.ts:7/108/110` — all exact.

7. ONE FACT THE EVIDENCE DOES NOT STATE and that materially closes the "future foreign client" hole: `init.client` is optional on both coordinator inits and is injected by NOBODY in production — the only two construction sites are `src/auth/codex/codexAuthAccess.ts:23` and `src/auth/xai/xaiAuthAccess.ts:19`, both `new XSessionCoordinator({ storage })` with no client. Every non-default client is test-only.

<details><summary>Verifier reasoning</summary>

I verified independently rather than trusting the write-up.

DUPLICATE / ALREADY DONE (R1, R2). `wrapProviderOAuthClient` still exists at HEAD (`providerAuthBridge.ts:42`) with both call sites intact, so round 1 did not do this. Repo-wide `rg wrapProviderOAuthClient` → 3 source hits (bridge + 2 coordinators) + 2 docs hits, both inside the round-1 verifier note for the codexAuthAccess candidate at `docs/proposals/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md:1571,1581`, which explicitly excludes this wrapper from its scope ("NOT by wrapProviderOAuthClient at :102, which only wraps the HTTP client"). That round-1 item shipped as #11421 (`git show --stat c11bf8b1f3` touches codexAuthAccess.ts, codexJwt.ts, config.ts, jwtDecode.ts, sessionAccess.ts — none of the three files here). `git log -12` on the three paths: last touch 8b71e6149d (#9990), nothing from #11416-#11422. `git log --all --grep=wrapProviderOAuthClient` → 0. `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "providerAuthBridge OR CodexAuthError OR subscription oauth"` → 3 closed issues, all different scope (#9328 credential-plane seam, #8506 IModelHandler shim, #8879 CodexAuthCoordinator fold); `--search "wrapProviderOAuthClient"` → 0.

CONSUMER COUNT (R3). Production consumers = 2, confirmed by repo-wide rg (no string-literal, command-id, config-key, YAML, prompt, or supabase surface can reach a TypeScript function symbol here; I still grepped all of src + packages). Test consumers = 0. Production coordinator constructions = 2, neither passing a client.

DELIBERATE JUSTIFICATION (R4). #9922's own body is the ruling that makes the wrapper dead, not one that protects it: it declares `SubscriptionOAuthError` the sole owner of the shape and the provider classes "thin named subclasses used only for instanceof narrowing at provider boundaries". No doc, ratchet, or checklist rule defends the downgrade hop. `providerAuthBridge` appears in none of `config/ratchets/`.

SETTLED SURFACE (R5). `src/auth/` is a VS Code-ALLOWED zone, not a frozen surface; no `@agent/*` deep import, no platform port, no AgentEvent/SessionFact split, no browser-safe utils list touched.

MASKING SITE (R7). `toShared` is not a masking site under checklist §15 M1-M6: it never swallows, never defaults, never logs-and-continues. It rethrows on every branch. Deleting it removes a re-mint, not a failure-hiding fallback. The kind/status/needsReauth classification survives untouched on the original instance.

CORRECTNESS OF THE NO-OP CLAIM — traced by hand:

- Before: client throws CodexAuthError → `toShared` re-mints a base `SubscriptionOAuthError` (cause = original) → `performRefresh:308` `instanceof SubscriptionOAuthError && kind==='fatal'` → storage delete → rethrow base → `mapErrors` → `rethrowAsProviderAuthError`: base is NOT instanceof CodexAuthError, so it hits the second branch and mints a NEW CodexAuthError (cause = base).
- After: client throws CodexAuthError → `:308` still true (subclass, since #9922) → same delete → `rethrowAsProviderAuthError`: `instanceof ErrorType` → rethrows the ORIGINAL instance.
  Both end at a CodexAuthError with identical message/kind/status/name/needsReauth. Only the `cause` chain loses one synthetic hop. No production reader walks it: the only `.cause` writers on this path are `modelHandlerCodex.ts:326`, `modelHandlerXAI.ts:180`, `codexAuthAccess.ts:61/73/81`, all of which SET a cause; nothing reads one. All ten `instanceof` readers (`ModelFactory.ts:517`, `modelHandlerCodex.ts:323`, `modelHandlerXAI.ts:178`, `SubscriptionUsageService.ts:281`, `codexDeviceLogin.ts:86`, `xaiDeviceLogin.ts:78`, `xaiOAuthClient.ts:109`, `codexAuthAccess.ts:56`, `providerAuthBridge.ts:30`, `SubscriptionOAuthCoordinator.ts:308`) still match: the subclass satisfies both the subclass check and the base check.
- Foreign-error path: anything not instanceof ErrorType was already rethrown untouched by `toShared:57`, so abort DOMExceptions and storage errors behave identically.
- Method-surface: `SubscriptionOAuthClient` has exactly two methods, both wrapped; passing the raw client loses nothing.

EMPIRICAL VERIFICATION (this is what moves it off the default REFUTED). I applied the exact proposed deletion plus the missed unused-import removal, then:

- `npx vitest run` on CodexSessionCoordinator, XaiSessionCoordinator, CodexDeviceLogin, ModelFactoryRouting → 4 files, 88/88 passing.
- `npm run typecheck` (full workspace: workspace, test-kernel, agent, extension, cli, trace-viewer, desktop) → clean.
- `npx eslint` on the three changed files → exit 0.
  Then `git checkout --` on the three files; `git status --porcelain` clean. The tree is back to HEAD.

R6 (net-adds/churn): pure deletion, zero new elements, no public-API widening, no unrelated file churn beyond the two one-line call sites.

R8 (too thin): -42 LoC and one exported function is on the thin side alone, but it removes a genuine dual-representation round trip (downgrade-then-re-upgrade of the same error) rather than dead weight, and it belongs in the same auth lane as other round-2 items. Batched, it stands.

</details>

#### Delete ModelHandler's abstract computePrice: seven implementations, one production caller, and the cost already rides on NormalizedUsage

- **Lens**: `rederivation-helpers` · **Kind**: dead-export · **Risk**: low
- **Net**: -35 LoC, -7 elements (claimed -45 LoC, -7 elements; verifier figure governs)

**Evidence**

`abstract computePrice(responseUsage: U): number` (src/agent/modelHandlers/ModelHandler.ts:1571) sits three lines above `abstract normalizeUsage(...)`, whose own doc (ModelHandler.ts:1573-1579) states: "This is the single source of truth for usage statistics. Cost is computed once here and should never be recomputed elsewhere." `NormalizedUsage.cost` already carries the fact `computePrice` re-derives.

Seven implementations, each a 1-4 line delegation or a stub: modelHandlerOpenAI.ts:917-919 (-> computeOpenAIPrice), modelHandlerOpenAIResponse.ts:2446-2458 (-> computeOpenAIResponsePrice with the subscription-aware config), modelHandlerAnthropic.ts:1378-1380 (-> computeAnthropicPrice), modelHandlerGoogleInteractions.ts:928-933 (-> computeGoogleInteractionsPrice), modelHandlerOpenRouterNative.ts:559-561 (-> computeOpenRouterPrice), modelHandlerValidation.ts:241-243 (`return 0`), modelHandlerVscodeLm.ts:400-402 (`return 0`).

Production callers of `handler.computePrice(...)`: exactly ONE — modelHandlerOpenAIResponse.ts:2480, the class calling its own `this.computePrice` inside its `normalizeUsage`. `rg -n "computePrice" src packages -g '!*.md'` filtered of the `config.computePrice`/`computePrice:` normalizer-config keys yields no other production site; zero hits anywhere under packages/cli, packages/extension, packages/desktop, packages/trace-viewer, packages/agent. Non-production callers: 14, across 4 suites — CodexSubscriptionFallback.vitest.ts:94,131,250,272; XaiLongContextPricing.vitest.ts:171,178,190,210,230,257,258,272; ModelHandlerAnthropic.vitest.ts:209; ModelHandlerOpenAIResponseBackground.vitest.ts:193. Every one of those assertions is reachable through the public `normalizeUsage(rawUsage, ms).cost`, which computes the identical number by the same route.

The port already excludes it deliberately: src/agent/types/IModelHandler.ts is a `Pick` of ModelHandler and its doc at :14-17 says "Omitted members are reached through the concrete class instead: this includes internal-only helpers such as `computePrice` and `supportsReasoningLevelOverride`". `computePrice` is absent from the Pick list (:34-77). packages/agent/src is only index.ts / node.ts / schemas.ts and `rg -n "modelHandlers" packages/agent/src` returns nothing, so this is not on the frozen SDK surface. `grep -c computePrice config/ratchets/knip-baseline.json` = 0, so no baseline row is involved either way.

The abstract member also actively costs correctness: commit 5c2dd9a66d ("revert unsound computePrice narrowing") had to restore nullable signatures and null guards on three free pricing functions because "a polymorphic caller holding a base-typed reference could legally call computePrice(null)". There is no such caller; the guard exists only because the abstract member makes the call legal. `normalizeUsage` early-returns on falsy raw usage (src/agent/modelHandlers/support/UsageNormalizer.ts:79-88) and hands `NonNullable<T>` to `config.computePrice`.

Dedupe: not in the round-1 index (the two model-handler entries there are "Fold googleHandlerShared back into the only Google handler" (shipped) and "Collapse the two content-stringification knobs in ModelHandlerOpenAI" (refuted)); grep of docs/proposals/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md for compatibilityInference/computePrice-adjacent symbols finds nothing. `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "computePrice"` returns only #7096 (CLOSED, "Delete the hand-mirrored IModelHandler contract; derive the port from ModelHandler") — that issue is what produced the `Pick` and the "internal-only helper" note, i.e. it supports this candidate rather than covering it. `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "normalizeUsage pricing"` is empty. `git log --all --grep=computePrice` shows only the 2026-08-08 narrowing revert and #6794 (routing Responses pricing through a shared helper) — neither attempted removal.

**Proposal**

1. Delete `abstract computePrice(responseUsage: U): number` and its doc comment (src/agent/modelHandlers/ModelHandler.ts:1566-1571). 2. Delete the six pure-delegation/stub overrides: modelHandlerOpenAI.ts:916-919, modelHandlerAnthropic.ts:1377-1380, modelHandlerGoogleInteractions.ts:928-933, modelHandlerOpenRouterNative.ts:559-561, modelHandlerValidation.ts:241-243, modelHandlerVscodeLm.ts:400-402. Each of those classes already calls its module-level `compute*Price` from inside `normalize*Usage`, so nothing needs rewiring. 3. In modelHandlerOpenAIResponse.ts, keep the pricing-config selection but make it a `private responsePricingConfig()` and change :2480 to `(usage) => computeOpenAIResponsePrice(usage, this.responsePricingConfig())`; drop the public `computePrice` (:2445-2458). 4. Retarget the 14 test assertions to `handler.normalizeUsage(usage, 0)?.cost` (or `.cost`, per handler nullability). 5. Drop the now-stale `computePrice` mention from the IModelHandler doc at src/agent/types/IModelHandler.ts:16. Follow-on worth noting in the PR body but not doing here: with the base-typed entry point gone, the nullable arms restored by 5c2dd9a66d in computeOpenAIPrice/computeOpenRouterPrice/computeGoogleInteractionsPrice become unreachable and can be tightened in a separate change.

**What we give up**

The ability to price a raw usage payload without also materializing a `NormalizedUsage`. Nothing in production does that, and `normalizeUsage(usage, 0).cost` is the same number by the same code path. Restoring the member if a future embedder needs it is a one-line abstract declaration.

**Verifier corrections to the evidence above**

1. LoC OVERSTATED (-45 claimed). Actual deletable production spans: ModelHandler.ts 1567-1571 + blank (~6), modelHandlerOpenAI.ts 916-919 + blank (5), modelHandlerAnthropic.ts 1377-1380 + blank (5), modelHandlerGoogleInteractions.ts 928-933 + blank (7), modelHandlerOpenRouterNative.ts 559-561 + blank (4), modelHandlerValidation.ts 241-243 + blank (4), modelHandlerVscodeLm.ts 400-402 + blank (4) = ~35. The Responses step is roughly line-NEUTRAL, not a deletion: modelHandlerOpenAIResponse.ts:2445-2458 (14 lines) is replaced by an ~11-12-line `responsePricingConfig()` plus the rewritten lambda at :2480, so it contributes about -2. Test retargeting (`handler.computePrice(x)` -> `handler.normalizeUsage(x, 0).cost`) is line-neutral. Realistic net ≈ -35 LoC, not -45.

2. "Seven implementations, each a 1-4 line delegation or a stub" is wrong for two of them. Google's is 6 lines (928-933), and the Responses override (2445-2458) is NOT a delegation — it branches on `getUsageProviderCapabilities()` (the subscription-aware zero-rating path) versus `standardPricingConfig()`. It carries real logic that must be relocated, not deleted; the proposal's step 3 does this but the evidence's framing understates it.

3. Line-span slips: the `normalizeUsage` JSDoc runs ModelHandler.ts:1573-1582 (not 1573-1579), with the abstract at 1583-1586. The UsageNormalizer falsy-usage early return is at src/agent/modelHandlers/support/UsageNormalizer.ts:82-90 (not 79-88). `computePrice` on the Responses handler is at :2446 with its doc at :2445 (the evidence cites both 2446-2458 and 2445-2458 in different places).

4. Retarget count is 13, not 14. CodexSubscriptionFallback.vitest.ts already asserts `handler.normalizeUsage(RAW_USAGE, 1000)` toMatchObject `{ cost: 0, usageRoute: 'chatgpt-subscription' }` immediately after the `computePrice(RAW_USAGE)` assertion at :272, so that one site is already redundant and should simply be deleted rather than retargeted.

5. Missing evidence that actually strengthens the case (equivalence proof): the cost equality is not just "same number", it is the same config object on both paths — openAI computePrice and normalizeOpenAIUsage both take `this.standardPricingConfig()` (modelHandlerOpenAI.ts:918 vs :939); anthropic both take `this.pricingConfig()` (:1379 vs :1394); google both take `this.standardPricingConfig()` (:931 vs :942); openrouter likewise (:560 vs :571); validation/vscodeLm are constant 0/undefined. Critically for the retarget, `ModelHandlerXAI.standardPricingConfig()` (modelHandlerXAI.ts:90-115) is where the long-context tier table AND the warn-once `tierGapWarned` side effect live, and `modelHandlerOpenAI.normalizeUsage` calls it exactly once per call — so the XaiLongContextPricing.vitest.ts:257-258 "warns once over two calls" assertion survives the retarget unchanged. Also note ModelHandlerXAI and ModelHandlerCodex do NOT declare `computePrice`; they inherit it, so the deletion has no per-subclass work there.

6. Missing prior art the PR body should cite: docs/proposals/2026-08-07-prod-structural-leads-triage.md:62-64 carries a verified lead and a "fix" ruling on these exact signatures ("narrow the three implementation signatures to the abstract's non-null U and drop the redundant guards"). That ruling was attempted and then reverted by 5c2dd9a66d, which is why the nullable arms exist. It does not rule against deletion — it is superseded by the revert — but the candidate cites only the revert, not the ruling that produced it.

7. Minor mischaracterization of kind: these are class members, not exports; knip never tracked them, so "dead-export" is a loose label (the `grep -c computePrice config/ratchets/knip-baseline.json = 0` I confirmed is therefore expected, not evidence of anything).

<details><summary>Verifier reasoning</summary>

Re-ran every check independently rather than trusting the write-up.

CONSUMER COUNT (kill criterion 3): `rg -n "computePrice" --glob '!node_modules' .` over the whole repo returns exactly 25 files: 7 handler declarations (ModelHandler.ts:1571 abstract, modelHandlerOpenAI.ts:917, modelHandlerOpenAIResponse.ts:2446, modelHandlerAnthropic.ts:1378, modelHandlerGoogleInteractions.ts:928, modelHandlerOpenRouterNative.ts:559, modelHandlerValidation.ts:241, modelHandlerVscodeLm.ts:400 — 8 declarations counting the abstract), the `computePrice:` normalizer-config keys and doc comments in openAIUsage.ts / anthropicUsage.ts / openRouterUsage.ts / googleInteractionsUsage.ts / UsageNormalizer.ts (a different symbol: the config field consumed at UsageNormalizer.ts:99), the IModelHandler doc mention at :16, 6 historical docs, and 14 test call sites in 4 suites. Exactly ONE production call of the METHOD: modelHandlerOpenAIResponse.ts:2480 `(usage) => this.computePrice(usage)` inside its own `normalizeUsage`. Zero hits in packages/cli, packages/extension, packages/desktop, packages/trace-viewer, packages/agent. No command IDs, no settings keys, no package.json contributions, no agent YAML, no prompts, no supabase function reference — the symbol is a class method, so there is no string/dynamic-dispatch surface either (I checked for `['computePrice']`-style access: none).

SETTLED SURFACE (criterion 5): `ls packages/agent/src` = index.ts, node.ts, schemas.ts; `rg -in "modelhandler" packages/agent/src` returns NOTHING, so ModelHandler is not on the frozen SDK barrel. `grep -c computePrice config/ratchets/knip-baseline.json` = 0. No host imports it, so host-agent-import-baseline is untouched. Not the PocketFlow kernel, not AgentEvent/SessionFact, not a vscode-free-zone edge.

EQUIVALENCE (the load-bearing claim): verified per handler that `normalizeUsage().cost` runs the identical pricing function with the identical config — openAIUsage.ts:119 `computePrice: (usage) => computeOpenAIPrice(usage, config)` fed `this.standardPricingConfig()` from modelHandlerOpenAI.ts:939, matching computePrice at :918; anthropicUsage.ts:142 with `pricingConfig()`; googleInteractionsUsage.ts:71; openRouterUsage.ts:56; ModelHandlerValidation.normalizeUsage hardcodes `cost: 0` matching its `return 0`; vscodeLm returns undefined and its computePrice is unreachable. The Responses handler is the only one where normalizeUsage calls the method, so inlining the lambda is mechanical.

TESTS: read all four suites. XaiLongContextPricing drives ModelHandlerXAI, which has no computePrice of its own — its tier pricing, cache rebate and warn-once tier-gap logic all live in `standardPricingConfig()` (modelHandlerXAI.ts:90-115), which `normalizeUsage` calls once per invocation, so all 8 assertions including the "warns once across two calls" case retarget cleanly. CodexSubscriptionFallback and ModelHandlerOpenAIResponseBackground drive ModelHandlerCodex (inherits the Responses computePrice) and one Codex site already asserts through `normalizeUsage`. ModelHandlerAnthropic:209 uses `pricingConfig()`, same route.

MASKING (criterion 7): nothing is being silently deleted — no catch, no `??` fallback, no `.catch()`. The `return 0` stubs in validation/vscodeLm are not masking sites; their normalizeUsage already states cost 0 / undefined explicitly, and the free pricing functions' null guards are explicitly left alone (the PR only notes them as a follow-on), so 5c2dd9a66d's soundness fix is not being reverted by stealth.

DEDUPE: not in round1-index.md (its only model-handler rows are the shipped googleHandlerShared fold and the refuted DashScope stringification collapse). `grep -c computePrice docs/proposals/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md` = 0. `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "computePrice"` returns only #7096 (CLOSED, the IModelHandler Pick derivation) — that issue produced the "internal-only helper" note that supports removal. `git log --all -S"computePrice" -- ModelHandler.ts` and `git log --all --grep=computePrice` show no prior removal attempt; recent commits on these files (78759a464d, 0f280570ed, 273ee5a633, b90fed503f) touch unrelated concerns, so it is not already done at HEAD.

RULINGS (criterion 4): searched docs. The only adjacent ruling is docs/proposals/2026-08-07-prod-structural-leads-triage.md:62-64, which rules on the nullable signatures, not on the member's existence, and was itself reverted by 5c2dd9a66d. docs/proposals/2026-07-15-agent-sdk-readiness-audit.md:186-196 counts computePrice among the "~16-18 abstract methods" a new provider must implement and argues that surface is too wide — it argues FOR shrinking, not against. The vscode-lm PRD line ("computePrice is never called for this handler, but must be implemented") is the strongest evidence that the abstract member is pure ceremony.

NET (criterion 6): strictly subtractive apart from one relocated private method. 8 member declarations out, 1 in => -7 elements; ~-35 LoC. No unrelated churn: no signature of any surviving public method changes, no test file is added, no ratchet baseline moves.

RISK: low. Only compile-time surface (abstract member + overrides) plus a mechanical test retarget; behavior is byte-identical because every cost path already runs through the same pricing config. The one thing an implementer must not get wrong is the Responses handler's capability-aware branch, which must be preserved verbatim in the extracted private config method.

</details>

#### Delete the never-passed `forceRefresh` knob from both OAuth session coordinators

- **Lens**: `speculative-generality` · **Kind**: speculative-generality · **Risk**: low
- **Net**: -24 LoC, -6 elements (claimed -30 LoC, 0 elements; verifier figure governs)

**Evidence**

THE KNOB. Two independent auth chains carry a `forceRefresh` boolean that no caller sets.

Chain A (Supabase/GoTrue): `AuthTokenProvider.ensureFreshToken(forceRefresh?: boolean)` (src/auth/TokenProvider.ts:24) → `SupabaseClient.getAccessToken(forceRefresh?: boolean)` (src/auth/SupabaseClient.ts:231, forwards at :238, plus a `@param forceRefresh` doc line at :229) → `SupabaseSession.ensureFreshToken(forceRefresh?)` (src/auth/SupabaseSession.ts:133-135) → `SupabaseSession.getFreshSession(forceRefresh?)` (:276-278), where it drives three live branch conditions: `if (forceRefresh || timeUntilExpiry < threshold)` (:287-289), `if (forceRefresh || timeUntilExpiry <= 0)` (:297), and a ternary that picks between two log strings (:300-302).

Chain B (subscription OAuth): `SubscriptionOAuthCoordinator.getFreshAccessToken(forceRefresh = false)` (src/auth/oauth/SubscriptionOAuthCoordinator.ts:275-278) → `getFreshSession(forceRefresh = false)` (:280) → `if (!forceRefresh && !this.isExpiringSoon(session)) return session;` (:289).

ARITY PROOF. `rg -n "getFreshSession\(true|getFreshAccessToken\(true|getAccessToken\(true|ensureFreshToken\(true" src packages` returns exactly ONE hit repo-wide, and it is a test: src/test-kernel/auth/SupabaseSessionLifecycle.vitest.ts:449. Every production call site passes nothing:

- Chain A production callers (6): src/telemetry/UsageLogService.ts:240, src/agent/remote/remoteAgentList.ts:76, src/agent/remote/RemoteAgentLoader.ts:41, src/controllers/settingsView/SettingsAgentControllerFactory.ts:110, and SupabaseClient's own :249 and :266 — all `getAccessToken()`. Note the settings-view port already declares the narrowed shape `getAccessToken(): Promise<string | null>` with no parameter (src/controllers/settingsView/SettingsRemoteAgentPromptController.ts:9), i.e. one seam has already dropped it.
- Chain B production callers (4): src/auth/codex/codexAuthAccess.ts:53, src/agent/modelHandlers/openai/modelHandlerXAI.ts:176, src/agent/modelHandlers/openai/modelHandlerCodex.ts:321, src/controllers/modelAccess/subscriptionUsage/SubscriptionUsageService.ts:87 — all no-arg. (The `forceRefresh` hits in SubscriptionUsageService.ts:185,196, packages/cli/src/runtime/apiStatus.ts:30,199,208 and desktopCredentialSettingsController.ts:114,223,228 are a DIFFERENT, live knob on the usage-snapshot cache; they never reach the coordinators' `getFreshSession`.)

WHY THE TAIL IS OPEN. `git log --all -S "getAccessToken(true)"` shows the callers died with `refactor!: remove the relay client plane — model calls are BYOK or provider subscriptions only` (2d82c8138e / 368ee4f601): the relay's 401-retry loop (`fix(relay): break infinite 401 loop when refreshed token is still rejected`) was the last force-refresh consumer, and docs/proposals/2026-08-01-backend-decoupling-plan.md:115 records it as `RetryState`'s relay-401 control flow calling `getAccessToken(true)`. An even earlier commit already ruled the concept unnecessary on its merits: 345f0a8a21 `refactor: remove forceRefreshToken, use getAccessToken instead` — "If token expired normally, ensureFreshToken() already handles it since timeUntilExpiry would be negative; if token was revoked, the refresh token is also revoked, so refresh fails anyway."

CONSUMER CLASSIFICATION. Production: 0 setters, 10 no-arg callers. Non-production: 1 (SupabaseSessionLifecycle.vitest.ts:449, which pins only the force path itself — per AGENTS.md "Testing discipline", a test pinning retired behavior is deleted with the behavior). Ambiguous (scripts/, docs/scripts/, supabase/functions/): zero hits for `forceRefresh` on either chain. Not a §15 masking site — this deletes a branch selector, not a catch or fallback; the expiry-driven refresh path at SupabaseSession.ts:287-296 and the `isExpiringSoon` path at SubscriptionOAuthCoordinator.ts:289 stay exactly as they are.

DEDUPE. Absent from the round-1 index (both lists) and from `grep -i "forceRefresh|ensureFreshToken|getFreshSession" docs/proposals/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md` (the one auth hit at line 1581 is about `instanceof` redundancy in the codex coordinator, a different finding). `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "forceRefresh"` returns #10748 (SubscriptionUsageService's staleness stack — the other, live knob) and #3345 (asking for read-count TESTS on ensureFreshToken, closed); neither covers this. `git log --oneline -15 -- src/auth/SupabaseSession.ts src/auth/oauth/SubscriptionOAuthCoordinator.ts` shows no prior attempt. No ratchet lists these symbols; nothing here is exported from packages/agent.

**Proposal**

1. Drop the parameter from `AuthTokenProvider.ensureFreshToken` (TokenProvider.ts:24) and from `SupabaseClient.getAccessToken` (SupabaseClient.ts:231), deleting the `@param forceRefresh` doc line at :229 and passing nothing at :238.
2. Drop it from `SupabaseSession.ensureFreshToken` (:133-135) and `SupabaseSession.getFreshSession` (:276-278); simplify :287-289 to `if (timeUntilExpiry < this.options.tokenRefreshThresholdMs)`, :297 to `if (timeUntilExpiry <= 0)`, and collapse the two-branch log ternary at :300-302 to the single 'Token expired and refresh failed, returning null' string.
3. Drop the defaulted parameter from `SubscriptionOAuthCoordinator.getFreshAccessToken` and `getFreshSession` (:275-280); simplify :289 to `if (!this.isExpiringSoon(session)) return session;`.
4. Delete the one force-refresh assertion in src/test-kernel/auth/SupabaseSessionLifecycle.vitest.ts:449 (and its enclosing case if the case exists only for it). Add no replacement test.
5. Run `npm run typecheck`, `npm test`, `npm run lint`.

**What we give up**

The ability to refresh a token before it is near expiry. Nothing exercises it: expiry-driven refresh still covers normal expiration, and 345f0a8a21 already ruled that a revoked token cannot be rescued by forcing a refresh. One caveat to state in the PR: docs/proposals/2026-08-18-relay-removal-and-recovery.md:195 names `isAccessTokenExpiringSoon()` then `getAccessToken(true)` in its relay recovery record, so a future relay restoration would have to re-add one optional parameter — a two-line reversal, and the recovery record documents already-deleted code rather than defending this parameter.

**Verifier corrections to the evidence above**

1. netLoc is overstated. Claimed -30; actual ≈ **-24** (source -8, test -16). Most of the deletion is signature/argument _text_ on lines that survive. Exact source arithmetic, verified by reading the files at HEAD:
   - `src/auth/SupabaseClient.ts:229` `@param forceRefresh` doc line: **-1**. Lines 231 and 238 change in place: **0**.
   - `src/auth/TokenProvider.ts:24`: in place, **0**.
   - `src/auth/SupabaseSession.ts:133-134`: in place, **0**.
   - `SupabaseSession.ts:276-278` (3-line signature collapses to `private async getFreshSession(): Promise<SupabaseSession | null> {`, 64 cols): **-2**.
   - `SupabaseSession.ts:287-290` (4-line `if (` block collapses to one 64-col line): **-3**.
   - `SupabaseSession.ts:297`: in place, **0**.
   - `SupabaseSession.ts:298-303` warn call, 6 lines → 4 (the single string still exceeds 80 cols at 10-space indent, so prettier keeps it multi-line): **-2**.
   - `SubscriptionOAuthCoordinator.ts:275, 276, 280, 289`: all in place, **0 lines**. Chain B contributes zero LoC — the candidate's per-chain accounting hides this.
   - Test case `SupabaseSessionLifecycle.vitest.ts:439-453` plus its separating blank line 438: **-16**.

2. netElements claimed 0 is wrong by this repo's own accounting convention. The nearest precedent PR in these exact files, #11190 (`f5ea9bea2a`), counts "-1 parameter" in its element tally. Correct figure is **-6 parameters** (`AuthTokenProvider.ensureFreshToken`, `SupabaseClient.getAccessToken`, `SupabaseSession.ensureFreshToken`, `SupabaseSession.getFreshSession`, `SubscriptionOAuthCoordinator.getFreshAccessToken`, `SubscriptionOAuthCoordinator.getFreshSession`) plus one deleted test case.

3. Line range off by one: the Chain-A branch condition spans **287-290**, not 287-289 — the closing `) {` is line 290. The `log.warn` call spans **298-303**; only the ternary is at 300-302.

4. The arity proof's method is weaker than stated. The quoted pattern (`getFreshSession\(true|…`) only catches the literal `true` and would miss a variable or an options-forwarded arg. I re-ran with an identifier-accepting pattern (`ensureFreshToken\([a-zA-Z]`, `getAccessToken\([a-zA-Z]`, `getFreshSession\([a-zA-Z]`, `getFreshAccessToken\([a-zA-Z]`) across the whole repo including `scripts/`, `supabase/functions/`, `packages/*`, `resources/` and `prompts/`. Result: only the four internal forwarding lines (`SupabaseSession.ts:134`, `SupabaseClient.ts:238`, `SubscriptionOAuthCoordinator.ts:276`) and the one test at `:449`. The conclusion holds; the evidence just did not earn it.

5. The implementor census missed a fifth `AuthTokenProvider` site: `packages/cli/scripts/tui-harness.tsx:667-669` installs a provider via `SupabaseClient.setAuthProvider({ ensureFreshToken: async () => accessToken, … })`. It needs no edit (structural typing accepts a zero-arg impl for a one-optional-arg member, and the reverse), but the PR should list it so a reviewer does not think it was overlooked.

6. The evidence omits the two nearest precedents, both of which strengthen it and should be cited: **#11190 / `f5ea9bea2a`** "delete the expiry-probe vertical and two unpassed seams" deleted the sibling `isTokenExpiringSoon` vertical across the same three layers (`SupabaseClient` static → `AuthTokenProvider` port member → coordinator method) plus two unpassed parameters, in these exact files, on 2026-08-20; and **#11394 / `d41424ecd1`** removed another `AuthTokenProvider` port member (`getSessionTokens`) on the same grounds. Neither touched `forceRefresh` and neither ruled on it, so this is the residual of a two-PR pattern, not a new claim.

7. `docs/proposals/2026-07-22-proactive-token-refresh-route-gate-explicit-refresh.md` is not cited at all, and it is the one doc a reviewer will raise: its recommended design A′ is "refresh the token explicitly," i.e. it wanted a forced refresh. It does not defend this parameter — its entire subject (`RetryState`'s proactive branch, `SupabaseClient.isTokenExpiringSoon`, the relay route) was deleted by `2d82c8138e` and #11190 — but the PR must say so rather than let the doc surface in review unaddressed. The `2026-08-18-relay-removal-and-recovery.md` citation checks out at :195, and the same doc also lists `getAccessToken(forceRefresh?)` at :85.

8. Test-coverage attribution is right but understated: the enclosing case (`it('force-refreshes native sessions without reloading storage')`, 439-453) exists solely for the force path, so all 15 lines go, and the read-count invariant it shares is still pinned by the immediately following case (`'returns refreshed session tokens without reloading storage'`, 454-464) plus `ensureFreshToken()` calls at :335, :523 and :587. Issue **#3345** (closed) is what asked for those read-count tests — worth naming, since deleting one of the pair it produced looks like a regression otherwise.

9. Confirmed as stated: zero hits for any of these symbols in `packages/agent/src` (not on the frozen SDK surface); no subclass in `src/auth/codex` or `src/auth/xai` overrides `getFreshSession`/`getFreshAccessToken`; the `forceRefresh` hits in `SubscriptionUsageService.ts:185,196`, `packages/cli/src/runtime/apiStatus.ts`, `desktopCredentialSettingsController.ts`, `settingsViewMessages.ts:880` and `SubscriptionsTab.ts` are the separate live usage-snapshot knob (issue #10748) and never reach either coordinator.

<details><summary>Verifier reasoning</summary>

Re-ran every claim from scratch at HEAD (d8c6dfc071).

**The knob exists as described.** Read `src/auth/TokenProvider.ts:24`, `src/auth/SupabaseClient.ts:229-241`, `src/auth/SupabaseSession.ts:133-136, 276-306`, `src/auth/oauth/SubscriptionOAuthCoordinator.ts:271-291`. Both chains carry it; three live branch conditions in Chain A (287-290, 297, 300-302 ternary) and one in Chain B (289).

**Rule 3 (consumer count) — re-grepped, holds.** A repo-wide `rg` for `forceRefresh` (all files, node_modules excluded) returns 60 hits; every one outside `src/auth/**` belongs to the _other_ knob — the usage-snapshot staleness cache in `SubscriptionUsageService`/`apiStatus`/`desktopCredentialSettingsController`/`settingsViewMessages.ts:880`/`SubscriptionsTab.ts` — or to `forceRefreshRemoteCatalog` (`TeamRosterApplication`, a different symbol entirely). Then a broader arity grep accepting any identifier argument (not just `true`) over the whole tree including `scripts/`, `supabase/functions/`, `packages/extension/resources/`, `prompts/`: the only argument-passing call sites are the four internal forwards inside the two coordinators, plus `SupabaseSessionLifecycle.vitest.ts:449`. Zero production setters, confirmed independently.

Chain A production consumers: `UsageLogService.ts:240`, `remoteAgentList.ts:76`, `RemoteAgentLoader.ts:41`, `SettingsAgentControllerFactory.ts:110`, plus internal `SupabaseClient.ts:249` (`getUser`) and `:266` (`isAuthenticated`) — 6, as claimed, and consistent with #11394's own "4 production call sites" (external only). Chain B: `codexAuthAccess.ts:53`, `modelHandlerXAI.ts:176`, `modelHandlerCodex.ts:321`, `SubscriptionUsageService.ts:87` — 4 no-arg, as claimed. Checked `src/auth/codex` and `src/auth/xai` for subclass overrides of `getFreshSession`/`getFreshAccessToken`/`isExpiringSoon`: none — the only definitions are on the base coordinator. No dynamic dispatch, no command IDs, no config keys, no schema field.

**Rule 1 (duplicate) — clean.** Grepped `round1-index.md`: the four auth-adjacent titles are the desktop themeTokens properties (18), the Codex-only oauth helpers (41), the `@auth/codex`/`@auth/xai`/`SupabaseSession` barrel re-exports (82, and the survey at :2525-2537 records it as refuted), and a byte-twin/bearerToken batch (95). None is this. Grepped the 49-candidate survey for all five symbols: the single hit at :1581-1582 is the `instanceof` redundancy in the codex coordinator — a different finding, and notably it discusses `getFreshSession` at :284 without touching the parameter. Checked all seven round-1 PRs #11416-#11422 for auth files: #11421 touched `src/auth/codex/codexAuthAccess.ts`, `codexJwt.ts`, `config.ts`, `oauth/jwtDecode.ts`, `oauth/sessionAccess.ts`; #11416 touched CLI auth commands; #11417 touched the extension frontend provider. None touched `TokenProvider.ts`, `SupabaseClient.ts`, `SupabaseSession.ts` or `SubscriptionOAuthCoordinator.ts`. Verified the parameter is still present at HEAD by reading the files directly, not from the evidence.

**Rule 2 (already filed/done) — clean.** `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "forceRefresh"` and a body+title search both return exactly #10748 (the other, live knob) and #3345 (closed, asked for read-count tests). No open issue, and the only open PR repo-wide is #11326 (Google retrieval). `git log --oneline -12` on the four files shows #11394, #11190, `2d82c8138e`, `771c0bd9d4` — none removed this parameter. `git log --all --grep=forceRefresh` surfaces the relay-401 fix and `345f0a8a21 refactor: remove forceRefreshToken, use getAccessToken instead`, both of which support the candidate rather than pre-empt it.

**Rule 4 (deliberately justified) — checked and beaten.** Two docs mention it. `2026-08-18-relay-removal-and-recovery.md:85,152,195` is explicitly an _attic/recovery record_ — a description of code already deleted, exactly as the candidate concedes. `2026-07-22-proactive-token-refresh-route-gate-explicit-refresh.md` is the real risk: it recommends design A′, "refresh the token explicitly," which would want a forced refresh. But I read its scope header — it targets `RetryState.ts`'s proactive branch, `CycleServices.ts`, `ModelHandler.refreshClient`, and `SupabaseClient.isTokenExpiringSoon` — and every one of those was deleted by the relay-plane removal (`2d82c8138e`) and #11190. It is a design proposal for a plane that no longer exists, not a ruling on this parameter. No ratchet in `config/ratchets/` lists these symbols; nothing in AGENTS.md or the review checklist defends unpassed parameters (the checklist's own §13/§15 push the other way).

**Rule 5 (settled surface) — no.** `rg 'SubscriptionOAuthCoordinator|SupabaseClient|AuthTokenProvider' packages/agent/src` returns zero hits, so the frozen SDK surface is untouched. `src/auth/` is a VS Code-_allowed_ zone and this change does not move code across any zone boundary. No `@agent/*` import is added, so the host-agent deep-import baseline is unaffected. No knip-baseline entry is involved (nothing is un-exported; a parameter is not a knip element).

**Rule 7 (masking site) — correctly classified.** The deleted expression is a branch _selector_ (`forceRefresh ||` / `!forceRefresh &&`), not a `catch`, a `??` over a failed read, a Zod `.catch`, or a `default: return`. Nothing becomes quieter: the expiry-driven refresh at 287-296 and `isExpiringSoon` at 289 survive verbatim, and the `log.warn` on a failed refresh survives — only its dead ternary arm goes. Not an M1-M6 pattern.

**Rule 6 (net-adds) — no.** Every edit is a deletion; no new symbol, no new file, no test added. Behavior is provably identical at every call site because the parameter is `undefined`/`false` in every production path.

**Rule 8 (too thin) — stands, barely.** -24 LoC and 6 parameters is small, but it is one coherent concept spanning two independent auth chains, and the repo has merged strictly smaller single-concept refactors this week (#11393 deleted one never-produced option). It also completes a pattern #11190 and #11394 started in the same module. It would batch naturally with any further auth-leftover sweep.

**Risk: low.** The single behavioral surface is "can no longer refresh a token before it is near expiry," and `345f0a8a21`'s reasoning already ruled that dead on its merits (expired → `timeUntilExpiry` is negative and the normal path refreshes; revoked → the refresh token is revoked too, so forcing does not help). Reversal cost if relay is ever restored is one optional parameter per chain. The only care needed is deleting the whole enclosing `it` block rather than just line 449, and confirming `makeSession` still has other users in the suite (it does).

</details>

#### Route xAI's Grok-subscription client through the shared OpenAI-compatible getClient instead of re-implementing it

- **Lens**: `model-handler-dup` · **Kind**: duplication · **Risk**: medium
- **Net**: -22 LoC, 0 elements

**Evidence**

`OpenAICompatibleModelHandler.getClient` (src/agent/modelHandlers/openai/OpenAICompatibleModelHandler.ts:52-77) is the shared client-construction path for every `openai`-SDK handler: resolve credential -> `new OpenAI({apiKey, baseURL, fetch: this.longRunningModelFetch, maxRetries: 0})` -> `logOpenAICompatibleClientConfig(...)` -> `rememberClientCredentialRoute(...)` -> optional `rememberClientUsageRoute(...)`.

`ModelHandlerXAI.getClient` (src/agent/modelHandlers/openai/modelHandlerXAI.ts:138-172) re-implements that body verbatim for the subscription route — the same `new OpenAI({apiKey, baseURL, fetch: this.longRunningModelFetch, maxRetries: 0})` literal at :154-160, the same `logOpenAICompatibleClientConfig(this.logger, this.config, client.baseURL, 'xai-subscription')` at :161-166, the same `rememberClientCredentialRoute` at :167-171. My clone scan over src/agent/modelHandlers reports OpenAICompatibleModelHandler.ts:60 and modelHandlerXAI.ts:158 as an exact 7-line duplicate window.

The shared path is ALREADY xAI-subscription-aware: `logOpenAICompatibleClientConfig` branches on `route === 'xai-subscription'` at src/agent/modelHandlers/openai/openAIChatHelpers.ts:53-54 and prints "Using Grok subscription. Model: … Base URL: …". So today a subscription request emits two near-identical debug lines: xAI's own `Using Grok subscription (xAI OAuth). Base URL: …` (modelHandlerXAI.ts:151-153) and the shared one two statements later.

What blocks adoption is one type: `ResolvedClientCredential.route` at src/agent/types/ModelHandlerContracts.ts:42-45 is `Exclude<ModelCredentialRoute, 'chatgpt-subscription' | 'xai-subscription'>` — the shared resolver is typed so it can never produce a subscription route. There is no prose justifying the exclusion; it simply records that today's `ModelHandler.resolveClientCredential` (ModelHandler.ts:502-527) only produces `api-key`/`openrouter`.

Consumers of `credential.route`, grepped: src/agent/modelHandlers/openai/OpenAICompatibleModelHandler.ts:70, src/agent/modelHandlers/anthropic/modelHandlerAnthropic.ts:417. Both feed it to `rememberClientCredentialRoute(client, route: ModelCredentialRoute, …)` (ModelHandler.ts:547-559) and to `logOpenAICompatibleClientConfig(…, route: ModelCredentialRoute | undefined)` — both already accept the full union, so widening the field is source-compatible.

The Codex sibling is NOT foldable and stays as-is: modelHandlerCodex.ts:292-315 needs `defaultHeaders` (originator/beta/account-id) and its own `codexFetch`, neither of which the shared constructor takes. xAI needs neither.

Error-route stamping does not depend on the fork: `attachSdkCredentialRoute` is called centrally at ModelHandler.ts:1116, and `parseXaiSubscriptionLimit` reads it at src/common/errors/sdkError/xaiSubscriptionDetection.ts:36.

Dedupe: not in the round-1 index and not in the 41 refuted. The 49-candidate survey touches this file only at line 2117 (delete `ModelHandlerXAI.extractResponse`, :186-203) — a different method, non-overlapping. `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "getClient xai subscription"` returns 0 rows. `git log --oneline -6 -- src/agent/modelHandlers/openai/modelHandlerXAI.ts` shows the last touches were pricing/tier fixes (#10441, #10464, #10495), not a defence of the fork. Not a settled surface: leaf provider handler, not `packages/agent/`, not the PocketFlow engine, no ratchet row.

**Proposal**

Land after (or independently of) the `getApiKey` deletion — with `getApiKey` gone, `resolveClientCredential` is the single credential path and this fold is unambiguous.

1. src/agent/types/ModelHandlerContracts.ts:42-45 — change `route` from the `Exclude<...>` to plain `ModelCredentialRoute`.
2. src/agent/modelHandlers/openai/modelHandlerXAI.ts — replace the `getClient` override (:138-172) with a `protected override async resolveClientCredential(selection)`: when `await this.wantsSubscriptionRoute(selection)` return `{ apiKey: await this.resolveAccessToken(), baseUrl: XAI_SUBSCRIPTION_BASE_URL, route: 'xai-subscription' }`; otherwise log the existing prefer-on-but-signed-out debug line (:142-146) and `return super.resolveClientCredential(selection)`.
3. Drop the now-unused `import OpenAI from 'openai'` (:1) and `import { logOpenAICompatibleClientConfig }` (:18), and the duplicate debug line at :151-153 (the shared helper already prints "Using Grok subscription").
4. Keep `XAI_SUBSCRIPTION_BASE_URL` (:30) as its own constant even though it equals `BASE_URLS[ModelProvider.XAI]` in src/agent/modelHandlers/support/ProxyConfigResolver.ts:38 — the comment at :27-29 makes that duplication deliberate (the OAuth token must never follow a per-provider custom endpoint), and the fold must not route the token through `resolveProxyEndpoint`.
5. Verify: `getLastCredentialUsageRoute()` still maps the credential route to `'xai-subscription'` via ModelHandler.ts:626-627 (xAI never calls `rememberClientUsageRoute`, and `credential.usageRoute` stays unset, so OpenAICompatibleModelHandler.ts:74-76 is a no-op) — usage accounting is unchanged.

**What we give up**

The explicit type-level guarantee that the shared resolver cannot emit a subscription route. After the change that invariant is enforced by code review rather than by the `Exclude`. Mitigation: only the xAI override may return `'xai-subscription'`, and it sets `baseUrl` to the hard-coded trusted origin in the same object literal, so the "never send the OAuth token to a custom endpoint or OpenRouter" rule stays local and reviewable in one place instead of two.

**Verifier corrections to the evidence above**

All corrections are to the evidence, not to the conclusion. I applied the exact proposed patch, ran the full typecheck and the model-handler suites, then reverted (`git status` clean).

1. WRONG CONSUMER COUNT for `credential.route`. The claim lists two sites ("OpenAICompatibleModelHandler.ts:70, modelHandlerAnthropic.ts:417"). `grep -rn "credential\.route" src packages/*/src --include="*.ts"` returns **six sites in four files**: `src/agent/modelHandlers/openai/OpenAICompatibleModelHandler.ts:67` and `:71` (not `:70`), `src/agent/modelHandlers/anthropic/modelHandlerAnthropic.ts:416` (not `:417`), `src/agent/modelHandlers/openrouter/modelHandlerOpenRouterNative.ts:150`, and `src/agent/modelHandlers/google/modelHandlerGoogleInteractions.ts:612` and `:633`. The Google one is the interesting miss: `:610-613` uses `cached.credential.route === credential.route` as part of a client-cache identity comparison, i.e. an equality read rather than a pass-through. It is still source-compatible under the widened union (equality over a wider union type-checks), so the conclusion holds — but the claim's "both feed it to helpers that already accept the full union" was not a complete survey.

2. MISSED CONSUMER of `resolveClientCredential` itself, and it carries the one real semantic change. `ModelHandler.getApiKey()` (`src/agent/modelHandlers/ModelHandler.ts:642-644`) is `return (await this.resolveClientCredential()).apiKey`. After the fold, `ModelHandlerXAI.getApiKey()` returns the **SuperGrok OAuth token** whenever the subscription route is live, detached from the trusted `baseUrl` that travels with it inside `ResolvedClientCredential`. That is exactly the leak shape the `Exclude` nominally guarded, and the evidence never mentions it. It is inert today — full-repo grep (excluding node_modules/dist; the module-level `getApiKey` in `src/model/apiProviders.ts:159` is a different symbol) finds **zero production callers** of the method: only the Codex override at `src/agent/modelHandlers/openai/modelHandlerCodex.ts:254-257` (which already returns its own OAuth token, so xAI would merely match the house shape) and `src/test-kernel/agent/modelHandlers/ModelHandlerApiKey.vitest.ts:22`. It belongs in the PR's "what we give up" section, and it is a second reason to land the `getApiKey` deletion first rather than "independently".

3. Wrong provenance claim. "`git log --oneline -6 -- modelHandlerXAI.ts` shows the last touches were pricing/tier fixes (#10441, #10464, #10495)" is false as stated: the three most recent commits touching that file are `d56d39c7c2` (#11013 cluster, dead-seam deletion), `2d82c8138e` (relay client-plane removal), and `1d17d1b29a` (#10875 simplification sweep). The pricing fixes are commits 4-6. The point the claim was making (no commit defends the fork) still holds.

4. `rememberClientCredentialRoute` spans `ModelHandler.ts:547-565`, not `:547-559`. (`resolveClientCredential` at `:502-527`, `logOpenAICompatibleClientConfig` xAI branch at `openAIChatHelpers.ts:53-54`, `ModelHandlerContracts.ts:42-45`, `modelHandlerXAI.ts:138-172` with the sub-spans at `:142-146`, `:151-153`, `:154-160`, `:161-166`, `:167-171`, `OpenAICompatibleModelHandler.ts:52-77`, `ModelHandler.ts:626-627`, `ModelHandler.ts:1116`, `xaiSubscriptionDetection.ts:36`, and `ProxyConfigResolver.ts:38` all check out exactly.)

5. Proposal step 3 is incomplete. Dropping `import OpenAI from 'openai'` (`:1`) and the `logOpenAICompatibleClientConfig` import (`:18`) is right, but the override's return type requires adding `ResolvedClientCredential` to the existing type import at `:3-6`. Imports net **-1 line**, not -2. (My applied patch does this and still lands at exactly -22.)

6. Evidence the claim missed, in its favour. (a) `ModelHandler.ts:506-509` documents this exact seam already: "The base resolver serves direct API keys and OpenRouter; subscription-capable handlers consult `selection` in their own overrides." No production handler overrides `resolveClientCredential` today — the change makes the code match its own documented extension point. (b) Provenance shows the `Exclude` is descriptive, not a policy: `cb6ea79b95` (#9030) introduced it as `Exclude<ModelCredentialRoute, 'chatgpt-subscription'>` when Codex built its client directly, and `39c39cd473` (#9709) merely appended `'xai-subscription'` in the same commit that added the route. No PR body, doc, or comment argues for it. (c) The guarantee being given up is weaker than the claim concedes: the identical mistake (OAuth token + `resolveProxyEndpoint` baseUrl) is fully expressible in a `getClient` override today — Codex already constructs `new OpenAI({apiKey: oauthToken, baseURL: ...})` outside the type's reach — so the `Exclude` only dictates which method a hypothetical bug is written in.

7. Coverage caveat the claim does not state. There is **zero** test coverage of the xAI subscription client path: `grep -rln "isXaiSignedIn|xaiCoordinator|XAI_SUBSCRIPTION|wantsSubscriptionRoute" src/test-kernel` returns nothing, and the only suite touching the handler (`src/test-kernel/agent/modelHandlers/XaiLongContextPricing.vitest.ts`) exercises pricing only. Equivalence rests on types plus reading, which is why medium risk is the right label.

<details><summary>Verifier reasoning</summary>

VERDICT: SURVIVES. I did not take the claim's word for anything — I applied the patch and measured.

**Empirical verification (the decisive step).** I wrote the exact proposed change: `ModelHandlerContracts.ts:42-45` `Exclude<...>` → `readonly route: ModelCredentialRoute;`, and replaced `ModelHandlerXAI.getClient` (`:138-172`) with a `protected override async resolveClientCredential(selection)` that returns `{apiKey: await this.resolveAccessToken(), baseUrl: XAI_SUBSCRIPTION_BASE_URL, route: 'xai-subscription'}` on the subscription branch and `super.resolveClientCredential(selection)` otherwise, keeping the prefer-on-but-signed-out debug line. Results: `git diff --stat` = **10 insertions, 32 deletions = exactly -22 net LoC**, matching the claim to the line. `npm run typecheck` passed across all six projects (root, cli, extension, desktop, trace-viewer, agent — including the agent package's "Validated 724 declarations" gate, so widening `route` does not disturb the built `@texra-ai/agent` d.ts surface). `npx vitest run src/test-kernel/agent/modelHandlers src/test-kernel/common/XaiSubscriptionDetection.vitest.ts` → 61 files / 761 tests pass, 1 file + 4 tests skipped. Tree restored clean.

**Behavioural equivalence, traced by hand.** Subscription branch after the fold: base `getClient` (`:55-76`) builds `new OpenAI({apiKey: <oauth token>, baseURL: 'https://api.x.ai/v1', fetch: this.longRunningModelFetch, maxRetries: 0})` — byte-identical construction to `:154-160`; calls `logOpenAICompatibleClientConfig(..., client.baseURL, 'xai-subscription')`, whose `route === 'xai-subscription'` branch (`openAIChatHelpers.ts:53-54`) prints "Using Grok subscription. Model: … Base URL: …", superseding the hand-written `:151-153` line; calls `rememberClientCredentialRoute(client, 'xai-subscription', apiKey)`, which at `ModelHandler.ts:556-557` still short-circuits the sha256 fingerprint to the stable route identity, so `getWireRouteKey`/`getModelRetryRouteKey` are unchanged; then `credential.usageRoute` is `undefined`, so `:74-76` is a no-op and `getLastCredentialUsageRoute()` still maps the credential route via `ModelHandler.ts:626-627` — the accounting claim checks out. Error stamping is untouched: `attachSdkCredentialRoute` fires centrally at `ModelHandler.ts:1116` off the active route, and `parseXaiSubscriptionLimit` reads it at `xaiSubscriptionDetection.ts:36`. `getBaseUrl()`'s xAI override (`:127-133`) keys off `activeCredentialRoute`, not off `getClient`, so retry endpoint pinning is unaffected. Step 4's "keep `XAI_SUBSCRIPTION_BASE_URL` separate" is correct and load-bearing: `ProxyConfigResolver.ts:38` does carry the same literal for `ModelProvider.XAI`, but routing the OAuth token through `resolveProxyEndpoint` would let a per-model `config.baseUrl` (`buildProxyConfig`, `ModelHandler.ts:534-536`) capture it — the fold must not do that, and as written it does not.

**Kill-check 1, DUPLICATE.** Not in the round-1 index (90 titles; I read both the 49-shipped and 41-refuted lists). The one adjacent line, "Drop three provider residues: an unreachable Kimi rule key, a log-only xAI override, and a local error-message duplicate," is in the REFUTED list and its surviving residual (survey `:2107-2117`) is `ModelHandlerXAI.extractResponse` (`:186-203`) plus the type imports at `:4` and `:24` — a different method. Confirmed non-overlapping at HEAD (`extractResponse` is still present). The two would in fact batch well into one ~-40 LoC single-file PR; only the import block overlaps, and they touch disjoint lines of it. The survey's other model-handler survivor (`resolveGoogleClient` inline, `:809-825`) is a different file and actually argues _for_ this direction — it cites `OpenAICompatibleModelHandler.ts:52-76` as the canonical inline-in-`getClient` house pattern that Google should converge on; this candidate converges xAI on the same base.

**Kill-check 2, ALREADY FILED / DONE.** `gh issue list --state all --search` for `resolveClientCredential`, `getClient credential route`, `xai subscription getClient` → 0 rows each. `modelHandlerXAI`, `Grok subscription`, and `model handler duplication` searches return only closed, unrelated issues (#10458/#10381/#10457 pricing, #8507 reasoning clamp, #5837/#9678/#6733 other duplication scopes). `git log --all --grep=resolveClientCredential` → empty. Present at HEAD in full, so not already done.

**Kill-check 3, WRONG CONSUMER COUNT.** This is where the evidence is wrong (see corrections 1 and 2) but not fatally: I found four `credential.route` consumer files instead of two, plus the entirely unmentioned `getApiKey` consumer of `resolveClientCredential`. Every one of them either passes the value into a helper already typed on the full `ModelCredentialRoute` union (`rememberClientCredentialRoute` at `:549`, `logOpenAICompatibleClientConfig` at `openAIChatHelpers.ts:44-48`) or compares it for equality, which is why the typecheck came back clean. I also swept the non-code surfaces the brief names: no `xai-subscription`/`getClient` reference in `packages/extension/package.json`, `packages/extension/src/commands.ts`, `src/shared/schemas/coreSettings.ts`/`stateSettings.ts`, `packages/extension/resources/`, `prompts/`, or `supabase/functions/`. The only host reference to the route vocabulary is `packages/cli/src/runtime/modelAccessRoute.ts`, which consumes the usage-route string, not `ResolvedClientCredential`.

**Kill-check 4, DELIBERATELY JUSTIFIED.** I searched for a ruling before accepting. `docs/proposals/2026-08-04-xai-grok-oauth-subscription.md` mentions the route only as an accounting tag (`:105`) and a work item (`:144`); it contains no `Exclude`, no `getClient`, and no argument for a separate client-construction path. No `docs/architecture/`, `docs/dev/audits/`, AGENTS.md, or review-checklist entry addresses `ResolvedClientCredential.route`. The dated audits that mention `modelHandlerXAI` (2026-07-08 `:164-165`, 2026-07-09 `:209-210`, 2026-05-29 `:531`) discuss only the `extractResponse` diagnostic override. Git provenance settles it: the `Exclude` was authored descriptively in #9030 and mechanically extended in #9709. Closed issue #9328 ("seam the credential plane out of ModelHandler") is the nearest governance and points the same way — its comments treat `resolveClientCredential` as the credential seam and flag `modelHandlerXAI.ts`'s `@auth/xai` import as the outstanding item; this change leaves that import exactly as-is and consolidates rather than fragments the credential decision.

**Kill-check 5, SETTLED SURFACE.** Leaf provider handler plus one shared contract type. `packages/agent/src` contains only `index.ts`/`node.ts`/`schemas.ts` — none touch model handlers; `packages/agent/dist` is build output and is not committed (`git ls-files packages/agent` lists 13 source/script files, no dist). No row in `config/ratchets/*.json` mentions `ModelHandlerContracts` or `ResolvedClientCredential`. No new export, so `check:dead-code-ratchet` is untouched; no new `@agent/*` deep-import specifier from a host, so `host-agent-import-baseline` is untouched. Not the PocketFlow engine, not `AgentEvent`/`SessionFact`, not a platform port, not a browser-reachable util, no `vscode` import anywhere near it.

**Kill-check 6, NET-ADDS / CHURNS.** Measured, not estimated: -22 LoC, three files, all inside `src/agent/`. No unrelated file is dragged in; no test needed rewriting (the repo's zero-new-tests default for a behaviour-preserving refactor applies cleanly). Element count is flat: one protected override replaces one public override.

**Kill-check 7, MASKING SITE.** Nothing deleted is a catch, a fallback, or a `??` over a failed read. The `try/catch` in `resolveAccessToken` (`:174-185`) — the only masking-shaped construct in the file, and a loud one that rethrows with `cause` — is untouched. What disappears is a duplicate `logger.debug` line whose replacement (the shared helper) prints strictly more information (it adds `Model: <fullName>`). The only informational loss is the "(xAI OAuth)" parenthetical.

**Kill-check 8, TOO THIN.** -22 LoC of exact duplicated client construction, an 18-line clone window deleted, a duplicate log line collapsed, and a stale type narrowing removed, in a security-relevant path — plus a natural batch partner in the same file (the survey's `extractResponse` residual, ~-18 more). Stands.

The one honest cost, which the claim states and I confirm is real but weaker than it sounds: the compile-time guarantee that the shared resolver cannot emit a subscription route goes away, and `getApiKey` becomes a second (currently unreachable) way for the OAuth token to escape without its trusted `baseUrl`. Both are reviewable in one file, and Codex already lives with the identical shape.
