#### Collapse three cross-file type twins that re-declare a shape their own neighbour already owns

- **Lens**: `type-bloat` · **Kind**: dual-representation · **Risk**: low
- **Net**: -21 LoC, -3 elements

**Evidence**

I normalised every `interface`/`type X = {…}` body in src/ and packages/*/src (1,557 declarations: comments stripped, members split at top-level separators, whitespace removed, members sorted) and hashed them. Cross-file exact-twin groups with >=4 members, after discarding pairs where one side is a vitest fixture: three.

(1) `TemplateAgentFilePlan` (src/controllers/settingsView/backend/templateAgentCreation.ts:37) vs `SettingsAgentTemplatePlan` (src/controllers/settingsView/SettingsAgentDirectoryController.ts:41). Both are `{baseName, description, fileName, filePath, templateKind: 'toolUse' | 'workflowSingle'}` — identical field names, identical types, identical literal union, differing only by `readonly` modifiers. The doc comment at templateAgentCreation.ts:36 asserts "Plan fields this module needs; a superset lives on the directory controller's plan" — that is factually false at HEAD: the two are the same five fields, so the comment documents a relationship that does not exist. The controller builds the plan and hands it to `writeTemplateAgentFile(plan: TemplateAgentFilePlan, …)` (templateAgentCreation.ts:55), so the dependency already points controller -> backend and no cycle is created by keeping one declaration in the backend module. Consumers: `SettingsAgentTemplatePlan` — 0 outside SettingsAgentDirectoryController.ts (`git grep -l -w` returns one file); `TemplateAgentFilePlan` — 0 outside templateAgentCreation.ts.

(2) `FileOpConfig` (packages/extension/src/commands/housekeeping/fileOpRunner.ts:11, 7 readonly fields: agent, model, inputFile, outputFiles, executionId?, skipProgressViewClear?, streamId?) is a hand-written mirror of `CleanConfig` = `z.infer<typeof CleanConfigSchema>` declared two files away at packages/extension/src/commands/housekeeping/fileOpSchemas.ts:48-50, where `CleanConfigSchema = FileOpParamsSchema.extend(fileOpConfigFields)` yields exactly `{inputFile, agent, model, outputFiles, streamId?, executionId?: ExecutionId, skipProgressViewClear?}`. Both call sites already hold the Zod-derived type and pass it straight in: `handlePack(config: PackConfig)` -> `runFileOp(config, …)` (packCommands.ts:49-50) and `handleClean(config: CleanConfig)` -> `runFileOp(config, …)` (cleanCommands.ts:65-67); `PackConfig` differs from `CleanConfig` only by `model: z.string().prefault('')` vs required, which infers to `string` either way, so it is assignable. Consumers of `FileOpConfig`: 1, its own file (`git grep -n -w FileOpConfig` -> fileOpRunner.ts:11,50 only). This is the exact shape CLAUDE.md's "Schemas are the single source of truth" rule exists to prevent: a hand-written twin of a `z.infer` type sitting in the same directory as the schema.

(3) `LoopbackAuthorizeRequest` (src/auth/oauth/loopbackLogin.ts:25) vs `SubscriptionAuthorizeRequest` (src/auth/oauth/SubscriptionOAuthCoordinator.ts:52) — both `{url, verifier, state, redirectUri}`, same directory. `LoopbackOAuthCoordinator.buildAuthorizeRequest(port): LoopbackAuthorizeRequest` (loopbackLogin.ts:33) is the port; its only implementor is `SubscriptionOAuthCoordinator.buildAuthorizeRequest(port): SubscriptionAuthorizeRequest` (SubscriptionOAuthCoordinator.ts:241), driven by `SubscriptionOAuthPolicy.buildAuthorizeRequest` at :90. Consumers of `LoopbackAuthorizeRequest`: 1, its own file. `SubscriptionAuthorizeRequest` is already the exported name that `src/test-kernel/auth/CodexLoopbackLogin.vitest.ts:9,19,72,76,100,103` imports to build loopback coordinators, so the tests already treat it as the one true name for this shape and need no edit.

DEDUPE. None of the six symbol names appears in `docs/proposals/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md` (0 regex matches over the whole file), so neither the shipped nor the refuted round-1 lists cover them. Round 1's refuted `auth` entry (proposal:2523-2541) is about the `@auth/codex` / `@auth/xai` / `SupabaseSession` barrel re-exports and the `pkce.ts` helpers, not about this twin. `docs/proposals/2026-08-07-prod-structural-leads-triage.md:271` mentions repointing a test to `SubscriptionAuthorizeRequest` under the test-only-export wave that shipped as `d3c55fbf7b` / #9849; that wave deleted `CodexAuthorizeRequest` and never touched `LoopbackAuthorizeRequest`, so this is the residual twin, not a re-file. `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all` searches for "single-file types", "un-export types zero cross-file" and "desktop un-export" return nothing covering any of the three. `git log --oneline -8` on each path shows the last touches were #11417/#11412/#11411 (unrelated feature and fix work).

**Proposal**

One `dedupe:` PR, three independent hunks.

(1) Delete `SettingsAgentTemplatePlan` (SettingsAgentDirectoryController.ts:41-47); export `TemplateAgentFilePlan` from templateAgentCreation.ts:37 and `import type` it in the controller, which already imports from that module. Replace the false "a superset lives on the directory controller's plan" comment at :36 with what the type actually is (the plan both the controller and the writer use).

(2) Delete `FileOpConfig` (fileOpRunner.ts:11-19) and type `runFileOp(config: CleanConfig, …)` against the Zod-derived type already exported from `./fileOpSchemas`. `handlePack`'s `PackConfig` stays assignable; no change to packCommands.ts or cleanCommands.ts.

(3) Delete `LoopbackAuthorizeRequest` (loopbackLogin.ts:25-30) and have `LoopbackOAuthCoordinator.buildAuthorizeRequest` return the `SubscriptionAuthorizeRequest` already exported from `./SubscriptionOAuthCoordinator` (same directory, no new module edge and no cycle — loopbackLogin.ts already sits below the coordinator in the auth/oauth layer).

No test edits: all six names have zero test consumers except `SubscriptionAuthorizeRequest`, which survives. Run `npm run typecheck`, `npm run lint`, `npm test`, `npm run check:dead-code-ratchet` (baseline should not need a row — `FileOpConfig`, `LoopbackAuthorizeRequest` and `SettingsAgentTemplatePlan` are all currently unexported-or-invisible and none has a baseline row).

**What we give up**

Each site loses the freedom to let its local shape drift from its neighbour's without a compile error — which is the point, since (1)'s comment shows the drift narrative already outran the code. Concretely: `runFileOp` becomes coupled to `CleanConfigSchema`, so a future clean-only field would widen the pack path's declared type too (still assignable, still compiles; the alternative is to name a `FileOpParams`-derived type at that point). `LoopbackOAuthCoordinator` becomes nominally tied to the subscription vocabulary, so a non-subscription loopback provider would have to re-introduce its own request type — but there is no such provider today and the shared `SubscriptionOAuthCoordinator` is the only implementor. Reversing any hunk is a five-line re-declaration.

**Verifier corrections to the evidence above**

Six factual errors in the evidence. None changes the verdict, but the implementing PR must not repeat them.

**C1 (serious) — the DEDUPE claim is false.** Evidence: "None of the six symbol names appears in `docs/proposals/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md` (0 regex matches over the whole file)". `grep -n FileOpConfig` on that file returns **line 565**, inside a candidate that SHIPPED as `16d66fd077` / #11417 ("Batch: drop the phantom onColorTheme activation event, the duplicate apiKeyCommands id home, and 11 file-local exports" — item 13 of the shipped list in round1-index.md). That candidate listed `FileOpConfig`/`FileOpActions` (`commands/housekeeping/fileOpRunner.ts:11,22`) among "Eleven exported types whose only references are inside their own declaring file". The related paths also appear at proposal lines 2006/2009/2043 (`templateAgentCreation.ts`, `writeTemplateAgentFile`) under the shipped CLI-template-bundle candidate. What round 1 did to `FileOpConfig` was **un-export it** — confirmed at HEAD: `interface FileOpConfig` at fileOpRunner.ts:11 carries no `export`. Hunk (2) is therefore a _residual_ of a shipped item, not a fresh find: the defect that remains (a hand-written twin of a `z.infer` type two files from its schema) is a different, still-unaddressed defect class, and un-exporting it did not fix it. Correct claim: "one of the three symbols was un-exported by #11417; the twin itself survives untouched."

**C2 — wrong caller for the template plan.** Evidence: "The controller builds the plan and hands it to `writeTemplateAgentFile(plan, …)`". The controller never calls `writeTemplateAgentFile`. The two **hosts** do the handoff: `packages/extension/src/settingsView/handlers/agentHandlers.ts:427` (`planTemplateAgent`) → `:433` (`writeTemplateAgentFile`), and `packages/desktop/src/main/desktopAgentSettingsController.ts:392` → `:398`. The plan crosses module boundaries only through host code today; the two declarations connect purely by structural typing.

**C3 — the controller does NOT already import that module.** Evidence: "`import type` it in the controller, which already imports from that module". `SettingsAgentDirectoryController.ts:1-5` imports exactly `node:path` and `@shared/schemas` — nothing else. Hunk (1) **adds a new module edge** `src/controllers/settingsView/SettingsAgentDirectoryController.ts → ./backend/templateAgentCreation`. It is a type-only edge, creates no cycle (templateAgentCreation imports nothing from the controller), and `src/test-kernel/architecture/dependencyDirection.vitest.ts` passes with it — but it is a real new edge, not a free ride on an existing import, and it should be disclosed in the PR body.

**C4 — three off-by-one line refs.** `LoopbackOAuthCoordinator.buildAuthorizeRequest` is at loopbackLogin.ts:**34**, not :33 (:33 is the interface header). `SubscriptionOAuthPolicy.buildAuthorizeRequest` starts at SubscriptionOAuthCoordinator.ts:**86** (:90 is its return-type annotation). `SettingsAgentTemplatePlan` occupies :41-47 and the blank line at :48 — hunk (1) removes 8 lines there, not 7.

**C5 — wrong recent-history attribution.** Evidence: "`git log --oneline -8` on each path shows the last touches were #11417/#11412/#11411". Only fileOpRunner.ts matches (#11417). Actual last touches: `SettingsAgentDirectoryController.ts` → `d4c6b38494` (#11062); `templateAgentCreation.ts` → `3fbbd49370` (no PR number); `loopbackLogin.ts` → `194c41e848` (no PR number); `SubscriptionOAuthCoordinator.ts` → `23bda83d59` (#10616). Neither #11412 nor #11411 touched any of these five paths.

**C6 — "export ... TemplateAgentFilePlan" understates the mechanics.** Both `TemplateAgentFilePlan` and `SettingsAgentTemplatePlan` are currently **unexported**, so hunk (1) is not a pure deletion: it deletes one declaration and **adds one export**. Net element count is still -3 (three interfaces removed, none added), but the PR widens `templateAgentCreation.ts`'s surface by one exported type. That export has a same-PR consumer, so AGENTS.md "Exports are contracts" is satisfied and `check:dead-code-ratchet` stays green (verified).

**Design note for the implementing PR (not an error, an improvement).** Hunk (2) as written types the pack-and-clean shared runner as `config: CleanConfig` — the shared entry point named after one of its two operations, which a reviewer can fairly call a readability regression. Use `import { type CleanConfig as FileOpConfig } from './fileOpSchemas';` and leave the `runFileOp(config: FileOpConfig, …)` signature verbatim. Same LoC, same SSOT win, no vocabulary regression, and no new schema element.

<details><summary>Verifier reasoning</summary>

## Method

I applied all three hunks to a clean tree at HEAD `d8c6dfc071` (note: HEAD has moved past the round-1 merges — #11431 is the tip), ran the full gate set, measured the real diff, then reverted. `git status --short` is clean.

## Gate results (all on the applied patch)

- `npm run typecheck` — pass, all six projects (root, agent, extension, cli, trace-viewer, desktop).
- `npx eslint` on all four changed files — zero output.
- `npm run check:dead-code-ratchet` — "394 combined vs 394 baselined. Dead-code ratchet OK: no new findings." No baseline row needed, as the candidate predicted.
- `npx vitest run src/test-kernel/auth/CodexLoopbackLogin.vitest.ts src/test-kernel/architecture/dependencyDirection.vitest.ts` — 25 passed. The auth suite compiles unchanged because `SubscriptionAuthorizeRequest` is the surviving name, exactly as claimed.
- Measured diff: `4 files changed, 10 insertions(+), 31 deletions(-)` = **net -21 LoC**, three `interface` declarations removed and none added = **net -3 elements**. The claimed numbers are exact.

## Kill-criteria walkthrough

**1. DUPLICATE — survives, but only just, and the evidence lied about it.** See C1. `FileOpConfig` _is_ in the survey doc and _was_ touched by a shipped round-1 PR (#11417). But the shipped action was the un-export, and at HEAD `interface FileOpConfig` still stands as a seven-field hand copy of `CleanConfig = z.infer<typeof CleanConfigSchema>` declared two files away in the same directory. Deleting it and pointing `runFileOp` at the Zod-derived type is a different action against a different rule (CLAUDE.md "Schemas are the single source of truth"), and it is not "already done at HEAD". Hunks (1) and (3) have zero proposal hits — I re-grepped all six symbol names myself; only `FileOpConfig` matches. Not in round1-index.md's 49 shipped or 41 refuted titles either: the nearest refuted entry ("Trim the 19 test-only re-exports and 6 identity aliases from the @auth/codex, @auth/xai and SupabaseSession barrels") is about barrel re-exports, and the nearest shipped auth entry ("Inline the two Codex-only helpers that sit in the shared oauth/ layer") deleted `pkce`-adjacent helpers, not this twin.

**2. ALREADY FILED — no.** `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "twin type duplicate shape"` returns four unrelated closed issues (#9446 settings-view vocabulary, #7067 OutputFileSummary, #9141 delegation, #9438 audit triage). None covers any of the three. `git log --all --grep` and per-path `git log` produced C5's corrected history: nothing has attacked these twins.

**3. WRONG CONSUMER COUNT — checked all six, all correct.** My own `git grep -n` (the candidate's `--include=*.ts` invocation would have failed under zsh globbing, so I re-ran it properly):

- `SettingsAgentTemplatePlan` → 2 hits, both in its own file (:41 decl, :117 return type). No string-literal, command-id, contributes.json, settings-schema, resources/, prompts/, or supabase/ surface touches a type name, and none of these three is a runtime value, so there is no dynamic-dispatch path to miss.
- `TemplateAgentFilePlan` → 2 hits, own file (:37, :55).
- `FileOpConfig` → 2 hits, own file (:11, :50) — matching the candidate's claim exactly.
- `LoopbackAuthorizeRequest` → 2 hits, own file (:25, :34).
- `SubscriptionAuthorizeRequest` → 4 production hits (:52, :90, :241 in its own file) plus 6 test hits in `CodexLoopbackLogin.vitest.ts:9,19,72,76,100,103` — the candidate's line list is exact.
- `runFileOp` → 2 production call sites, `packCommands.ts:50` and `cleanCommands.ts:67`, both already holding the Zod-derived type. `PackConfig` is assignable to `CleanConfig` because `model: z.string().prefault('')` and `model: z.string().min(1)` both infer output `string`; typecheck confirms.

**4. DELIBERATELY JUSTIFIED — no ruling found the other way, and one existing doc points toward the change.** `docs/proposals/2026-08-07-prod-structural-leads-triage.md:271` recorded "repoint the test to `SubscriptionAuthorizeRequest` from `@auth/oauth`" under the test-only-export wave that shipped as #9849 / `1a33d951eb`; that wave deleted `CodexAuthorizeRequest` and left `LoopbackAuthorizeRequest` standing, so hunk (3) is the residual of an in-flight consolidation, not a reversal of one. The doc comment at `templateAgentCreation.ts:36` ("a superset lives on the directory controller's plan") is not a ruling — I diffed the two bodies field by field and they are identical modulo `readonly`, so the comment is stale prose, exactly as claimed.

**5. SETTLED SURFACE — untouched.** No `@agent/*` specifier is added or changed (the host-agent-deep-import ratchet is unaffected — none of the four files is a host file adding an `@agent/*` import). No PocketFlow, no platform port, no browser-safe util, no AgentEvent/SessionFact boundary. `src/auth/` is a VS Code-allowed zone and gains no `vscode` import. All five ratchets green.

**6. NET-ADDS — no, measured.** -21 LoC is real and I verified it with `git diff --stat` on the applied patch rather than trusting the estimate. No unrelated churn: four files, no test edits, no baseline edits, no formatting cascade.

**7. MASKING SITE — N/A.** No `catch`, no `??` fallback, no Zod `.catch`, no `default:` arm is touched. This is pure type-declaration removal.

**8. TOO THIN — batched, and lands at the low end of what round 1 shipped.** Three hunks, three files' worth of deletions, -21/-3. Comparable to shipped round-1 items such as "Collapse three single-purpose residues" and "Sweep three settings-view leftovers". It would be too thin as a single hunk; as a three-hunk `dedupe:` PR it clears the bar.

## Residual judgement calls the PR must own

Two of the three hunks buy their deletion with a new cross-module type edge, and the PR body should say so rather than claim the edge already existed (C3):

- (1) `SettingsAgentDirectoryController → backend/templateAgentCreation` (new, type-only, no cycle).
- (3) `loopbackLogin → SubscriptionOAuthCoordinator` (new, type-only, no cycle — `SubscriptionOAuthCoordinator.ts` imports only `./pkce`, `./providerAuthBridge`, `./subscriptionOAuthError` from the directory). The cost is that `LoopbackOAuthCoordinator<S>`, documented as "Minimal coordinator surface the loopback flow needs" and kept generic so Codex and xAI can both satisfy it, now names the subscription vocabulary in its signature. `SubscriptionOAuthCoordinator` is its only implementor today (`codexLoopbackLogin.ts:17` and `xaiLoopbackLogin.ts:13` both take the port, and only the shared coordinator satisfies it), so nothing breaks — but this is the one hunk a reviewer could reasonably push back on, and it is the one to drop first if the PR needs trimming.

The "what we give up" section already names both costs honestly, which is why they are notes rather than kills.

#### Delete ModelHandler.getApiKey and its ModelHandlerCodex override — a second credential path with zero production callers

- **Lens**: `model-handler-dup` · **Kind**: test-only · **Risk**: low
- **Net**: -13 LoC, -3 elements (claimed -15 LoC, -2 elements; verifier figure governs)

**Evidence**

`ModelHandler.getApiKey()` is defined at src/agent/modelHandlers/ModelHandler.ts:641-644 (`protected async getApiKey() { return (await this.resolveClientCredential()).apiKey; }`). `ModelHandlerCodex` overrides it at src/agent/modelHandlers/openai/modelHandlerCodex.ts:252-258 to return the OAuth access token when the ChatGPT-subscription preference is configured.

Grep for every spelling of the symbol across the whole repo (`rg -n "getApiKey" src packages docs --glob '*.{ts,tsx,md}'`) returns, outside `src/model/apiProviders.ts` (an unrelated free function of the same name):

- production: src/agent/modelHandlers/ModelHandler.ts:72 (import of the FREE function from @model/apiProviders), :487 (call of that free function inside `fetchApiKeyOrThrow`), :642 (the method definition), :652 (a prose comment); src/agent/modelHandlers/openai/modelHandlerCodex.ts:254,257 (the override and its `super.getApiKey()` call). **Zero `this.getApiKey()` call sites anywhere** — no handler, no runtime, no host, no CLI, no packages/*.
- non-production: src/test-kernel/agent/modelHandlers/ModelHandlerApiKey.vitest.ts:21-23 (`class ExposedKeyHandler extends ModelHandlerOpenRouterNative { exposeGetApiKey() { return this.getApiKey(); } }`) and its three assertions at :77, :93, :103.
- ambiguous (scripts/): zero hits.
  Because nothing calls the base method, the Codex override at :252-258 is production-dead too — it exists only to change the behaviour of a method the test file is the only caller of.

The same test file already exposes the real production path: `exposeResolveClientCredential` at :25-29, used at :115. `getClient` (OpenAICompatibleModelHandler.ts:52-77, modelHandlerAnthropic.ts:403-419) reaches credentials through `resolveClientCredential(selection)` only, so `getApiKey()`'s selection-less `resolveClientCredential()` call is a second, divergent decision path over the same resolver.

Dedupe: not in the round-1 shipped index, not in the 41 refuted, and `grep -in "getApiKey" docs/proposals/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md` returns 0. `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "getApiKey ModelHandler"` returns only #6641 (CLOSED, "Tighten ModelHandler visibility…", which made it `protected` — already applied) and two unrelated rows. `git log --all --grep=getApiKey` shows only unrelated desktop/settings key-dialog commits. `git log --oneline -6 -- src/agent/modelHandlers/ModelHandler.ts` shows no recent defence of this method. No ratchet row (`grep -n getApiKey config/ratchets/*.json` = 0); the symbol is not exported, so knip never saw it.

Disclosure: docs/proposals/2026-08-10-simplifier-fleet-round1-strategy.md:38 lists this exact pair as a planned round-2 test-kernel sweep item ("`ModelHandler.ts` `getApiKey` + `ModelHandlerCodex` override — sole consumer `ModelHandlerApiKey.vitest.ts`; also a duplicate credential-routing decision path vs `resolveClientCredential`"). It was never implemented — the code is still on main 15 days later, and no PR or issue claims it.

**Proposal**

1. Retarget src/test-kernel/agent/modelHandlers/ModelHandlerApiKey.vitest.ts at the production entry point: delete `exposeGetApiKey` (:20-23) and rewrite the three assertions at :77, :93, :103 from `await handler.exposeGetApiKey()` to `(await handler.exposeResolveClientCredential()).apiKey`. Coverage is unchanged — the deleted method was a one-line `.apiKey` read of that same call.
2. Delete `ModelHandler.getApiKey` (src/agent/modelHandlers/ModelHandler.ts:641-644) in the same PR (build-implies-delete).
3. Delete `ModelHandlerCodex.getApiKey` (src/agent/modelHandlers/openai/modelHandlerCodex.ts:252-258). Keep `hasConfiguredSubscriptionProfile` — it still has a live reader at :268 (`getBaseUrl`) — and keep `resolveAccessToken`, still called from `getClient` at :292.
4. `npm run typecheck` + the one vitest file; no ratchet or baseline edit is needed (nothing exported).

**What we give up**

Nothing at runtime: no production code path reads either method today. If a future handler wants "the key for the configured route" it calls `resolveClientCredential()` directly, which is what `getClient` already does. The only loss is the Codex-specific rule "getApiKey returns the OAuth token while the subscription preference is on", which no caller has ever observed; the equivalent live rule stays in `ModelHandlerCodex.getClient` (:292-315) and `getBaseUrl` (:261-272).

**Verifier corrections to the evidence above**

1. LINE SPANS OFF BY ONE BOUNDARY IN THREE PLACES. (a) `ModelHandler.getApiKey` body is src/agent/modelHandlers/ModelHandler.ts:642-644; line 641 is the doc comment `/** Resolve the key from the same atomic route used by client construction. */`. The deletable block including the trailing blank is 641-645 (5 lines), not "641-644". (b) In modelHandlerCodex.ts the doc comment is 252-253 and the method is 254-258; block+blank = 252-259 (8 lines). (c) The proposal's step 1 says delete ModelHandlerApiKey.vitest.ts ":20-23" — line 20 is `class ExposedKeyHandler extends ModelHandlerOpenRouterNative {`. Deleting it removes the class header. Correct span is 21-24 (method + trailing blank). The evidence itself gives both ":20-23" and ":21-23" in different sentences.

2. THE GREP REPORT IS INCOMPLETE. The evidence claims `rg -n "getApiKey" src packages docs --glob '*.{ts,tsx,md}'` returned only the listed lines. My re-run over src/packages/scripts/config/prompts/supabase returns two hits the evidence omits: `src/test-kernel/agent/modelHandlers/ModelHandlerApiKey.vitest.ts:57` (`describe('ModelHandler.getApiKey resolution', ...)`, which the PR must rename) and, under docs, `docs/proposals/2026-06-22-copilot-oauth-handler-prd.md:164` ("Bearer comes from the auth coordinator, **not** `getApiKey()`"). Neither is a consumer, but the evidence asserted an exhaustive enumeration that was not exhaustive.

3. MISSED THE CHECKLIST REFERENCE. `.claude/skills/code-review/references/review-checklist.md:136` names `getApiKey` as an example of an **L5 decide-once precedence owner** (alongside `decideRunModel`, `deriveResumability`). This is NOT a ruling that protects the method — the L5 role moved to `resolveClientCredential` in #9030 (cb6ea79b95), and the free function `src/model/apiProviders.ts:159` also carries the name — but the reference is ambiguous and dangles after the deletion. The PR should retarget that example at `resolveClientCredential`. The evidence never searched the review checklist, which criterion 4 explicitly requires.

4. MISSED AN IN-FILE COMMENT THE PR MUST EDIT. src/agent/modelHandlers/ModelHandler.ts:652, inside `getBaseUrl`: "Use centralized check to ensure consistency with getApiKey()". The evidence lists it as "a prose comment" in the grep census but the proposal never includes rewording it; leaving it points at a deleted method.

5. LOC CLAIM IS WRONG AS THE PROPOSAL IS WRITTEN. `.prettierrc` sets no `printWidth`, so prettier's 80-column default applies. The literal step-1 rewrite `assert.equal((await handler.exposeResolveClientCredential()).apiKey, 'openrouter-key');` is 91 characters and prettier expands it to 5 lines. Three assertions × +4 lines = +12 test lines, which against −12/−13 production lines and −4 test lines nets roughly **−4 LoC, not −15**. The fix is to destructure first (`const { apiKey } = await handler.exposeResolveClientCredential();` then `assert.equal(apiKey, 'openrouter-key');`, +1 line each), giving net ≈ −13.

6. ELEMENT COUNT UNDERSTATED BY ONE. Two production methods plus the test's `exposeGetApiKey` = −3 elements, not −2 (the deleted `exposeGetApiKey` was counted as a "consumer" but it is itself a defined element).

7. TWO SMALL CODEX LINE ERRORS in "what we give up": `resolveAccessToken` is called from `getClient` at :293 (not :292) and is defined at :319; `getClient` starts at :273, not :292. `hasConfiguredSubscriptionProfile`'s surviving reader at :268 is correct (definition :227, other reads at :203/:222/:280 are of `configuredSubscriptionCapabilities`).

8. EVIDENCE OMITTED ITS OWN STRONGEST HISTORY POINT. `git log -S "resolveClientCredential()).apiKey" -- src/agent/modelHandlers/ModelHandler.ts` returns exactly one commit, cb6ea79b95 (#9030, "refactor(runtime): snapshot model credential routes"). That commit introduced `resolveClientCredential` and reduced `getApiKey` to a one-line delegate, i.e. it removed the last production caller and left the husk behind. The Codex override (c8c9ee7aec) predates it and was live at the time. That is a cleaner provenance story than "no recent defence of this method".

9. NOVELTY CAVEAT (disclosed by the candidate, but understated). docs/proposals/2026-08-10-simplifier-fleet-round1-strategy.md:38 names this exact pair as item A1/area-18 of a planned test-kernel sweep. I confirmed the sweep never ran: sibling A1 items are still on main (`StreamSnapshotStore.deleteStream` at :1302, `ModelFactory.shouldUseResponsesAPI` at :180). Note the strategy doc is not authoritative — its `shouldUseResponsesAPI` "test-only export" claim is false (production caller at ModelFactory.ts:268) — but for this item it is accurate. Recommend shipping batched with the other still-present A1 items rather than alone.

Re-ran every check myself rather than trusting the evidence.

CONSUMER COUNT (criterion 3) — HOLDS. `rg -n "getApiKey" src packages scripts config prompts supabase --glob '*.{ts,tsx,mts,mjs,js,json,yaml,yml,md}'` returns exactly 10 lines: src/model/apiProviders.ts:103,159 (the unrelated free function and its doc); ModelHandler.ts:72 (import of that free function), :487 (its only call, inside `fetchApiKeyOrThrow`), :642 (the method), :652 (prose); modelHandlerCodex.ts:254,257 (override + `super.getApiKey()`); ModelHandlerApiKey.vitest.ts:22 (`this.getApiKey()` inside the test-only `exposeGetApiKey`), :57 (a describe title). There is no bracket access, no aliasing, no string-literal dispatch, no interface member (`src/agent/types/ModelHandlerContracts.ts` has no `getApiKey`), and the method is `protected`, so it is not on any exported surface. No hit in packages/extension/package.json contributions, packages/extension/src/commands.ts, src/shared/schemas/*Settings.ts, packages/extension/resources/, prompts/, or supabase/functions/. Zero production `this.getApiKey()` call sites confirmed; the Codex override is therefore production-dead too. `ModelHandlerXAI` does not override it (it reaches its token via `resolveAccessToken` inside `getClient` at :150), so Codex is the only override.

EQUIVALENCE OF THE PROPOSED TEST RETARGET — HOLDS. `ModelHandler.getApiKey` is literally `(await this.resolveClientCredential()).apiKey`, and `resolveClientCredential` defaults `_selection` to `'configured'` (ModelHandler.ts:502-503), which is the same default `getClient(selection = 'configured')` uses. `ModelHandlerOpenRouterNative` (the class the test subclasses) does not override `resolveClientCredential` — its overrides are `supportsManualCompaction`, `requiresBatchedParallelToolResults`, `getRetryEndpoint`, `sdkErrorTagger`, `supportsForcedToolChoice`, `createResponseImpl` — so virtual dispatch is identical and the three assertions keep their exact meaning.

CRITERION 1 (DUPLICATE) — CLEAR. No `getApiKey`/credential row in the round-1 index (its only credential line is "Delete isSupabaseConfigured and the statically unreachable placeholder-credentials branch"). `grep -in "getApiKey"` over docs/proposals/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md returns 0; the nearby survey rows are different subjects (line 821 = Google client-cache/media, line 1556 = the `@auth/oauth` Codex helpers). Not already done at HEAD — I read both methods on main.

CRITERION 2 (FILED/DONE) — CLEAR. `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "getApiKey"` returns #6891, #6641, #7880, all CLOSED and none about this method (#6641 is the visibility change that made it `protected` — already applied). `git log --all --grep=getApiKey` and `git log --oneline -8` over all three paths show no removal or defence; the last touches are generic sweeps.

CRITERION 4 (DELIBERATE RULING) — CLEAR but with the checklist caveat above. No dated proposal or architecture doc rules that this delegate must exist. docs/proposals/2026-08-01-backend-decoupling-plan.md:62 and docs/architecture/2026-07-26-embedding-the-agent-runtime.md:87 both name `resolveClientCredential` as the credential surface, not `getApiKey` — they cut in favour of the deletion. `grep -rn getApiKey config/ratchets/` = 0.

CRITERION 5 (SETTLED SURFACE) — CLEAR. Nothing on the frozen `@agent/*` SDK (packages/agent tracks only scripts/manifests here, and no `getApiKey` hit under packages/), no PocketFlow engine, no platform port, no browser-safe utils set, no AgentEvent/SessionFact change, no ratchet or baseline edit.

CRITERION 6 (NET-ADDS) — HOLDS ONLY IF THE TEST REWRITE AVOIDS THE PRETTIER WRAP. As literally proposed it is ≈ −4 LoC because the rewritten assertion is 91 chars against an 80-col default; with the destructure form it is ≈ −13. Either way it removes elements rather than relocating them and forces no unrelated churn.

CRITERION 7 (MASKING SITE) — N/A. Neither method contains a catch, a `??` fallback, or a Zod `.catch`. The one try/catch nearby (`fetchApiKeyOrThrow`, :486-493) is untouched and is an L4 classifier that rethrows with a marker.

CRITERION 8 (TOO THIN) — borderline but passes: it deletes a second credential-decision path on a security-relevant surface, not just a dead line. It should be batched with the still-present siblings from the same A1 group (`StreamSnapshotStore.deleteStream`, `PRPollingSource.fetchAnnotations`, `setupLaunch.selectDesktopSetupModel`, `streamTree.getStreamBranchActivity`) rather than shipped as a lone −13-line PR.

Risk is low: the deleted code is unreachable from production, `protected` (so nothing outside the class hierarchy can bind to it), and every remaining reference is compile-visible, so `npm run typecheck` plus the single vitest file is a complete gate. The only live Codex subscription rules stay in `getClient` (:273-315) and `getBaseUrl` (:260-271).

</details>

#### Hoist convertContentToStringUnlessVision into ReasoningModelHandlerOpenAI — all four subclasses now set it identically

- **Lens**: `model-handler-dup` · **Kind**: duplication · **Risk**: low
- **Net**: -10 LoC, -3 elements

**Evidence**

`ReasoningModelHandlerOpenAI` (src/agent/modelHandlers/openai/reasoningModelHandlerOpenAI.ts:36-48) exists precisely to hold "the overrides that are _identical across every_ such handler" (its own doc, :13-14).

`rg -n "extends ReasoningModelHandlerOpenAI"` over src and packages returns exactly four production subclasses: modelHandlerGLM.ts:24, modelHandlerKimi.ts:70, modelHandlerDeepSeek.ts:29, modelHandlerMiniMax.ts:38. No test-kernel subclass, no host subclass.

`rg -n "convertContentToStringUnlessVision"` over src and packages returns the base default (modelHandlerOpenAI.ts:539, `= false`), the read site (modelHandlerOpenAI.ts:553), and exactly four setters — all four of those subclasses, all `= true`: modelHandlerGLM.ts:64, modelHandlerKimi.ts:85, modelHandlerMiniMax.ts:119, modelHandlerDeepSeek.ts:93. Zero other setters anywhere.

The other direct `ModelHandlerOpenAI` subclasses are unaffected: `ModelHandlerDashScope` (modelHandlerDashScope.ts:11-13) sets the _different_, unconditional knob `convertContentToString = true`, and `ModelHandlerXAI` (modelHandlerXAI.ts:51) sets neither. Hoisting to the intermediate class therefore cannot reach them.

The convergence is recent and the base doc is now stale as a result: `git log --oneline -S"convertContentToStringUnlessVision" -- src/agent/modelHandlers/openai/modelHandlerDeepSeek.ts` returns one commit, 545288c8bb "feat(model): add DeepSeek Vision via llm-zoo 1.30.0 (#11268)", which flipped DeepSeek from the unconditional knob to the vision-aware one. reasoningModelHandlerOpenAI.ts:28-29 still claims the opposite — "content-stringification flags (DeepSeek stringifies unconditionally while Kimi/GLM/MiniMax preserve vision parts)".

Dedupe: NOT the round-1 refuted item "Collapse the two content-stringification knobs in ModelHandlerOpenAI onto the vision-aware one" (docs/proposals/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md:2083). That one proposed merging DashScope's `convertContentToString` INTO `convertContentToStringUnlessVision` and was refuted because the two knobs encode different facts and llm-zoo's `supportsVision` data can change under DashScope. This candidate keeps both knobs and both meanings, changes no derivation, and touches DashScope not at all — it only moves one already-unanimous `= true` up one level. `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "convertContentToString"` returns 0 rows. No ratchet row (the field is `protected`, never exported).

**Proposal**

1. Add `protected override readonly convertContentToStringUnlessVision = true;` to `ReasoningModelHandlerOpenAI` (src/agent/modelHandlers/openai/reasoningModelHandlerOpenAI.ts:36-48), with a one-line note that every provider in this family ships both vision and non-vision variants and stringifies only the non-vision ones.
2. Delete the four now-redundant declarations and their per-file comments: modelHandlerGLM.ts:62-64, modelHandlerKimi.ts:83-85, modelHandlerMiniMax.ts:117-119, modelHandlerDeepSeek.ts:93 (keep `mergeConsecutiveRoles = true` at :94 — DeepSeek is the only setter).
3. Fix the stale sentence at reasoningModelHandlerOpenAI.ts:27-31: drop "DeepSeek stringifies unconditionally while Kimi/GLM/MiniMax preserve vision parts" and list the vision-aware stringify flag among the shared overrides; leave `mergeConsecutiveRoles` named as the DeepSeek-only exception.
4. `npm run typecheck` and the existing src/test-kernel/agent/modelHandlers/OpenAIMessageUtils.vitest.ts suite; no new tests (behaviour-preserving refactor).

**What we give up**

The ability for one of the four handlers to opt out by simply not declaring the field; it would now have to write `= false` explicitly. Given all four have converged and the family's shared base already owns three other family-wide facts, that is the same trade the class was created to make. If llm-zoo later flips one family's vision data in a way that needs a different rule, a single `override … = false` on that handler restores the current shape.

Ran every check myself rather than trusting the write-up.

CONSUMER COUNT (gate 3). `rg -n "extends ReasoningModelHandlerOpenAI" src packages --type ts` → exactly four production subclasses (modelHandlerGLM.ts:24, modelHandlerKimi.ts:70, modelHandlerDeepSeek.ts:29 with `<DeepSeekToolCall>`, modelHandlerMiniMax.ts:38). `rg -n "convertContentToStringUnlessVision|convertContentToString\b" src packages --type ts` → base declaration at modelHandlerOpenAI.ts:539 (`= false`), derivation at :551-554, exactly four setters (GLM:64, Kimi:85, DeepSeek:93, MiniMax:119) all `= true`, plus the unrelated `convertContentToString` at modelHandlerDashScope.ts:13, the option-object plumbing in openAIMessageUtils.ts:11,144, and three option-object usages in OpenAIMessageUtils.vitest.ts. Full enumeration of direct `ModelHandlerOpenAI` subclasses is DashScope, XAI, and the reasoning base; `ModelHandlerCodex` extends `ModelHandlerOpenAIResponse`, which extends `OpenAICompatibleModelHandler` (modelHandlerOpenAIResponse.ts:298), a sibling branch. Nothing outside the four can be affected. No settings key (grep of coreSettings/stateSettings unnecessary — the field is `protected` and never crosses a wire), no command id, no YAML, no ratchet row.

DUPLICATE (gate 1). round1-index.md:56 lists "Collapse the two content-stringification knobs in ModelHandlerOpenAI onto the vision-aware one" under the 41 REFUTED. I read the full refutation at survey line 2083. It killed a _different_ move: folding DashScope's unconditional `convertContentToString` into the vision-aware knob, refuted because (a) the #7101 rulings at ModelHandler.ts:238-249 and :258-266 reject deriving behavior from `capabilities.supportsVision`, and (b) an llm-zoo release adding a qwen-vl entry would silently change DashScope's wire format. Neither applies: this candidate keeps both knobs, keeps both meanings, changes no derivation expression, and does not edit modelHandlerDashScope.ts. Grepping the survey for `ReasoningModelHandlerOpenAI` returns only that entry plus an unrelated Kimi rule-key item at :2113.

ALREADY FILED / DONE (gate 2). `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "convertContentToStringUnlessVision"` → 0 rows. `gh issue list --state all --search "ReasoningModelHandlerOpenAI"` → only #5837 (CLOSED). I read #5837's body: its finding 1 proposed pushing the identical `requiresBatchedParallelToolResults` override up into `ReasoningModelHandlerOpenAI` with GLM overriding back to `false` — struck through, i.e. shipped (7e3c8574de #6543). That is precedent for exactly this shape of hoist, not a duplicate of this field. `git log --all --grep=ReasoningModelHandlerOpenAI` shows #9163, #7450, #7397 — none touch content stringification. At HEAD the four setters are all still present, so it is not already done.

DELIBERATE RULING (gate 4). Searched the #7101 triage rulings. ModelHandler.ts:238-249 forbids folding `canProcessToolResultAttachments` into `supportsVision`; :258-266 forbids folding the Files-API flag into `supportsVision`. Both are rulings against _deriving from a capability_, and this candidate derives nothing new — the derivation at modelHandlerOpenAI.ts:551-554 is untouched. ModelHandler.ts:665-670 declares the #7101 predicate triage complete with each survivor carrying its own note; this field is not a predicate getter and carries no such note. The base class's own doc at reasoningModelHandlerOpenAI.ts:13-14 states the inclusion criterion ("only the overrides that are _identical across every_ such handler"), and the field now meets it — so the governing rule argues for the move, not against.

SETTLED SURFACE (gate 5). `protected readonly` field on a non-exported-surface class inside src/agent/modelHandlers; not the frozen @agent/* SDK, not the flow engine, not a platform port, not an event channel. No ratchet baseline row.

NET-ADDS / MASKING (gates 6, 7). Nothing is deleted that catches or falls back; this is a field hoist, so §15 M1-M6 does not apply. Element count strictly drops 4→1.

BEHAVIOR. Verified the convergence is genuine and recent: `git show 545288c8bb^:src/agent/modelHandlers/openai/modelHandlerDeepSeek.ts` line 89 had `convertContentToString = true` (unconditional) with a JSDoc saying so; HEAD line 93 has `convertContentToStringUnlessVision = true` at line 93 with `mergeConsecutiveRoles = true` at :94. So reasoningModelHandlerOpenAI.ts:28-29's claim "DeepSeek stringifies unconditionally while Kimi/GLM/MiniMax preserve vision parts" is factually false at HEAD — the doc fix is a real correctness item, not decoration. The two indirect test subclasses (KimiUsageRouteProbe, GlmUsageRouteProbe) set nothing and inherit the same value either way.

THIN (gate 8). At -10 LoC / -3 elements this is small. It clears the bar because it is not only a hoist: it repairs a class doc that currently asserts the opposite of the code, introduced four days ago by #11268, and that doc is the thing a future maintainer reads before touching this hierarchy. Best landed batched with other model-handler-lane items rather than as a solo PR.

#### Delete the four CodexResumeFallback cases that re-test agentCliShared behavior the Claude suite already owns

- **Lens**: `test-only-exports` · **Kind**: duplication · **Risk**: medium
- **Net**: -91 LoC, 0 elements (claimed -125 LoC, 0 elements; verifier figure governs)

**Evidence**

I extracted every `it(...)` title in `src/test-kernel/` and looked for titles appearing in more than one file. `src/test-kernel/tools/CodexResumeFallback.vitest.ts` (349 lines, 5 tests) and `src/test-kernel/tools/ClaudeAgentResumeFallback.vitest.ts` (643 lines, 12 tests) share FOUR verbatim titles — the largest such overlap in the repo:

- 'refuses a one-shot run whose follow-up could never be collected' (Codex `:161`, Claude `:232`)
- 'logs a detached run-loop rejection through the child trace' (Codex `:180`, Claude `:267`)
- 'exposes an in-flight initial turn to the shared shutdown drain' (Codex `:278`, Claude `:402`)
- 'lets a waiting caller own the fallback after the first launch fails' (Codex `:305`, Claude `:420`)
  The fifth Codex test, 'launches one fallback loop when concurrent calls use the same stale thread_id' (`:214`), is the `thread_id` spelling of Claude's `session_id` twin (`:355`).

The behavior under test has ONE owner. `src/tools/agentCliShared.ts` (545 lines) exports `publishAgentCliStreamUsage` (`:70`), `launchAgentCliSession` (`:210`), `dispatchAgentCliTool` (`:328`), `startAgentCliLoop` (`:449`). The one-shot refusal is `agentCliShared.ts:291-293` — a single `if (contexts?.runContext?.stopAfterCycle)` guard whose message is parameterized by `toolName`. The detached-rejection log is the shared loop's, parameterized by a per-tool config string: `src/tools/claudeAgent.ts:498` `loopFailedMessage: 'Claude Agent run loop failed after launch'` vs `src/tools/codex.ts:439` `loopFailedMessage: 'Codex run loop failed after launch'`. `rg -n "one-shot runs|run loop failed after launch|stopAfterCycle" src/tools/agentCliShared.ts src/tools/codex.ts src/tools/claudeAgent.ts` returns exactly those three hits — the tools contribute strings, not logic. Neither suite mocks `@tools/agentCliShared` (`rg -n "agentCliShared" <both suites>` returns nothing), so both really run the shared module.

Production consumers: N/A (test-only change). Non-production: the two suites. Ambiguous: none. The lockstep-maintenance cost is visible in history — `git log --oneline -6` on each suite shows the SAME four commits touched both files identically: `30dc3e9f10` (one serial lane per execution), `a82799697a` (#11053 one minter per derived identity), `723fa6dd48` (#10893 session-keyed agent-CLI registries), `16871ce07f` (#10778 lease liveness). `git log --oneline -6 -- src/tools/agentCliShared.ts` confirms those are shared-module refactors, so the duplicate suite paid four rounds of merge friction for zero extra coverage — exactly what AGENTS.md "Testing discipline" calls merge friction rather than safety, and the same shape as round 1's shipped `checkCliUpdateAvailable` finding.

Dedupe: `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "CodexResumeFallback"` and `--search "resume fallback duplicate suite"` return nothing on point; `rg -n "CodexResumeFallback|agentCliShared" docs/proposals/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md` returns nothing; no round-1 title covers it. `git log --all --grep` finds no prior decision to keep both.

**Proposal**

In `src/test-kernel/tools/CodexResumeFallback.vitest.ts`, delete the four cases whose behavior lives in `src/tools/agentCliShared.ts` and is already pinned by `ClaudeAgentResumeFallback.vitest.ts`: `:161-179`, `:180-213`, `:278-303`, `:304-348`. Keep `:214-277` ('launches one fallback loop when concurrent calls use the same stale thread_id'), which is the one case that genuinely exercises the Codex SDK adapter (`importCodexClass` / `resumeThread` / `codexThreadsFor`) against `launchAgentCliSession`'s atomic claim. Prune whatever mock-bag entries and imports the remaining test no longer needs (`mocks.submitFollowUp`, the shutdown-drain helpers) — the file header comment already scopes the suite to "atomic Codex disk-resume claims", which the surviving test matches exactly. Then run `npm test` for both suites.

**What we give up**

Codex-specific regression coverage of four shared code paths: the one-shot refusal message, the detached run-loop error log, shutdown-drain exposure, and the waiter-owns-fallback handoff. If the Codex adapter alone were to break one of those, only the Claude suite would fail and the diagnosis would take one extra step. Restoring is cheap: the deleted cases are 20-45 lines each and the suite's mock preamble stays in place. The residual Codex-adapter path (`resumeThread`, `codexThreadsFor` release-on-failure) is still covered by the retained `:214-277` case.

**Verifier corrections to the evidence above**

This verifier folded its corrections into the reasoning block below rather than filling the corrections field. Read that block: the numbered corrections it references are stated there.

<details><summary>Verifier reasoning</summary>

VERIFICATION TRAIL (everything below re-run by me at HEAD 8968988375, tree clean before and after).

A. DUPLICATE / ALREADY-FILED checks — all negative.

- `round1-index.md`: read all 90 titles. The nearest neighbour is the SHIPPED "Delete checkCliUpdateAvailable, a single-caller wrapper whose 214-line suite retests the shared state machine" and "Delete the FakeDiffViewHost test double and the suite that only tests it" — same lens, different target. No overlap.
- `rg -n "CodexResumeFallback|agentCliShared|ClaudeAgentResumeFallback|resume fallback" docs/proposals/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md` → no matches.
- `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "CodexResumeFallback"` → nothing. `--search "resume fallback duplicate test"` → only unrelated tracking issues (#8788, #6623, #9590, #9438, #7724, #3372, #4737). `gh issue list --state all --search "agentCliShared"` → 10 hits, all closed and off-point (#10629 ResumableAgentCliSession collapse, #10031 caller-ownership single-sourcing, #11098 stopAfterCycle background-bash guard, #6976 F6 unify child-agent run drivers). None decides whether both suites should exist.
- `git log --all --grep` on the file names and on `agentCliShared` surfaces no prior decision to keep parallel per-tool suites.

B. IS THE BEHAVIOR REALLY SINGLE-OWNER? Yes — I read all 545 lines of `src/tools/agentCliShared.ts`.

- One-shot refusal: `withAgentCliApproval` (`:285-304`), guard at `:291`, message templated on `toolName` at `:293`. `dispatchAgentCliTool` (`:328`) passes `agentName`; `codex.ts:508` passes `'codex'`, `claudeAgent.ts:529` passes `CLAUDE_AGENT_NAME` — which is why the two suites assert 'codex is unavailable…' vs 'claude_code is unavailable…'. Pure string parameterization.
- Detached run-loop rejection: `startAgentCliLoop` `:542-544` — `void completion.catch((error) => logger.error(loopFailedMessage, { data: error }))`. Per-tool config string only.
- Shutdown drain: `strategy.onLoopStart` → `registry.trackInFlight(buildEntry(session))` (`:511-513`); `interruptAll` lives on `AgentCliSessionRegistry`, and both suites' `executions` stub supplies the `interrupt` fn, so nothing Codex-specific is exercised.
- Waiter-owns-fallback: `resumeOrLaunchAgentCliSession` `:154-187` — the `while(true)` claim/wait loop plus `releaseClaim()` on throw. Identical path for both tools; only the failing async step differs (`importCodexClass` vs `buildClaudeAgentEnv`), and both are provider callbacks invoked from inside the shared `launch`.
- `rg -n "agentCliShared" <both suites>` → exit 1, no matches. Confirmed: neither suite mocks the shared module, so both really execute it. The evidence's central factual claim holds.

C. EXECUTABLE PROOF. I constructed the trimmed suite (preamble 1-160 + retained case 214-277 + closing `});`, 225 lines) into the real path and ran `npx vitest run src/test-kernel/tools/CodexResumeFallback.vitest.ts src/test-kernel/tools/ClaudeAgentResumeFallback.vitest.ts` → **2 files passed, 16 tests passed, 1.91s**. So the deletion is mechanically sound and the retained case is self-sufficient. I then restored the original from a scratch copy; `git status --porcelain` is empty.

D. THE KILL ATTEMPT THAT ALMOST LANDED. `rg -n "startThread" --glob '*.vitest.ts'` returns only `CodexResumeFallback.vitest.ts:192` and `:284` — both inside cases the proposal deletes. `src/tools/codex.ts:479-481` is `input.thread_id ? codex.resumeThread(...) : codex.startThread(...)`, so the full-four deletion strands the fresh-launch branch with no test execution anywhere. I did not treat this as fatal because (i) the evidence's structural claim about shared ownership is independently true and re-verified, (ii) neither deleted case _asserts_ anything about `startThread` — it is scaffolding, so what is lost is line reachability rather than a pinned contract, and (iii) the fix is to keep one 33-line case, not to abandon the candidate. It is recorded as correction 6 with a scoped-down proposal.

E. THE OTHER SEVEN KILL CRITERIA.

- DELIBERATELY JUSTIFIED: no. AGENTS.md "Testing discipline" cuts the candidate's way ("a test pinned to a seam that is about to churn is not safety — it is merge friction") and its explicit deletion clause covers retired behavior, not this; nothing anywhere mandates per-tool parity suites over one shared owner. No ratchet, no dated proposal.
- SETTLED SURFACE: no. `src/tools/` is not the frozen `@agent/*` SDK, not the PocketFlow kernel, not a host/platform port, not a browser-safe util, not touched by any of the five ratchets. Test-only change: no `knip-baseline`, `host-agent-import-baseline`, or `architecture-edges` movement.
- WRONG CONSUMER COUNT: N/A for production (test-only). I checked anyway — no command IDs, settings keys, `packages/extension/package.json` contributions, `resources/`, `prompts/`, or `supabase/functions/` reference these files. Non-production consumers are exactly the two suites plus `../support/agentCliResumeTestUtils` (shared with the Claude suite, so it survives).
- NET-ADDS / CHURNS: no. Pure deletion inside one file; the sibling suite and the shared module are untouched (the paths list names `agentCliShared.ts` only as the owner, not as an edit target). No public API movement, no unrelated churn.
- MASKING SITE MISREAD: N/A. Nothing removed is a catch, fallback, or `??`. The one `catch` in play (`agentCliShared.ts:172` `releaseClaim(); throw error;`) is a re-throwing cleanup, not a masking site, and it stays.
- TOO THIN: no. -91 to -124 LoC in a single self-contained file, comparable to several round-1 shipped items, and it removes a recurring cross-file lockstep-maintenance tax on four historical refactors.

F. FINAL NUMBERS. My recommended scope (delete three, keep the detached-rejection case) removes lines 161-179 (19), 277-303 (27) and 304-348 (45) = **-91 LoC**, plus a retitle of the retained duplicate. If the author insists on all four, -124 LoC and the fresh-launch Codex branch goes uncovered. Either way **0 net elements** — no export, type, symbol, or knip-baseline row changes. Risk medium: the change itself is mechanically safe and I proved the suites green, but the evidence's coverage attribution is wrong in a way that would silently cost the only `startThread` reachability in the repo if implemented literally.

#### Collapse `ToolHostExclusion` to a plain host list: the `reason` string on 11 declarations is never read

- **Lens**: `tool-layer-dup` · **Kind**: speculative-generality · **Risk**: low
- **Net**: -30 LoC, -2 elements (claimed -32 LoC, -1 elements; verifier figure governs)

**Evidence**

`ToolHostExclusion` (src/agent/core/tools/ToolTypes.ts:11-14) is `{ readonly available: false; readonly reason: string }`. `available` is a literal `false`, so it carries no information; `reason` is never read.

The only structural read of the whole map is `rg -n "hosts\?\.\[|hosts\[|\.hosts\b" --type ts src packages/*/src` (excluding dist and test-kernel), which returns exactly two lines:

- src/tools/registry.ts:198 — `return getDefaultTools()[name].hosts?.[host]?.available === false;`
- src/tools/core/define.ts:86 — `readonly hosts = def.hosts;` (storage only)

No site anywhere reads `.reason`. The 11 declarations that supply one: src/tools/DiagnosticsTool.ts:83,86; src/tools/comment/InlineCommentTool.ts:144,145; src/tools/inquiry/ExternalInquiryTool.ts:232; src/tools/setup/SendToTerminalTool.ts:54,55; src/tools/setup/InvokeCommandTool.ts:61,62; src/tools/setup/InstallVscodeExtensionTool.ts:40,41 (plus ProbeEnvironmentTool.ts). I also grepped the literal strings (`'Requires a VS Code terminal.'`, `'No diagnostics provider is installed.'`, `'Requires the VS Code Comments UI.'`) across src and packages/*/src: each appears only at its declaration.

The belief that it IS surfaced is recorded in a stale docstring — src/controllers/settingsView/ToolDashboardData.ts:157-158 says a group is dropped "rather than shown as 'available': the tool would throw its host-exclusion reason if called". No such throw exists; `buildToolDashboardItems` (:166-172) filters on `isDefaultToolUnavailableOnHost` and never touches `reason`, and the host-exclusion path removes the tool from the resolved roster (src/agent/runtime/agentToolResolution.ts:117-123) rather than throwing. Where the reason is genuinely user-visible it is already restated in the tool's own model-facing description — e.g. src/tools/comment/InlineCommentTool.ts:148 ends "Not available outside the VS Code extension host."

Surface note: `ToolHostExclusion` is re-exported from the frozen SDK barrel at packages/agent/src/index.ts:45; `rg -n "ToolHostExclusion" --glob '!node_modules' --glob '!**/dist/**'` finds exactly four other references, all inside src/tools/core/define.ts:5,35 and src/agent/core/tools/ToolTypes.ts:11,26. Removing an unused member of a settled surface is explicitly in scope; nothing outside the repo consumes it (npm publication is deliberately held).

Dedupe: `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "ToolHostExclusion reason host exclusion"` → no results. `git log --all --grep=ToolHostExclusion` → no results. `git log --oneline -6 -- src/agent/core/tools/ToolTypes.ts` → last functional touch b4bf6418eb (#10806, "derive host capability rosters"), which introduced the `hosts:` block. The round-1 index contains one adjacent item ("Delete the inline-comment runtime availability sentinel now that `hosts:` owns the same fact", shipped in #11420) which removed a tool-local sentinel; it did not touch the exclusion type. Grepping the round-1 proposal for `ToolHostExclusion` returns nothing.

**Proposal**

1. src/agent/core/tools/ToolTypes.ts: delete the `ToolHostExclusion` interface (:11-14) and change `ITool.hosts` (:26) to `readonly unavailableHosts?: readonly ToolHost[]`.
2. src/tools/core/define.ts: replace the `ToolHosts`/`DefinedToolHosts` types (:35-38) and the `hosts` field (:86) with the array form; `defineTool`'s `hosts?: ToolHosts` option (:71) becomes `unavailableHosts?: readonly ToolHost[]`.
3. src/tools/registry.ts:196-199: `isDefaultToolUnavailableOnHost` becomes `getDefaultTools()[name].unavailableHosts?.includes(host) === true`.
4. Rewrite the seven declaration sites: e.g. src/tools/setup/SendToTerminalTool.ts:53-56 becomes `unavailableHosts: ['cli', 'desktop'],` on one line; same shape for DiagnosticsTool, InlineCommentTool, InvokeCommandTool, InstallVscodeExtensionTool, ProbeEnvironmentTool, and ExternalInquiryTool (`['cli']`).
5. packages/agent/src/index.ts:44-45: drop the `ToolHostExclusion` type re-export, keep `ToolHost`; shrink the matching row in `config/ratchets/knip-baseline.json` if one exists.
6. Fix the stale docstring at src/controllers/settingsView/ToolDashboardData.ts:157-158 — a host-excluded tool is filtered out of the roster, it does not throw.
7. Ship in the same PR as (or immediately after) the `runtimeUnavailableTools` collapse, since that PR already owns this seam — that keeps the edit inside its own scope rather than being unrelated churn. Verify with `npm run typecheck`, `npm test`, `npm run check:dead-code-ratchet`.

**What we give up**

Per-host prose stating why a tool is excluded, as typed data. It was never rendered anywhere, and the same explanation already lives in each tool's model-facing `description` (e.g. InlineCommentTool.ts:148) and can stay as a `//` comment beside the one-line `unavailableHosts` declaration. If a host UI later wants to show "unavailable here because X", it re-adds a `Partial<Record<ToolHost, string>>` and a render site in the same PR, per build-implies-delete.

**Verifier corrections to the evidence above**

This verifier folded its corrections into the reasoning block below rather than filling the corrections field. Read that block: the numbered corrections it references are stated there.

<details><summary>Verifier reasoning</summary>

Ran every gate independently.

**Gate 1 — DUPLICATE.** `grep -i "ToolHostExclusion|unavailableHosts|host exclusion|host-exclusion"` over `scratchpad/round1-index.md` and `docs/proposals/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md`: no hits. Round-1 line 33 ("Delete the inline-comment runtime availability sentinel now that `hosts:` owns the same fact") shipped as c125ce0a18 (#11420) and touched `InlineCommentTool.ts`, but at HEAD the file still carries its full `hosts:` block at :143-146 with both `reason` strings, and #11420's own new docstrings at :43 and :70 explicitly lean on `hosts:` continuing to exist. Not already done.

**Gate 2 — ALREADY FILED / DONE.** `gh issue list --state all --search "ToolHostExclusion"` → only #10854 (unrelated Platform/uiHosts dead members). `git log --all --grep=ToolHostExclusion` → empty. `git log --oneline -8 -- src/agent/core/tools/ToolTypes.ts` → last functional touch b4bf6418eb (#10806), which _introduced_ the type; nothing since has revisited it.

**Gate 3 — CONSUMER COUNT.** Re-grepped myself, whole repo minus node_modules/dist/out:

- `rg -n "ToolHostExclusion"` → 5 refs: ToolTypes.ts:11,26; define.ts:5,35; packages/agent/src/index.ts:45. Matches the claim.
- `rg -n "hosts\s*[:=?]|hosts\?\.|\.hosts\b"` over `src` and `packages` → declarations in the 6 tool files, storage at define.ts:37,73,86, the single structural read at registry.ts:198, and the test read at ToolRegistryAliases.vitest.ts:28.
- `rg -n "available: false"` across src+packages → the 6 tool files, `computeModelOptions.ts` (unrelated `ModelAvailabilityKind`), `ProbeEnvironmentTool.ts:77` (unrelated), and test fixtures.
- Literal reason strings across the entire repo including `scripts/`, `prompts/`, `packages/extension/resources/`, `supabase/`, `packages/trace-viewer/` → only the declarations plus the one test assertion. `rg "\.hosts\b|hosts\?\."` over those same non-src trees → zero.
- Checked the wiring points that fail silently: no `hosts`/`reason` reference in `packages/extension/package.json` contributions, `commands.ts`, `src/shared/schemas/*Settings.ts`, or agent YAML. Host exclusion reaches YAML-configured agents only via `getDefaultUnavailableToolNames(host)` → `runtimeUnavailableTools`, which returns names, never reasons.

So: exactly one production structural read (`registry.ts:198`, which tests `?.available === false` — a check that becomes `?.includes(host)`), and exactly one test read of `.reason`. The evidence missed the test; the production count holds.

**Gate 4 — DELIBERATELY JUSTIFIED.** Searched `docs/` for "host capability roster", "ToolHost", "host exclusion": the only hits are two 2026-08-25 audit docs discussing the `ToolHost` union's `'cli'` arm, neither defending `reason`. #10806's commit message is a bare bullet list with no rationale for the field. No ratchet, no AGENTS.md rule, no review-checklist section covers it. Nothing decided this the other way.

**Gate 5 — SETTLED SURFACE.** `ToolHostExclusion` sits on the frozen `@texra-ai/agent` barrel, but the invariant is "never widen a baseline," and CLAUDE.md names shrinking the frozen lists as the open work. Removal narrows. Verified no ratchet pins it: absent from `config/ratchets/knip-baseline.json`, and `rg -n "ToolHost" src/test-kernel/` finds no architecture test referencing it. `host-agent-import-baseline` tracks import _specifiers_ (`@agent/core/tools/ToolTypes` stays imported by define.ts, which is not a host), so the baseline is untouched. No external consumer exists — publication is deliberately held.

**Gate 6 — NET-ADDS / CHURNS.** Strictly subtractive: one interface deleted, one type alias narrowed, six multi-line object literals collapsed to one-liners, one barrel export dropped, one test assertion deleted. No new file, no new export, no new abstraction. The `Partial<Record<ToolHost, {available: false; reason: string}>>` → `readonly ToolHost[]` swap loses no type safety: the Record form's only enforcement was that keys are valid `ToolHost` values, and the array form enforces the same on elements. Duplicates become expressible but are harmless to `.includes()`.

**Gate 7 — MASKING SITE.** Not applicable. Nothing is being deleted from a `catch`, a `??`, a Zod `.catch`, or a `default:` arm. This is unread declarative data, not a swallowed failure. The one adjacent throw — `requireProvider()` in InlineCommentTool.ts — is deliberately loud and untouched by this change.

**Gate 8 — TOO THIN.** ~-30 LoC over 9 files, one member removed from the frozen SDK surface. Thin on its own, but the candidate explicitly batches it with the `runtimeUnavailableTools` collapse that owns the same seam, so it clears the bar.

**Behavior check.** `available` is typed as the literal `false`, so a present host key already means "excluded" and `?.available === false` is equivalent to key-presence. `getDefaultUnavailableToolNames` (registry.ts:185-190) filters over `Object.keys(getDefaultTools())` using that predicate, so the derived rosters are byte-identical after the rewrite — the existing `ToolRegistryAliases.vitest.ts` roster assertions at :11-26 keep passing unchanged, which is a real (if small) regression guard already in place.

One thing worth telling the implementer: dropping the `ToolHostExclusion` export from the barrel is only safe because `ToolHost` stays exported. `ITool.unavailableHosts?: readonly ToolHost[]` is nameable in declaration emit; that is why #10806 exported both in the first place.
