#### Derive DetachedChildRunInputBase from ChildRunLoopParams instead of restating nine of its fields

- **Lens**: `dual-representation` · **Kind**: dual-representation · **Risk**: low
- **Net**: -17 LoC, -9 elements (claimed -32 LoC, -1 elements; verifier figure governs)

**Evidence**

Two shapes for one fact: what a child run loop is launched with.

Owner: `ChildRunLoopParams<TTurn>` at src/agent/runtime/childRunLoop.ts:300-362 (11 fields: childStream, childStreamId, parentStreamId, executionId, agentName, strategy, recordCost, budgeted, notify, onTurnSettled, afterArtifactsDrained).

Mirror: `DetachedChildRunInputBase` at src/tools/delegation/detachedChildRun.ts:76-96 restates 9 of those 11 (everything except `strategy` and `childStream`, which the launch guard supplies itself). The file already admits it is a mirror: three of the nine are written as indexed lookups back into the owner — `readonly notify?: ChildRunLoopParams<never>['notify']` (:92), `onTurnSettled` (:94), `afterArtifactsDrained` (:96) — while the other six are hand-copied with their own JSDoc. So `Omit<ChildRunLoopParams<never>, 'strategy' | 'childStream'>` is exactly the mirror, and the import already exists (detachedChildRun.ts:18-22 imports `startChildRunLoop`, `type ChildRunLoopParams`, `type ChildRunStrategy` from @agent/runtime/childRunLoop) — no new module edge, no ratchet touched.

The mirror has already drifted: `recordCost?: (totalCostUsd: number | undefined) => void` (detachedChildRun.ts:81) vs `recordCost?: (totalCostUsd: number | undefined) => void | Promise<void>` (childRunLoop.ts:333-335). Benign today only because TS lets a `Promise<void>`-returning function satisfy a `void` return, but it is a silent narrowing of the owner's contract.

Second tax: the same nine fields are hand-copied a third time at the call site, detachedChildRun.ts:141-159, as a field-by-field literal with four conditional spreads (`...(input.notify !== undefined && { notify: input.notify })` etc.). Those spreads are unnecessary: `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` is not enabled (rg over tsconfig.json and every packages/_/tsconfig_.json returns only `"strict": true` at tsconfig.json:167), and the loop reads every one of them by truthiness/optional chaining, never by key presence — childRunLoop.ts:688 `params.onTurnSettled?.(`, :871 `if (params.notify)`, :905 `params.budgeted ?`, :1097 `params.recordCost?.(`, :1201 `await params.afterArtifactsDrained?.()`. Grep for `'notify' in` / `Object.hasOwn` in childRunLoop.ts: zero hits.

Consumers, production: `startDetachedChildRunLoop` has 4 production call sites, all object literals — src/tools/bash.ts:530, src/tools/delegation/inBandSubagentExecution.ts:401, src/tools/delegation/subagentExecution.ts:217, src/tools/delegation/WorkflowScriptTool.ts:497. `DetachedChildRunInput` is imported once outside the file, src/tools/delegation/inBandSubagentExecution.ts:42, used only as `NonNullable<DetachedChildRunInput<never>['onTurnSettled']>` (:118) — i.e. another indexed lookup that keeps working. Non-production: no test-kernel file references either type name.

Dedupe: `git log --oneline -10` on both paths shows #11413, #11329, and the single-owner-sessions series — none attempts this collapse. `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "childRunLoop"` returns 6 closed issues (#11099 launch-guard ChildStream ownership, #9319 cost latch, #9141 strategy clone, #9433, #7427, #10595), none about the param mirror; `--search "detachedChildRun"` returns zero. `grep -i 'detachedChildRun|ChildRunLoopParams|DetachedChildRunInput'` over docs/proposals/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md: zero hits, and no round-1 index title matches.

**Proposal**

1. Replace the interface body at src/tools/delegation/detachedChildRun.ts:76-96 with `type DetachedChildRunInputBase = Omit<ChildRunLoopParams<never>, 'strategy' | 'childStream'>;`. Keep the doc comment that explains what a detached launch is; drop the six hand-copied field declarations and the three indexed-lookup declarations. `ChildRunLoopParams`'s TTurn parameter only appears on `strategy`, which Omit removes, so `<never>` is sound.
2. At the call site (detachedChildRun.ts:141-159) destructure the launch-only members and spread the rest: `const { createChildStream: _c, buildLaunch: _b, budgeted, ...loopParams } = input;` then `startChildRunLoop({ ...loopParams, ...(childStream !== undefined && { childStream }), strategy: launch.strategy, budgeted: budgeted ?? true })`. `budgeted` stays explicit because its `?? true` default is real behavior (documented at :156-158).
3. Move the JSDoc that only exists on the mirror (the `budgeted` in-band note at :83-87) onto the owner's `budgeted` field if it is not already there; delete it otherwise.
4. No ratchet, baseline, or test changes: `check-dead-code-ratchet` is unaffected (nothing exported changes), and no test-kernel suite names either type.

**What we give up**

The per-field JSDoc currently duplicated on the mirror (six fields) disappears from the detached-launch file; readers follow one hop to ChildRunLoopParams, where the same prose already lives for three of them. Also gives up the ability to narrow a field for detached callers only — `recordCost`'s narrower return type today is the only instance and it is an accidental drift, not a designed narrowing.

**Verifier corrections to the evidence above**

SEVEN factual errors / omissions in the evidence. The most serious is a missed prior ruling.

**C1 (SERIOUS — missed prior ruling; dedupe search was incomplete).** The evidence claims the dedupe grep over docs found nothing. It searched only `docs/proposals/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md`. A repo-wide `rg -l "detachedChildRun|DetachedChildRunInput|ChildRunLoopParams" docs` hits four proposals, and one of them adjudicates this exact bag:

`docs/proposals/2026-08-16-services-injection-audit.md:170`, under the heading "## 3. Rejected and thin candidates (do not refile)" → "Thin — measured and declined":

> "**`DetachedChildRunInput` collapse** — 8-of-9 verbatim FWD, but it carries the lease-guard invariant with 3 callers. Keep."

That row counted the same 9 fields the candidate counts (8 forwarded verbatim, `budgeted` re-derived with `?? true`), so the observation is not new. It survives only because the _action_ is different: that audit's lens was forwarding-chain depth, and the declined action was collapsing the hop — deleting `startDetachedChildRunLoop`/`DetachedChildRunInput` so the four callers call `startChildRunLoop` directly. Its stated blocker ("carries the lease-guard invariant") is untouched here: this candidate keeps the wrapper, the guard, the exported bag name, and every call site's shape, and only changes how the base type is _spelled_. The implementing PR MUST cite `2026-08-16-services-injection-audit.md:170` and state that distinction explicitly, or it reads as re-litigating a "do not refile" row. Note also the audit says "3 callers"; there are 4 (see C2) — its own count was stale.

**C2 (consumer count, minor).** `startDetachedChildRunLoop` has 4 production call sites — confirmed exactly at `src/tools/bash.ts:530`, `src/tools/delegation/inBandSubagentExecution.ts:401`, `src/tools/delegation/subagentExecution.ts:217`, `src/tools/delegation/WorkflowScriptTool.ts:497`. `DetachedChildRunInput` imported once outside the file (`inBandSubagentExecution.ts:42`, used at `:118`). Both correct in the evidence. But "no test-kernel file references either type name" is true-but-misleading: seven test files exercise this launch path (`src/test-kernel/agent/runtime/ChildRunLoop.vitest.ts`, `tools/WorkflowScriptTool.vitest.ts`, `tools/SubagentExecutionChildStreamId.vitest.ts`, `tools/NativeSubagentStrategy.vitest.ts`, `tools/ClaudeAgentResumeFallback.vitest.ts`, `tools/CodexResumeFallback.vitest.ts`, `agent/followUp/ToolUseWaitNode.vitest.ts`). I ran the first four: 81 tests, all pass on the patched tree.

**C3 (line numbers in the mirror are all off by 1–2).** Actual at HEAD: `interface DetachedChildRunInputBase` spans 76–96 (correct), but inside it `recordCost` is line **83** (claimed :81), the `budgeted` JSDoc is **84–88** with the field at **89** (claimed ":83-87"), `notify` is **91** (claimed :92), `onTurnSettled` **93** (claimed :94), `afterArtifactsDrained` **95** (claimed :96 — that is the closing brace).

**C4 (owner line numbers off by 5).** `ChildRunLoopParams` spans 300–362 (correct), but `recordCost?: (...) => void | Promise<void>` is at **328–330**, not 333–335.

**C5 (missed a read site).** The evidence enumerates five loop read sites (688, 871, 905, 1097, 1201 — all five verified correct) and concludes "never by key presence". It missed a sixth: `childRunLoop.ts:1007` forwards `onTurnSettled: params.onTurnSettled` unconditionally into `deliverTurn`. This strengthens the case (the loop itself already destroys key-presence for that field) but the enumeration was incomplete. `'notify' in` / `Object.hasOwn` / `in params`: zero hits — confirmed.

**C6 (understated the recordCost drift).** The evidence calls the mirror's `=> void` narrowing "benign". The loop actually _consumes_ the return value: `childRunLoop.ts:1097-1100` does `const observed = params.recordCost?.(bestCostUsd); void Promise.resolve(observed).catch(err => logger.warn('Child cost observer rejected', …))`. So the owner's `Promise<void>` arm is load-bearing (it is how an async cost observer's rejection gets logged), and the mirror's narrowed type is a genuine — if structurally-tolerated — misstatement of a live contract. Widening it is the real correctness value of this change.

**C7 (LoC claim is ~2x overstated).** Claimed -32. I applied the exact proposal and measured `git diff --numstat`: **17 insertions, 34 deletions = -17 net**, in one file, prettier-clean. If proposal step 3 is honored (relocating the in-band `budgeted` note onto the owner) it is roughly **-13**; I dropped it instead, since the call-site comment at what is now `detachedChildRun.ts:141` already states the same fact. The claimed "-1 elements" is also wrong: an interface with 9 property signatures becomes a type alias with 0, so top-level declaration count is unchanged (1 → 1); the reduction is the 9 duplicated field declarations.

**Verified as stated (no correction needed):** `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` is set nowhere in the workspace — repo-wide rg returns zero hits, and `tsconfig.json:167` is `"strict": true` exactly as claimed. The import already exists at `detachedChildRun.ts:18-22`, so no new module edge and no `host-agent-import-baseline` exposure (and `src/tools/` is not a host anyway). `ChildRunLoopParams`'s `TTurn` appears only on `strategy`, so `<never>` under `Omit` is sound. `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "childRunLoop"` returns exactly the 6 closed issues named (#11099, #9319, #9141, #9433, #7427, #10595), none about the param mirror; `--search "detachedChildRun"` and `--search "ChildRunLoopParams"` and `--search "DetachedChildRunInput"` all return zero. Zero hits in the 49-candidate survey doc; no round-1 index title matches (the only nearby row, "Collapse the three single-caller delegation-availability wrappers into one annotator", is unrelated). Round-1 PRs #11416–#11422 touched neither file — latest commits are `30dc3e9f10` (detachedChildRun.ts) and `79b4f1143a` / #11413 (childRunLoop.ts).

<details><summary>Verifier reasoning</summary>

**I implemented the exact proposal and validated it, then reverted; the tree is clean.**

**Step 1 — the two shapes are real.** `ChildRunLoopParams<TTurn>` (`src/agent/runtime/childRunLoop.ts:300-362`) has 11 members: childStream, childStreamId, parentStreamId, executionId, agentName, strategy, recordCost, budgeted, notify, onTurnSettled, afterArtifactsDrained. `DetachedChildRunInputBase` (`src/tools/delegation/detachedChildRun.ts:76-96`) restates exactly the other 9. Three are already written as indexed lookups back into the owner (`ChildRunLoopParams<never>['notify' | 'onTurnSettled' | 'afterArtifactsDrained']` at :91, :93, :95) — the file already concedes it is a mirror. So `Omit<ChildRunLoopParams<never>, 'strategy' | 'childStream'>` is exactly the mirror. Confirmed.

**Step 2 — key-presence safety.** The proposal replaces four conditional spreads with a rest spread. Two independent reasons this is safe, both verified: (a) `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` is off repo-wide; (b) more fundamentally, a rest destructure copies only _own_ properties, so a caller that omits `notify` still yields a `loopParams` with no `notify` key — presence is preserved exactly as passed, not merely tolerated. I checked all four call sites for an explicit `field: undefined`: none passes one (`inBandSubagentExecution.ts:410` uses its own conditional spread). And the loop never tests presence: `'notify' in`, `Object.hasOwn`, `in params` → zero hits in childRunLoop.ts; every read is truthiness, optional-call, or unconditional forward (:688, :871, :905, :1007, :1097, :1201).

**Step 3 — no unknown-key leak.** All four call sites pass object literals, so excess-property checking applies, and I grepped each literal for a top-level `...variable` spread that would bypass it: the only top-level spread anywhere is `inBandSubagentExecution.ts:410`'s conditional `notify`; the spreads at `WorkflowScriptTool.ts:550,568` and `inBandSubagentExecution.ts:440,457,469` are nested inside `buildLaunch` return objects, not the input literal.

**Step 4 — narrowing survives the destructure.** The `if (input.createChildStream)` discriminant that selects the 1-arg vs 0-arg `buildLaunch` overload cannot be destructured (it is not a literal discriminant). I kept the narrowing on `input.*` and placed the destructure after `launch` is built. Rest-on-a-union typechecked without complaint.

**Step 5 — I actually ran it.** Applied:

- `detachedChildRun.ts:76-96` → `type DetachedChildRunInputBase = Omit<ChildRunLoopParams<never>, 'strategy' | 'childStream'>;` plus a 5-line doc comment naming the two members the guard supplies itself.
- Call site 141-159 → `const { createChildStream: _createChildStream, buildLaunch: _buildLaunch, budgeted, ...loopParams } = input;` then `startChildRunLoop({ ...loopParams, ...(childStream !== undefined && { childStream }), strategy: launch.strategy, budgeted: budgeted ?? true })`, keeping the budget comment.

Results: `npm run typecheck` (all seven projects: root, agent, cli, cli scripts, extension, desktop ×4, trace-viewer) → **EXIT=0**, no errors. `npx eslint src/tools/delegation/detachedChildRun.ts` → **EXIT=0** (underscore-prefixed unused destructure targets are allowed). `npx prettier --check` → clean. `npx vitest run` on ChildRunLoop + WorkflowScriptTool + SubagentExecutionChildStreamId + NativeSubagentStrategy → **81/81 pass**. `git diff --numstat` → **17 insertions, 34 deletions**. Then `git checkout --` and confirmed `git status --porcelain` empty.

**Step 6 — kill-criteria sweep.**

1. DUPLICATE: no. Zero hits in the 49-candidate survey; no round-1 title match; round-1 PRs #11416–#11422 touched neither file, and I read both files at HEAD with the mirror intact.
2. ALREADY FILED/DONE: no issue matches (four `gh` searches); no commit matches `git log --all --grep`.
3. CONSUMER COUNT: re-grepped myself. 4 call sites (evidence said 4 — correct; the 2026-08-16 audit's "3 callers" is the stale count). One external type consumer, an indexed lookup that still resolves (typecheck proves it). Nothing dynamic: these are direct function references, not command IDs, config keys, event names, or YAML/prompt strings — I checked no `packages/extension/package.json` contribution, `commands.ts`, `*Settings.ts`, or `resources/` reference exists (the symbols are internal TS only).
4. DELIBERATELY JUSTIFIED: **this is the one that nearly killed it.** `docs/proposals/2026-08-16-services-injection-audit.md:170` declines a "`DetachedChildRunInput` collapse" under a "do not refile" heading. I read the full section (lines 140-185) and its framing (§1). That audit's unit of analysis is the _forwarding chain_ — its worst-10 table is "frames SET→USE (pure-FWD in parens)", and every disposition in that list is about deleting or shortening a hop. The declined action is therefore deleting the wrapper so callers reach `startChildRunLoop` directly, and the blocker it gives — "it carries the lease-guard invariant" — is a reason to keep the _function_, not the nine hand-copied field declarations. This candidate keeps the function, the guard, the exported bag, and every caller's shape byte-for-byte; it changes only the spelling of the base type from restated to derived. The ruling's rationale does not reach it, and the change is empirically net-negative and green. It beats the ruling — narrowly, and only if the PR cites and distinguishes it.
5. SETTLED SURFACE: untouched. `src/tools/` is not a host, so `host-agent-import-baseline` is not in play; no new `@agent/*` specifier regardless (the import exists at :18-22); no ratchet, no vscode-free-zone edge, no AgentEvent/SessionFact change, no PocketFlow engine change.
6. NET-ADDS/CHURNS: no — measured -17, one file, nothing exported changes so `check-dead-code-ratchet` is inert, and no test edits were needed.
7. MASKING SITE: no. The change deletes zero `catch`, zero `??` fallback, zero `.catch()`. The lease-guard `try/catch` and the `AggregateError` finalize path at :160-176 are untouched. The conditional spreads it removes are type-plumbing, not error handling. §15 does not apply.
8. TOO THIN: borderline and worth flagging. -17 LoC in one file, 0 net top-level declarations. It should ride a tools-lane batch rather than stand as its own PR — which is how round 1 shipped. What lifts it above pure cosmetics is C6: the mirror silently narrows a contract the loop actually consumes.

**Verdict: survives, low risk, but two conditions.** Batch it, and cite `2026-08-16-services-injection-audit.md:170` in the PR body with the derive-vs-delete-the-hop distinction spelled out.

</details>

#### Delete KVStoreCache: three memoizations of a stateless two-field KVStore handle

- **Lens**: `storage-paths` · **Kind**: speculative-generality · **Risk**: low
- **Net**: -180 LoC, -8 elements (claimed -160 LoC, -8 elements; verifier figure governs)

**Evidence**

WHAT IS CACHED. `KVStore` (`src/common/storage/KVStore.ts:45-52`) holds exactly two fields: a relative `dir` string and an `indent`. Every operation re-resolves the absolute path per call — `keyToPath` (`KVStore.ts:17-19`) joins `dir`, and `StorageFS` inherits `RelativeFS.resolvePath` (`src/utils/files/relativeFS.ts:20-25`) which calls `this.getBasePath()` -> `platform().storage.getStoragePath()` (`src/utils/files/storageFS.ts:17-20`) on EVERY read/write/stat. `KVStore.write` (`KVStore.ts:62-67`) additionally re-`ensureDir`s on every write, with the comment: "Ensured on every write, not latched once: `dir` is storage-root relative, so a cached store outlives the root it first saw (workspace switch, test temp platform) and a latch would write into a directory that no longer exists."

So a `KVStore` is a pure function of its key, and `KVStoreCache`'s own doc concedes it (`src/common/storage/KVStoreCache.ts:12-14`): "Handles are stateless wrappers over a directory path, so dropping one is lossless."

THE STATED REASON FOR INVALIDATION IS FALSE. `KVStoreCache.ts:6-9` and `StreamLogStore.ts:1367-1372` claim handles must be dropped "after a storage-root replacement" / "a stream's directory may have moved (staged-deletion rollback, storage-root refresh)" so the next access "re-resolves against the new root". Because the dir is relative and the root is resolved per operation, a re-created handle is byte-identical to the invalidated one. Every `invalidate`/`invalidateAll` call is semantically a no-op. Same-file proof: `deletePersistedStreamLog` (`StreamLogStore.ts:180-188`) already does `new KVStore(STREAM_LOGS_DIR, { compactJson: true }).delete(...)` per call, ten lines from the class that caches the identical handle.

THE THREE OWNERS (production, all in src/):

1. `src/transcript/StreamLogStore.ts:405-407` — `new KVStoreCache<string>((dir) => new KVStore(dir, { compactJson: true }))` keyed by DIRECTORY NAME, over a two-element constant key space: `kv()` -> `STREAM_LOGS_DIR` (`:446-448`), `summaryKv()` -> `STREAM_LOG_SUMMARIES_DIR` (`:450-452`). One `invalidateAll()` at `:1372`.
2. `src/transcript/StreamSnapshotStore.ts:486-488` — `new KVStoreCache<StreamTabId>((streamId) => new KVStore(streamDataDir(streamId)))` with NO `max`, i.e. an unbounded Map keyed by stream id (a leak surface bounded only by `evict`). Reached via `kv()` (`:607-612`), `invalidateKvHandles()` (`:614-617`), `evict` (`:1210`), `evictAll` (`:1224`), `:1979`.
3. `src/agent/storage/ExecutionKVStore.ts:411-414` — `max: 50`, the only owner that passes `max` at all; `getExecutionStore` (`:416-418`), `clearStoreCache` (`:421-423`).

The cache leaks into a cross-file port: `StagedDeletionHost.invalidateKvHandles` (`src/transcript/StagedDeletionCoordinator.ts:97`), called at `:291` and `:434`, wired at `StreamSnapshotStore.ts:501`.

ABSTRACTION COST (checklist §13). Extracted by #10335 (`6f5d616b6e refactor(transcript): extract shared per-stream KV-store base`). Since then FOUR fix PRs and FIVE closed tech-debt issues, all about the `max` knob and its tests, none about the cache's value: `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "KVStoreCache"` -> #10345, #10387, #10388, #10443, #10444 (all CLOSED, area:storage-backend); `git log --all --grep=KVStoreCache` -> #10364, #10427, #10477, #10495. `MAX_BOUNDED_HANDLES` and its 14-line RangeError guard (`KVStoreCache.ts:24-66`) exist solely to police a parameter that exactly one owner passes, with one constant value.

DEDUPE. Not in the round-1 index (90 titles, checked). `grep -in "KVStoreCache|clearStoreCache|storeCache" docs/proposals/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md` returns exactly one hit, line 2171, and it is a REFUTATION of an unrelated perf claim that itself observes "KVStoreCache caches handles not content" — supporting evidence, not a prior filing. No `docs/proposals`/`docs/architecture` doc justifies the cache. Not a settled surface (it is not one of the five ratchets, not @agent/*, not the flow engine, not a host seam).

CONSUMER CLASSIFICATION. Production: the three owners above + `packages/extension/src/extension.ts:346` (`clearStoreCache()` on deactivation) — this call and `getExecutionStore`'s instance identity are PRESERVED by the proposal below. Non-production: `src/test-kernel/common/KVStoreCache.vitest.ts` (60 lines, tests only the deleted class's `max` guard and LRU recency); `src/test-kernel/transcript/StagedDeletionCoordinator.vitest.ts:70-71,230` (asserts the removed port call). 13 test files / 40 call sites use `clearStoreCache` — none of them are touched.

RATCHET WIN. `config/ratchets/knip-baseline.json` currently baselines four production-dead exports that only exist because the two directory constants must be visible to the cache factory: `src/transcript/StreamLogStore.ts` STREAM_LOGS_DIR, `src/transcript/StreamLogStore.ts` STREAM_LOG_SUMMARIES_DIR, `src/transcript/index.ts` STREAM_LOGS_DIR, `src/transcript/index.ts` STREAM_LOG_SUMMARIES_DIR. With two `private readonly` KVStore fields they become module-private and all four rows leave the baseline (baseline shrinks, never widens).

**Proposal**

1. `StreamLogStore`: replace `kvHandles` (`:405-407`) with two fields — `private readonly logsKv = new KVStore(WORKSPACE_STORAGE_LAYOUT.streamLogs, { compactJson: true })` and `private readonly summariesKv = new KVStore(WORKSPACE_STORAGE_LAYOUT.streamLogSummaries, { compactJson: true })`; point `kv()`/`summaryKv()` (`:446-452`) at them; delete `this.kvHandles.invalidateAll()` (`:1372`) and the stale "re-resolve against the new root" comment above it. Unexport `STREAM_LOGS_DIR`/`STREAM_LOG_SUMMARIES_DIR`, drop them from `src/transcript/index.ts`, and delete the four matching rows from `config/ratchets/knip-baseline.json`.
2. `StreamSnapshotStore`: replace `kvHandles` (`:486-488`) with `private kv(streamId) { this.getOrCreateRecord(streamId); return new KVStore(streamDataDir(streamId)); }`; delete `invalidateKvHandles` (`:614-617`) and its call sites (`:1210`, `:1224`, `:1979`) and the `invalidateKvHandles` entry in the `StagedDeletionHost` literal (`:501`).
3. `StagedDeletionCoordinator`: delete the `invalidateKvHandles` member from `StagedDeletionHost` (`:97`) and its two calls (`:291`, `:434`); delete the corresponding fake + assertion in `StagedDeletionCoordinator.vitest.ts:70-71,230`.
4. `ExecutionKVStore`: replace the `KVStoreCache` (`:411-414`) with a plain `new LRUCache<ExecutionId, StorageFSKVStore>({ max: 50 })` and get-or-create inside `getExecutionStore`. `getExecutionStore` still returns a stable instance per id and `clearStoreCache` still clears it, so the 13 test files that call `clearStoreCache` and the `vi.spyOn(store, ...)` suites (e.g. `src/test-kernel/agent/SessionResumeRetrieval.vitest.ts`, `src/test-kernel/tools/WorkflowScriptTool.vitest.ts:181`) are untouched.
5. Delete `src/common/storage/KVStoreCache.ts` and `src/test-kernel/common/KVStoreCache.vitest.ts`. `lru-cache` stays a dependency (7 other importers).

**What we give up**

Nothing measurable. Two allocations of a two-field object per transcript sidecar write, and one per stream-snapshot sidecar write, are recreated instead of memoized; each such call already performs at least one filesystem syscall. Lost capability: none — the cache stores no data, and its `invalidate`/`invalidateAll` operations are provably no-ops given per-operation root resolution. Restoring it is a `git revert`. The `ExecutionKVStore` LRU (the only cache with a real bound and a real reason — instance identity for `clearStoreCache` and spies) is deliberately kept.

**Verifier corrections to the evidence above**

The core claim (KVStoreCache memoizes a provably stateless handle; every invalidate/invalidateAll is a semantic no-op) is CORRECT and I re-verified it end to end. But the evidence contains one leg that must be struck entirely and several smaller errors.

1. FATAL TO ONE LEG — the "RATCHET WIN" (proposal step 1's unexport + four knip-baseline row deletions) is a DUPLICATE of a round-1 REFUTED candidate and is ruled against by a dated doc.
   - Round-1 refuted item: "Drop eight transcript exports with zero production consumers and shrink the knip baseline" (round1-index.md REFUTED list; full refutation at docs/proposals/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md:2563-2569). Point (c) of that refutation covers exactly STREAM_LOGS_DIR/STREAM_LOG_SUMMARIES_DIR: unexporting them "turns one canonical constant into four local copies of WORKSPACE_STORAGE_LAYOUT.streamLogs" across src/test-kernel/transcript/StreamLogStoreLoad.vitest.ts:25, src/test-kernel/cli/History.vitest.ts:104-105, src/test-kernel/cli/CliPersistenceFlush.vitest.ts:18, src/test-kernel/desktop/DesktopAgentExecution.vitest.ts:69 — R5/R6 relocation, not removal. Point (d) cites the dated ruling docs/proposals/2026-08-19-dead-code-gate-blind-spots.md:184-197 (verified: "must be baselined, not deleted"; baseline burn-down is a separate scheduled sweep) plus scripts/check-dead-code-ratchet.mjs.
   - The evidence's factual premise for this leg is also FALSE. The claim "these four rows only exist because the two directory constants must be visible to the cache factory" does not survive a grep: both constants are consumed by module-level production code in the same file independently of the cache — StreamLogStore.ts:182, :184 (deletePersistedStreamLog), :213, :510, :1577 — and are exported for the four test files above. Deleting the cache changes nothing about their export status. Strike step 1's unexport/knip clause; keep only the field swap.

2. Consumer/count errors (all minor, none fatal):
   - "lru-cache stays a dependency (7 other importers)" — there are 12 other importers, not 7: src/tools/github/RepoPollingSource.ts, src/utils/core/boundedIdSet.ts, src/utils/system/platformPaths.ts, src/utils/git/worktreeInfo.ts, src/shared/markdown/createMarkdownProcessor.ts, src/replacement/engine.ts, src/model/computeModelOptions.ts, src/model/apiProviders.ts, packages/extension/src/progressView/frontend/slices/inquiryDraftState.ts, packages/extension/src/progressView/frontend/components/LogList.ts, packages/cli/src/chat/tui/render/ansiMarkdown.ts, packages/cli/src/chat/tui/state/transcriptLines.ts.
   - "13 test files / 40 call sites use clearStoreCache" — exact for src/test-kernel only; repo-wide it is 17 files / 44 sites once packages/extension/src/extension.ts:11,:346, src/agent/storage/index.ts:21 and the definition are counted. The conclusion (none touched) still holds.
   - Missed one commit that touched the file: 1d17d1b29a "refactor: sweep local simplifications…" (#10875) also edited KVStoreCache.get. So it is five touching commits, not four fix PRs.

3. Line-number errors (verified against HEAD):
   - StreamLogStore.kv() is :445-447, not ":446-448"; summaryKv() is :449-452, not ":450-452". (:446 and :451 are the two `kvHandles.get(...)` lines.)
   - deletePersistedStreamLog starts at :179, not ":180-188"; the KVStore construction is at :182/:184 as stated.
   - StreamSnapshotStore.invalidateKvHandles is :615-617 with its doc comment on :614 (cited as ":614-617" — the doc line is included).
   - "MAX_BOUNDED_HANDLES and its 14-line RangeError guard (KVStoreCache.ts:24-66)" — the const doc is :22-30, the const :31, the guard :49-66 (18 lines including its 9-line comment). Range is loose, the substance is right.
   - Verified exact: StreamLogStore kvHandles :405-407 and invalidateAll :1372 (comment :1369-1371); StreamSnapshotStore kvHandles :486-488, kv() :607-612, evict :1210, evictAll :1224, seed refresh :1979, port literal :501; StagedDeletionCoordinator :97, :291, :434; ExecutionKVStore :411-414, :416-418, :421-423.

4. LoC understated. Excluding the struck knip-JSON leg, the deletion is larger than claimed: KVStoreCache.ts is 87 lines, its vitest 60, plus ~36 lines of plumbing across four files, minus ~6 added (two KVStore fields + the inline LRU get-or-create in ExecutionKVStore). Real net is about -180, not -160 — unless the claimed -160 already netted the ~24 JSON lines it can no longer claim, in which case the source-only figure is still ~-180.

5. Two supporting facts the evidence did not state but that strengthen it, and which the implementing PR should carry as the correctness argument:
   - The staged namespace is NEVER a KVStore dir. stagedStreamDataDir appears only in StagedDeletionCoordinator.ts:273, :412, :504, :523, :587 as a raw StorageFS rename/delete/exists target; the only KVStore dir for a stream is streamDataDir(streamId) (StreamSnapshotStore.ts:487), a pure function of the id. So a handle cannot ever point at the wrong namespace, which is what makes both `invalidateKvHandles` calls no-ops.
   - ExecutionKVStore's StorageFSKVStore (:183-210) is likewise stateless — it holds only executionId, and resolveRunStoragePath (src/platform/defaults/workspaceStorage.ts:82-84) returns a root-relative posix path — so the kept LRU is pure memoization, not root-latching.
   - src/test-kernel/transcript/StreamLogStoreLoad.vitest.ts:935-951 ("prepares transcript directories again after a storage-root reload") asserts ensureDir-per-write, not handle identity, so it passes unchanged after the invalidateAll deletion. Worth naming in the PR since it looks like a cache test and is not one.

6. Dedupe/ruling checks I re-ran and that came back clean for the CORE claim: not in round1-index.md (95 lines, zero KVStore/StreamSnapshotStore/ExecutionKVStore/StagedDeletion hits); the only survey hit is :2171, a refutation of an unrelated perf claim; `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "KVStoreCache"` returns only #10345/#10387/#10388/#10443/#10444, all about the `max` guard and its tests, none about the cache's value; no open issue mentions KVStore; KVStoreCache is not exported from packages/agent/src (the dist .d.ts is transitive type emission, not the frozen SDK surface); no architecture ratchet under src/test-kernel/architecture references it; #10335 extracted the class as a dedupe of three hand-rolled lifecycles but never ruled that the invalidation itself is load-bearing.

<details><summary>Verifier reasoning</summary>

VERIFICATION TRAIL

A. Is the handle really stateless? Read src/common/storage/KVStore.ts in full: the class holds exactly `dir` (relative) and `indent`. keyToPath (:17-19) joins per call. Every op goes through StorageFS, which is RelativeFS (src/utils/files/relativeFS.ts:20-25) resolving against `getBasePath()` -> platform().storage.getStoragePath() (src/utils/files/storageFS.ts:17-20) on EVERY call. write (:62-67) re-ensureDirs each time with an explicit comment rejecting latching. CONFIRMED: a re-created handle is indistinguishable from the cached one.

B. Could a handle ever point at a stale/moved directory? streamDataDir (src/transcript/streamDataPaths.ts:76-78) is path.join(STREAM_DATA_DIR, encodeStreamId(id)) — pure. stagedStreamDataDir is used only as a raw FS rename target inside StagedDeletionCoordinator, never as a KVStore dir. resolveRunStoragePath is posix.join(RUNS_STORAGE_DIR, ...) — relative. CONFIRMED: invalidate/invalidateAll cannot change any resolved path; all five production invalidation sites (StreamLogStore:1372, StreamSnapshotStore:1210/:1224/:1979, plus the two coordinator-driven ones at StagedDeletionCoordinator:291/:434) are no-ops.

C. Consumer count. grep over src+packages (excluding dist) for KVStoreCache: exactly three production owners (StreamLogStore:405, StreamSnapshotStore:486, ExecutionKVStore:411) plus one test file. invalidateKvHandles: coordinator port :97, calls :291/:434, snapshot store :501/:615/:1979, and the vitest fake :70-71/:230. No command IDs, settings keys, YAML, prompts, or supabase functions involved. clearStoreCache/getExecutionStore identity is preserved by the proposal, so the ~44 call sites are untouched.

D. Duplicate/filed/done. Round-1 index: zero hits for any symbol in this candidate. Survey doc: one hit at :2171, a refutation that itself notes "KVStoreCache caches handles not content" — supporting, not prior filing. gh issue search: five CLOSED issues, all about the `max` knob. git log --all --grep=KVStoreCache: #10335 (extraction) + four `max`-guard fixes + #10875 sweep. HEAD still contains the class, so nothing is already done.

E. Deliberate justification. #10335's rationale is dedupe of three hand-rolled get-or-create+invalidate lifecycles. That is a consolidation ruling, not a ruling that the lifecycle is needed. No dated proposal/architecture doc, ratchet, AGENTS.md rule, or checklist section defends the cache. The one dated ruling I found (2026-08-19-dead-code-gate-blind-spots.md) kills only the knip-baseline leg, not the deletion.

F. Settled surface. Not @agent/* public (nothing in packages/agent/src imports it), not the flow engine, not a platform port, not one of the six browser-reachable utils, not AgentEvent/SessionFact, not one of the five ratchets, no architecture test references it.

G. Masking-site check (§15). Nothing being deleted is a catch, a `??` over a failed read, a Zod .catch, or a default-drop. The deleted code is a cache-invalidation call, not error handling. M1-M6 do not apply.

H. Net direction. Removals: 87-line class + 60-line test + ~36 lines of plumbing across four production files and one test. Additions: two KVStore fields in StreamLogStore, one inline LRUCache get-or-create in ExecutionKVStore (~6 lines). No new exports, no new indirection, no relocated constants once the knip leg is struck. Elements: -KVStoreCache class, -get, -invalidate, -invalidateAll, -MAX_BOUNDED_HANDLES, -StreamSnapshotStore.invalidateKvHandles, -StagedDeletionHost.invalidateKvHandles, -two kvHandles fields, +two KVStore fields => about -8.

I. Side benefit worth keeping in the PR body: StreamSnapshotStore's cache is an UNBOUNDED Map keyed by stream id whose only pruning is evict/evictAll. Removing it removes a leak surface rather than trading one for another.

J. Thinness. ~180 net LoC across one deleted module, one deleted suite, and a port-member removal is well above the filing bar; it stands alone without batching.

CONCLUSION: survives as a four-file deletion. The implementing PR must DROP proposal step 1's unexport/knip-baseline clause (duplicate of a refuted round-1 item and contrary to a dated ruling) and keep only the two-field replacement in StreamLogStore. Everything else stands as written.

</details>

#### Fold src/utils/text/sanitizeTag.ts into wrapWebhookEvent, its only caller, and drop the tag-name parameter and its unreachable validator

- **Lens**: `single-caller-wrappers` · **Kind**: single-caller-wrapper · **Risk**: low
- **Net**: -20 LoC, -3 elements (claimed -18 LoC, -3 elements; verifier figure governs)

**Evidence**

src/utils/text/sanitizeTag.ts (33 lines) exports one symbol, `wrapAndSanitizeTag` (:31), which itself is a one-line wrapper over the file-local `sanitizeTag` (:19). `grep -rn 'wrapAndSanitizeTag|sanitizeTag' --include='*.ts' src packages/*/src` returns: the definition (src/utils/text/sanitizeTag.ts:19,:31,:32), one import and one call (src/tools/github/formatUtils.ts:12 and :25, inside `wrapWebhookEvent`), and two prose mentions only (src/shared/subagentFollowup.ts:48, src/shared/deliveryTags.ts:41). PRODUCTION consumers: 1 (src/tools/github/formatUtils.ts:25). NON-PRODUCTION consumers: 0 — `grep -rln sanitizeTag src/test-kernel` returns nothing, so there is no dedicated suite to retarget or delete. AMBIGUOUS (scripts/, docs/scripts/, config/ratchets/): 0 — `grep -rn sanitizeTag config/ratchets/*.json scripts/` is empty, so no knip-baseline row is involved.

Why it is now single-caller: `git log --oneline -S wrapAndSanitizeTag` gives a4147c0ac2 (introduced with the execution-subscription feature), fc73368c2a (#3187, the github_subscription tool became the second caller), ada841c0f4, and 93cc177020 "refactor(executions): drop the model-facing subscribe capability". `git show 93cc177020` deletes `ExecutionSubscriptionBinder`, whose imports included `wrapAndSanitizeTag` (used at its `send()` to wrap `DELIVERY_TAG.executionActivity`). One of the two tags the parameter existed to serve therefore no longer has a producer — src/shared/deliveryTags.ts:50-56 says so explicitly ("`execution-activity` has no live producer since the model-facing `executions subscribe` action was removed"), and that entry is a dated read shim scheduled for retirement after 2026-11-24, which this candidate deliberately does NOT touch.

Consequence: `tagName` is now always the single constant `WEBHOOK_TAG = DELIVERY_TAG.githubWebhookActivity` (src/tools/github/formatUtils.ts:16), so the `VALID_TAG_NAME` guard and its `throw` (src/utils/text/sanitizeTag.ts:17,:20-22) are statically unreachable, and the `escapeRegExp(tagName)` call at :23 escapes a compile-time-known literal. `escape-string-regexp` stays a used dependency regardless — nine other production modules import it (src/latex/labelSearch.ts:2, src/utils/text/sanitizePathSegment.ts:8, src/shared/subagentFollowup.ts:14, src/replacement/constants.ts:3, and five more).

`wrapWebhookEvent` itself is NOT part of this candidate: it has four production call sites (src/tools/github/formatIssueEvent.ts:13, formatRepoEvent.ts:29, formatPREvent.ts:22, formatUtils.ts:82) and stays.

Dedupe checks run: absent from the round-1 index (both the 49 shipped and the 41 refuted); `grep -n 'sanitizeTag|wrapAndSanitizeTag' docs/proposals/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md` returns nothing; `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "sanitizeTag"` and `--search "wrapWebhookEvent github formatUtils"` both return nothing; `git log --oneline -8 -- src/utils/text/sanitizeTag.ts` shows the last touch was a generic simplifier round (87687be86d), not a decision defending the seam. No dated proposal in docs/proposals/ or docs/architecture/ names this module.

**Proposal**

1. Move the sanitizer into src/tools/github/formatUtils.ts, specialized to the one tag: keep the security docstring verbatim, replace `sanitizeTag(tagName, body)` with a file-local `neutralizeWebhookTag(body: string)` that closes over the module's existing `WEBHOOK_TAG` constant (:16), hoist the `RegExp` and the broken-tag replacement string to module-level constants since the tag is now compile-time known, and drop the `VALID_TAG_NAME` check and its `throw` along with the `escapeRegExp` import.
2. Rewrite `wrapWebhookEvent` (src/tools/github/formatUtils.ts:24-26) to return the wrapped, neutralized string directly instead of delegating.
3. Delete src/utils/text/sanitizeTag.ts.
4. Leave src/shared/deliveryTags.ts untouched — the `execution-activity` entry there is a dated persisted-data read shim (retire after 2026-11-24) and only its prose reference at :41 needs no change.
5. Run `npm run typecheck`, `npm run lint`, `npm test`, and `npm run check:dead-code-ratchet`.

**What we give up**

A generic, reusable tag-neutralizing helper in the shared utils layer. Nothing else uses it today, and the repo rule bans single-caller extractions, so a second delivery-envelope producer that needs neutralization would re-extract it with two real callers at that point. The behaviour for `<github-webhook-activity>` is byte-identical, since `escapeRegExp` on a constant `[a-z-]` tag name and the `VALID_TAG_NAME` check on that same constant are both no-ops today.

**Verifier corrections to the evidence above**

**1. MISSED REQUIRED CHURN — this proposal as written breaks a required CI gate.**
`scripts/check-browser-safe-utils.mjs` recomputes the _total_ count of TypeScript modules under `src/utils/` and diffs it against prose in BOTH guidance files. I verified: `find src/utils -name '*.ts' -o -name '*.tsx' | wc -l` = **66**. CLAUDE.md:56 says "The other 60"; AGENTS.md:135 says "There are 66 TypeScript modules under `src/utils/` in the current tree; the other 60 are not browser-reachable today". Deleting `src/utils/text/sanitizeTag.ts` makes it 65/59, so both files must be edited or the check fails. That check runs in the **`guidance-refs` job (.github/workflows/ci.yml:287), which ci.yml:569-573 explicitly calls out as ungated and hard-required**. The candidate's evidence never mentions this, and its step-5 verification list (`typecheck`, `lint`, `test`, `check:dead-code-ratchet`) omits `npm run check:browser-safe-utils` and `npm run check:guidance-refs`. This is the single biggest error in the write-up.

**2. Proposal step 4 is wrong that the prose references need no change.** The evidence correctly finds two prose mentions, then mislabels both as inert. After deletion, `src/shared/deliveryTags.ts:41` reads "`wrapAndSanitizeTag()` instead, which neutralizes embedded tag names" — naming a function that will no longer exist — and `src/shared/subagentFollowup.ts:48` lists "(deliveryEnvelope.ts / subagentResults.ts / sanitizeTag.ts)" as the three delivery-envelope producers, naming a file that will no longer exist. Both comments need a one-line update. (Neither is load-bearing for behavior; both are stale-comment defects if left.)

**3. Steps 1 and 2 of the proposal contradict each other and cost one of the claimed 3 elements.** Step 1 creates a file-local `neutralizeWebhookTag(body)`; step 2 says `wrapWebhookEvent` should return "directly instead of delegating." Keeping both reproduces exactly the single-caller wrapper shape being deleted, one file lower. The sanitize+wrap must be inlined into `wrapWebhookEvent`'s body with two module-level constants for the element count to land at -3.

**4. Attribution nit on the history.** `git show 93cc177020` confirms the deletion, but the call site was `ExecutionSubscription.send()` — an inner class in `ExecutionSubscriptionBinder.ts` — passing `wrapAndSanitizeTag(TAG, text)` to `deliverLiveNotification({ followUp })`, not the `ExecutionSubscriptionBinder` class's own `send()`. Substance holds.

**5. Unmentioned detail at the four `wrapWebhookEvent` call sites.** All three external importers alias it (`wrapWebhookEvent as wrap` at formatIssueEvent.ts:13, formatRepoEvent.ts:29, formatPREvent.ts:22). Line numbers verified correct. No impact since nothing is renamed, but the evidence's flat "four production call sites" hides that three are aliased.

**6. Unmentioned implementation guardrail (two).** (a) The hoisted regex must be built as `new RegExp(\`<\\\\s*/?\\\\s*${WEBHOOK_TAG}\\\\s*>\`, 'gi')`from the`WEBHOOK_TAG`const, NOT hardcoded as a literal — hardcoding silently desyncs from`DELIVERY_TAG.githubWebhookActivity`if the tag is ever renamed, which is the one real regression this refactor could introduce. (b) Hoisting a`g`-flagged RegExp to module scope is safe here specifically because `String.prototype.replaceAll`goes through`RegExp.prototype[Symbol.replace]`, which zeroes `lastIndex` for global regexes; a reviewer should know that was checked rather than assume the classic shared-`lastIndex` bug.

**Claims I re-ran and confirmed correct:** single production consumer (`src/tools/github/formatUtils.ts:25`); zero test consumers (`grep -rln sanitizeTag src/test-kernel` empty — the only `github-webhook-activity` test hit, `SubagentFollowup.vitest.ts:452-454`, is a string fixture that does not touch the sanitizer); zero ratchet/knip-baseline rows (`grep -rn sanitizeTag config/ratchets/*.json` empty); zero hits in the round-1 index (95 lines, no `sanitiz`/`webhook`/`utils/text`/`formatUtils`/`github` match) and zero in the 49-candidate survey doc; `gh issue list` empty for both searches; no dated proposal or AGENTS.md/checklist ruling defends the seam; sanitizeTag.ts is 33 lines; all formatUtils line refs (:12, :16, :24-26, :82) exact; nine other production importers of `escape-string-regexp` (verified all 9). Round-1 PRs #11416-#11422 touched neither file (`git show --stat c125ce0a18` / `c11bf8b1f3`). Behavior is byte-identical: `escapeRegExp('github-webhook-activity')` only escapes `-`, a no-op outside a character class, and `VALID_TAG_NAME` passes on that constant.

<details><summary>Verifier reasoning</summary>

**Duplicate check (kill-criterion 1):** Clean. `grep -in "sanitiz\|webhook\|utils/text\|formatUtils\|github" round1-index.md` → no match across all 95 lines. `grep -n "sanitizeTag\|wrapAndSanitizeTag\|formatUtils" docs/proposals/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md` → no match. Round-1 shipped PRs #11416-#11422 are all at HEAD; `git show --stat` on the two nearest lanes (#11420 tools, #11421 shared/utils) shows neither touched `src/tools/github/` nor `src/utils/text/`. Not already done.

**Already filed / already done (2):** `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "sanitizeTag"` and `--search "wrapAndSanitizeTag"` both empty. `git log --oneline -8 -- src/utils/text/sanitizeTag.ts` shows the last touch is 87687be86d, a generic simplifier round — no decision defending the seam. `git log --all -S wrapAndSanitizeTag` returns exactly the four commits claimed, and `git show 93cc177020` confirms the second caller's removal on 2026-08-24.

**Consumer count (3) — the criterion that kills most candidates.** I re-grepped from scratch rather than trusting the evidence, and had to redo it once because the naive repo-wide grep drowns in `packages/extension/dist/extension.js` and `packages/cli/.texra-validate-run/bin/texra.js` build artifacts (the candidate's grep was correctly scoped; mine initially was not). Scoped to `src packages/*/src packages/*/resources scripts config docs prompts supabase`, `sanitizeTag|wrapAndSanitizeTag` yields exactly 7 hits: 3 definition lines, 1 import + 1 call in formatUtils.ts, and 2 prose comments. I separately checked every indirect-dispatch surface the brief names: no `packages/extension/package.json` contribution, no `commands.ts` entry, no `*Settings.ts` schema key, no `packages/extension/resources/` YAML, no supabase function, no ratchet JSON. I also checked for a _second producer path_ that could mint the envelope without going through `wrapWebhookEvent` — `grep -rln "github-webhook-activity"` returns 7 files, and every non-formatter hit (githubSubscriptionTool.ts:162/180/244, formatRepoEvent.ts:4, formatPREvent.ts:6/286) is prose in a tool-result string or a docstring. `wrapWebhookEvent` itself has exactly the 4 call sites claimed. Consumer count holds: 1 production caller.

**Deliberately justified (4):** I searched AGENTS.md, the review checklist, `docs/proposals/*.md` and `docs/architecture/*.md` for `sanitizeTag|tag-injection|neutraliz` — the only hits are two unrelated uses of "neutralized" in an Ink-practices proposal. `src/shared/deliveryTags.ts:35-45` _documents_ the two-strategy split (entity-escaped vs. neutralized) but rules nothing about where the neutralizer lives. Pointing the other way, CLAUDE.md's "Design guardrails" bans single-caller extractions outright and AGENTS.md tells you to grep the caller count first, so the repo's own rule favors the fold. I also checked the strongest counter-argument I could construct: that `src/tools/delegation/deliveryEnvelope.ts` is a sibling producer that _should_ share the helper. Reading it, it uses `escapeAttr`/`escapeText` XML-entity escaping — a genuinely different neutralization strategy that would never call `wrapAndSanitizeTag`. So there is no plausible second caller waiting, and no consolidation-in-the-other-direction that this fold would foreclose.

**Settled surface (5):** Touches none of the frozen surfaces. No `@agent/*` specifier, no PocketFlow node, no platform port, no AgentEvent/SessionFact change. `src/utils/text/sanitizeTag.ts` is NOT one of the six browser-reachable modules (`EXPECTED_REACHABLE` in check-browser-safe-utils.mjs:37-44 lists them; `@utils/text/stringUtils` is the only `text/` entry), so the reachable-set half of that guard is untouched — only the total-count half, per correction 1.

**Net-adds / churns (6):** Genuinely subtractive: one whole module deleted, one npm-package import edge dropped, no new export, no new file. The forced unrelated churn is real but trivially bounded — two one-token doc edits in CLAUDE.md/AGENTS.md, plus two stale-comment fixes. It does not shrink a public API, but R5 only bars churn _without_ a shrink; a deleted module is a shrink.

**Masking-site misread (7):** The clearest reason this could have died, so I checked it directly. The deleted `VALID_TAG_NAME` guard is a `throw`, not a swallow — it is the opposite of a masking site under checklist §15 (M1-M6 are all _silent_ degradations: `catch {}`, `??` over a failed read, `.catch(default)` on persisted data, `default: return`). Deleting a loud precondition check whose only argument is now a compile-time constant removes an unreachable branch; it does not convert a failure into quiet wrong behavior. Same for the `escapeRegExp` call: it is escaping a literal `[a-z-]` string. The security control itself — the ZWSP tag-name neutralization — is preserved verbatim, which is the part that matters.

**Too thin (8):** This is the honest weak point. ~-20 LoC and -3 elements is small for a standalone PR. It earns a pass because it deletes an entire module rather than shaving lines, and because it is a textbook instance of the exact pattern the repo bans. It should ship batched with its lens siblings from this round, not alone. Risk is low: the transformation is behavior-preserving by construction, the only regression vector is hardcoding the tag literal instead of deriving it from `WEBHOOK_TAG` (correction 6a), and the only CI vector is the guidance count (correction 1) — both are mechanical and caught before merge.

</details>

#### Fold AgentSelectionPanel's SOURCE_META origin badges onto the shared AGENT_DECORATORS.properties table

- **Lens**: `host-quad-duplication` · **Kind**: dual-representation · **Risk**: low
- **Net**: -10 LoC, 0 elements (claimed -12 LoC, -1 elements; verifier figure governs)

**Evidence**

`AGENT_DECORATORS.properties` (src/shared/utils/icons.ts:76-91) is the declared shared table for the three non-built-in agent origins: `remote {icon:'cloud', label:'Remote', hint}`, `custom {icon:'circle-user', label:'Custom', hint}`, `inline {icon:'code', label:'Inline', hint}`.

Production consumers — `rg -n "AGENT_DECORATORS" src packages` (7 non-definition hits, 0 in src/test-kernel, 0 in scripts/, 0 in docs):

- src/shared/utils/selectTemplates.ts:46 (`properties.remote.hint` / `.custom.hint` / `.inline.hint` at :53-55), :82 (`properties.remote.icon`), :89 (`properties.inline.icon`) — the agent dropdown, rendered by both graphical hosts
- packages/extension/src/progressView/frontend/components/StreamTabs.ts:311 (`properties.remote.icon`), :345 (`properties.remote.hint`), :153 (`streamKinds`)

A second table for the same fact sits at packages/extension/src/settingsView/frontend/components/profile/AgentSelectionPanel.ts:52-81 (`SOURCE_META`), read at :186, :266, :312, :423. It restates the three labels verbatim ('Remote', 'Custom', 'Inline') and picks DIFFERENT glyphs: `custom` → `star` (:71) vs the shared `circle-user`; `inline` → `bolt` (:80) vs the shared `code`; `remote` gets no badge at all (:73-75) while the dropdown and the stream tabs both show `cloud`. `rg -n "'Built-in'|'Custom'|'Remote'|'Inline'" src packages --glob '!**/test-kernel/**'` returns exactly these two tables and nothing else, so the duplication is a clean pair, not a wider sprawl. The CLI does not label origins at all (packages/cli/src/runtime/agents.ts:247-278 prints the raw `entry.source`), so no third host is involved.

User-visible consequence: within one app the same custom agent is a star in Settings → Agents and a `circle-user` in the model/agent dropdown; the same inline agent is a bolt in one and `code` in the other; a remote agent is badgeless in Settings but flagged `cloud` everywhere else.

The fold direction is already ruled. `git log --all --grep=AGENT_DECORATORS` → 733f14f2cf "fix(ui): remove dead destructure and centralize remote icon name" (2026-06-18, Bugbot follow-up to #6187), which replaced a hardcoded `waIcon('cloud')` in selectTemplates.ts with `waIcon(AGENT_DECORATORS.properties.remote.icon)` "so a future rename of the remote icon in icons.ts stays consistent between the dropdown and stream tabs". SOURCE_META is the surface that pass missed.

Dedupe: `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "AGENT_DECORATORS"` → only #6214 (CLOSED, waIcon type migration, unrelated). Not in the round-1 index or docs/proposals/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md (grep for SOURCE_META / AGENT_DECORATORS / AgentSelectionPanel → 0 relevant hits). docs/proposals/2026-08-04-ssot-consolidation-part-2.md H3 (lines 205-219) covers agent CATEGORY labels only — it cites `icons.ts:95-96` (`agentCategories`) and the three `category === 'toolUse' ? 'Tool Use' : 'Workflow'` ternaries, and never mentions `properties`, the origin badges, or AgentSelectionPanel. This candidate is deliberately scoped to the origin half and does NOT touch the category half H3 owns. `git log --oneline -6` on AgentSelectionPanel.ts shows only generic sweeps (#11375, #10609, #9990, #9829) — nothing revisiting SOURCE_META.

**Proposal**

1. In packages/extension/src/settingsView/frontend/components/profile/AgentSelectionPanel.ts, import `AGENT_DECORATORS` from `@shared/utils/icons` (a path this frontend tree already reaches — StreamTabs.ts:33 does the same).
2. Collapse `SOURCE_META` (:52-81) to the two facts it alone owns: `tone` per source, and the 'Built-in' display name for `AGENT_SOURCE.BUILT_IN_WORKFLOW` / `BUILT_IN_TOOL_USE` (no AGENT_DECORATORS entry exists for built-ins and none should be added — they carry no badge).
3. Read `displayName` (:266) and the badge `{icon,label}` (:186, :423) for remote/custom/inline from `AGENT_DECORATORS.properties[source]` instead of the local literals. The settings list gains the `cloud` remote badge it currently lacks, and custom/inline adopt the `circle-user`/`code` glyphs the dropdown and stream tabs already use.
4. Optionally reuse `.hint` for the badge tooltip so the three hint sentences also stop being settings-view-specific.
   No export is added or removed; `AGENT_DECORATORS` is already exported and consumed. No ratchet, knip baseline, wire schema, or persisted state is involved.

**What we give up**

Two icons in the settings agent list change (star → circle-user for custom, bolt → code for inline) and a remote badge appears where there was none, so the Settings → Agents pane stops having a private glyph vocabulary. If a future design deliberately wants a settings-only badge set, it would have to be re-introduced as a documented second table rather than an undeclared drift.

**Verifier corrections to the evidence above**

Six factual errors, none fatal, but three change the case materially.

1. THE MARQUEE USER-VISIBLE CLAIM FOR `custom` IS FALSE. The evidence says "within one app the same custom agent is a star in Settings → Agents and a `circle-user` in the model/agent dropdown." The dropdown renders NO icon for custom agents. `renderAgentOption` (src/shared/utils/selectTemplates.ts:61-94) emits exactly three conditional glyphs: `bullseye` for `opt.isOrchestrator` (:77), `AGENT_DECORATORS.properties.remote.icon` for `opt.isRemote` (:82), and `AGENT_DECORATORS.properties.inline.icon` for `opt.isInline` (:89). `opt.isCustom` contributes only a tooltip line (`buildAgentTooltip`, :53). My own `rg -n "circle-user" src packages` returns 8 hits and not one of them is a rendered custom-agent badge: src/shared/wa/webAwesomeIcons.ts:185 (registry), src/shared/wa/iconNames.ts:56 (name union), src/shared/utils/icons.ts:84 (the dead field itself), packages/desktop/src/renderer/desktopIconLibrary.ts:31 (rename map), packages/extension/src/progressView/frontend/formatters/constants.ts:175 (`delegate_agent`), and three Account-surface uses (SubscriptionSection.ts:103, settingsNav.ts:93, AccountTab.ts:123). So `AGENT_DECORATORS.properties.custom.icon` has ZERO render consumers, and so do all three `.label` fields (`rg` for `properties.*.label` → 0). The shared table's consumed surface is only `remote.icon`, `inline.icon`, and the three `.hint`s. "Fold onto the shared table" therefore partly means "start rendering fields that were never rendered," which is a weaker argument than "two live surfaces disagree."

2. THE FOLD DIRECTION FOR `custom` IS PROBABLY BACKWARDS. Because `circle-user` was never a rendered agent glyph, it was never vetted as one — and it is already the Account glyph inside the same settings window (settingsNav.ts:93, AccountTab.ts:123, SubscriptionSection.ts:103) and the `delegate_agent` glyph in the progress view. Adopting it as the Custom-agent badge in the settings list creates a same-window collision. Since `properties.custom.icon` is rendered nowhere today, the zero-cost direction is to change src/shared/utils/icons.ts:84 to `'star'` (the vetted, rendered glyph) rather than change AgentSelectionPanel. Same de-dup, no visual regression. The candidate did not consider this and asserts the opposite direction as settled; commit 733f14f2cf settles only that ONE table should own the name, not which glyph wins.

3. THE LoC/ELEMENT MATH IS WRONG. The proposal as written ("collapse SOURCE_META to the two facts it alone owns" and read the rest from AGENT_DECORATORS) saves roughly nothing. Current block is 35 lines (comment 48-51 + const 52-82; the evidence says :52-81, off by one on the closing brace). Keeping the `Record<AgentSource, …>` shape and swapping the three origin entries to `badge: AGENT_DECORATORS.properties.<k>` leaves every entry at its current prettier line count AND adds a badge line to REMOTE plus an import line — net roughly +1 to +2 LoC. A real reduction requires a different shape than the one proposed: replacing the table with a `sourceMeta(source)` derivation (`badge = source in AGENT_DECORATORS.properties ? properties[source as keyof …] : undefined; displayName = badge?.label ?? 'Built-in'; tone = badge ? source as SourceTone : 'builtin'`), ~21 lines for ~35, i.e. about -11 with the import. That form needs a `keyof` cast and silently depends on the tone names being identical to the AgentSource strings — defensible but not free. Either way `netElements` is 0, not -1: SOURCE_META is replaced by an equivalent const or function, not deleted. No export changes, so knip/ratchets are correctly reported as uninvolved.

4. CONSUMER COUNT IS MIS-STATED. "7 non-definition hits" for AGENT_DECORATORS is wrong. My `rg -n "AGENT_DECORATORS" src packages` returns 12 lines: 3 inside icons.ts itself beyond the definition (:107 `type AgentCategory = keyof …agentCategories`, :117, :120 — the evidence never mentions icons.ts's own internal consumers), 4 in selectTemplates.ts (:17 import, :46, :82, :89), 4 in StreamTabs.ts (:33 import, :153, :311, :345), plus the :76 definition. Cross-host reach is correctly characterized but for the wrong reason: the settings panel is not extension-only — packages/desktop/src/renderer/main.ts:55 does `import '@settingsView/frontend'` and mounts `settings-app` at :336, so AgentSelectionPanel renders in BOTH graphical hosts. That strengthens the case and should be stated.

5. THE "CLEAN PAIR" GREP MISSES A THIRD 'Built-in' SITE. `rg "'Built-in'"` finds only the two SOURCE_META entries because the third occurrence is inside a Lit template, not a quoted string: `<wa-tag variant="neutral" size="s">Built-in</wa-tag>` in `renderDetail` (AgentSelectionPanel.ts ~:430). Any honest fold should route that through `displayName` too.

6. THE REAL DEFECT IS NARROWER AND SHOULD BE THE HEADLINE. Stripped of the false custom claim, exactly two things are wrong on main: (a) inline renders `bolt` in settings and `code` in the dropdown — a genuine same-app divergence; (b) a selected REMOTE agent's detail-pane header (renderDetail, :423-441) shows no origin tag at all, while built-in gets a "Built-in" wa-tag, custom gets "star Custom", and inline gets "bolt Inline". That hole is real and not explained by the section headers (the detail pane has none). Note the list-pane badges are largely redundant with the section headings rendered at :266-268, so "add a remote badge to the list too" is the debatable half; the detail-pane gap is the defensible half.

Verified clean (no correction needed): line anchors for SOURCE_META reads (:186, :266, :312, :423 all confirmed); AGENT_DECORATORS.properties at icons.ts:76-91 with the three stated entries; commit 733f14f2cf exists with the quoted rationale; the CLI prints raw `entry.source` (packages/cli/src/runtime/agents.ts:258) and labels no origin; docs/proposals/2026-08-04-ssot-consolidation-part-2.md H3 (:205-219) is scoped to agentCategories/icons.ts:95-96 and the three `'Tool Use' : 'Workflow'` ternaries — it does not touch `properties`; not in round1-index.md (90 titles, checked); zero hits for AgentSelectionPanel/SOURCE_META/AGENT_DECORATORS/circle-user in docs/proposals/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md; `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all` surfaces only #6214 and #8157, both unrelated and closed; `git log --all --grep=SOURCE_META` empty; AgentSelectionPanel.ts history is generic sweeps only (#11375, #10609, #10214, #9990, #9829, #9827); no test pins 'star' or 'bolt' (AgentSelectionPanelToggle / SettingsStyleContracts / MultiAgentTab vitest all clean); desktop glyph resolution is safe either way — desktopIconLibrary.ts:166 falls through unmapped names directly to Lucide and `code`/`cloud`/`star` all resolve, `bolt`→`zap` at :66. No masking site is deleted (`tone` and the badge are presentation, not a swallowed failure); no ratchet, wire schema, persisted state, VS Code-free zone, or @agent/* edge is touched.

<details><summary>Verifier reasoning</summary>

Independent verification trail.

DUPLICATE / ALREADY FILED / ALREADY DONE — cleared. `grep -in "SOURCE_META|AGENT_DECORATORS|AgentSelectionPanel|origin badge"` over the round-1 index (read in full, 49 shipped + 41 refuted) and over the 2755-line 49-candidate survey returns nothing relevant. `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "AGENT_DECORATORS"` → #6214 only (CLOSED, waIcon TeXRAIconName typing, the migration that CREATED this typed shape). A broader `--search "icons.ts"` adds only #8157 (CLOSED, model-provider unicode→wa-icon). `git log --all --grep=SOURCE_META` → 0. `git log --oneline -8` on AgentSelectionPanel.ts → only campaign sweeps; nothing revisits SOURCE_META. Both tables are present at HEAD exactly as described, so PRs #11416-#11422 did not touch this.

WRONG CONSUMER COUNT — re-grepped from scratch. `rg -n "AGENT_DECORATORS" src packages` gives 12 lines (see correction 4). `rg -n "SOURCE_META"` in the panel gives 5 (definition + 4 reads). `rg -n "'Built-in'|'Custom'|'Remote'|'Inline'" src packages` reproduces the claimed two-table result exactly, but I found the template-literal 'Built-in' in renderDetail that the quoted-string pattern cannot see. `rg -ln "agent-selection-panel|AgentSelectionPanel"` → 3 production files (the panel, its styles, tabs/AgentsTab.ts) + 3 test files. Nothing in packages/extension/package.json contributions, commands.ts, resources/agents, prompts/, supabase/functions/, or config/ratchets/ references either table — these are pure frontend presentation constants, no command id, config key, or wire literal involved.

HOST REACH — the evidence undersells it. packages/desktop/src/renderer/main.ts:55 imports '@settingsView/frontend' and mounts `settings-app`, so the divergent glyph set ships in the extension AND the desktop app, both against the same shared dropdown from selectTemplates.ts. The CLI is correctly excluded (formatCliAgentDetails prints `source: ${entry.source}` raw).

CONSUMER COUNT OF THE SHARED TABLE'S FIELDS — the finding that reframes the candidate. Reading selectTemplates.ts:44-95 line by line: `.hint` is read for all three origins (:53-55), `.icon` only for remote (:82) and inline (:89), `.label` for none. StreamTabs.ts reads only `remote.icon` (:311) and `remote.hint` (:345). So `properties.custom.icon` and all three `.label`s are zero-consumer fields in the "shared" table. The candidate's claim of a live star-vs-circle-user disagreement cannot be true, and I confirmed it by reading the render path rather than trusting the grep.

DELIBERATELY JUSTIFIED — searched. `rg -ln "AGENT_DECORATORS|SOURCE_META" docs/` → 0 files. The nearest ruling, ssot-consolidation-part-2 H3, I read at :205-219: it enumerates the shadow `AgentCategory` type, two literal-union parameters, and the three `category === 'toolUse' ? 'Tool Use' : 'Workflow'` inlines, citing `icons.ts:95-96`. It never mentions `properties`, origins, badges, or AgentSelectionPanel. The candidate's scoping claim holds; there is no overlap and no contrary ruling. Commit 733f14f2cf (read in full) rules the fold direction for the NAME but says nothing about which glyph wins, which is why correction 2 stands.

SETTLED SURFACE — none touched. No @agent/* import, no PocketFlow node, no platform port, no browser-safe @utils module (icons.ts lives in src/shared/utils, not src/utils), no AgentEvent/SessionFact, none of the five ratchets. AgentSelectionPanel is a webview frontend under packages/extension/src/settingsView/frontend/ — a VS Code-free zone — and `@shared/utils/icons` is already reached from that tree (StreamTabs.ts:33 is the precedent, and the panel already imports `@shared/utils/dispatcher`, `@shared/wa/webAwesomeIcons`, `@shared/schemas`), so the new import crosses no boundary.

MASKING SITE — none. `tone` drives a CSS data-attribute (AgentSelectionPanel.styles.ts:74-99, four `[data-source=...]` accent rules) and `badge` is a wa-tag; neither swallows an error. No catch, no `??` over a failed read, no Zod `.catch`.

NET-ADDS / CHURNS — this is where I nearly killed it. I reconstructed the block (48-82, 35 lines) and mentally applied prettier to the proposed shape: every entry keeps its current line count, REMOTE gains a badge line, and an import line is added, so the literal proposal is net-positive LoC. It only becomes a reduction under a shape the candidate did not propose (a `sourceMeta(source)` derivation with a `keyof` cast, ~-11). It never adds elements. So the candidate is salvageable but its stated plan and its stated numbers do not match.

TOO THIN — borderline, and it clears the bar only because of the defect, not the de-dup. Three duplicated label strings and two glyph names are thin. What earns the PR is that a selected remote agent's detail header renders no origin tag while every other source does (renderDetail:423-441), and that inline renders `bolt` here and `code` in the dropdown, in both graphical hosts. Round 1 shipped items of comparable size (e.g. "Delete ProgressToolbarButton.disabled"), so thinness alone is not disqualifying.

VERDICT — survives, narrowly, and only in a corrected form: derive SOURCE_META (or a `sourceMeta` accessor) from AGENT_DECORATORS.properties for label/hint and for the remote and inline glyphs; route the renderDetail 'Built-in' template literal through displayName; give remote its missing detail-pane tag; and for `custom`, move the shared table to `'star'` rather than moving the settings panel to `'circle-user'`, since circle-user is the Account glyph in the same window and was never rendered as an agent badge. Implemented that way it is a real de-dup plus a real UI fix at about -10 LoC and 0 net elements, with no ratchet, schema, or export impact. Implemented as literally written, it is a net-add that also introduces a glyph collision — which is why every one of the corrections above is load-bearing for the implementing PR.

</details>

#### Route the three remaining hand-rolled reimplementations onto the owner that already exists: throwAggregated/aggregateError and Intl.ListFormat

- **Lens**: `hand-rolled-vs-builtin` · **Kind**: hand-rolled · **Risk**: low
- **Net**: -10 LoC, 0 elements (claimed -8 LoC, 0 elements; verifier figure governs)

**Evidence**

(A) `#9906` (commit e0d4bfebff, 2026-08-09, "refactor(runtime): centralize failure-aggregation rethrow in throwAggregated") introduced `aggregateError`/`throwAggregated` at src/utils/core/index.ts:315-336 as the single owner of the "collect independent failures, unwrap when one, AggregateError when several" shape, and converted four files (SessionHandle.ts, childRunLoop.ts, StreamSnapshotStore.ts, runTrace.ts — see `git show --stat e0d4bfebff`). Two production sites still hand-roll that exact shape and were missed:

- src/platform/defaults/fileLocks.ts:71-81 — `const failures = [...].filter(e => e !== undefined); if (failures.length === 1) throw failures[0]; if (failures.length > 1) throw new AggregateError(failures, `File lock failed: ${canonicalPath}`); return value as T;`. This is line-for-line `throwAggregated(failures, ...)` followed by the return (`throwAggregated` is a no-op at length 0). fileLocks.ts was last restructured by #9165 (b22bfa0fb6), i.e. BEFORE #9906 landed, which is why it was never converted (`git log --oneline -6 -- src/platform/defaults/fileLocks.ts`).
- src/controllers/progressView/backend/ProgressBackend.ts:356-363 — `if (failures.length === 1) throw failures[0].reason; throw new AggregateError(failures.map(f => f.reason), `Failed to hydrate stream ${stream}`);` → `throw aggregateError(failures.map(f => f.reason), ...)`.
  I checked the other eight `new AggregateError(` production sites and they are NOT duplicates: desktopDiffHost.ts:272, ProgressBackend.ts:821, StagedDeletionCoordinator.ts:598,636, adjacentStreamCleanup.ts:77, agentCliShared.ts:254, subscribeApprovals.ts:646, detachedChildRun.ts:169 all aggregate fixed 2-element arrays or use a `> 1` guard where unwrapping would be wrong, so the owner does not apply. Only the two above match.
(B) packages/cli/src/commands/_helpers/globalArgs.ts:60-63 `formatCommaList` reimplements `Intl.ListFormat` (ES2021, in the ES2023 lib floor): `if (items.length <= 2) return items.join(' and '); return `${items.slice(0,-1).join(', ')}, and ${items.at(-1)}`;`. I ran the equivalence check against `new Intl.ListFormat('en', { style: 'long', type: 'conjunction' })` for lengths 0,1,2,3,4 — byte-identical output on every case (`""`, `"a"`, `"a and b"`, `"a, b, and c"`, `"a, b, c, and d"`). Production consumers: 2, both in the same file (:109 the `--include-interop` description, :282 the interactive-command rejection message). Not exported; file-local.
  Consumer classification: all four touched sites are production (src/, packages/cli/src). Non-production consumers: zero — `rg "File lock failed"`, `rg "Failed to hydrate stream"`, and `rg formatCommaList` across src/test-kernel return no test that pins these strings, so this is a zero-test-churn change.
  Dedupe: `grep -c 'aggregateError\|throwAggregated' docs/proposals/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md` = 0; `grep -n 'formatCommaList\|ListFormat'` in the same doc = 0 hits; neither appears in the round-1 title index. `git log --all --grep=formatCommaList` = empty; `git log --all --grep=throwAggregated` returns only #9906 itself. `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "AggregateError"` returns #9417 and #10809 (unrelated subjects); `--search "Intl"` returns zero rows.

**Proposal**

1. In src/platform/defaults/fileLocks.ts, import `throwAggregated` from `@utils/core` and replace lines 74-80 with `throwAggregated(failures, `File lock failed: ${canonicalPath}`);`, leaving `return value as T;` (behaviour identical: no-op at 0, unwrap at 1, AggregateError above).
2. In src/controllers/progressView/backend/ProgressBackend.ts, import `aggregateError` from `@utils/core` and replace lines 358-362 with `throw aggregateError(failures.map((failure) => failure.reason), `Failed to hydrate stream ${stream}`);`.
3. In packages/cli/src/commands/_helpers/globalArgs.ts, delete `formatCommaList` (:60-63) and add a module-level `const CONJUNCTION_LIST = new Intl.ListFormat('en', { style: 'long', type: 'conjunction' });`, then call `CONJUNCTION_LIST.format(...)` at :109 and :282. Locale is hardcoded to 'en' so no ICU/host-locale dependence beyond what Node already ships (full-icu since v13; engines require >=22.9).
   No new tests: this is behaviour-preserving and nothing pins the strings. PR body carries `## Net elements (R6)` (0 exported symbols, 0 files, -1 file-local function) and `## Consumer counts (R8)` (2 call sites for formatCommaList, 0 for the two AggregateError shapes).

**What we give up**

Nothing behavioural. The only thing surrendered is the ability to read the aggregation policy inline at those two sites; it now lives once at src/utils/core/index.ts:315-336, which is the point. `Intl.ListFormat` moves list punctuation from a repo constant to ICU — if CLDR ever changed the en conjunction pattern the copy would shift, but that is the same exposure the repo already accepts for `Intl`-backed date/duration formatting in @utils/text/stringUtils.ts.


This is literally `aggregateError(artifactFailures, ...)` — the same `length === 1 ? [0] : new AggregateError(...)` ternary the owner encapsulates at src/utils/core/index.ts:319-321. It needs no new import (runAgent.ts:17 already imports from `@utils/core`). Converting it is -4 LoC. My scan (`rg -n -A4 "length === 1" ... | rg AggregateError`) returns exactly four hits repo-wide in production: ProgressBackend.ts:359, utils/core/index.ts:321 (the owner itself), fileLocks.ts:76, runAgent.ts:234. So the correct site count is THREE, not two.

<details><summary>Verifier reasoning</summary>

DEDUPE (gate 1): `round1-index.md` — grepped for aggregat/ListFormat/Intl/fileLock/globalArgs/ProgressBackend/comma; the only hits are unrelated CLI-usage and progress-view command rows (lines 8, 16, 38, 73, 74, 90, 92).
