ALREADY DONE (gate 2): all three shapes are still present at HEAD. `git show HEAD:src/controllers/progressView/backend/ProgressBackend.ts | grep -n "failures.length === 1"` → :358 (working tree byte-identical there; a concurrent session has that file marked M but `git diff --stat` on it is empty at this block). fileLocks.ts:74-80 and globalArgs.ts:60-63 read straight from the tree. `git log --all --grep=throwAggregated` returns only #9906's four cherry-picks; `--grep=formatCommaList` and `--grep=ListFormat` return nothing. `gh issue list --state all --search "throwAggregated OR aggregateError OR ListFormat"` returns #9417, #10809, #8712, #10773 — all unrelated (executeAgent resume wiring, JSONL freeze list, delegate_workflow_script, transcript JSONL). Not filed.

CONSUMER COUNT (gate 3): re-grepped myself rather than trusting the writeup.

- `rg -n "new AggregateError\(" src packages -g '*.ts' -g '!*test*'` → 25 production occurrences across 20 files. I read ±10 lines around every one of the 13 the evidence did not name. Result: the evidence's classification of its eight is right, but runAgent.ts:230-237 is a genuine third duplicate it missed (see corrections). The narrow ternary scan `rg -A4 "length === 1" | rg AggregateError` gives the true answer: ProgressBackend:359, utils/core:321 (owner), fileLocks:76, runAgent:234.
- `formatCommaList`: 3 hits, all in globalArgs.ts (:60 definition, :109, :282). Not exported. But the _strings_ it produces have real pins — Completion.vitest.ts.snap (~10 lines) and CliRootArgs.vitest.ts:490-521 — which the evidence denied. `INTEROP_SKILL_DIRS` resolves to src/skills/skillSources.ts:18-23, a 4-element tuple, so the production call is always the n=4 case.
- Checked the surfaces the brief names: no command IDs, no `packages/extension/package.json` contribution, no `commands.ts` registration, no Zod settings key, no `packages/extension/resources/`, `prompts/`, or `supabase/functions/` reference to any of these symbols or strings (`rg "File lock failed|Failed to hydrate stream|Also include|is interactive and does not support"` returns only the definition sites plus the test pins plus an unrelated `latexToolchain.ts` "Also included with MacTeX").

EQUIVALENCE (the part that actually decides this):

- `throwAggregated(failures, msg)` = `if (len>0) throw len===1 ? f[0] : new AggregateError(f,msg)` (src/utils/core/index.ts:315-336, read in full). fileLocks' `=== 1 throw` / `> 1 throw AggregateError` / fall-through-to-return is exactly that plus the no-op-at-zero tail. `return value as T` must stay — `throwAggregated` returns `void`, so TS will not narrow `value` from `T | undefined`, and the existing cast already covers it. No behavior change.
- `aggregateError` returns `unknown`; `throw <unknown>` is legal TS and already precedented at runTrace.ts:79. ProgressBackend's `closeTranscriptLease()` runs before the throw and stays put.
- `Intl.ListFormat`: I ran the real production inputs, not just synthetic. `['.agents/skills','.claude/skills','.codex/skills','.gemini/skills']` → both produce ".agents/skills, .claude/skills, .codex/skills, and .gemini/skills" (matches the snapshot byte-for-byte). n=0..5 all identical, including the n=2 "a and b" (no Oxford comma) and n=1 "--print" cases the CliRootArgs test pins. Types are available: `lib.es2021.intl.d.ts` (32 `ListFormat` references) is inside the ES2023 lib floor declared at tsconfig.json:6, and `format(list: Iterable<string>)` accepts `readonly string[]`. Runtime is safe: packages/cli/package.json:36 requires node >=22.9.0, which ships full-icu, and 'en' is present even in small-icu builds.

GATE 4 (deliberately justified): searched docs/proposals, docs/architecture, AGENTS.md, CLAUDE.md and the review checklist for a ruling on aggregation policy or hand-rolled formatting. The only ruling in this territory is #9906's own commit, which points the same direction as the candidate. No doc defends inline aggregation at these sites.

GATE 5 (settled surface): nothing here touches the frozen `@agent/*` surface (no new `@agent/*` deep-import specifier — all imports are `@utils/core`, and `src/platform/disposable.ts:1` is existing precedent for that exact import inside src/platform), the PocketFlow engine, platform ports, the AgentEvent/SessionFact split, or any of the five ratchets. `@utils/core` is already one of the six browser-reachable modules, so the reachable set and the count checked by scripts/check-browser-safe-utils.mjs are unchanged; fileLocks.ts is not a browser entry anyway. No `emitCliResult`/cliOutput.ts field touched, so the texra-action result-JSON contract is untouched.

GATE 6 (net-adds/churns): net is negative, ~-10 LoC, 0 elements. Two of the three legs are LoC-neutral though (see corrections 4 and 8) — the real LoC comes from fileLocks and the missed runAgent site. The elements column is honestly 0: one exported owner already exists and gains callers, one file-local function is swapped for one file-local const.

GATE 7 (masking site): section-15 check clears. None of these deletes a catch, a `??`, a `.catch(default)`, or a `default: return`. `throwAggregated`/`aggregateError` are strictly louder-or-equal: every failure that is thrown today is still thrown, with the same message and the same unwrap-at-one semantics. fileLocks' one genuinely subtle policy — the `if (!compromised) releaseFailure = error;` at :68 that keeps an ERELEASED cleanup error from displacing the compromise cause — sits above the replaced block and is untouched.

GATE 8 (too thin): the aggregation leg alone is 3 sites in 3 files converging on an owner that already has 14 call sites in 10 files; batched with the ListFormat leg it is a coherent one-lens PR. Not too thin.

Verdict: survives, with a materially corrected site list (three, not two), corrected LoC composition, and the test-pin correction that raises the verification bar for the implementer.

</details>

#### Replace AgentOptionData's three one-hot source booleans with the AgentSource enum the registry already carries

- **Lens**: `dual-representation` · **Kind**: dual-representation · **Risk**: low
- **Net**: -8 LoC, -2 elements (claimed -14 LoC, -2 elements; verifier figure governs)

**Evidence**

One fact — an agent's provenance — travels the main-view wire in two shapes.

Canonical shape: `AGENT_SOURCE` / `AgentSourceSchema` / `AgentSource` at src/shared/schemas/agent.ts:40-58 (custom, builtInWorkflow, builtInToolUse, remote, inline). Used as a first-class enum field elsewhere on the same wire: src/shared/schemas/settingsViewMessages.ts:247 and :777 (`source: AgentSourceSchema`), :724 (`agentSource`), src/shared/schemas/proposalFields.ts:14.

Mirror shape: `AgentOptionDataSchema` at src/shared/schemas/mainView/state.ts:107-113 carries `isRemote`, `isCustom`, `isInline` as three independent optional booleans, fanned out one-hot at src/agent/index/agentRegistry.ts:556-558 — `isRemote: entry.source === 'remote'`, `isCustom: entry.source === 'custom'`, `isInline: entry.source === 'inline'` — from the very `entry.source` field the canonical enum types. Three booleans encode 8 states for a 5-value enum; 'all three true' and the two built-in sources are both unrepresentable-or-wrong.

Counter-example in the same file, 18 lines below: `TeamOptionDataSchema` (mainView/state.ts:131) models the identical kind of provenance as `source: z.enum(['built-in', 'custom'])`, and src/shared/utils/selectTemplates.ts:183 reads it as `opt.source === 'custom'`. Agents and teams disagree on how to say the same thing on one wire.

Consumers of the three booleans, grepped repo-wide (`rg "isRemote|isCustom|isInline"` over src and packages, minus test-kernel): production readers are exactly src/shared/utils/selectTemplates.ts:53, :54, :55, :70, :71, :72, :80, :87. Nothing else. The other `isRemote`/`isCustom` hits are unrelated facts (stream-tab remoteness at src/controllers/session/streamTabInfo.ts:59, LaTeXTab's 'value differs from default' at packages/extension/src/settingsView/frontend/tabs/LaTeXTab.ts:633+). CLI touches `AgentOptionData` at packages/cli/src/chat/tui/forms/AgentListForm.tsx:31-36 but only `label`, `value`, `isOrchestrator`.

A table keyed by the enum already exists: `AGENT_DECORATORS.properties` at src/shared/utils/icons.ts:76-95 has exactly the keys `remote`, `custom`, `inline`, each with `icon`/`label`/`hint`, and selectTemplates.ts:53-55 already indexes it three times by hand.

Bonus dead output found while verifying: the three data attributes emitted at selectTemplates.ts:70-72 (`data-remote`, `data-custom`, `data-inline`) have zero style consumers — `rg "data-remote|data-custom|data-inline|data-tool-use"` over src and packages (.ts/.tsx/.css) hits only their own emission plus src/shared/styles/selectStyles.ts:85, which styles `wa-option[data-tool-use='true']` only, and one assertion at src/test-kernel/shared/SelectTemplatesModelProviderIcon.vitest.ts:77.

Not persisted, so no migration: `AgentOptionDataSchema` appears only in wire messages — src/shared/schemas/mainView/outbound.ts:37-38, src/shared/schemas/progressView/outbound.ts:197, mainView/state.ts:261 (in-memory view state). No stateSettings/coreSettings key, no storage reader.

Dedupe: `grep -i 'AgentOptionData|isOrchestrator|selectTemplates|AGENT_DECORATORS'` over docs/proposals/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md returns only line 1505 and 2657, both about unrelated things (the absence of a src/shared/utils barrel; the browse-all-agents sentinel). No round-1 index title matches. `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "AgentOptionData"` returns zero; `--search "agent source flags"` returns four unrelated closed issues (#8885, #10669, #6945, #9590). `git log --all --grep=AgentOptionData` shows only #3767 and #3002, both from the original orchestrator-UX work that introduced the flags. Precedent in the same direction already merged: ce30386bf1 "refactor(webview): delete write-only profile and team wire fields (#10873)".

**Proposal**

1. In src/shared/schemas/mainView/state.ts, replace lines 110-112 (`isRemote`/`isCustom`/`isInline`) with `source: AgentSourceSchema.optional()`, importing the schema that agent.ts:55 already exports. Leave `isToolUse` and `isOrchestrator` alone — they are not provenance and `isToolUse` is the one flag with a real style consumer.
2. In src/agent/index/agentRegistry.ts:556-558, replace the three comparisons with `source: entry.source`.
3. In src/shared/utils/selectTemplates.ts, type `AGENT_DECORATORS.properties` lookups off the source: `const decorator = opt.source ? AGENT_DECORATORS.properties[opt.source] : undefined;` (widen the table's type to `Partial<Record<AgentSource, {icon; label; hint}>>` in src/shared/utils/icons.ts, which is a type-only change — the two built-in sources simply have no row). `buildAgentTooltip` pushes `decorator?.hint`; the two icon spans key off `opt.source === 'remote'` / `opt.source === 'inline'` so today's rendering is byte-identical (custom deliberately shows no inline icon).
4. Delete the three unstyled attributes at selectTemplates.ts:70-72; keep `data-tool-use` (styled at selectStyles.ts:85). Update the single assertion at src/test-kernel/shared/SelectTemplatesModelProviderIcon.vitest.ts:77 to assert on whatever the option now carries, or delete that one expectation with the attribute it pins (Testing discipline: tests pinning retired behavior go with the behavior).

**What we give up**

Nothing at the render level: the tooltip text and the two icons are reproduced exactly. Gives up the ability for a row to claim two provenances at once, which was never meaningful. Also gives up the three `data-*` hooks as future CSS selectors — a single `data-source` attribute can be emitted instead if that is wanted, at zero extra cost.

**Verifier corrections to the evidence above**

This verifier folded its corrections into the reasoning block below rather than filling the corrections field. Read that block: the numbered corrections it references are stated there.

<details><summary>Verifier reasoning</summary>

Verified independently, not by re-reading the claim.

READ THE CODE. `src/shared/schemas/agent.ts:40-58` defines `AGENT_SOURCE` (custom, builtInWorkflow, builtInToolUse, remote, inline), `AgentSourceSchema` at :56, `AgentSource` at :58. `mainView/state.ts:107-113` carries `isToolUse/isOrchestrator/isRemote/isCustom/isInline`. `agentRegistry.ts:550-560` `entriesToOptionData` fans `entry.source` out into the three booleans. Confirmed by reading each file.

CONSUMER RE-GREP. `rg "isRemote|isCustom|isInline"` over src+packages (.ts/.tsx, node_modules excluded) returns the three schema lines, the three registry lines, selectTemplates 53/54/55/70/71/72/80/87, and otherwise only unrelated facts: stream remoteness (`streamTabInfo.ts:59-60`, `SessionFactApplier.ts:653`, `stream.ts:273`, `progressEvents.ts:29`, `AgentLaunchContext.ts:430`, `CommonCycleTypes.ts:107`, `debugMessageSaver.ts:17`), LaTeXTab's "differs from default" (:633+), `isInlineCriticismEnabled`, `isInlineWhitespace`, `AgentSelectionPanel.ts:313` (which already uses `agent.source === AGENT_SOURCE.CUSTOM` — the settings view is the correct-shape precedent H1 cites). Producer is unique. Passing hosts (extension MainViewProvider, desktop controller, CLI AgentListForm, ProposalRequestPanel) never read provenance. No non-TS consumer: `rg "data-remote|data-custom|data-inline|data-tool-use"` over .ts/.tsx/.css hits only the emission site, `selectStyles.ts:85` (`data-tool-use` only), and one test assertion.

DUPLICATE / FILED / DONE. round1-index.md (95 lines) has no matching title — nearest are "Collapse modelOptionsByCategory" (ModelOptionData, different) and "Collapse two alias-only MainView event pairs" (different). The 49-candidate survey doc hits only lines 1505/2657, both unrelated as claimed. `gh issue list --state all` searches for AgentOptionData / AgentOptionDataSchema / the boolean names / "agent provenance booleans" return nothing on point. `git log --all --grep` shows only #3002/#3767 (origin) and #9344 (which added `inline` and its boolean). Round-1 PRs #11416-#11422 are all in other areas and the booleans are present at HEAD. NOT already done, NOT filed as an issue — but IS written up as §H1 of a dated proposal (see corrections), which the candidate missed.

DELIBERATE JUSTIFICATION. Searched docs/, AGENTS.md, and the review checklist. The only rulings found argue FOR the change: H1 above, and the in-file precedent `SessionTypeSchema = AgentCategorySchema` (state.ts:26-33, "derived from the canonical one rather than redeclared — the two vocabularies cannot drift"), which is a stronger precedent than the TeamOptionData one the candidate cites. #8844 ("collapse AgentSource const -> AgentSourceSchema") went the same direction. No ratchet, no knip-baseline row, no architecture doc protects the flags.

SETTLED SURFACE / NET-ADDS / MASKING. None apply. Shared wire schema, both hosts rebuild the rows in-process every launch, nothing persisted, no `.catch`, no fallback deleted. Element count strictly drops (3 optional schema fields -> 1) and three rendered attributes with zero consumers go with them.

THINNESS. This is the one real weakness. Corrected net is ~-8 LoC / -2 elements — genuinely small, and on its own it is a marginal PR. It should ride a shared-schemas batch (the shape of round-1's #11421) rather than stand alone. It survives because the defect is representational rather than cosmetic (a new `AgentSource` silently renders no badge today, with no type error), it closes the last unshipped half of an already-half-shipped documented item, the change is byte-identical at the render level, and the risk is near zero.

</details>

#### Delete the retired `texra.agent.rounds` config read: the key is in no settings catalog, so housekeeping's scan depth is already the hardcoded default

- **Lens**: `compat-past-window` · **Kind**: expired-compat · **Risk**: low
- **Net**: -6 LoC, -2 elements (claimed -10 LoC, -1 elements; verifier figure governs)

**Evidence**

`texra.agent.rounds` is read in exactly two production sites and declared nowhere.

Production readers (2):

- src/housekeeping/utils.ts:126 — `const maxRounds = getConfig<number>('texra.agent.rounds', DEFAULT_MAX_ROUNDS);`, fed to `getFilePatterns(baseName, model, agent, maxRounds)` at :129.
- src/housekeeping/pack.ts:253 — same read, then `for (let i = 0; i < maxRounds; i++)` at :256.

Non-production (1): src/test-kernel/shared/LegacyWorkflowOutput.vitest.ts:35 injects `config: { 'texra.agent.rounds': 2 }` into the memory config provider. That is the ONLY place the knob is ever set anywhere in the repo.

Declaration: none. `rg -F 'agent.rounds'` over the whole repo returns exactly those three lines (plus zero hits in docs/, scripts/, prompts/, supabase/functions/, packages/extension/resources/). Cross-checked against the settings SSOT: `rg -F 'agent.rounds' src/shared/schemas/coreSettings.ts src/shared/schemas/stateSettings.ts` = 0 hits, while every _other_ literal key read through `getConfig` in production IS declared there — I enumerated all 21 of them (texra.debug.saveModelIO, texra.model.useBackgroundResponses, texra.model.gpt5ReasoningSummary, texra.model.useGoogleBackgroundResponses, texra.model.openaiParallelToolCalls, texra.model.useOpenAIResponsesAPI, texra.model.useGoogleInteractionsServerState, texra.bib.defaultPath, texra.bib.zoteroPort, texra.audio.soxPath, texra.maxImageDimension, texra.agentOutputs.autoOpenFinal, agentReview.runOnCommit, texra.latex.{enabledReplacements,customReplacements,enabledReplacementsRegex,customReplacementsRegex,tikzInputDirectory,includeWorkspaceInTexinputs,tikzTemplate}) and `texra.agent.rounds` is the sole orphan.

Why the read is therefore inert in every host: CLAUDE.md states `packages/extension/package.json` must NOT contribute `configuration` (enforced by scripts/sync-package-contributes.mjs), so the VS Code provider has no such key; the Node/CLI/desktop provider is src/platform/defaults/jsonConfigProvider.ts:40-48, which reads the flat `texra.*` key from the workspace store, then the global store, then `getCoreSettingDefault(storedKey)` — an undeclared key can only be produced by a hand-edited or pre-catalog `.texra/config.json`, and the native settings view cannot write it. So `maxRounds` is `DEFAULT_MAX_ROUNDS` (src/housekeeping/constants.ts:29 = 5) in practice.

When the replacement shipped: rounds are now per-run state on the run config, not a global setting — src/agent/core/definition/AgentDataclass.ts:43 `rounds: z.int().positive().prefault(2)` (and :82 as an optional override). `git log --all -S 'agent.rounds'` shows the key last moved in 621c9a1590 (2026-07-13, 'refactor: move legacy output grammar to shared'), which only relocated the reads; the key itself dates to 1c0aa8371d (2025-08-31, 'feat: support multi-round reflection'). It never appears in `src/shared/schemas/coreSettings.ts` or `stateSettings.ts` at HEAD, i.e. it did not survive the native-settings consolidation (c41552c2f1 / 958460a0d5 'refactor: retire remaining settings compatibility', 2026-08-02). Retirement window on that cutover expired 2026-11-02… correction: the cutover was >3 weeks ago; the relevant policy line is AGENTS.md 'New native settings begin with the current defaults. Do not import retired VS Code settings' — this is a _reader left behind_ by that retirement, not a dated compat path.

Dedupe (all clean): `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search 'agent.rounds' --limit 20` = 0 rows; `--search 'housekeeping rounds'` returns only #8913/#9050/#8787, and I read #8913's body — its housekeeping row is 'Pass rounds explicitly to runDiffBetweenRounds' (a different function, in src/latex/latexdiff) and its do-not-redo ledger fences the _filename-era/mid-era pattern arms_ behind the 2027-04-21 workspace-reader horizon, not this config read. `git log --all --oneline --grep='agent.rounds'` returns only 2025-era feature commits. `rg -i 'agent\.rounds|DEFAULT_MAX_ROUNDS|getFilePatterns|resolveHousekeepingTargets' docs/proposals/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md` = 0 hits, and no round-1 index title touches housekeeping.

Settled surfaces untouched: no ratchet row, no `@agent/*` deep import, no PocketFlow engine, no platform port added or removed, no browser-safe `@utils` module, no AgentEvent/SessionFact change, no CLI result-JSON contract field. The D3-fenced filename-era/mid-era glob grammar in src/shared/constants/workflowOutput.ts is deliberately NOT modified — only the depth source feeding it.

**Proposal**

1. src/housekeeping/utils.ts: delete line 126 and the two-line comment above it (:124-125 'Pass the raw agent; getFilePatterns derives...' keeps its first half); drop the `numRounds` parameter from `getFilePatterns` (:39, :44) and loop to `DEFAULT_MAX_ROUNDS` directly inside it; update the single call at :129 to `getFilePatterns(baseName, model, agent)`; remove the now-unused `import { getConfig } from '@utils/config/configUtils'` at :15 (it has no other use in the file).
2. src/housekeeping/pack.ts: replace :253 with a direct use of `DEFAULT_MAX_ROUNDS` in the `for` bound at :256; remove the now-unused `getConfig` import at :14 (no other use in the file).
3. src/test-kernel/shared/LegacyWorkflowOutput.vitest.ts: drop the `config: { 'texra.agent.rounds': 2 }` line at :35 (leaving `workspacePath`). The suite's fixtures only use r0 and r1 (:105-107), and the assertion at :131-134 is a set-membership check, so raising the effective depth from 2 to 5 changes nothing — the extra patterns match no files on disk.
4. No settings-catalog change: there is no row to delete, which is the point of the finding.

Behavior is bit-identical to today in every host, because the read already resolves to `DEFAULT_MAX_ROUNDS` everywhere. Optionally note in the commit body that the depth is fixed at 5: a pre-2026-04-21 workspace whose old `texra.agent.rounds` was >5 already loses those rounds today, so this makes an existing limitation explicit rather than introducing one.

**What we give up**

The ability to re-point housekeeping's legacy-workspace scan depth via a setting — which is not actually available today, since no host can surface or persist the key. If a future need arises, the depth becomes one constant in src/housekeeping/constants.ts, or a real catalog row in coreSettings.ts, either of which is a smaller change than the current inert read.

### M6-desktop — Desktop host

**Files**: `packages/desktop/src/main/desktopDiffHost.ts`, `packages/desktop/src/main/desktopIpcTypes.ts`, `packages/desktop/src/main/desktopPreviewHost.ts`, `packages/desktop/src/main/desktopProtocolCallbacks.ts`, `packages/desktop/tests/e2e/screenshots.spec.ts`, `packages/desktop/tests/e2e/verify-fixes.spec.ts`, `packages/desktop/tests/e2e/workspaceTabs.spec.ts`, `src/test-kernel/desktop/DesktopDiffHost.vitest.ts`, `src/test-kernel/desktop/DesktopPreviewHost.vitest.ts`, `src/test-kernel/desktop/DesktopProtocolCallbacks.vitest.ts`, `src/test-kernel/webview/MainAppDesktopGating.vitest.ts`

#### Delete packages/desktop/tests/e2e/verify-fixes.spec.ts, the one-off "verify the fixes in this PR" spec from #3829

- **Lens**: `desktop-dead-on-arrival` · **Kind**: test-only · **Risk**: low
- **Net**: -198 LoC, -1 elements (claimed -200 LoC, -1 elements; verifier figure governs)

**Evidence**

`git log --diff-filter=A --format="%h %ad %s" --date=short -- packages/desktop/tests/e2e/verify-fixes.spec.ts` -> `4708c3f922 2026-05-10 Desktop: scroll fix, host-aware UI, secrets refactor, badge polish (#3829)`. The file is literally the "verify these three fixes" spec of that PR, kept for 3.5 months. `git log --oneline -15` on it shows 11 subsequent commits, three of which are pure repair churn caused by the file itself: `075fb5319d fix(desktop): retarget verify-fixes.spec.ts off the moved settingsViewCommands module`, `90d5eb0fa3 fix(desktop): address Copilot nits in verify-fixes.spec.ts`, `5522e822bb fix(desktop): wait for tools-panel content before scroll-race probe`, plus drags through `#10866`, `#10811`, `#10609`, `#9216`.

Test 1 (`verify-fixes.spec.ts:55-79`, 'main view no longer renders inner Launcher/Progress toolbar') is a strict duplicate of a cheaper unit test. `src/test-kernel/webview/MainAppDesktopGating.vitest.ts:84-95` runs `it.each([{host:'extension',visible:true},{host:'desktop',visible:false}])` asserting `hasSection(element,'latexdiffs-section')` and `hasSection(element,'.view-header')` — both directions, which the e2e cannot do (it only ever mounts the desktop host). The third selector the e2e adds, `.view-tabs`, is nested inside `.view-header`: `src/shared/wa/viewHeader.ts:48` renders `class="view-tabs"` inside the view header, and `src/shared/styles/viewTabStyles.ts:11` / `packages/extension/src/progressView/frontend/progressAppStyles.ts:40` both select it only as `.view-header wa-tab-group.view-tabs`. So asserting `.view-header` absent already implies it.

Test 2 (`verify-fixes.spec.ts:81-142`, 'command palette keeps each icon and label in one compact row') duplicates the setup of `packages/desktop/tests/e2e/screenshots.spec.ts:95-109` verbatim — same `.task-workbench-close` visibility guard, same `.task-header-button[aria-label="Show Commands"]` click, same Escape dismissal — and adds only pixel-geometry pins (`row.item.height <= 52`, `row.icon.right <= row.copy.left`, `uniqueIconCount > 8`). `uniqueIconCount > 8` is a tautology: `DESKTOP_COMMAND_ICONS` in `packages/desktop/src/shared/desktopCommandSurface.ts:126-145` declares 18 distinct icon names and is `satisfies Record<DesktopCommandId, TeXRAIconName>`, so the type checker already guarantees a full row set. Palette logic is separately covered by `src/test-kernel/desktop/DesktopCommandPalette.vitest.ts` (294 lines).

Test 3 (`verify-fixes.spec.ts:144-216`) is the only place in the desktop e2e corpus that asserts scrollability: `grep -rn "scrollHeight|scrollTop" packages/desktop/tests/e2e/*.spec.ts` returns hits only inside verify-fixes.spec.ts. That is its one non-duplicated assertion pair (`:214-215`).

Other cost carried by the file: three `console.log` debug statements left in production CI (`:71`, `:180`, `:203`); screenshots written to `testInfo.outputPath('verify-fixes', ...)` that nothing reads (they are the artifacts filling the gitignored `packages/desktop/tests/e2e/test-results/` tree — 1110-line and 662-line PNGs plus `error-context.md` files were sitting there at survey time); and an inlined duplicate of a wire constant, `const SET_TAB_COMMAND = 'setTab'` (`:19`), re-declaring `SETTINGS_VIEW_COMMANDS.SET_TAB`.

Dedupe: `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "verify-fixes" --limit 20` and `--search "desktop e2e" --limit 20` return no matching issue (nearest are CLOSED #5532, #5530, #3777, #3807 about other desktop-e2e hygiene). `grep -rn "verify-fixes" docs/` returns nothing; `grep -c verify-fixes docs/proposals/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md` is 0. Not in the round-1 index.

**Proposal**

1. Move the two unique assertions from `verify-fixes.spec.ts:213-215` (`probeBefore.scrollHeight > probeBefore.clientHeight` and `probeAfter.scrollTop > 0`) plus the `readToolsPanelMetrics` probe into the existing `packages/desktop/tests/e2e/workspaceTabs.spec.ts` test at :478 ('loads tools, centers every compact nav icon, and customizes shortcuts'), which already navigates to the Tools tab — roughly 15 added lines, no new file (R7 compliant).
2. Delete `packages/desktop/tests/e2e/verify-fixes.spec.ts` entirely.
3. No production code changes; no new tests.

**What we give up**

The pixel-geometry pins on the command-palette row (height <= 52px, icon left of label) stop being enforced in CI, and the desktop-host absence of `.view-header`/`latexdiffs-section` is enforced only at the unit level (MainAppDesktopGating.vitest.ts) rather than also in a launched Electron window. Restoring either is a matter of re-adding assertions; nothing structural is lost.

**Verifier corrections to the evidence above**

Fourteen factual errors / omissions in the evidence. None kills the candidate; several change the _reason_ it is right.

1. **"11 subsequent commits" → 10.** `git log --oneline -- packages/desktop/tests/e2e/verify-fixes.spec.ts` returns 11 lines _including_ the adding commit `4708c3f922`. Ten commits touched it after creation.

2. **`console.log` line numbers wrong.** They are at `:74`, `:184`, `:203` — not `:71`, `:180`, `:203`.

3. **"left in production CI" is FALSE, and this is the biggest miss — it makes the case _stronger_, not weaker.** The desktop Playwright suite is not run by any CI workflow. `grep -rn "test:e2e\|desktop:e2e" .github/ package.json scripts/` returns **zero** hits; `test:e2e` exists only in `packages/desktop/package.json:25`. `.github/workflows/desktop-package.yml:154` installs `playwright install-deps chromium` solely for the packaged-app smoke step (`desktop:package:smoke`), which never invokes a spec. `packages/desktop/tests/e2e/README.md` states outright: "The suite is intentionally separate from `vitest` and is **not** wired into the default `npm test` flow." So none of the three tests' assertions is enforced anywhere — the "what we give up" section overstates the loss (nothing was being enforced in CI to begin with), and the lens ("no public release can require it") is satisfied more completely than argued.

4. **`DESKTOP_COMMAND_ICONS` is at `packages/desktop/src/shared/desktopCommandSurface.ts:127-146`, not `:126-145`.** `const DESKTOP_COMMAND_ICONS = {` is line 127; the closing `} as const satisfies Record<DesktopCommandId, TeXRAIconName>` is line 146. The 18-distinct-icons count is correct.

5. **Coverage misattributed.** The full-row-set / icon coverage is in `src/test-kernel/desktop/DesktopCommandSurface.vitest.ts` — `:77` asserts `expect(entries.map((entry) => entry.id)).toEqual(DESKTOP_COMMAND_IDS)` and `:97-144` pins each command's icon name — **not** in `DesktopCommandPalette.vitest.ts`. That file (294 lines, count correct) covers filtering, selection wrap, Enter dispatch, click dispatch, arrow keys, stream-switch entries. Cite `DesktopCommandSurface.vitest.ts` as the duplicate-of-record.

6. **"`uniqueIconCount > 8` is a tautology … the type checker already guarantees a full row set" is overstated.** `satisfies Record<DesktopCommandId, TeXRAIconName>` makes the icon map _total over ids_; it says nothing about how many distinct rows the palette renders at runtime. It is a weak runtime assertion, not a type-level one. It survives deletion because `getDesktopCommandMenuEntries` (`:285-316`) applies no filtering — it maps every `DESKTOP_COMMAND_IDS` entry — and `DesktopCommandSurface.vitest.ts:77` pins that, while `screenshots.spec.ts` polls `commandPaletteEntryCount > 0`. Conclusion holds; the stated reasoning does not.

7. **The inlined `SET_TAB_COMMAND = 'setTab'` (`:19`) is NOT unaccounted cost — it is a documented, deliberate pattern.** `verify-fixes.spec.ts:12-18` and `packages/desktop/tests/e2e/README.md` § "Cross-package imports" both say Playwright's ESM loader cannot resolve a relative `.js` import of a TS file under `src/shared`, and prescribe inlining with a source-of-truth comment. `settingsPersistence.spec.ts` does the same, and `workspaceTabs.spec.ts:495` inlines the literal `'setTab'` outright. Drop this bullet from the case; it is a misread of a justified pattern (criterion 4 in miniature).

8. **Wrong SSOT symbol name.** The file's own comment names `SETTINGS_VIEW_CMD.SET_TAB` in `src/shared/ipc.ts`, not `SETTINGS_VIEW_COMMANDS.SET_TAB`.

9. **"1110-line and 662-line PNGs" is nonsense** (PNGs are binary). At HEAD the gitignored tree holds `command-palette.png`, `main-view.png`, `settings-tools-top.png` and several `error-context.md` files.

10. **Missed signal, and a real risk to the transplant:** `settings-tools-bottom.png` is _absent_ from `test-results/verify-fixes-settings-work-0ac42-.../` while `error-context.md` is present — i.e. the scroll test was **failing** on the last local run (so were `responsiveOnboarding` and `workspaceTabs`' narrow-panel test, which also carry `error-context.md`). Per the "validate-tui local baseline" precedent, do not chase this as a regression, but do verify the transplanted assertions pass in `workspaceTabs.spec.ts` before landing, and do not attribute a failure to the move.

11. **Transplant caveat the evidence omits.** `workspaceTabs.spec.ts:478` is the right target and already does `window.postMessage({command:'setTab',tab:'tools'})` and waits for `tools-tab tool-card` (`:494-499`), and `:380` already uses the `settings-app[data-desktop-view="settings"]` selector — so the probe drops in cleanly. But that test first resizes `.task-main-split` to `positionInPixels = 440` and asserts `settings-app` width `<= 520`. The scroll assertion would therefore be pinned at a narrow settings width rather than the default 1280x800 viewport. Geometrically safe (narrower panel → taller content → `scrollHeight > clientHeight` more likely), but state it.

12. **Drag-through was broader than listed.** Beyond #10866 / #10811 / #10609 / #9216, the file was also edited by `00151e331d` (#9797), `a9c9fbc1e4` (route protocol), and `560c5bc13b`.

13. **Repair-churn claim verified and sharpened.** All three of `075fb5319d`, `90d5eb0fa3`, `5522e822bb` are single-file commits touching only `verify-fixes.spec.ts`, all dated **2026-07-10** — two months after the originating PR, so they are standalone maintenance, not review churn inside #3829.

14. **`.view-tabs` implication argument: verified correct, with better citations.** `renderViewHeader` in `src/shared/wa/viewHeader.ts` emits `<div class="view-header">` at `:43` wrapping `class="view-tabs"` at `:48`, and a repo-wide grep shows it is the **only** source-side producer of `.view-tabs` (other hits are built `traceViewer` bundles and the CSS selectors `src/shared/styles/viewTabStyles.ts:11,17` / `progressAppStyles.ts:40`, all scoped `.view-header wa-tab-group.view-tabs`). `MainApp.ts:50` imports it and renders it only via `this.renderViewHeader()` at `:322`, `:368`, `:592`; `MainApp.ts:236` merely _queries_ `.view-tabs wa-tab[panel="launcher"]`. So `.view-header` absent ⇒ `.view-tabs` absent. Line refs `MainAppDesktopGating.vitest.ts:84-95` and `screenshots.spec.ts:95-109` are both correct (files are 96 and 110 lines).

<details><summary>Verifier reasoning</summary>

Ran every kill check independently.

**1. DUPLICATE.** Not among the 90 round-1 titles (49 shipped / 41 refuted) in the scratchpad index — nothing there touches desktop e2e or Playwright. `grep -c "verify-fixes" docs/proposals/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md` = 0. `grep -rn "verify-fixes"` across the whole repo excluding node_modules/.git/test-results returns hits **only inside the file itself** — no script, config, doc, README, or workflow references it. Not already done: the file exists at HEAD at 216 lines.

**2. ALREADY FILED.** `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "verify-fixes"` → no match. `--search "verify-fixes.spec"` → empty. `--search "desktop e2e"` → nearest are CLOSED #5532, #5530, #3777, #3807, #10856, all about other e2e hygiene (tab indexes, storage ids, waitForTimeout, multi-launch fixture). `git log --all --grep=verify-fixes` returns only the three known repair commits and their rebase duplicates. Clean.

**3. CONSUMER COUNT.** A Playwright spec has no importers; discovery is by `testDir: './tests/e2e'` glob in `packages/desktop/playwright.config.ts`. Deleting the file removes three tests and nothing else. Verified no reference from `packages/extension/package.json`, `commands.ts`, settings schemas, `resources/`, or any workflow. Confirmed by the repo-wide grep above.

**4. DELIBERATELY JUSTIFIED.** Read `packages/desktop/tests/e2e/README.md` end to end. It describes the suite as a screenshot harness for "the three primary shell routes" with committed baselines under `__screenshots__/` (`launcher.png`, `progress.png`, `settings.png` — all produced by `screenshots.spec.ts` via `getScreenshotPath`, which redirects to the baseline dir under `TEXRA_UPDATE_E2E_SCREENSHOTS=1`). `verify-fixes.spec.ts` is _not_ part of that contract: it writes to `testInfo.outputPath('verify-fixes', …)`, which never becomes a baseline and is never diffed. No dated doc, ratchet, AGENTS.md rule, or review-checklist section protects it. The one genuinely-ruled item the evidence mislabeled as cost is the inlined `setTab` constant (correction 7).

**5. SETTLED SURFACE.** Untouched — no `@agent/*` import, no PocketFlow, no platform port, no browser-safe util, no AgentEvent/SessionFact, no ratchet, no vscode-free zone. Test-only, desktop-only.

**6. NET-ADDS.** Deletes 216 lines; adds ~18 to an existing test in `workspaceTabs.spec.ts` (R7-compliant: no new file). Net ≈ -198. No production code changes, no new tests, no knip-baseline or ratchet edits. Cannot net positive.

**7. MASKING SITE.** No `catch`, no `??` fallback, no Zod `.catch`, no silent `default:` is being deleted. The only null-tolerant path is `readToolsPanelMetrics()` returning `null`, which the test immediately asserts non-null against (`:210-211`) — a test guard, not a production masking site. Section 15 does not apply.

**8. TOO THIN.** A 216-line whole-file deletion with documented one-off provenance ("verify these three fixes" from #3829, 2026-05-10, 3.5 months old) and demonstrated ongoing carrying cost: three dedicated single-file repair commits on 2026-07-10 plus seven drag-through edits from unrelated refactors. Stands alone as a low-risk PR.

**Duplication re-verified, not taken on faith.**

- Test 1: `MainAppDesktopGating.vitest.ts:84-95` runs `it.each` over `{extension, visible:true}` / `{desktop, visible:false}` asserting `latexdiffs-section` and `.view-header` in **both** directions; the e2e only ever mounts the desktop host, so the unit test is strictly stronger. The third selector `.view-tabs` is structurally implied — verified `renderViewHeader` is the sole producer and always nests it inside `.view-header` (correction 14). Strict duplicate. Confirmed.
- Test 2: setup is verbatim-duplicated from `screenshots.spec.ts:95-109` (same `.task-workbench-close` guard, same `.task-header-button[aria-label="Show Commands"]` click, same Escape). The icon/row-set fact is pinned at the unit level by `DesktopCommandSurface.vitest.ts:77,97-144`. Only the three pixel-geometry pins (`item.height <= 52`, `icon.right <= copy.left`, `main` within `item`) are unique — and they are enforced by nothing today, since the suite never runs in CI. Confirmed as near-total duplicate.
- Test 3: `grep -rn "scrollHeight\|scrollTop" packages/desktop/tests/e2e/*.spec.ts` returns hits **only** in `verify-fixes.spec.ts`. Its `:214-215` pair is genuinely unique in the corpus. Confirmed — this is the only part worth transplanting, and the proposal transplants it.

**Verdict:** survives. The file is a stale one-off whose two cheap tests duplicate existing unit and e2e coverage, whose one unique assertion pair moves into an existing test in ~18 lines, and which has cost three dedicated repair commits while being enforced by no CI job and no release path.

</details>

#### Batch: drop three never-read desktop main-process surfaces — DesktopProtocolCallback.rawUrl, routeArgv's discarded count, and the never-set forceExternal overlay opt-out

- **Lens**: `desktop-dead-on-arrival` · **Kind**: dead-export · **Risk**: low
- **Net**: -48 LoC, -4 elements (claimed -45 LoC, -3 elements; verifier figure governs)

**Evidence**

(a) `DesktopProtocolCallback.rawUrl` — declared at `packages/desktop/src/main/desktopProtocolCallbacks.ts:10`, populated at `:81`. `grep -rn "rawUrl" packages/desktop/src src` (dist/node_modules excluded) shows every other hit is an unrelated local parameter name (`desktopProtocol.ts:6`, `desktopBrowserViews.ts:54,64`, and the `routeUrl(rawUrl)`/`parseDesktopProtocolCallback(rawUrl)` parameters at `:24,72,110,128`). Zero readers of the _field_: the only real consumer of a parsed callback is `packages/desktop/src/main/desktopSupabaseAuth.ts`, which reads `callback.query` (:283, :420, :425) and `callback.path` (:419) and never `callback.rawUrl`. Non-production: 2 whole-object `toEqual` assertions at `src/test-kernel/desktop/DesktopProtocolCallbacks.vitest.ts:86` and `:134`. Direct precedent on the same interface: `5259581ed9 refactor(desktop): drop unread fragment from DesktopProtocolCallback` (2026-08-03, closes #9650) deleted the sibling `fragment` field for exactly this reason and left `rawUrl` behind.

(b) `routeArgv`'s return value — `packages/desktop/src/main/desktopProtocolCallbacks.ts:25` declares `routeArgv(argv: readonly string[]): number`, and `:126-133` maintains a `routedCount` accumulator. Both production call sites discard it: `:164` (`app.on('second-instance', (_event, argv) => { router.routeArgv(argv); ... })`) and `:176` (`router.routeArgv(options.argv ?? [])`). `packages/desktop/src/main/index.ts` never calls it. The only consumer of the number is `src/test-kernel/desktop/DesktopProtocolCallbacks.vitest.ts:141-149`, a test whose whole point is the count.

(c) `DesktopOverlayPostOptions.forceExternal` — declared at `packages/desktop/src/main/desktopIpcTypes.ts:22-28` with the comment 'Useful for headless tests and as an opt-out if the in-app overlay misbehaves', and consumed in exactly one place, the disjunct at `:41` (`if (!options.postToRenderer || options.forceExternal) return false;`). `grep -rn "forceExternal" packages src docs` returns 8 hits total: the declaration and the disjunct, two doc-comment mentions (`desktopDiffHost.ts:31`, `:169`), and four lines in two tests that exist only to exercise the flag (`src/test-kernel/desktop/DesktopPreviewHost.vitest.ts:331,340` — 'forceExternal=true skips the overlay path entirely'; `src/test-kernel/desktop/DesktopDiffHost.vitest.ts:240,247` — 'honors forceExternal even when postToRenderer is wired'). Zero production setters: both construction sites in `packages/desktop/src/main/index.ts` (`createDesktopPreviewHost` at :432-441, `createDesktopDiffHost` at :548-560) pass only `shell`/`showErrorMessage`/`openPath` and `postToRenderer`, never `forceExternal`. The real fallback the hosts rely on is the `!options.postToRenderer` half plus the `postToRenderer(...) !== false` result check, both of which stay.

Dedupe: `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "protocol callback"` and `--search "forceExternal overlay"` return no matching issue (nearest CLOSED are #3581/#3582/#3583, about other desktop auth/model surfaces). `git log --all --grep` finds nothing for `forceExternal` or `routedCount`. `grep -c` for `forceExternal`, `rawUrl`, `routeArgv`, `tryShowInRenderer`, `DesktopProtocolCallback` in `docs/proposals/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md` is 0 for all five, and none appear in the round-1 index. Distinct from #11418, which touched the Lucide icon map, themeTokens.css and body classes only.

**Proposal**

1. Delete `rawUrl` from the `DesktopProtocolCallback` interface (`desktopProtocolCallbacks.ts:10`) and from the object literal at `:81`; drop it from the two `toEqual` shapes in `DesktopProtocolCallbacks.vitest.ts:86,134`. Mirrors 5259581ed9 exactly.
2. Change `routeArgv` to return `void` (`:25`), drop the `routedCount` accumulator and the `if (routeUrl(rawUrl)) routedCount++` bookkeeping at `:126-133` in favour of a plain `for (const url of findDesktopProtocolUrls(argv)) routeUrl(url);`, and rewrite the assertion in `DesktopProtocolCallbacks.vitest.ts:141-149` to assert the delivered callbacks instead of the count.
3. Delete `forceExternal` from `DesktopOverlayPostOptions` (`desktopIpcTypes.ts:22-28`), simplify `tryShowInRenderer`'s guard at `:41` to `if (!options.postToRenderer) return false;`, drop the two stale mentions in `desktopDiffHost.ts:31,169`, and delete the two tests that exist only to cover the flag (`DesktopPreviewHost.vitest.ts:331-345`, `DesktopDiffHost.vitest.ts:240-252`).
4. Optional follow-on the same PR can take if it stays small: with `parseDesktopProtocolCallback`, `findDesktopProtocolUrls`, `createDesktopProtocolCallbackRouter` and `DesktopProtocolApp` reachable through `installDesktopProtocolCallbackLifecycle`, un-exporting them lets four `production-dead` rows leave `config/ratchets/knip-baseline.json` (lines 1121-1143). Skip it if repointing `DesktopProtocolCallbacks.vitest.ts` at the lifecycle entry point turns into churn.

**What we give up**

No way left to force the OS-application fallback for the PDF and diff overlays from a config flag — a caller wanting that would pass no `postToRenderer`, or the escape hatch would have to be re-added (it has never been used in production). The count of routed protocol URLs stops being observable, and a `texra://` callback's original URL string stops being carried on the parsed callback (nothing reads it, and the parsed `path`/`query` remain).

**Verifier corrections to the evidence above**

Ten factual errors / imprecisions in the evidence, none fatal:

1. WRONG LINE RANGE (c). `forceExternal` is declared at `packages/desktop/src/main/desktopIpcTypes.ts:24-28` (doc comment 24-27, field at 28), not `:22-28`. Lines 22-23 are the tail of `postToRenderer`'s doc comment and the `postToRenderer?(message: unknown): boolean | void;` declaration itself — deleting 22-28 would delete the field that must stay. The deletable block is exactly 5 lines (24-28).

2. WRONG LINE RANGE (b). The `routeArgv` body is `desktopProtocolCallbacks.ts:126-132` (126 `routeArgv(argv) {`, 127 `let routedCount = 0;`, 128 `for (…)`, 129 `if (routeUrl(rawUrl)) routedCount++;`, 130 `}`, 131 `return routedCount;`, 132 `},`), not `:126-133`.

3. MISCHARACTERIZED TEST (b). `DesktopProtocolCallbacks.vitest.ts:141` is titled "routes argv callbacks when routeArgv is passed as a standalone function"; the `it` block runs 141-152 and its stated point is that `routeArgv` survives destructuring (no `this` binding) — it already asserts the delivered callback at :151-153 via `expect(listener).toHaveBeenCalledWith(expect.objectContaining({ query: 'state=standalone' }))`. The count is incidental, confined to the `expect(...).toBe(1)` at :147-149. So step 2 is a 3-line → 1-line trim of one assertion, NOT "rewrite the assertion to assert the delivered callbacks" (that assertion already exists). Claiming "a test whose whole point is the count" is wrong.

4. WRONG TEST RANGES (c). `DesktopPreviewHost.vitest.ts` forceExternal test spans 331-346 (16 lines), not 331-345. `DesktopDiffHost.vitest.ts` spans 240-254 (15 lines), not 240-252.

5. STEP 4 IS INFEASIBLE AS WRITTEN. The optional un-export follow-on names only `DesktopProtocolCallbacks.vitest.ts` as the blocker. A second test file also imports the same symbols: `src/test-kernel/desktop/DesktopSupabaseAuth.vitest.ts:8-11` imports `createDesktopProtocolCallbackRouter`, `parseDesktopProtocolCallback`, and `type DesktopProtocolCallbackRouter` from `@desktop/main/desktopProtocolCallbacks`, and uses them at :127 and :175. `DesktopProtocolApp` is additionally used as a fake-app base type at `DesktopProtocolCallbacks.vitest.ts:19`. Un-exporting the four baselined symbols would break two suites, not one. Drop step 4 from the PR; the four `production-dead` knip rows (config/ratchets/knip-baseline.json:1120-1143 — createDesktopProtocolCallbackRouter, findDesktopProtocolUrls, parseDesktopProtocolCallback, DesktopProtocolApp) stay.

6. INCOMPLETE rawUrl GREP (a). The evidence lists `desktopProtocol.ts:6`, `desktopBrowserViews.ts:54,64` and the parameters at `:24,72,110,128`. The full grep also hits `packages/extension/src/frontend/lm/languageModelToolInvocationMessage.ts:45,46`, `desktopProtocol.ts:8`, `desktopBrowserViews.ts:55,65`, and `desktopProtocolCallbacks.ts:74,111,129`. All are unrelated local bindings/parameters; the conclusion (zero readers of the field) holds. Only `:10` (declaration), `:81` (literal), and the two test lines `:86`/`:134` touch the field.

7. `desktopSupabaseAuth.ts` imports `DesktopProtocolCallback` as a TYPE-ONLY import (`import type { … }` at :28-31, names at :29-30). The cited reads are correct (`callback.query` at :283 and :425, `callback.path`/`callback.query` at :419-420), plus a param at :412 and a queued field at :249/:255. No `rawUrl`, no whole-object spread, JSON.stringify, or IPC post of the callback — so the field is genuinely unobserved.

8. INDEX.TS RANGE. `createDesktopDiffHost` in `packages/desktop/src/main/index.ts` spans 548-~562, not 548-560. The claim that neither site passes `forceExternal` is verified: preview (432-441) passes `shell`, `showErrorMessage`, `postToRenderer`; diff (548-…) passes `openPath`, `postToRenderer`.

9. PRECEDENT COMMIT. `5259581ed9` exists, but the same commit message appears four times in history (`4a69537aa5`, `132140ab8d`, `32e1752bc3`, `5259581ed9`) from rebase copies. Fine as precedent; do not cite a single SHA as if unique.

10. LoC/ELEMENT COUNTS. Recomputed: (a) -4, (b) -6, (c) -38 (5 decl lines + 2 comment-line trims in desktopDiffHost.ts:31,169 + 16+1 and 15+1 test lines) ≈ **-48 LoC**, not -45. Elements: two interface members (`rawUrl`, `forceExternal`) plus two deleted `it` cases = **-4**; `routeArgv: number → void` is a return-contract narrowing and the `routedCount` local is a local, neither is a named element. Claimed -3 undercounts.

One non-error worth stating explicitly: this is NOT a masking site per checklist §15. `tryShowInRenderer`'s real degradation guard is `!options.postToRenderer` plus the `postToRenderer(...) !== false` result check and the `catch` that logs at `console.error` before falling back — all of which survive untouched. `forceExternal` is speculative generality introduced alongside the overlay in #3815/#3816 and never wired, and its own doc comment ("Useful for headless tests and as an opt-out if the in-app overlay misbehaves") is a comment, not a dated ruling, ratchet, or AGENTS.md rule.

<details><summary>Verifier reasoning</summary>

Re-ran every claim at HEAD d8c6dfc071 (main, clean).

DUPLICATE (criterion 1): `grep -c` in docs/proposals/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md returns 0 for forceExternal, rawUrl, routeArgv, tryShowInRenderer, DesktopProtocolCallback, desktopIpcTypes, desktopProtocolCallbacks. Read all 90 titles in round1-index.md: the five desktop entries are the Lucide icon map, themeTokens.css orphans, the texra-<kind> body classes, the CLI agent-creator templates, and the Sentry path (refuted) — none touch these files. Not a duplicate.

ALREADY FILED / DONE (criterion 2): `gh issue list --state all --search` for "desktop protocol callback", "forceExternal", "rawUrl desktop" returns nothing relevant (nearest are CLOSED #3581/#3582/#3583, other desktop auth surfaces, plus unrelated #5451/#9422). `git log --all --grep` finds no forceExternal/routedCount commit. `git log -- desktopProtocolCallbacks.ts` last touched by e7f8514155 (simplifier round 1 part 1) and 5259581ed9 (the cited fragment-drop precedent); `desktopIpcTypes.ts` last touched by a39311a995. All three surfaces are still present at HEAD — verified by reading both files in full.

CONSUMER COUNT (criterion 3) — re-grepped myself across packages/ src/ docs/ config/ (node_modules and dist excluded):

- `rawUrl` field: declared desktopProtocolCallbacks.ts:10, written :81. All other hits are local params/bindings (languageModelToolInvocationMessage.ts:45-46, desktopProtocol.ts:6,8, desktopBrowserViews.ts:54-55,64-65, and the `rawUrl` params at :24,:72,:74,:110-111,:128-129). The only production consumer of a parsed callback, desktopSupabaseAuth.ts, type-imports the interface and reads `.path`/`.query` only (:249,:283,:412,:419-420,:425). Non-production readers: two `toEqual`/`objectContaining` shapes at DesktopProtocolCallbacks.vitest.ts:86,134. Confirmed the callback never crosses IPC or is stringified. Zero production readers of the field.
- `routeArgv` return: both production call sites discard it (desktopProtocolCallbacks.ts:164 in the `second-instance` handler, :176 at startup). index.ts imports only `installDesktopProtocolCallbackLifecycle` (:73) and never calls routeArgv. `DesktopProtocolCallbackRouter` is type-used in desktopSupabaseAuth.ts:73, which only calls `subscribe`. Single reader of the number: the `.toBe(1)` in DesktopProtocolCallbacks.vitest.ts:147-149. Note `routeUrl`'s boolean IS read (`const didRoute = router.routeUrl(url)` at :167) and stays.
- `forceExternal`: exactly 8 hits repo-wide, matching the evidence — decl :28, disjunct :41, two doc mentions in desktopDiffHost.ts:31,169, four test lines in two suites. `DesktopOverlayPostOptions` is extended only by `DesktopDiffHostOptions` (desktopDiffHost.ts:27) and `DesktopPreviewHostOptions` (desktopPreviewHost.ts:36); both factories have exactly one production call site each (index.ts:432, :548) and I read both — neither passes `forceExternal`, and neither spreads a config object that could carry it. No settings key, no package.json contribution, no command id, no YAML/prompt/resource reference (checked packages/extension/package.json, commands.ts path, src/shared/schemas/*Settings.ts by grep across src and packages).

DELIBERATELY JUSTIFIED / SETTLED (criteria 4, 5): searched docs/proposals and docs/architecture — no ruling on any of the three. `forceExternal` was introduced in #3815/#3816 (the in-app diff/PDF overlay features) as a self-described escape hatch, and its sibling justification (PR #3815's "never a silent failure" requirement) is carried entirely by the `!postToRenderer` guard, the `!== false` result check, and the logged `catch` — none of which this touches. None of the three files sits behind a ratchet: the four knip-baseline rows here cover different symbols (createDesktopProtocolCallbackRouter, findDesktopProtocolUrls, parseDesktopProtocolCallback, DesktopProtocolApp) and are untouched if step 4 is dropped. Desktop main-process code is not the frozen @agent SDK, not PocketFlow, not a platform port, not a browser-safe util, not AgentEvent/SessionFact, not a vscode-free zone concern.

NET-ADDS (criterion 6): pure deletion in all three parts; no new abstraction, no new test, no public-API widening. The only edits outside deletions are a `number → void` signature narrowing and two doc-comment rewrites.

MASKING (criterion 7): checked §15 taxonomy. `forceExternal` is not a fallback that hides a failure — it is a never-set forcing flag whose removal leaves the entire loud-fallback chain (undefined-postToRenderer skip, `!== false` result check, `console.error` in the catch before falling back to the external app) intact. M1-M6 do not apply. Same for (a) and (b): no catch, no `??`, no `.catch()`, no silent default.

TOO THIN (criterion 8): individually each part is 4-38 lines, but the candidate is explicitly batched as three same-area main-process deletions, ~-48 LoC total, and round 1 shipped batches of exactly this shape (e.g. the phantom activation event + 11 file-local exports batch).

Residual risk: near zero. All three deletions are compile-checked — removing a required interface field surfaces at the one write site, and the `number → void` change surfaces at the one test assertion. `npm run typecheck` plus the three desktop vitest files is sufficient verification. The one real trap is step 4, which I verified is infeasible without also repointing DesktopSupabaseAuth.vitest.ts; the PR should drop it rather than "skip if it turns into churn."

### M7-cli — CLI

**Files**: `packages/cli/src/runtime/defaultAgents.ts`, `packages/cli/src/runtime/githubToken.ts`, `packages/cli/src/runtime/logSinks.ts`, `packages/cli/src/runtime/providerApiKey.ts`, `packages/desktop/src/main/desktopSettingsIpc.ts`, `packages/extension/src/commands/extensionCommandHandlers.ts`, `packages/extension/src/settingsView/handlers/githubSubscriptionHandlers.ts`, `src/common/secrets/storeCredential.ts`, `src/controllers/settingsView/SettingsProfileKeyController.ts`, `src/test-kernel/architecture/approvalPolicyAuthorityRatchet.vitest.ts`, `src/test-kernel/architecture/progressEventHandlerRetirement.vitest.ts`, `src/test-kernel/architecture/sessionFactAmbientHelperRetirement.vitest.ts`, `src/test-kernel/cli/ChatDefaults.vitest.ts`, `src/test-kernel/cli/DefaultAgents.vitest.ts`, `src/test-kernel/commands/ExtensionCommandRegistry.vitest.ts`

#### Retire two production surfaces that exist only to let a test restate a fact, plus a triplicated scanner self-check

- **Lens**: `test-only-exports` · **Kind**: test-only · **Risk**: low
- **Net**: -42 LoC, -2 elements (claimed -23 LoC, -2 elements; verifier figure governs)

**Evidence**

`npm run check:dead-code-ratchet` reports "394 combined (unused=8, productionDead=386) vs 394 baselined — no new findings", so the find has to be baseline rows that can LEAVE. I cross-referenced every `production-dead` row in `config/ratchets/knip-baseline.json` against whether its defining file uses the name at all. Two rows are production surface that exists purely to hand a test a value it can already compute.

(a) `EXTENSION_REGISTRY_CATALOG_COMMAND_IDS` (`packages/extension/src/commands/extensionCommandHandlers.ts:144-150`; knip-baseline row: file `packages/extension/src/commands/extensionCommandHandlers.ts`, category production-dead, kind exports). Its own docstring at `:139-142` concedes it: "Runtime mirror of `ExtensionRegistryCatalogCommandId`, **used by tests** to assert `EXTENSION_COMMAND_HANDLERS` registers every catalog entry tagged `extensionRegistry: true`". `rg -n "EXTENSION_REGISTRY_CATALOG_COMMAND_IDS" src packages docs --glob '!node_modules'` returns exactly three hits: the declaration, and `src/test-kernel/commands/ExtensionCommandRegistry.vitest.ts:8` and `:89`. Zero occurrences in its own file outside the declaration, zero in `packages/extension/package.json` contributions, zero in `packages/extension/src/commands.ts`. It runs a `commandCatalog.filter(...).map(...)` at extension module load on every startup, for a test. And the test file ALREADY derives the identical set inline in the very next case (`:98-101`: `commandCatalog.filter(entry => entry.extensionRegistry === true).map(entry => entry.id)`), importing `commandCatalog` and `CommandCatalogEntry` at `:12-15`. Introduced by `c85be4ba3b` (#7111 "Derive extension command registration from the shared catalog"); `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "EXTENSION_REGISTRY_CATALOG_COMMAND_IDS"` returns nothing and it is absent from docs/proposals/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md.

(b) `BUILTIN_DEFAULT_CHAT_AGENT` (`packages/cli/src/runtime/defaultAgents.ts:7`; knip-baseline row: file `packages/cli/src/runtime/defaultAgents.ts`, category production-dead, kind exports). `rg -n "BUILTIN_DEFAULT_CHAT_AGENT" packages/cli/src src/test-kernel` gives 6 in-file uses (`:7,14,16,24,32,49` — all inside `defaultAgents.ts`) and exactly two external uses, both pure literal restatements: `src/test-kernel/cli/DefaultAgents.vitest.ts:14` `expect(BUILTIN_DEFAULT_CHAT_AGENT).toBe('assistant')` and `src/test-kernel/cli/ChatDefaults.vitest.ts:126` the same. Both are already implied by public-function assertions in the same files: `DefaultAgents.vitest.ts:46` asserts `pickDefaultToolUseAgent([])` returns `'assistant'`, and `ChatDefaults.vitest.ts:128` runs `expectChatDefaults` over `resolveChatDefaults`. AGENTS.md "Testing discipline" bans testing what a schema or the type system already guarantees; a `toBe` against the literal one line below the declaration is the purest case. No production consumer outside `defaultAgents.ts`.

(c) A triplicated meta-assertion. `rg -n "expectRealCoverage" src/test-kernel` returns the definition (`src/test-kernel/support/repoScan.ts:74-80`, a three-line `expect(scanned).toBeGreaterThan(min)`) and three byte-identical call sites, each wrapped in an `it('actually scans the production source roots')` with the identical argument `ALL_HOST_PRODUCTION_ROOTS`: `src/test-kernel/architecture/approvalPolicyAuthorityRatchet.vitest.ts:103-105`, `src/test-kernel/architecture/progressEventHandlerRetirement.vitest.ts:63-65`, `src/test-kernel/architecture/sessionFactAmbientHelperRetirement.vitest.ts:32-35`. It asserts a property of the shared scanner, not of any one ratchet, so two of the three restate a fact the third already owns. This touches none of the five settled ratchets under `config/ratchets/` — `expectRealCoverage` is a `src/test-kernel/support/` helper, and the kernel architecture tests keep their hardcoded allowlists untouched.

Production consumers across (a)-(c): zero. Non-production: the six test files cited. Ambiguous (`scripts/`, `docs/scripts/`): none — I checked that `scripts/sync-package-contributes.mjs` consumes `commandKeybindings`/`packageCommandContributions` from `src/shared/commands/catalog.ts`, not these symbols. `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all` searches for `BUILTIN_DEFAULT_CHAT_AGENT` and `expectRealCoverage` return nothing.

**Proposal**

1. Delete `EXTENSION_REGISTRY_CATALOG_COMMAND_IDS` and its docstring (`packages/extension/src/commands/extensionCommandHandlers.ts:138-150`). In `src/test-kernel/commands/ExtensionCommandRegistry.vitest.ts`, hoist the derivation the sibling test already performs at `:98-101` into one file-local `const catalogRegistryIds` and use it at `:89`; drop the import at `:8`. Keep the `ExtensionRegistryCatalogEntry`/`ExtensionRegistryCatalogCommandId` types (still used at `:110-115`, `:138`) and demote the now type-only `commandCatalog` import at `:28` to `import type` if lint asks.
2. Drop the `export` keyword from `packages/cli/src/runtime/defaultAgents.ts:7`. Delete `src/test-kernel/cli/DefaultAgents.vitest.ts:12-15` and its import at `:4`, and `src/test-kernel/cli/ChatDefaults.vitest.ts:126` and its import at `:17` — the surrounding `pickDefaultToolUseAgent([])` (`:46`) and `expectChatDefaults` (`:128`) assertions already pin the same fact through the public path.
3. Keep the `expectRealCoverage(ALL_HOST_PRODUCTION_ROOTS)` self-check in `src/test-kernel/architecture/approvalPolicyAuthorityRatchet.vitest.ts:103-105` (the widest scanner user) and delete the two copies at `src/test-kernel/architecture/progressEventHandlerRetirement.vitest.ts:63-65` and `src/test-kernel/architecture/sessionFactAmbientHelperRetirement.vitest.ts:32-35`, plus the now-unused `expectRealCoverage` imports at `:10` in each.
4. Remove the two resolved rows from `config/ratchets/knip-baseline.json` in the same PR and run `npm run check:dead-code-ratchet`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run lint`, `npm test`.

**What we give up**

(a) If someone tags a new `commandCatalog` entry `extensionRegistry: true` without a handler, the failure now surfaces from a test-local derivation instead of a production constant — same assertion, same failure, one fewer startup computation. (b) A dedicated named assertion that the CLI's built-in chat agent is `assistant`; the value is still pinned end-to-end by `pickDefaultToolUseAgent([]) === 'assistant'` and by `resolveChatDefaults`. (c) Two redundant confirmations that the shared repo scanner reached real files; one survives, and any scanner regression still fails it. All three are one-line restorations if a reviewer disagrees.

**Verifier corrections to the evidence above**

All three legs are real at HEAD (d8c6dfc071, past the #11416-#11422 round-1 merges). But the evidence has eight factual errors, and leg (c) has a prior-decision problem the evidence omits entirely.

**(a) EXTENSION_REGISTRY_CATALOG_COMMAND_IDS — confirmed, with line-number errors**

1. Wrong docstring range. The docstring is lines **139-143** (`/**` at 139, `*/` at 143), not ":139-142". The const is 144-150 as claimed. The proposal says to delete ":138-150" — line 138 is the blank line separating the const from `ExtensionRegistryCommandId`; deleting 138 through 150 leaves no blank before the next docstring at 152. Correct deletion range is **139-151** (docstring + const + trailing blank).

2. Wrong location for the sibling derivation. It is at **`ExtensionCommandRegistry.vitest.ts:100-104`**, not ":98-101" (:96-99 is a four-line comment). It also is not "the identical set": it builds a **`Set`**, not an array, and it filters with `(commandCatalog as readonly CommandCatalogEntry[]).filter((entry) => entry.extensionRegistry === true)` rather than the production predicate `(entry): entry is ExtensionRegistryCatalogEntry => 'extensionRegistry' in entry && entry.extensionRegistry === true`. Same result set, different shape — the hoisted const must be an array (test 1 spreads it into a sorted array), and the Set at :100-104 becomes `new Set(catalogRegistryIds)`.

The type system already enforces both directions of the coverage the runtime constant exists to let a test re-check. 5. **The "demote `commandCatalog` at :28 to `import type`" step is unnecessary churn.** `grep consistent-type-imports eslint.config.mjs` returns nothing, and TypeScript does not report a value import used only under `typeof` (line 111) as unused. Leave the import as-is — AGENTS.md's anti-churn bar applies.

6. The "runs a `filter().map()` at extension module load on every startup" framing is true but is not a cost worth citing (a 60-entry array filter). Drop it from the PR body; the argument is zero production consumers, not perf.

**(b) BUILTIN_DEFAULT_CHAT_AGENT — confirmed, with two off-by-one errors**
