#### Serialize the CLI's NDJSON stdout sink with p-queue instead of the hand-rolled drain latch

- **Lens**: `cli-output-parity` · **Kind**: hand-rolled · **Risk**: low
- **Net**: -22 LoC, -2 elements (claimed -30 LoC, 0 elements; verifier figure governs)

**Evidence**

`NdjsonStdoutSink` (packages/cli/src/runtime/logSinks.ts:197-297) hand-rolls a single-concurrency async serializer: a `CliNdjsonRecord[]` backlog (:198), a `drainPromise` latch (:199), `ensureDrain()` (:224-238) whose `finally` hook re-arms itself when the backlog refilled during the previous drain, `drain()` (:240-262) which shifts records and awaits backpressure, and a `flush()` (:214-218) that spins `while (this.drainPromise || this.queue.length > 0)`.
CLAUDE.md "Design guardrails" states the rule verbatim: "**Serialize async work with `p-queue`**, never a hand-rolled promise chain." `p-queue` is already a dependency of BOTH the root and the CLI package (package.json:159 `"p-queue": "^9.3.3"`; packages/cli/package.json:81 same), so this adds no dependency. It is already the repo's serializer of choice in ten production modules, including inside this same CLI runtime tree: packages/cli/src/runtime/approval/approvalPrompts.ts:65-79 keys a `PQueue({concurrency: 1})` per `CliContext`; also src/tools/lean/direct/directLspAdapter.ts:19, src/tools/memory/memoryFileSystem.ts:11, src/auth/SupabaseClient.ts:1, src/auth/oauth/SubscriptionOAuthCoordinator.ts:10, src/transcript/StreamLogStore.ts:3.
The hand-rolled version has already shipped one production race: `gh issue view 7803` ("Add regression test for NdjsonStdoutSink drain-queue race", CLOSED/COMPLETED) records that #7793 fixed `ensureDrain()` assigning a stale already-settled promise via `??=`, which stranded subsequently queued records, and that two independent reviewers flagged the fix as untested. That is the exact failure mode `p-queue` exists to remove.
The swap is test-safe: the class already takes an injectable `NdjsonWritable` (:202) and the whole suite drives that seam — src/test-kernel/cli/LogSinks.vitest.ts:15-36 builds a `stdout` stub with `usable`/`write`/`once`/`off` and asserts only through `writeRecord`/`write`/`flush` (:39-120, five cases including the drain-latch regression at :40-53 and the backpressure case at :56+). No test reaches `queue`, `drainPromise`, `ensureDrain`, or `drain`.
Consumer surface is unchanged: the module-level singleton is consumed by `writeNdjsonStdout` (:300, used by packages/cli/src/commands/_helpers/output.ts:57 and packages/cli/src/runtime/sessionProgressSubscription.ts:186), `flushNdjsonStdout` (:305), and `createCliLogSink` (:313). None of them touches the removed internals.
Dedupe: `git log --oneline -8 -- packages/cli/src/runtime/logSinks.ts` shows the last touches were unrelated (a442caa1e4, 9c57b5a01a dead-props sweep). `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "NdjsonStdoutSink"` returns only the closed test issue #7803; `--search "p-queue hand-rolled"` returns only #10493 (closed, desktop disposal chaining), which never covered this file. Not in the round-1 index and `grep -n "NdjsonStdoutSink\|logSinks\|p-queue\|PQueue" docs/proposals/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md` -> no match.

**Proposal**

In packages/cli/src/runtime/logSinks.ts, replace the hand-rolled scheduler inside `NdjsonStdoutSink` with `p-queue`:

1. `import PQueue from 'p-queue';` and replace fields :198-199 with `private readonly queue = new PQueue({ concurrency: 1 });`. Keep `stdoutClosed` (:200) and the injected `stdout` (:202).
2. `writeRecord` (:208-212) becomes: return early on `isClosed()`, else `void this.queue.add(() => this.writeLine(record));`.
3. Add `private async writeLine(record: CliNdjsonRecord): Promise<void>` carrying the body currently inside `drain()`'s loop for a single record: bail on `isClosed()`, `JSON.stringify(record)` + newline, `this.stdout.write(...)` inside try/catch that calls `closeQueue()`, and `if (!canContinue && !(await this.waitForStdoutDrain())) this.closeQueue();`. It must never throw, so the `void queue.add(...)` cannot reject.
4. `flush()` (:214-218) becomes `return this.queue.onIdle();`; `close()` (:220-222) keeps delegating to `flush()`.
5. `closeQueue()` (:267-270) sets `stdoutClosed = true` and calls `this.queue.clear()` instead of `this.queue.length = 0`.
6. Delete `ensureDrain()` (:224-238) and `drain()` (:240-262) entirely. `isClosed()` and `waitForStdoutDrain()` are unchanged.
7. Validate with the existing suite (`src/test-kernel/cli/LogSinks.vitest.ts`, unmodified — it is the regression suite from #7803), then `npm run typecheck`, `npm run lint`, and `pnpm --filter @texra-ai/cli validate:run` (the headless NDJSON/JSON byte-parity harness wired at .github/workflows/ci.yml:487) so the frozen `--output-format ndjson`/`json` contract consumed by texra-ai/texra-action is proven unchanged.

**What we give up**

Nothing observable: record order, backpressure handling, EPIPE/close short-circuit, and `flush()` semantics are all preserved; only the scheduler changes. The cost is a runtime dependency edge from the CLI's lowest-level output module to `p-queue` (already bundled for approvalPrompts.ts on the same code path, so no bundle-size change), and losing the ability to inspect a pending-record array in a debugger — `queue.size`/`queue.pending` replace it. Risk is medium only because this module writes the machine-readable stdout contract that texra-ai/texra-action parses, so the change must be gated on `validate:run` rather than unit tests alone.

**Verifier corrections to the evidence above**

Ten factual errors in the evidence; none changes the verdict, but the numbers and consumer list are wrong.

1. WRONG LoC. Claimed "-30 LoC". I implemented the exact proposal and measured: packages/cli/src/runtime/logSinks.ts goes 313 -> 291 lines; `git diff --stat` = 19 insertions, 41 deletions. Net is **-22 LoC**, not -30.

2. WRONG ELEMENT COUNT. Claimed "0 elements". Actual: `ensureDrain()` and `drain()` deleted, `writeLine()` added (net -1 method); field `drainPromise` deleted and `queue` retyped (net -1 field); +1 module import edge. Net **-2 elements**, not 0.

3. WRONG LINE NUMBERS (minor). The class is 197-293, not "197-297" (293 is `}`; 313 is EOF). `flushNdjsonStdout` is declared at :304, not :305. `createCliLogSink` is at :311, not :313. The singleton `ndjsonStdoutSink` is at :296 and `writeNdjsonStdout` is declared at :299 (its call to the sink is at :300). Everything else checks out exactly: fields :198-199, `stdoutClosed` :200, ctor/`stdout` :202, `writeRecord` :208-212, `flush` :214-218, `close` :220-222, `ensureDrain` :224-238, `drain` :240-262, `isClosed` :263, `closeQueue` :267-270, `waitForStdoutDrain` :272.

4. MISSED CONSUMERS (two production call sites). Evidence says `writeNdjsonStdout` is "used by packages/cli/src/commands/_helpers/output.ts:57 and packages/cli/src/runtime/sessionProgressSubscription.ts:186". Re-grepped: there are FOUR production call sites — packages/cli/src/runtime/cliPresentationHost.ts:149 and packages/cli/src/runtime/doctor.ts:480 were missed entirely; output.ts is :59 not :57; sessionProgressSubscription.ts is :195 (and it is a _default parameter_ `writeRecord: CliNdjsonProgressRecordWriter = writeNdjsonStdout`, not a call), not :186. None touches the removed internals, so the conclusion holds.

5. MISSED CONSUMERS (flush/createCliLogSink). Evidence listed these exports without consumers. `flushNdjsonStdout` is consumed at packages/cli/src/bin/texra.ts:32 and packages/cli/src/runtime/initPlatform.ts:103 (and mocked in src/test-kernel/cli/InitPlatformSignalHandlers.vitest.ts). `createCliLogSink` is consumed at packages/cli/src/runtime/cliPresentationHost.ts:86.

6. WRONG PRECEDENT. "packages/cli/src/runtime/approval/approvalPrompts.ts:65-79 keys a `PQueue({concurrency: 1})` per `CliContext`" is false: that file has `import type PQueue from 'p-queue'` (:21, type-only) and delegates construction to `getOrCreatePQueue` from `@utils/core/perKeyQueue` (:16, called at :69). The cited region is :65-70. Therefore the "already bundled for approvalPrompts.ts on the same code path, so no bundle-size change" argument is wrong as stated — the conclusion survives via real value imports in the same package: packages/cli/src/runtime/cliSecrets.ts:4, packages/cli/src/chat/chatSessionController.ts:6, chatSubmitDriver.ts:9, chat/tui/runChatTui.tsx:9, chat/tui/state/subscribeApprovals.ts:14.

7. UNDERCOUNT. "ten production modules" is off by 3x. `grep -rn "from 'p-queue'"` over src/ and packages/ (excluding node_modules/dist) returns 38 hits, of which 30 are production modules (25 value imports, 5 type-only).

8. WRONG TEST DESCRIPTION. src/test-kernel/cli/LogSinks.vitest.ts is not "five cases" — it runs 8 tests: the `NdjsonStdoutSink` describe has 5 `it` statements expanding to 6 tests (`it.each(['error','close'])`), plus 2 in a `CLI pipe errors` describe in the same file. The backpressure case is :78-91, not ":56+"; :56 is the "preserves order across logger and public-record writes" case. The claim that no test reaches `queue`/`drainPromise`/`ensureDrain`/`drain` is correct — verified.

9. PROPOSAL BUG (step 3). `JSON.stringify(record)` currently sits OUTSIDE the try in `drain()` (only `stdout.write` is guarded). Carrying that layout into a `void this.queue.add(...)` converts a non-serializable record (BigInt/circular) from "flush() rejects" into an unhandled rejection that can kill the process. Fix: move `JSON.stringify` inside the try, or append `.catch(() => undefined)` to the `void queue.add(...)` — the same swallow `writeRecord` already has today at :211.

10. UNSTATED BEHAVIOR DELTA. `PQueue.clear()` is not equivalent to `queue.length = 0`: I verified empirically that `clear()` leaves the `add()` promises of dropped tasks permanently unsettled. Harmless here because they are voided and `isClosed()` short-circuits further writes after `closeQueue()`, but it is a real difference the evidence claims does not exist ("Nothing observable").

Also: the rule text is AGENTS.md:661 ("Serialize async work with `p-queue`, not hand-rolled promise chains") and .claude/skills/code-review/references/review-checklist.md:159 ("A diff that touches an existing hand-rolled chain should migrate it, not grow it"). Both confirmed verbatim.

<details><summary>Verifier reasoning</summary>

I did not take the evidence on faith — I implemented the change and ran every gate, then reverted to a clean tree.

DEDUPE (kill criterion 1). round1-index.md is 93 lines; `grep -in "queue|serial|latch|drain|sink"` returns zero hits. `grep -in "NdjsonStdoutSink|logSinks|p-queue|PQueue|drain"` over docs/proposals/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md returns only one unrelated `#drain()` hit at :2217 (CoalescedSnapshotWriter in src/agent/workflowScript). Not a round-1 item, not in the survey. `git log --oneline -8 -- packages/cli/src/runtime/logSinks.ts` confirms the last touches are unrelated (a442caa1e4 host sweep, 9c57b5a01a dead-props). Not done at HEAD — the hand-rolled latch is still there.

ALREADY FILED (criterion 2). `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "NdjsonStdoutSink"` returns only #7803 (CLOSED/COMPLETED), which I read in full: it is the _regression-test_ follow-up from #7793, and the evidence's characterization of it is accurate word for word — `this.drainPromise ??= this.drain()` assigned a stale already-settled promise and stranded queued records; two independent reviewers flagged the fix as untested. `--search "p-queue hand-rolled"` returns #10493 (CLOSED/COMPLETED), which I read: it is desktop-only (packages/desktop/src/main/index.ts:1104 chain-then + test sleeps) and never mentions logSinks. Nothing filed for this swap. `git log --all --grep=NdjsonStdoutSink` returns only PRD doc commits from #3661, not implementation history.

CONSUMER RE-GREP (criterion 3). Full repo grep for NdjsonStdoutSink/writeNdjsonStdout/flushNdjsonStdout/createCliLogSink/logSinks excluding node_modules and dist: 4 production `writeNdjsonStdout` call sites, 2 `flushNdjsonStdout`, 1 `createCliLogSink`, plus ~40 unrelated imports of the sibling text writers. The removed symbols (`queue` array, `drainPromise`, `ensureDrain`, `drain`) are all `private` and appear nowhere outside the file — confirmed. The test suite drives only the injectable `NdjsonWritable` seam. packages/cli/scripts/check-host-imports.mjs mentions logSinks.ts only in `processOutputAllowedFiles` (a process.stdout allowlist); its `forbiddenImports` set is `{vscode, electron}` only, so a `p-queue` import is not gated.

RULINGS (criterion 4). Searched for a counter-ruling; found only rulings IN FAVOR. AGENTS.md:661 mandates p-queue and names `streamApprovalQueue.ts` as the precedent, recording that a 2026-07-25 sweep already migrated every remaining hand-rolled chain. review-checklist.md:159 makes a hand-rolled serializer a review finding. #10493 shows an identical swap accepted and shipped. No doc, ratchet, or checklist rule protects this latch.

SETTLED SURFACE (criterion 5). This is CLI host code, not the frozen @agent SDK, not the PocketFlow engine, not a platform port, not a browser-reachable util (`src/utils` is untouched), not AgentEvent/SessionFact, not vscode-free-zone. None of the five ratchets covers packages/cli/src/runtime.

MASKING SITE (criterion 7). Checked. The `catch { this.closeQueue(); }` around `stdout.write` and the `waitForStdoutDrain() -> false` path are EPIPE/close handling, and the refactor PRESERVES both verbatim inside the new `writeLine`. Nothing is deleted silently. Not an M1-M6 misread.

EMPIRICAL VERIFICATION. I wrote the refactor exactly as proposed (PQueue field, `void queue.add(() => this.writeLine(record))`, `flush() = queue.onIdle()`, `closeQueue()` -> `queue.clear()`, ensureDrain/drain deleted) and ran:

- `npx vitest run src/test-kernel/cli/LogSinks.vitest.ts` -> 8/8 passed, including the #7803 drain-latch regression and both stdout-error/close discard cases.
- `npx tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` -> exit 0.
- `npx eslint packages/cli/src/runtime/logSinks.ts` -> exit 0.
- `node packages/cli/scripts/check-host-imports.mjs` -> exit 0.
- `npx prettier --check` -> clean.
- `pnpm --filter @texra-ai/cli validate:run` (the harness wired at .github/workflows/ci.yml:487) -> "CLI run validation passed". The frozen `--output-format ndjson`/`json` contract that texra-ai/texra-action parses is byte-identical.
  Then `git checkout --` and confirmed a clean tree.

I also probed p-queue@9.3.3 semantics directly rather than trusting the API, because two of them are load-bearing for the existing tests: (a) `add()` starts the task SYNCHRONOUSLY when under concurrency — this is what makes the `emit('drain')` in the ordering and backpressure tests land on an already-registered listener, and it is why the swap does not deadlock those cases; (b) `onIdle()` still resolves after `clear()` drops queued tasks while one is running, but the dropped tasks' `add()` promises never settle. Both confirmed by running a probe script against the installed node_modules/.pnpm/p-queue@9.3.3.

RISK. Downgraded from the claimed medium to low: the module does write the machine-readable contract, but the CI gate that guards that contract exists and I ran it green against the actual refactor. The only residual is the step-3 unhandled-rejection hole (correction 9), which is a one-line fix in the PR.

VERDICT: survives. It is a real hand-rolled-serializer deletion the repo's own written rule mandates, on a module with a documented production race from exactly this hand-rolling, with the regression suite and the contract harness both proving the swap behavior-preserving. Not thin at -22 LoC / -2 elements for a single self-contained file with an existing gate.

</details>

#### Let storeCredential own the placeholder/empty-credential messages instead of restating them at five call sites in three hosts

- **Lens**: `host-quad-duplication` · **Kind**: dual-representation · **Risk**: low
- **Net**: -2 LoC, -2 elements (claimed -8 LoC, -1 elements; verifier figure governs)

**Evidence**

`storeCredential` (src/common/secrets/storeCredential.ts:8-31) makes `placeholderMessage` a REQUIRED option and `emptyMessage` optional, so every caller hand-writes the copy for a decision `storeCredential` itself makes (it owns the `looksLikeCredentialPlaceholder` call at :26 and the empty check at :21).

Production callers — `rg -n "storeCredential" src packages` → 5 call sites, 0 test callers, 0 script callers:

- packages/cli/src/runtime/githubToken.ts:16-23 — `kind:'github'`, `emptyMessage:'GitHub token is empty.'`, placeholder literal
- packages/desktop/src/main/desktopSettingsIpc.ts:328-334 — `kind:'github'`, NO emptyMessage, byte-identical placeholder literal
- packages/extension/src/settingsView/handlers/githubSubscriptionHandlers.ts:61-67 — `kind:'github'`, NO emptyMessage, byte-identical placeholder literal
- packages/cli/src/runtime/providerApiKey.ts:25-30 — no `kind`, `emptyMessage:'API key is empty.'`, placeholder interpolating the RAW provider id
- src/controllers/settingsView/SettingsProfileKeyController.ts:79-83 — no `kind`, NO emptyMessage, placeholder interpolating `getProviderDisplayName(provider)`

Literal grep confirms three hosts carry the same GitHub sentence verbatim: `rg -n "This looks like a placeholder" src packages` → packages/cli/src/runtime/githubToken.ts:22, packages/desktop/src/main/desktopSettingsIpc.ts:333, packages/extension/src/settingsView/handlers/githubSubscriptionHandlers.ts:66 (identical), plus the two provider variants at packages/cli/src/runtime/providerApiKey.ts:29 and src/controllers/settingsView/SettingsProfileKeyController.ts:82. Non-production consumers: 0 (`rg -n "This looks like a placeholder|storeCredential|placeholderMessage" src/test-kernel` → 0 hits). Ambiguous (scripts/): 0.

Two live drifts fall out of the restatement. (a) Provider naming: the CLI rejects a masked OpenRouter key with "…rather than a openRouter API key" while the settings view says "…rather than a OpenRouter API key" — same provider registry (`providerDisplayName`, src/shared/constants/providers.ts:186-193) is applied on one side only. (b) Empty-value handling: only the CLI passes `emptyMessage`, so an empty GitHub token throws a message in the CLI and silently returns `false` with no feedback in the extension and desktop (storeCredential.ts:21-24).

This is the residual of an already-implemented item, not a re-file. `git log --all --grep=placeholderMessage` → 8cc42c7402 "fix(settings): reject placeholder API keys on every host" (2026-08-19), which implemented docs/proposals/2026-08-03-ssot-consolidation-plan.md F2's "Fold the CLI's `looksLikePlaceholder` key rejection into the shared commitProviderKey" (line 519-522). That PR moved the PREDICATE to shared code and copied the MESSAGE to each host — F2 says nothing about the message copy. `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "storeCredential"` and `--search "placeholderMessage"` → 0 issues. Not in docs/proposals/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md (grep for credential/placeholder/storeCredential → only unrelated `instructionPlaceholder` hits) and not in the round-1 index. Not in docs/proposals/2026-08-15-cross-host-consolidation.md's auth plane (C9 is subscription sign-in descriptors; the C-minor auth row is `clearAllCaches`).

Precedent for the shared home: the GitHub token's other user-facing strings already live in one place — `GITHUB_TOKEN_PROMPT` (src/tools/github/githubAuth.ts:30), `GITHUB_TOKEN_SAVED_MESSAGE` (:34), `GITHUB_TOKEN_REMOVED_MESSAGE` (:37) — and all three hosts import them. The placeholder sentence is the one member of that family that was left per-host.

**Proposal**

1. In src/common/secrets/storeCredential.ts, make `kind` required (`'provider' | 'github'`) and add an optional `label?: string` (the provider display name). Delete `placeholderMessage` and `emptyMessage` from `StoreCredentialOptions`.
2. Derive both messages inside `storeCredential` from `kind` + `label`: github → the existing GitHub sentence (moved next to `GITHUB_TOKEN_PROMPT` in src/tools/github/githubAuth.ts if the import direction is preferred, otherwise file-local); provider → `This looks like a placeholder rather than a ${label ?? 'provider'} API key. Enter the key issued by the provider.` and `${label ?? 'API'} key is empty.`
3. Update the five call sites to drop the two message fields; packages/cli/src/runtime/providerApiKey.ts:25-30 passes `kind:'provider'` and `label: providerDisplayName(provider)` (import from @shared/constants/providers, already a CLI dependency at packages/cli/src/runtime/apiStatus.ts:10), and src/controllers/settingsView/SettingsProfileKeyController.ts:79-83 passes its existing `displayName`.
4. Both empty-value paths now behave identically across hosts: the message exists for every caller, so the silent `return false` on the extension/desktop provider path becomes the same thrown-and-reported error the CLI already gives.
   No ratchet, knip baseline, wire schema, or persisted format is touched; no test pins these strings.

**What we give up**

Per-call-site freedom to word the rejection differently. The GitHub and provider wordings become fixed by `kind`; a future third credential kind adds one arm to the table instead of a new literal at its call site. The CLI's provider message changes from the raw id to the display name (a fix, not a regression), and the extension/desktop provider path starts reporting empty keys instead of silently doing nothing.

**Verifier corrections to the evidence above**

1. **`git log --all --grep=placeholderMessage` returns NOTHING.** I ran it (and `--grep=storeCredential`); both are empty. `8cc42c7402 fix(settings): reject placeholder API keys on every host` is real and is the right attribution, but it is only findable via `git log -- src/common/secrets/storeCredential.ts` (which returns `ed645f2c64`, `29925ac85d`, `8cc42c7402`). The cited command is fabricated.

2. **"Non-production consumers: 0" and "no test pins these strings" are both FALSE.** The grep was too narrow (it searched for `storeCredential|placeholderMessage|This looks like a placeholder` inside `src/test-kernel`, which indeed returns 0) but the behavior is pinned through the wrappers:
   - `src/test-kernel/cli/SaveProviderApiKey.vitest.ts:33` — `await expect(saveProviderApiKey('anthropic', '   ')).rejects.toThrow('empty')`; `:54` — `.rejects.toThrow('placeholder')` over 10 placeholder cases; `:95` — `await expect(saveGitHubToken(placeholder)).rejects.toThrow('placeholder')` over 4 cases.
   - `src/test-kernel/controllers/SettingsProfileKeyController.vitest.ts:114-118` — `assert.match(failures[0], /looks like a placeholder/)`; `:171-180` — `it('does nothing when committing an empty provider key')` pins today's silent-empty behavior _in its name_.
   - `src/test-kernel/cli/ConfigForm.vitest.ts:87,91` and `src/test-kernel/cli/OnboardingFlow.vitest.ts:9,16,122` mock the wrappers.
     Good news: the proposed messages keep the substrings `empty`, `placeholder`, and `looks like a placeholder`, so all assertions still pass. Bad news: the "does nothing when committing an empty provider key" test now describes the opposite of what happens and must be renamed/rewritten — unbudgeted churn.

3. **"all three hosts import them" is FALSE for the precedent.** `rg -l "GITHUB_TOKEN_SAVED_MESSAGE|GITHUB_TOKEN_PROMPT|GITHUB_TOKEN_REMOVED_MESSAGE"` returns exactly three files: the definition (`src/tools/github/githubAuth.ts`) plus **two** hosts — `packages/desktop/src/main/desktopSettingsIpc.ts` and `packages/extension/src/settingsView/handlers/githubSubscriptionHandlers.ts`. The CLI imports none of them (`packages/cli/src/runtime/githubToken.ts` imports only `GITHUB_TOKEN_STORAGE_KEY` and `resolveGitHubTokenSource`). The line anchors :30/:34/:37 are correct.

4. **Line-number nit.** `storeCredential.ts:8-31` conflates two declarations: the interface is :8-14, the function :17-31. The precise anchors (`placeholderMessage` :13, empty check :21-24, `looksLikeCredentialPlaceholder` :26) are all correct, as are all five call-site ranges.

5. **The claimed −8 LoC is wrong; the true net is ≈ −2, and ≈ 0 after the test rename.** Recount of the proposal exactly as written:
   - `storeCredential.ts`: interface −1 (drop `emptyMessage`+`placeholderMessage`, add `label?`); the two throw sites go 7 → 15 lines (two four-line ternaries) = **+8**; `return true` deleted with `Promise<boolean>`→`Promise<void>` = −1. **+6.**
   - `githubToken.ts` −3; `providerApiKey.ts` −2 fields +2 fields +1 new `providerDisplayName` import = **+1**; `desktopSettingsIpc.ts` −2 −1 (`if (!stored) return;`) = −3; `githubSubscriptionHandlers.ts` −3; `SettingsProfileKeyController.ts` −1 +2 −1 = **0**. **−8.**
     Net ≈ **−2 LoC**. Elements: −2 option fields +1 (`label`) = −1, plus the dropped boolean return contract = −2 total. (The proposal also forgot to state that dropping `emptyMessage` makes the `boolean` return dead — that deletion is where three of its eight lines actually come from.)

6. **The evidence missed the one move that makes the numbers real.** `@utils/text/credentialPlaceholder` has exactly ONE consumer in the entire repo — `src/common/secrets/storeCredential.ts:2` (verified: `rg -n "looksLikeCredentialPlaceholder|credentialPlaceholder"` → 3 hits, all definition/self). Folding that 27-line module into `storeCredential.ts` puts the patterns and their messages in one file and takes the net to ≈ **−6 LoC / −3 elements**. Cost: `scripts/check-browser-safe-utils.mjs` parses the documented module count out of both CLAUDE.md ("The other 60 TypeScript modules") and AGENTS.md (script lines 591/596), so both docs must be decremented in the same PR.

7. **An overlapping OPEN ledger item is not declared.** `docs/proposals/2026-08-15-shared-contracts-and-retirement.md` §2.5 (lines 358-364; status 2026-08-19 at 722-724; ledger row 829) tracks `SettingsCredentialActions` — an unstarted extraction of exactly this credential wiring (ext ~140 L, desktop `desktopSettingsIpc.ts:344-375` ~180 L, CLI `providerApiKey.ts`), flagged **net-≤0** and gated on a maintainer ruling. It does not rule against this candidate — its own clause "fold the CLI-only placeholder-key rejection into the shared `commitProviderKey`" was executed by `8cc42c7402`, so the doc's "the CLI-only `sk-xxxxxx` rejection stand[s]" line is stale — but this candidate is a strict subset of that row's territory and the PR body must say so.

8. **Drift (a) is confirmed and understated.** `EXTRA_API_KEY_PROVIDER_IDS = ['openRouter','kimiCode']` (src/shared/constants/providers.ts:167) and `EXTRA_DISPLAY_NAMES` (:170-175) mean the CLI also emits "a **kimiCode** API key" where the hosts say "Kimi Code". Also: the controller does **not** use `providerDisplayName` — it injects `getProviderDisplayName` (`src/utils/config/providerConfig.ts:86`, region-aware) wrapped at `SettingsProfileController.ts:97-100`. So `label` must stay caller-supplied exactly as proposed; `storeCredential` must never call `providerDisplayName` itself or it would silently drop the region variant.

9. **Drift (b) is partly overstated.** The empty-key silent path is NOT reachable from the extension's `setApiKey` command — `packages/extension/src/commands/api/apiKeyCommands.ts:111` already guards `if (!apiKey) return;`. It IS reachable from (i) the settings-view `setProviderKey` handler with `message.apiKey === ''` (`packages/desktop/src/main/desktopCredentialSettingsController.ts:178-183`, mirrored in `SettingsViewMessageHandler.ts`), and (ii) `SettingsProfileKeyController.setProviderKey`'s own prompt path when the input box returns `''` (`:41` guards only `== null`).

10. **Blast radius of step 4 is safe but unverified in the evidence.** I checked all three: desktop throws land in `createCommandHandler`'s `result.catch(reportAsyncError)` (`packages/desktop/src/main/desktopIpcTypes.ts:143`); extension throws land in `withHandlerErrorHandling` ("Failed to save GitHub token"); controller throws land in `run()`'s catch → `reportFailure` (`SettingsProfileKeyController.ts:94-101`). No unhandled rejection anywhere. But three host paths change from _silent no-op_ to _error toast_ when a user submits an empty field — a user-visible behavior change, not a pure dedup, and it belongs in the PR title.

<details><summary>Verifier reasoning</summary>

**Duplicate / already-done checks (all clear).**

- `round1-index.md`: greps for `credential|placeholder|storeCredential|secret` hit only "Derive the instruction placeholder…" (line 13) and "isSupabaseConfigured / placeholder-credentials branch" (line 43) — neither is this.
- `docs/proposals/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md`: `storeCredential|placeholderMessage|credentialPlaceholder|githubToken|providerApiKey` → 0 hits.
- Round-1 PRs #11416-#11422: `git log --oneline -8` over the five paths shows only `16d66fd077 (#11417)` touched `githubSubscriptionHandlers.ts`; the placeholder literals are still verbatim-triplicated at HEAD. Not done.
- `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "storeCredential OR placeholderMessage OR credential placeholder"` → only #7724 (SDK north star, unrelated). Broader search surfaces #10969 (CLOSED, "Separate refresh-tail failures from key-action failures in `SettingsProfileKeyController.run`") — adjacent file, different defect.

**Consumer count re-grepped myself.** `rg -n "storeCredential"` across the whole tree → exactly 5 production call sites + 1 definition + 5 import lines. No dynamic dispatch, no string-literal keys, no `packages/extension/package.json` contribution, no settings-schema key, no `packages/extension/resources/` or `prompts/` or `supabase/functions/` reference. `rg -n "This looks like a placeholder"` → exactly the 5 sites the candidate names, with the three GitHub ones byte-identical. `rg -n "key is empty|token is empty"` → only the two CLI `emptyMessage` fields. `rg -n "looksLikeCredentialPlaceholder"` → definition + one consumer.

**Settled-surface / ratchet check.** No `@agent/*` import is added (the CLI would import `@shared/constants/providers`, already used at `packages/cli/src/runtime/apiStatus.ts:10`, so the host-agent-deep-import baseline is untouched). `src/common/secrets/` and `src/controllers/` are not in the shared-schemas or architecture-edges frozen lists' path. No wire schema, no persisted format, no VS Code-free-zone violation (`storeCredential.ts` imports only `@utils/*`). The only ratchet in play is `scripts/check-browser-safe-utils.mjs`, and only if the optional `credentialPlaceholder` fold is included.

**Masking-site check (criterion 7).** The `if (options.emptyMessage) throw …; return false;` at `storeCredential.ts:21-24` is a genuine M-class masking site: on the extension and desktop paths an empty credential is swallowed with zero feedback. The candidate's step 4 fixes it _loudly_ (throw, reported by each host's existing error reporter) rather than deleting the fallback silently. That is the correct treatment, not a violation.

**Deliberate-justification check (criterion 4).** Two dated docs touch this ground. `docs/proposals/2026-08-03-ssot-consolidation-plan.md:519-522` (F2) and `docs/proposals/2026-08-15-shared-contracts-and-retirement.md:358-364` (§2.5) both say "fold the CLI-only placeholder-key rejection into the shared `commitProviderKey`". Both are about the **predicate**, and both were satisfied by `8cc42c7402` — which moved `looksLikeCredentialPlaceholder` into shared code and _copied the sentence to each host_. Neither doc rules on the message copy. §2.5's `SettingsCredentialActions` extraction (which would subsume this) is explicitly "never written" as of 2026-08-19 and is a flagged net-≤0 item behind a maintainer ruling, so it is not a live blocker. This is a genuine residual, not a re-file.

**Verdict.** Real, novel, not filed, not done, correctly scoped, and it fixes two verified live drifts (`a openRouter API key` / `a kimiCode API key` in the CLI vs. display names elsewhere; a silent empty-credential no-op on the settings-view paths). It survives — but the arithmetic does not: it is a ≈−2 LoC change as written, not −8, and it is too thin to carry a standalone PR on reduction grounds. Ship it batched with the other cross-host copy-owner items (the `b7109de2ce collapse/cross-host-copy-owners` precedent), and include the `credentialPlaceholder` fold from correction #6 to make the net honestly negative.

</details>

### M8-scripts — Scripts, CI, build verifiers

**Files**: `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `CLAUDE.md`, `eslint.config.mjs`, `packages/agent/scripts/fsWalk.mjs`, `scripts/check-dead-code-ratchet.mjs`, `scripts/extension-package-utils.mjs`, `scripts/sync-package-contributes.mjs`, `scripts/verify-desktop-build-artifacts.mjs`, `scripts/verify-desktop-package.mjs`, `scripts/verify-extension-package-invariants.mjs`, `scripts/verify-vsix-contents.mjs`, `src/README.md`, `src/test-kernel/architecture/dependencyDirection.vitest.ts`

#### Delete the duplicated repo-source VS Code-free scan from the two desktop artifact verifiers

- **Lens**: `scripts-and-ratchets` · **Kind**: duplication · **Risk**: medium
- **Net**: -97 LoC, -6 elements (claimed -96 LoC, -6 elements; verifier figure governs)

**Evidence**

Two _artifact_ verifiers each contain a byte-equivalent scan of the **repo source tree** asserting the VS Code-free-zone invariant, which is already enforced twice per PR.

The two copies:

- `scripts/verify-desktop-package.mjs:464-492` `checkDesktopSourceBoundaries(failures)`, wired at `:698`, fed by `:29-35`.
- `scripts/verify-desktop-build-artifacts.mjs:93-119`, fed by `:49-55`. Same loop, same two regexes, same two failure strings ("Desktop-shared source imports the VS Code-backed state barrel instead of @common/state/stateKeys" / "Desktop-shared source imports the VS Code runtime module").

Both read the same shared list: `scripts/extension-package-utils.mjs:20-37` (`desktopSharedSourceDirSegments` = packages/desktop/src, src/agent, src/controllers, src/eventBus, src/latex, src/logger, src/model, src/replacement, src/shared, src/tools, src/utils; `desktopVscodeFreeSourceDirSegments` adds src/common/errors) via `getDesktopSharedSourceDirs`/`getDesktopVscodeFreeSourceDirs` (`:39-49`), plus `vscodeRuntimeImportPattern` (`:6`) and `vscodeBackedStateImportPattern` (`:9`).

The same invariant already runs on every PR, in two documented layers:

1. `eslint.config.mjs:60-76` `VSCODE_FREE_ZONE_DIRS` + the `no-vscode-import-in-free-zones` rule at `:389-420` (ci.yml:187-188 `pnpm run lint`).
2. `src/test-kernel/architecture/dependencyDirection.vitest.ts:28-43` `VSCODE_FREE_ZONES` + `:146-155` (ci.yml:337-338 test shards). Its `:20-25` block says the eslint/vitest pair is deliberately two layers.
   The `@common/state` half is also already covered for `src/**` by the same test's "Production core never imports host layers" (`:71-84` `HOST_LAYER_IMPORT_SPECIFIERS`/`HOST_LAYER_IMPORT_PREFIXES` include `@common/state` and `@common/state/`, scanned over all production src at `:188-197`).

Coverage delta I verified, dir by dir: every `src/*` dir in the script list is inside `VSCODE_FREE_ZONE_DIRS` **except `src/logger`**; `src/common/errors` is covered by the broader `src/common`. The only roots not covered by the canonical lists are `packages/desktop/src` and `src/logger` — both are clean today: `git grep -n "from 'vscode'|require('vscode')|import('vscode')" -- packages/desktop/src` → 0 hits; `git grep -n vscode -- src/logger` → 0 hits; `git grep -n "@common/state" -- packages/desktop/src` → 0 hits.

Production vs non-production consumers of the deleted symbols (`git grep -n vscodeBackedStateImportPattern|getDesktopSharedSourceDirs|getDesktopVscodeFreeSourceDirs`): production = the two scripts only. Non-production = none. `requiredMonacoWorkers` and `vscodeRuntimeImportPattern` have other live callers (`verify-vsix-contents.mjs:15,203`, `verify-desktop-package.mjs:344,632`, `verify-desktop-build-artifacts.mjs:73,86`, `src/test-kernel/scripts/DesktopPackagePayload.vitest.ts:25`) and stay.

Where the duplicate runs: `check:desktop-build-artifacts` fires only in the manual Windows job (`.github/workflows/desktop-package.yml:225`) plus the dev-only `build:initial` chain (package.json:15,49); `check:desktop-package` fires in all three manual installer jobs (`desktop-package.yml:111,168,242`). Neither workflow runs on PRs (`desktop-package.yml:3` is `workflow_dispatch` only) — so the third/fourth copies buy nothing on the PR path.

Misattribution smell: `src/test-kernel/scripts/DesktopPackagePayload.vitest.ts:132,152` spawns verify-desktop-package.mjs against a _fake_ package root, and `:76-77` expects `status === 0`. Because `checkDesktopSourceBoundaries` ignores `TEXRA_DESKTOP_PACKAGE_ROOT` and walks the real repo, a stray `vscode` import in `src/agent` today fails a _desktop packaged-payload_ test with `expected 1 to be 0`, not the lint rule or the zone ratchet.

Dedupe checks run: not in the round-1 index (49 shipped / 41 refuted) — the refuted "one duplicated zone list" sub-item is `docs/proposals/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md:2689`, which is about coupling `dependencyDirection.vitest.ts` to `eslint.config.mjs` via dynamic import; this candidate keeps that mirror intact and deletes a _third and fourth_ list elsewhere. `grep -n "checkDesktopSourceBoundaries|desktopSharedSourceDirSegments|vscodeBackedStateImportPattern" docs/proposals/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md` → no hits. `git log --all --grep=checkDesktopSourceBoundaries` → empty. `git log --oneline -8 -- scripts/verify-desktop-package.mjs scripts/verify-desktop-build-artifacts.mjs` → 63ceb641af, ad965a97f5, 0cec457113, dbf76be919, ae61ba5a7e, 17dc012f07, 19756dd0aa, 151b194326 — none touched this block. `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "verify-desktop-package"` and `--search "desktop source boundaries"` returned no match (nearest precedent, #11255 "Consolidate six hand-rolled recursive directory walkers in scripts/", is closed and shows scripts/ consolidations do ship here).

**Proposal**

1. Add `'packages/desktop/src'` and `'src/logger'` to `VSCODE_FREE_ZONE_DIRS` (eslint.config.mjs:60-76) and to `VSCODE_FREE_ZONES` (src/test-kernel/architecture/dependencyDirection.vitest.ts:28-43), and add both to the prose list in CLAUDE.md and src/README.md:42-44 — CLAUDE.md mandates those copies stay in sync. Both roots are clean today, so this is additive with zero churn.
2. To keep the desktop `@common/state`-barrel half, add one `no-restricted-imports` entry for `@common/state` and `@common/state/stateManager` (leaving `@common/state/stateKeys` allowed) to the existing `packages/desktop/src/**/*.ts` block at eslint.config.mjs:528.
3. Delete `checkDesktopSourceBoundaries` (scripts/verify-desktop-package.mjs:464-492), its call at `:698`, its four const lines at `:29-35`, and the three now-unused imports at `:8,:9,:11`.
4. Delete the identical block in scripts/verify-desktop-build-artifacts.mjs:93-119, the consts at `:49-55`, and the imports at `:7,:8,:11`. Keep `collectFiles` (still used for `rendererAssets`) and `vscodeRuntimeImportPattern` (still used at `:86` against the built main bundle).
5. Delete from scripts/extension-package-utils.mjs: `vscodeBackedStateImportPattern` (`:9-10`), `desktopSharedSourceDirSegments`/`desktopVscodeFreeSourceDirSegments` (`:20-37`), and `getDesktopSharedSourceDirs`/`getDesktopVscodeFreeSourceDirs` (`:39-49`).
6. Verify with `npm run lint`, `npx vitest run src/test-kernel/architecture/dependencyDirection.vitest.ts src/test-kernel/scripts/DesktopPackagePayload.vitest.ts`.

**What we give up**

Nothing in coverage: after step 1 the vscode-import invariant covers a strict superset of what the scripts scanned, and it moves from a manual `workflow_dispatch` desktop job to every PR. The `@common/state`-barrel guard for `packages/desktop/src` is the one rule with no existing home; step 2 rehomes it in six lines, and skipping step 2 would drop a guard with zero current violations. Restoring any of this is a git revert of one PR.

**Verifier corrections to the evidence above**

This verifier folded its corrections into the reasoning block below rather than filling the corrections field. Read that block: the numbered corrections it references are stated there.

<details><summary>Verifier reasoning</summary>

**Gate 1 — duplicate.** `round1-index.md` (95 lines, 49 shipped + 41 refuted) has two adjacent script/config rows. Line 93 "Batch three small script/config cleanups: one dead export, one single-caller module, one duplicated zone list" is under the REFUTED half; I read the full refutation at survey:2679-2691. Its sub-item (b) touches `verify-desktop-package.mjs` but is about _inlining `desktop-package-metafile-paths.mjs`_, and its sub-item (c) is about coupling `dependencyDirection.vitest.ts` to `eslint.config.mjs` via dynamic import. Neither is this candidate: this one keeps all three documented mirrors intact and deletes a fourth/fifth undocumented copy in two packaging scripts. `grep -niE "checkDesktopSourceBoundaries|desktopSharedSourceDirSegments|vscodeBackedStateImportPattern|verify-desktop"` on the survey → one hit, at 2687, the (b) paragraph. Not a duplicate.

Zero other consumers — no test, no workflow, no `packages/extension/package.json` contribution, no settings schema, no resources/prompts/supabase reference. `extension-package-utils.mjs` has 5 code importers (`verify-desktop-package.mjs`, `verify-desktop-build-artifacts.mjs`, `verify-extension-package-invariants.mjs`, `verify-vsix-contents.mjs`, `DesktopPackagePayload.vitest.ts`); the last three import only `requiredMonacoWorkers` / `readJson` / `collectRelativeFiles`, all of which stay. `requiredMonacoWorkers` and `vscodeRuntimeImportPattern` both keep live callers and stay. Consumer count holds.

That defends two copies and prescribes exactly the sync step 1 performs. Nothing anywhere defends the packaging-script copies: `docs/proposals/2026-08-01-directory-organization.md:367-368` names both scripts only as sites that would need renaming under an `@extCommon/*` rename, not as intentional enforcement. No ratchet, no AGENTS.md rule, no review-checklist item covers them.

But it does not re-litigate it: `packages/desktop/src` and `src/logger` are additive, semantically correct entries (an Electron host and a logging primitive must not import `vscode`), and both are clean today. Verified: `git grep -nE "from 'vscode'|require\('vscode'\)|import\('vscode'\)" -- packages/desktop/src src/logger` → 0 hits; `git grep -n "@common/state" -- packages/desktop/src` → 0 hits. Nothing about the frozen `@agent/*` surface, PocketFlow, platform ports, browser-safe utils, AgentEvent/SessionFact, or the five ratchets is affected.

**Coverage delta, re-derived dir by dir.** Script list (`extension-package-utils.mjs:20-37`): `packages/desktop/src`, `src/agent`, `src/controllers`, `src/eventBus`, `src/latex`, `src/logger`, `src/model`, `src/replacement`, `src/shared`, `src/tools`, `src/utils`, plus `src/common/errors`. `VSCODE_FREE_ZONE_DIRS` (`eslint.config.mjs:60-76`) and `VSCODE_FREE_ZONES` (`dependencyDirection.vitest.ts:28-43`) are byte-identical 15-entry lists covering all of those except `packages/desktop/src` and `src/logger` (`src/common/errors` ⊂ `src/common`; `ls src/common` confirms `errors/` is a subdir). So the delta is exactly the two the candidate names. Both are clean, so step 1 is a zero-violation additive edit.

**Enforcement mechanics I checked so step 1 actually works.** `npm run lint` (`package.json:10`) explicitly passes `packages/desktop/src` on the CLI. The `local/no-vscode-import-in-free-zones` rule is enabled at `eslint.config.mjs:648`, inside the config object whose `files:` array (`:524-533`) includes `packages/desktop/src/**/*.ts` and `src/**/*.ts` — so both new zones would be linted. `packages/desktop/src` has 85 `.ts` files and **zero `.tsx`** (`git ls-files … | sed 's/.*\.//' | sort | uniq -c` → 4 css, 1 html, 1 json, 85 ts), so the block's `.ts`-only glob is not a hole. `src/logger` is two files, both `.ts`. On the vitest side, `sourceFilesUnder` resolves `REPO_ROOT + zone` and already handles non-`src` roots (`packages/agent/src`, three extension frontend dirs), and the `> 100` scan guard is aggregate. **The `@common/state` half.** For `src/**` it is already covered — and more strictly — by "Production core never imports host layers" (`dependencyDirection.vitest.ts:70-84` `HOST_LAYER_IMPORT_SPECIFIERS` includes `@common/state`, `HOST_LAYER_IMPORT_PREFIXES` includes `@common/state/`, scanned over `sourceFilesUnder('src', {excludeTestKernel:true})` at `:100-102`, i.e. including `src/logger`), and by `no-restricted-imports` pattern `@common/state/**` at `eslint.config.mjs:99` over `src/**` + `packages/agent/src/**`. The only genuinely unguarded surface is `packages/desktop/src` — and as noted in correction 6 that guard is vacuous today, since neither specifier the regex matches resolves to anything. So the "what we give up" section is right in outcome but for the wrong reason, and step 2 is optional-and-should-be-rewritten rather than load-bearing.

**Gate 6 — net-adds.** Deletions: `verify-desktop-package.mjs` ≈ 43 (function 464-495, call site + blank, consts 29-35, three import lines); `verify-desktop-build-artifacts.mjs` ≈ 37 (loop 93-120, consts 49-55, three import lines); `extension-package-utils.mjs` ≈ 35 (`:9-10`, `:20-37`, `:39-49`, blanks, plus the now-unused `node:path` import at `:2`). ≈ 115 removed. Additions: 2 eslint zone entries + 2 vitest zone entries + ~2 CLAUDE.md lines + ~12 for a correctly-placed desktop-only eslint block (step 4's `src/README.md` edit drops out per correction 4) ≈ 18. Net ≈ **-97**. Not a net-add; the candidate's -96 is essentially right.

**Gate 7 — masking-site misread.** Nothing here is a `catch`, `??`, `.catch(default)`, or silent `default:`. It removes a redundant _assertion_, and the one non-redundant assertion is explicitly re-homed rather than dropped. Not an M1-M6 pattern.

**Gate 8 — too thin.** ~115 lines, 6 exported/named elements (`checkDesktopSourceBoundaries`, `vscodeBackedStateImportPattern`, two segment arrays, two getters) plus 10 module-level locals across the two scripts, and it moves a real invariant from a `workflow_dispatch`-only path onto every PR. Verified `desktop-package.yml:1-11` is `on: workflow_dispatch` with no `pull_request`/`push` trigger, and `check:desktop-build-artifacts` appears only at `:225` plus the dev-only `build:initial` chain (`package.json:18,49`), `check:desktop-package` at `:111,168,242`. Not thin.

**One reviewer objection worth flagging as scope, not refutation:** step 1 adds `packages/desktop/src` but not `packages/cli/src` or `packages/trace-viewer/src`, which is asymmetric for a list titled "VS Code-free zones". Either extend to all non-extension host packages or say in the PR body why only desktop (it is the only one the deleted scripts scanned). That is a design choice a reviewer may push on; it is not a correctness problem.

**Bonus finding the PR should carry:** the misattribution smell is real and worth stating — `checkDesktopSourceBoundaries` ignores `TEXRA_DESKTOP_PACKAGE_ROOT` (`verify-desktop-package.mjs:25-26` sets `packageRoot` from the env var, but `desktopSourceBoundaryDirs` at `:29-35` is built from `repoRoot`), so today a stray `vscode` import anywhere in `src/agent` fails `DesktopPackagePayload.vitest.ts:78` with `expected 1 to be 0` from a _packaged-payload_ test rather than from the lint rule or the zone ratchet. Deleting the block fixes that misattribution too.

**Verdict: SURVIVES.** Real third-and-fourth copy, correct consumer count, no prior filing or ruling, net negative, mechanically verified. Risk medium — it edits the governance-sensitive zone list in three synced places and rewrites a shared `scripts/` module used by four other scripts, so the PR must run `npm run lint` and `npx vitest run src/test-kernel/architecture/dependencyDirection.vitest.ts src/test-kernel/scripts/DesktopPackagePayload.vitest.ts`.
