### `lifecycle-mirrors`

- **Give the direct Lean adapter one owner per liveness fact: p-queue's own drain, the AbortController, and the RPC handle**

  <details><summary>Why it was refuted</summary>

  REFUTED on kill criterion 3 (the central equivalence claim is factually false, verified empirically) plus criterion 8 (what survives is too thin and unbatched).

  **Step 1 is wrong, and shipping it silently regresses the bug fix that introduced the code.**

  The evidence asserts: "`startsInFlight` is incremented at `:346` INSIDE the queued task and decremented in its `finally` at `:350-351` — i.e. it is exactly `startQueue.pending`" and "`waitForStartsIdle()` resolves when `startsInFlight === 0` — i.e. exactly `startQueue.onPendingZero()`."

  That is false in exactly the one situation `waitForStartsIdle()` is ever used in. `disposeAll` (`src/tools/lean/direct/directLspAdapter.ts:328-341`) aborts first and waits second:

  ```
  startGeneration += 1;                                   // :329
  startAbort.abort(new Error(ADAPTER_STOPPED_MESSAGE));   // :332
  startAbort = new AbortController();                     // :333
  await Promise.all([...sessions.keys()].map(disposeSession)); // first sweep
  await waitForStartsIdle();                              // :337
  await Promise.all([...sessions.keys()].map(disposeSession)); // second sweep
  ```

  p-queue 9.3.3 wraps a running task's function in `Promise.race([operation, abortRejection])` (`node_modules/p-queue/dist/index.js:391-399`). When the signal aborts a task that is ALREADY RUNNING, the race rejects immediately, `run`'s `finally` fires, `queueMicrotask(() => this.#next())` runs, and `#next()` does `this.#pending--; if (this.#pending === 0) this.emit('pendingZero')` (`:152-155`) — **while the wrapped async function is still executing**. The hand-rolled counter does not do that: `startsInFlight -= 1` lives in the _inner_ lambda's `finally` (`:350`), so it tracks the real settlement of `getOrStartSessionLocked`, not the abort race.

  I ran this against the pinned 9.3.3 build:

  ```
  before abort: pending= 1 inFlight= 1
  add() rejected: stopped
  after onPendingZero: pending= 0 inFlight= 1 taskCompleted= false
  later: inFlight= 0 taskCompleted= true
  ```

  `onPendingZero()` resolved with the task still running. So `await startQueue.onPendingZero()` at `:337` would resolve essentially instantly (the abort fired 8 lines earlier), the second dispose sweep would run before the in-flight `startFreshSession` (`:394-425`) finishes registering and tearing down its `lake env lean --server`, and `disposeAll` would return while a Lean child process is still being spawned. That is precisely the leak fixed by `68d6dc8b21` "fix(lean): stop leftover CLI and desktop language servers (#10248)" — the same commit that introduced `waitForStartsIdle`, `startGeneration`, and `spawned` (`git log -S"waitForStartsIdle"` and `-S"this.spawned"` both return only 68d6dc8b21). No test in `src/test-kernel/tools/lean/DirectLspAdapter.vitest.ts` covers dispose-during-an-in-flight-start (the eight `it(...)` cases are at `:156, :164, :219, :358, :411, :461, :511` and none does), so the regression would be silent — the worst failure mode for a process-leak fix.

  `onIdle()` is not a rescue either: it has the same `#pending` accounting, and aborted queued tasks are removed from the queue (`:420-434`), so it degenerates to the same early resolve. **No p-queue API expresses "wait until the wrapped function actually settles" once a signal can abort a running task.** Step 1 has no correct form; it must be dropped entirely, not rewritten.

  **What actually survives is too thin.** Step 2 (delete `startGeneration`, thread `AbortSignal` instead) is semantically sound — both are mutated only in `disposeAll` at `:329` and `:332-333`, so `startGeneration !== captured` and `capturedSignal.aborted` are equivalent — but it buys **-1 element and ~-2 LoC** while rewriting 11 call sites (`:111, :310, :363, :367, :378, :390, :400, :421, :441, :443, :445`) and four function signatures (`throwIfStopped`, `getOrStartSessionLocked` `:360`, `startFreshSession` `:396`, `evictOthersForExhausted` `:308`). That is checklist §13 / R5 churn: a signature-wide reflow of the exact liveness path #10248 hardened, for one variable. Step 3 (delete `child`, keep `spawned`) is correct — `this.child` and `this.rpc` are provably set together (`leanSession.ts:207` and `:256`, no await between) and cleared together (`:220`/`:222` in `finalizeServer`, `:95`/`:97` in `dispose`), so `if (rpc && child)` at `:103` is `if (rpc)` — but it is **-1 field and ~-5 LoC** in a different file.

  Residual after deleting the refuted step: **~-7 LoC, -2 elements** across two files, with the larger half being pure churn over a shutdown-safety path. That does not earn a PR on its own and the candidate is not batched with anything.

  Dedupe/prior-art checks all came back clean and do NOT kill it — recorded so the next pass does not redo them: not in `round1-index.md` (its two Lean lines are maxSessions and the registry `toolchain`/`pid` fields, both shipped — `maxSessions` and `sessionFreedWaiters` are confirmed gone from `directLspAdapter.ts` at HEAD `d8c6dfc071`); the survey doc's Lean hits are only lines 1075-1107 and 1359-1400; `git log --oneline -12 -- src/tools/lean/direct/` last touch is `c125ce0a18` (#11420), which did not touch these; no `tech-debt` issue matches; no ruling in `docs/proposals/`, `docs/architecture/`, or `docs/guide/lean.md`; no settled surface touched. The candidate dies on its own physics, not on prior art.

  </details>

- **Interrupt agent-CLI children from the one map that holds every live loop, and drop the dedupe set the second map forces**

  <details><summary>Why it was refuted</summary>

  REFUTED on a false premise plus thinness, with a safety-margin trade the repo's own rules argue against.

  1. THE CENTRAL PREMISE IS FACTUALLY WRONG. The candidate's whole lens rests on "the dedupe set is the tell that one liveness fact has two representations" — that `interrupted = new Set<ExecutionId>()` exists _because_ of the second map. It does not. One execution routinely occupies MULTIPLE rows of `sessions` on its own: `resolveSessionIds` returns two ids per turn for both providers — `[fallbackSessionId, turn.sessionId]` (src/tools/claudeAgent.ts:478) and `[fallbackThreadId, thread.id]` (src/tools/codex.ts:428) — and `onTurnSuccess` (src/tools/agentCliShared.ts:515-518) calls `registerSessionId` for each, so `sessions` holds an alias-per-id for the same `executionId`. The repo's own test pins exactly this: `registry.register('session-a', entry(executionA, …))` and `registry.register('session-a-alias', entry(executionA, …))` in src/test-kernel/tools/AgentCliSessionRegistry.vitest.ts:155-163. So even with `inFlight` deleted entirely, iterating `sessions` alone would still need per-execution dedupe. The set is an alias-multiplicity guard, not a dual-representation artifact. The "tell" the candidate builds its case on does not exist.

  2. THE MECHANISM IT DELETES WAS ADDED DELIBERATELY BY A SHUTDOWN BUG FIX, AND IT IS THE _OTHER_ LOOP. `git log -S 'inFlight' -- src/tools/agentCliSessionRegistry.ts` returns exactly one commit: 95e31c8cb8 "fix(cli): register the agent shutdown handlers (#9229) … Fixes #9235". Before it, `interruptAll` iterated `sessions` only; that PR added `inFlight` to close the pre-session-id window and _kept_ the sessions loop, rewriting `interruptAll` into the two-loop + dedupe form verbatim as it stands today. The redundancy is not accreted debt — it is the deliberate output of the one PR whose subject was shutdown-drain completeness. That does not trigger kill-criterion 4 literally (no docs/proposals ruling, no ratchet), but it is direct evidence that a reviewer already weighed exactly this shape and chose belt-and-braces in a teardown path.

  3. IT TRADES ~10 LoC FOR A NEW SILENT-DEGRADATION MODE, WHICH CLAUDE.md FORBIDS. Today `interruptAll` is self-contained: whatever the registry knows about gets interrupted, independent of caller discipline. After the change its completeness depends on a two-call-site protocol maintained in a different file (`trackInFlight` at agentCliShared.ts:512 must always precede `register` at :482). The candidate's own "what we give up" names the failure: a future `register` without a preceding `trackInFlight` leaves a child un-interrupted at host shutdown — no log, no error, a leaked agent-CLI child process. "Silent degradation is a defect. A fallback that masks a failure must be loud … or not exist" cuts both ways here: the four lines being deleted are the thing that makes the drain unconditionally complete. A doc comment is not an enforcement mechanism, and the guarantee it replaces costs four lines.

  4. TOO THIN, AND NOT BATCHED (kill-criterion 8). Realizable yield is ~10 net LoC in one file, and the proposal itself mandates spending part of it back on an expanded invariant doc comment. The round-1 shipped set was overwhelmingly deletion of _dead_ surface; this is live, working, shutdown-path code whose removal buys a single-digit line count.

  5. MISSED CONSUMER (kill-criterion 3, partial). The "CONSUMERS" paragraph surveys only production and concludes "none of them observe iteration order or the dedupe set". It missed that an existing suite pins the `sessions`-loop path directly: src/test-kernel/tools/AgentCliSessionRegistry.vitest.ts:203-247 ('interrupts each child through the execution registry that owns the session') calls `register('session-a', …)` and `register('session-b', …)` with NO `trackInFlight` and asserts both children are interrupted. Under the proposal that test fails and must be rewritten — the proposal's "extend the existing agent-CLI registry coverage only if a suite already asserts interrupt fan-out" reads as if no such suite exists; it does, and it asserts precisely the branch being deleted.

  What DID hold on re-verification, for the record: the containment invariant is real. `onLoopStart` fires at src/agent/runtime/childRunLoop.ts:845 at the top of the setup `try`, before the turn loop; `onTurnSuccess` fires only in the `else if (turn != null)` arm of `prepareParentDelivery` (:1017), and both the interrupted (:1010-1012) and turnFailed (:1014-1016) arms call `releaseSessionOwnershipOnce()` and then `break` out of the loop (:1029-1038, :1058, :1061), so no `register` can ever follow a release; `unwindSetup` also releases on setup failure (:835). `releaseByExecutionId` deletes from both maps (:134-141). `register` and `trackInFlight` each have exactly one production call site. So the change would _work_ — it is just not worth what it costs. Not a duplicate (absent from round1-index.md and from the 49-candidate survey), not filed (gh tech-debt search empty), not already done at HEAD.

  </details>

- **Batch: delete two exact liveness mirrors — a boolean shadowing an AbortSignal, and a Set queried only for its newest member**

  <details><summary>Why it was refuted</summary>

  REFUTED on criteria 6 (net-adds/churns) and 8 (too thin), reinforced by criterion 4 (the survey doc already ruled this exact shape the other way). The factual claims about consumers are correct — this dies on measured yield, not on a missed importer.

  **The batch nets ZERO lines, not -8.** I applied both edits to scratch copies and ran the repo's prettier over before/after:

  - (A) `packages/cli/src/runtime/runExecution.ts`: 595 → 593 formatted lines. **-2 LoC**, -1 element (one function-local `let`).
  - (B) `src/controllers/progressView/backend/ProgressBackend.ts`: 916 → **918** formatted lines. **+2 LoC**, **0 elements**.

  Batch total: **0 LoC, -1 element**, against a claimed -8 LoC / -2 elements. The overclaim is 8 lines and 1 element.

  **(B) is not a dual representation, and it removes nothing.** `activationGeneration` owns "which activation is newest"; `inFlightActivationGenerations` owns "which activations are currently hydrating." Those are two different facts that happen to be intersected at the two read sites. The real claim is speculative generality (an over-general container), not mirroring — and the proposed cure is a 1:1 field swap: `private readonly inFlightActivationGenerations = new Set<number>()` becomes `private inFlightActivation?: number`. One private field in, one private field out. Nothing is deleted. Prettier expands `:337` from one line to two (`curly: 'off'` at `eslint.config.mjs:586`, so it stays brace-free but still wraps at 80 cols), and the proposal's own "add a one-line comment naming activationGeneration as the single owner" adds a third. That is a rewrite of a live concurrency guard on the progress-view activation path that costs 2 lines and buys zero elements.

  I did confirm (B)'s equivalence argument is sound, which is why this is a yield refutation rather than a correctness one: `generation = ++this.activationGeneration` at `:308` and `add(generation)` at `:332` are separated by no `await` (the intervening branches are synchronous `presentation.select` / renderer calls, and `ensureLoaded` at `:329` is a sync call returning a promise), so adds are strictly monotonic and the current `activationGeneration` can never be a non-maximal set member. `activationGeneration` is only ever incremented (`:233`, `:308`, `:912`), never decremented. So `set.has(activationGeneration)` and `inFlightActivation === activationGeneration` agree on every interleaving, including the gen-5/gen-6 case the evidence traced. The change is safe. It is also worth nothing.

  **(A) alone is a 2-line deletion and cannot carry the filing.** The evidence's exhaustiveness holds — I re-ran `rg -n 'shutdownRequested|launchAbortController'` and got exactly the six lines claimed, and the repo-wide `.abort()` / `= true;` corroborating grep returns exactly `runExecution.ts:382`. `launchAbortController` is a function-local `const` referenced only at `:299`, `:383`, `:453`, so nothing else can abort that signal and the flag and `signal.aborted` are provably equal. But the whole item is: delete `let shutdownRequested = false`, delete `shutdownRequested = true`, retarget one `if`. The survey's batching rule was invoked precisely because (A) is too thin to stand alone — and its batch partner contributes 0 elements and +2 lines, so the batch is one 2-line deletion wearing a second item as a hat.

  **The survey already ruled on this exact shape.** `docs/proposals/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md:2661` refutes a sibling candidate with the identical reasoning: part (b) there was "legitimate in kind … But it is a two-optional-marker change worth ~0 LoC … so it nets positive," and the filing was dropped as "two unrelated micro-items batched into one filing — which is not a bounded deletion … refuted-in-part and too thin." Three more round-1 refusals share the shape: "Sweep three residues in the progress-view backend," "Remove three defensive copies and one single-caller wrapper that #11402 left behind," and "Batch: drop a defensive array copy and 21 per-site `satisfies` annotations." A ~0-net micro-batch is the single most consistently refuted category in this survey.

  Secondary, not load-bearing: (A) also trades an intent name for a mechanism read on the exit-code path. `shutdownLaunchAborted` (`:490`, `:512`) decides the process exit code at `:576`. Today the flag and the signal cannot disagree, but the rewritten read binds exit-code classification to "was this controller aborted for any reason" rather than "did shutdown request it." For 2 lines, that is not a trade worth making.

  </details>

### `defensive-after-handoff`

- **Drop four redundant defensive copies/re-parses on same-process typed handoffs (RunScope, workflow hydrate, tool-use resume channels, CLI NDJSON status)**

  <details><summary>Why it was refuted</summary>

  REFUTED on two independent grounds: a dated ruling the candidate cites but did not read to the end, and a real payoff that is roughly half the claim and entirely churn-class.

  (1) A DATED PROPOSAL ALREADY RULED THIS FAMILY AN EXPLICIT KEEP, AND SAID SO SPECIFICALLY TO STOP THIS RE-AUDIT.

  `docs/proposals/2026-08-16-overdefensive-top10.md:522` opens "## 4. Runners-up (all explicit KEEP — recorded so later waves don't re-audit)", and :528-530 reads:

  "- Defensive clones/spreads/freezes (~12 clone sites) — hand-calibrated with written rationales; hot paths already tuned (`openAIMessageUtils.ts:149`, `StreamLog.ts:570`)."

  That is this candidate's lens verbatim — "defensive copies/freezes … after same-process typed handoffs" — and three of its four hunks (2, 3, 4) are clone/spread/freeze sites. The doc shipped as #10732 (MERGED) with the whole follow-up tree #10756/#10757-#10769; every one of the ten entries that WAS actionable got its own issue. The clone/spread/freeze family deliberately did not, and §4's parenthetical states why.

  The candidate's dedupe check is not wrong so much as shallow: it ran `gh issue list --search "defensive"` and correctly found the #10732 program, then concluded "none of these four sites is named in either." §4 is a family-level KEEP written precisely so that later waves stop enumerating individual sites. The candidate never quotes §4, never argues against it, and therefore does not beat it (kill criterion 4).

  I checked whether the ruling's own justification ("hand-calibrated with written rationales") actually covers site 2, since that is the one site the doc's corpus provably includes — production `structuredClone` call sites number 7 across the whole repo (`src/agent/workflowScript/runWorkflowScript.ts:254`, `workflowExecutionState.ts:103` and `:527`, `src/agent/modelHandlers/ModelHandler.ts:283`, `src/agent/node/persistedFlow.ts:330`, `src/shared/schemas/coreSettings.ts:540`), so `workflowExecutionState.ts:527` was inside the audited set. It is true that :527 carries no written rationale, which mildly dents the doc's characterization. But the remedy the candidate proposes for that site is to delete one line and ADD a one-line ownership comment — net zero. Denting a KEEP's phrasing to buy zero lines is not beating the ruling.

  (2) CHURN-CLASS: ~-3 LoC, 0 ELEMENTS, ACROSS FOUR UNRELATED SUBSYSTEMS — AND ROUND 1 ALREADY REFUTED THE IDENTICAL SHAPE.

  Real accounting, hunk by hunk (I re-derived each; see corrections for the full arithmetic): hunk 1 = -1 line (the `RunOutcomeSchema,` import at `packages/cli/src/runtime/history.ts:24`; line :460 itself is a same-line edit). Hunk 2 = -1 line, +1 comment the proposal itself asks for = net 0. Hunk 3 = 0 lines (`return Object.freeze({ ...scope });` → `return Object.freeze(scope);` is a same-line edit). Hunk 4 = -2 lines. Total ≈ -3, 0 elements, 0 exports, no knip-baseline row, no ratchet row.

  Checklist §14 R5/R6 (`.claude/skills/code-review/references/review-checklist.md:127-128`) is the governing bar and there is nothing to report under R6 because the element delta is zero. Round 1 of this very campaign refuted a structurally identical candidate — "Drop the last defensive copies in syncSlice and the TOOL_ICON_MAP entry for a tool named 'runs'" — with exactly this reasoning: "the payoff is 2 lines, not 6, and the spreads were never real defensive copies anyway … churn-level under checklist 13/14 R5/R6." Same lens, same magnitude, same verdict. A second round-1 refutation ("Batch: drop a defensive array copy and 21 per-site `satisfies`…") was killed partly on the same inflated-LoC arithmetic. Filing this is re-litigating a bar that has now been set twice.

  The batching does not rescue it under criterion 8: the four hunks share no file, no module, no owner, and no deletion. They span `packages/cli/src/runtime/`, `src/agent/workflowScript/`, `src/agent/runtime/`, and `src/agent/implementations/flows/tooluse/nodes/` — a scattershot bundle whose only common thread is the lens that §4 already closed.

  (3) SUPPORTING POINT (not load-bearing, but it removes the last reason to make an exception): two of the four hunks are not copy REMOVALS at all, they are contract changes bought for zero lines.

  Hunk 3 changes `createRunScope` from "returns a frozen snapshot of what you passed" to "freezes YOUR object and hands it back" — an observable side effect on the argument, for -0 lines. Closed issue #7669 ("Restore readonly on AgentLaunchContext.runScope…") is the record that this freeze is load-bearing: it justifies tolerating the dropped `readonly` on `AgentLaunchContext.runScope` with "`createRunScope` already returns an `Object.freeze`'d object, so there's no runtime risk." The freeze survives the proposal, so this is not a kill by itself — but it means hunk 3 trades a semantics change on the run-identity constructor for literally nothing.

  Hunk 4 likewise turns ToolUsePrepareNode from a pure reader of `resumeShared` into a mutator of it (freezing `resumeShared.stateSlices.userChannels.input` in place; `resumeShared` originates as Zod output at `src/agent/runtime/SessionResumeRetrieval.ts:171-176`). I verified it is inert today — grep over `userChannels` shows `input` is never written anywhere in production; only `transient` is (`ToolUseCycleNode.ts:174`, `ToolUseWaitNode.ts:194`), and `modelSwitchState.ts:18` passes `input` through by reference — so the change is safe. It is just a -2-line safety-margin trade, which is the trade §4 already declined.

  Hunk 1 is the only genuinely clean item: `RunOutcomeSchema.parse(status)` at `history.ts:460` is a §15 M6 defensive wrapper around code that cannot throw (TypeScript narrows `status` to `RunOutcome` after the two literal early returns at :454-457, given `HISTORY_RUN_STATUS`'s `as const` shape at `src/shared/schemas/historyRunStatus.ts:6-12`), it is a re-validation rather than a clone so §4's KEEP does not cover it, and it is a residue of `22f41dd7cd` (#10790) which converted `safeParse`+branch into narrowing+`parse` and left the now-dead `parse` behind. It is worth -1 line. That is not a candidate; it is a line to fix in passing on the next PR that touches `packages/cli/src/runtime/history.ts`.

  </details>

### `tool-layer-dup`

- **Derive host tool exclusions inside tool resolution and delete the `runtimeUnavailableTools` parameter threaded through 16 production files**

  <details><summary>Why it was refuted</summary>

  REFUTED on three independent grounds; any one is fatal.

  **1. DELIBERATELY JUSTIFIED — a dated ruling with an explicit do-not-refile covers exactly this chain, and the candidate's dedupe missed it.**
  `docs/proposals/2026-08-16-services-injection-audit.md` C13 ("Approval trio: single owner for a launch-pinned fact") is an adversarially-verified audit of precisely this plumbing — it names `runtimeUnavailableTools` riding "6 pure-FWD frames on the workflow-script grandchild chain (A″) and 4 on the delegate_agent chain (A′)" through `subagentExecution.ts` → `nativeSubagentStrategy.ts` → `executeAgent.ts`, and confirms "no intermediate hop reads or branches". Its disposition (:229, §6 Resolution addendum, 2026-08-16): **"C13 — approval trio is resolved; do-not-refile.** Current main post-#10647 already freezes `approvalPromptsUnavailable`, `runtimeUnavailableTools`, and `stopAfterCycle` in the per-launch `ToolPolicy` (`src/agent/core/flows/BaseFlowServices.ts:26-45`, frozen by `createToolPolicy`) … That is the accepted single-owner shape: launch-scoped policy frozen at the boundary." The doc also pre-rejects the candidate's shape by name: "The explicitly rejected shapes: session-owned mutable trio, and **implicit child inheritance of parent ALS values** (dual-ownership)." Deriving the fence from a process global so "subagents inherit the fence structurally" is that rejected shape with a different global. The doc's own escape hatch — "If no shape clears §15's single-owner bar, the answer is to keep the explicit threading — it is ugly but honest" — is the operative sentence here, and the candidate's shape does not clear the bar (see 3). The candidate's dedupe searched `gh issue list`, `git log --grep`, the round-1 index, and the 49-candidate survey, but never `docs/proposals/`, which is where this was adjudicated.

  **2. It reverses the SDK-readiness direction that #10594/#10647 shipped.** `docs/proposals/2026-08-15-agent-sdk-readiness-reverify.md` calls C-1 "the highest-value core item" and states the goal at :124 — "cycle flow drivable without an ALS frame — the property an SDK embedder wants" — closed at :373-375 by #10647 (commit `04cbb6f44e`), which converted the `useLaunchRunContext()` reads in `ResponseCycleFlow` and `runToolUseFlow` into injected `services.toolPolicy` fields. The candidate replaces that injected field with a module-global lookup at the _same two call sites_ (`agentToolResolution.passesRuntimeGates` and `ResponseCycleFlow.ts:216-218`). The global is a different singleton (`getSetupPlatform()` instead of ALS), but the property being defended — the flow resolves its tool roster from injected state, not from process-wide ambient state — is exactly what is lost. The candidate's dismissal ("#10594's ruling is about _how_ the field reaches the flows, not who computes it") inverts the ruling: how it reaches the flows _is_ the ruling.

  **3. The mechanism is broken, and the advertised win does not exist.**
  (a) `getSetupPlatform()` **throws** when unregistered — `src/tools/setup/platform.ts:189-192`: `if (!override) throw new Error('Setup platform has not been initialized.')`. The proposal's "when no host has been registered it returns the empty set, which is byte-identical to today's default" therefore requires either a `try {} catch {}` swallow (a §15 M-class masking site: "no host registered" silently becomes "no tools fenced") or a second module global stashed by `setSetupPlatform`. Either way it adds an ambient element rather than removing one, and makes the agent core's tool gating depend on the _setup-agent_ platform singleton — a surface whose declared purpose (`SetupPlatform`: `signIn`, `commands`, `extensions`, `terminal`) is host setup capabilities, not runtime host identity. An SDK embedder would have to call `setSetupPlatform({host, signIn})` to get correct tool availability.
  (b) It makes tool availability order-dependent at activation. The candidate concedes this and proposes moving `setSetupPlatform` (`packages/extension/src/extension.ts:555`) above `registerCommands` (:545) — unrelated, medium-risk activation-order churn in the extension composition root (criterion 6).
  (c) **The advertised deletion is largely illusory.** At every one of the claimed plumbing sites the field is one property of an object that still carries its siblings: `subagentExecution.ts:166-168`, `nativeSubagentStrategy.ts:299-301` and `:385-387`, `workflowScriptAgentRunner.ts:315-317`, `executeAgent.ts:422-423` and `:566-567`, `resumeRun.ts:372-374`, `AgentLaunchContext.ts:150-151`, `runToolUseFlow.ts:186-187` all forward `approvalPromptsUnavailable` and/or `onApprovalPolicyDenial`/`stopAfterCycle` on the adjacent lines. `ResponseCycleFlow.ts:222` reads `services.toolPolicy.approvalPromptsUnavailable` in the same filter expression, so the `toolPolicy` service field survives too. None of the 16 files loses its parameter, its interface, or a frame — each loses one line. The "delete the parameter threaded through 16 production files" framing is false; the chain stays exactly as long with 2 of 3 fields intact. The prior audit sized the whole trio at "−40/−50 LoC across ~14 files if solved"; this candidate claims −105 for one of the three fields.

  **Residual worth keeping (thin, batch-only):** the sub-claim that `CliExecuteOptions.runtimeUnavailableTools` has zero producers is TRUE and verified — `packages/cli/src/runtime/runExecution.ts:65` declares it, `:474-477` folds it, and the only three production callers (`packages/cli/src/commands/workflow.ts:217`, `packages/cli/src/commands/agentsRun.ts:81`, `packages/cli/src/commands/multiAgent.ts:259`) pass `enforceCategory`/`stopAfterCycle`/`categoryMismatchMessage`/`executionId` and never this field. Deleting the option and collapsing `:474-477` to `getDefaultUnavailableToolNames('cli')` is ≈ −6 LoC, −1 element, zero risk — too thin to stand alone (criterion 8), fine as a line item in an unrelated CLI dead-surface batch.

  </details>

### `storage-paths`

- **Batch three storage-layout residues: two single-consumer "shared" dir constants and a redundant pre-create**

  <details><summary>Why it was refuted</summary>

  REFUTED. Two of the three legs die, and the survivor is too thin and mis-evidenced to stand alone.

  LEG (a) — the two "shared" constants — DIES on churn (kill #6) and on a misread of the very comment it quotes (kill #3/#4).

  1. The premise "Neither is shared" misreads the comment. `src/common/storage/storageLayout.ts:19` reads "Global (non-workspace-scoped) storage directory names, **shared across hosts**." "Shared across hosts" describes the _storage location_, not the constant's import count: both directories live under `GlobalStorageFS`, whose own doc comment is "global storage (shared across workspaces)" and whose base path is `platform().storage.getGlobalStoragePath()` (`src/utils/files/storageFS.ts:24-30`) — the shared `~/.texra` root that all three hosts read (memory: cross-host-shared-storage-shipped, #8624). The comment is factually correct as written. The candidate treats an accurate statement about directory semantics as a false claim about code centralization, and the entire "speculative centralization" argument rests on that substitution.

  2. It reverses a deliberate consolidation that is 13 days old. `git show d17eb54274` (#10120, 2026-08-13, "refactor: merge globalStorage.ts constants into storageLayout.ts") is a 5-file commit that DELETED `src/platform/defaults/globalStorage.ts` — a file whose entire contents were exactly these two lines — and moved them into `storageLayout.ts`, authoring the "shared across hosts" comment in the same diff. So these two constants have now been deliberately kept together in a central home twice (a dedicated 2-line module, then this section). The candidate's own dedupe section cites #10120 but reads it backwards ("these two rode in as part of a file merge, not as a deliberate sharing decision") — the commit's title and its only substantive content ARE that decision. Scattering them back into their consumers 13 days later is a round trip: `globalStorage.ts` → `storageLayout.ts` → back to call sites. That is textbook checklist §13/§14 R5/R6 relocation churn, and the survey doc itself already refuted a sibling item on exactly this ground (`docs/proposals/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md:2567` (c): "turns one canonical constant into four local copies ... R5/R6 relocation, not removal").

  3. The payoff does not justify it. Leg (a) is -3 lines in `storageLayout.ts` (comment + 2 exports), -1 import in `externalInquiryStorage.ts`, and 0 net in `AgentDirectoryService.ts` (-1 import, +1 module-local const). Total ≈ -4 LoC, -2 exported names, +1 local const. And the "one consumer each" framing is incomplete: `'custom_agents'` is already hardcoded at `packages/cli/scripts/validate-run.mjs:938` and at `src/test-kernel/agent/AgentDirectoryService.vitest.ts:78,81,94,118,121`; `'ei_threads'` at `src/test-kernel/tools/InquiryStorage.vitest.ts:47,100`. Adding a third and fourth hand-written copy of a shared-root directory name is the direction that produces collisions in a namespace two hosts write to, not the direction that prevents them.

  LEG (b) — deleting `prepareSummaryCache` — is mechanically correct but its coverage evidence is FALSE, and once (a) is stripped it is too thin (kill #3, kill #8).

  4. FALSE COVERAGE CLAIM. The candidate states: "No test references `prepareSummaryCache`; the only test-kernel mention of the directory is `src/test-kernel/agent/runtime/SessionRestartRepair.vitest.ts:158`." Both halves are wrong. `src/test-kernel/transcript/StreamLogStoreLoad.vitest.ts` contains a purpose-built fixture for this exact code path — `summaryEnsureDirError?: Error` (`:40`), destructured at `:212`, and thrown from the `StorageFS.ensureDir` spy at `:271-273` guarded by `if (target === STREAM_LOG_SUMMARIES_DIR && summaryEnsureDirError)` — feeding a dedicated test at `:725-742`, "opens from authoritative logs when the summary directory cannot be prepared", which asserts `expect(warnSpy).toHaveBeenCalledWith('StreamLogStore', expect.stringContaining('Failed to prepare transcript summary cache'))`. That fixture and that assertion exist for no other purpose than `prepareSummaryCache`. The same file also references `STREAM_LOG_SUMMARIES_DIR` at `:26,60,137,271,332,1472,1478,1503`, and `src/test-kernel/cli/History.vitest.ts:107,1148` constructs a `KVStore(STREAM_LOG_SUMMARIES_DIR)`. The stated consumer count is off by a whole test suite; per the brief, one missed consumer is disqualifying, and here the missed consumer is the _only_ thing that pins the behavior being deleted.

  5. What survives is thin and not free. The mechanism does hold — I verified `KVStore.write` calls `await StorageFS.ensureDir(this.dir)` on every write (`src/common/storage/KVStore.ts:64`, with an explicit comment that it is deliberately not latched), `readPersistentSummaries` lists `this.kv()` (the LOGS dir, `:1388`) not the summaries dir, and `read`/`modifiedAt`/`listKeys`/`deleteDir` all route through `withMissingFallback`, so summaries-dir absence is fully tolerated. I also confirmed the "prepares transcript directories again after a storage-root reload" test at `:935-951` filters on `STREAM_LOGS_DIR` only, so it is unaffected. But the leg is not behavior-preserving: the detection point moves from `open()` to the first summary write, the operator-visible warning text changes from "Failed to prepare transcript summary cache" to "Failed to write transcript summary cache for <streamId>", and a store that opens with zero streams now emits nothing at all. Paying for that with the deletion of a purpose-built regression fixture, for -12 production lines, is not an obvious win — and standing alone after (a) falls, it is exactly kill #8.

  Net: the batch is 2/3 churn against a fresh owner-authored consolidation, and the residual third rests on a coverage claim that is false. If leg (b) is ever re-filed, it must be filed honestly as "delete an eager mkdir and the regression test written for it, accepting a later and differently-worded warning" — and batched with something real.

  </details>

### `settings-surface`

- **Delete `label` and `description` from `ModelsTabSurface` — every one restates the catalog row's own `title`/`description`**

  <details><summary>Why it was refuted</summary>

  REFUTED. The headline premise is false for half the rows, and the one place where source-level duplication is real turns out to be only three rows, not fourteen.

  **1. "Every one restates the catalog row's own `title`/`description`" is false for 7 of 14 entries.**

  I read both sides of all 14. Only 7 are true restatements, and even that count collapses under inspection:

  - **4 of those 7 are the `PROVIDER_ROUTING_SETTINGS` rows, and they contain ZERO source duplication.** src/shared/schemas/stateSettings.ts:971-972 reads `title: copy.label, description: copy.description` and :982 reads `models: [{ provider, ...copy }]` — the string literals are authored **once**, in the tuple's `copy` object (:917-923, :931-936, :941-950, :955-962), and referenced twice. The candidate calls this "literally the same two strings twice on one row"; it is the same two strings _referenced_ twice. There is no drift risk, no maintenance burden, and no line to delete. This is already the deduplicated form the candidate is asking for.
  - **3 are genuine restatements**: `USE_OPENROUTER` (:1390-1392 vs :1406-1409, byte-identical — I diffed them in Python), `KIMI_CODE_PREFER` (:1418-1420 vs :1439-1441), `GLM_CODING_PLAN` (:1450-1452 vs :1466-1468). That is the entire real duplication: ~6 literal lines.
  - **7 carry deliberately distinct prose**, which the candidate labels "drift" without checking: all six Core model rows (:417-430, :439-447, :458-465, :477-484, :496-503, :515-521) plus `WEBSOCKET_OPENAI` (:1338-1345). Their row-level descriptions resolve through `schemaDescription()` (:356-362, applied at :699) to coreSettings.ts:249-275, and every one of the six differs _systematically_, not accidentally: the schema text is provider-qualified for a flat config file ("Use **OpenAI's** newer Responses API…", "Store **Google Interactions** conversation state…", "Let **OpenAI models** use multiple tools…", "Keep long-running **OpenAI** requests alive…") while the models text is terse because it renders inside that provider's disclosure group. That is a two-register design, not decay.

  **2. The two "already DRIFTED" labels are load-bearing disambiguation, and one of them was authored 24 hours ago.**

  `model.useGoogleBackgroundResponses` landed in commit 8a6081a084 ("fix: default Google background responses off (#11364)", 2026-08-25) — the same commit that added the row _deliberately_ set `title: 'Google background responses'` and `label: 'Background responses'`, because `model.useBackgroundResponses` (:489) already owns the bare title 'Background responses'. `title` is the context-free identifier (it is what SettingsViewMessageHandler.ts:555/561/566 and desktopSettingsIpc.ts:263 put in error toasts, which carry no provider context); `label` is the in-group display, where the provider prefix would be redundant. `WEBSOCKET_OPENAI` follows the identical pattern ('OpenAI WebSocket' vs 'WebSocket transport'). Collapsing to one field forces a regression on one surface or the other for both rows, and the candidate's step 3 waves this away as "pick one wording each". A one-day-old deliberate authoring is the opposite of residue.

  **3. For the six Core rows the proposal does not remove a representation — it adds one.**

  Today those rows author exactly one string per role: the Zod `.describe()` owns the config-file documentation, the models surface owns the toggle copy, and the row _inherits_ the schema text with no override (`spec.description ?? schemaDescription(schema)`, :699). Step 3 ("keep the models wording by adding it as the row's explicit `description`") adds a third field per row that shadows the schema's, while coreSettings.ts:249-275 keeps its own text because `boolField` requires it. After the change each of the six rows has two authored descriptions instead of one inherited plus one surfaced. That is a net SSOT regression on 6 of the 14 rows the candidate is nominally cleaning.

  **4. The change churns well past its stated scope, in the settings SSOT file.**

  Making `title` mandatory on `SurfacedSettingEntry` (:262-266) also requires editing the **runtime** type guard `isSurfaced` (:1529-1535) to test `entry.title !== undefined`. Any surfaced row without a title then silently vanishes from `SURFACED_SETTINGS` (:1537-1538), hence from `CLI_STATE_SETTINGS` (:1590-1591), `SETTINGS_VIEW_SETTINGS_BY_KEY` (:1540-1548) and every snapshot — a silent-degradation trap on the catalog that gates the whole settings view. The candidate handles it by adding six titles to LaTeX rows, which is an unrelated user-visible `/config` copy change bundled into a "duplication removal". `modelsTabSettings` (:1605-1614) must also change its return type from `StateSettingEntry` (title optional) to `SurfacedSettingEntry`, or step 5's `label: entry.title` is `string | undefined` against `ProviderSettingDefSchema`'s required `label: string` (src/shared/constants/providers.ts:266-273) — the candidate never mentions this and its proposal as written does not typecheck. Plus a tuple restructure of `PROVIDER_ROUTING_SETTINGS` that buys nothing (see point 1).

  **Net:** the defensible core is three rows' worth of restated literals, roughly -10 LoC, against a rewrite that touches the settings catalog's type guard, six unrelated LaTeX rows, two deliberately-distinct user-facing labels, and six schema/row description pairs it makes worse. That is rule 6 (churns without shrinking a public API — `ProviderSettingDefSchema` is explicitly unchanged) on top of a premise that misreads 7 of 14 rows. Not worth an implementing PR in this form.

  Not killed by rules 1, 2, 5, 7: the consumer census is accurate, no issue is filed, no ratchet or settled surface is touched, and no masking site is removed. It dies on premise and value.

  </details>

### `type-bloat`

- **Drop `export` from the 182 file-local type declarations outside the CLI, and close the open blind-spot question that hid them**

  <details><summary>Why it was refuted</summary>

  REFUTED. The census is real and I reproduced it exactly — but the candidate dies on three of the kill criteria, and carries one material factual error about the SDK declaration build.

  **1. DUPLICATE (criterion 1) — proposed PR #2 is a strict superset of a round-1 REFUTED item.**
  `docs/proposals/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md:2333` — "Demote 11 file-local type exports, drop the TerminalRunResult alias re-export, and delete two never-used validators" (REFUTED, listed in round1-index.md). Its 11 type exports are, verbatim: `src/tools/approval/tempFileManager.ts:40`, `src/tools/executions/conversationFormat.ts:17`, `src/tools/executions/processOutput.ts:35`, `src/tools/inquiry/ExternalInquiryTool.ts:145`, `src/tools/inquiry/inquiryActions.ts:57`, `src/tools/memory/memoryFileSystem.ts:42/:50/:290`, `src/tools/setup/platform.ts:31`, `src/tools/support/externalBinaryUtils.ts:99`. I re-derived the candidate's own src/tools list of 32 and **all 11 are in it**. The refutation's ground (d) is the exact argument this candidate makes and loses: "Un-exporting a type removes a keyword, not a line — net LoC is exactly zero for all 11. The identical class in the identical directory was already swept 7 days ago by #10855 -> merged as #10866 ... and the same chore has been filed and closed four more times in the last four days (#11253, #11385, #11386, plus #11401). This is recognized recurring maintenance, not a non-obvious simplification finding." The candidate's dedupe missed this because it regex-tested the 182 _symbol names_ against the survey doc; the refuted item names _paths and line numbers_, not symbols, so a name-based scan cannot see it.

  **2. ALREADY FILED (criterion 2) — the class is a dated, recorded lead, and the candidate's own filter hid that.**
  `docs/proposals/2026-08-07-prod-structural-leads-triage.md` records this class in at least five separate leads with explicit fixes:
  - "*~26 src/tools _Input z.infer types exported with zero external consumers — one unexport PR_" → fix: "Drop the export keyword on the remaining ~23 types in one sweep PR (same edit as the already-landed zotero trio). **Element-neutral** but shrinks the frozen export surface; verify knip baseline stays green." That is proposed PR #2's headline sub-finding, already written down with the same fix and the same net accounting, 19 days ago.
  - "Unexport micro-sweep: MediaKind, ViewBundle/PanelOptions/MessageHandlerOptions ... exported but referenced only inside their own files" → "drop the `export` keyword on all four type declarations. Trivial; batch with the knip dead-export sweep".
  - "Unexported-but-still-exported types (CreateLifecycleHostOptions, StatCapableFs, FileLockTuning, NodePlatformServices, **etc.**) have no non-test importers" → "drop `export` keyword or delete the type".
  - "**15 zero-production-caller exported types** (AppendFollowUpResult, CurrentToolContexts, SeverityCounts, ...) prunable by type-export audit".
  - "ModelProviderFlags (userVars.ts) and SaveDebugParams (debugMessageSaver.ts) are exported with zero importers anywhere" and "UsageMonitorMetadata/UsageMonitorModelInfo/UsageMonitorContext exported but referenced only inside UsageMonitor.ts".
    The candidate's stated soundness argument — "Because the index includes `docs/**/*.md`, anything a dated proposal names by symbol is already excluded" — is precisely the flaw. These leads are _class_ rulings that name only exemplars ("etc.", "~26", "15"); excluding the named exemplars from the set does not exclude the class from the ruling, it only makes the residual _look_ novel. Round 1 already used this same doc to kill the `shared-schemas` candidate on exactly this ground (proposal:2493): "an arbitrary 2-of-472 slice of a class that was triaged 18 days ago and deliberately left as a single wave". Being the whole wave instead of a slice answers the fragmentation half of that ruling, but not the "recognized recurring maintenance" half.

  **3. NET-ADDS / BELOW BAR (criterion 6) — the headline value of -182 elements is zero.**
  Net LoC is 0 by the candidate's own admission. Net _elements_ is also 0: every one of the 182 type declarations survives, still occupies its line, still has its references. Nothing is deleted. The survey has ruled this twice: proposal:2493 (shared-schemas, ground 4) — "net LoC 0 and net elements 0. Both type declarations stay declared and stay used; only two `export` keywords disappear. CLAUDE.md's bar is 'fewer elements'; this removes none, and section 13/14 R5/R6 puts a 2-keyword, 0-line, tool-invisible edit below any filing bar"; and proposal:2491ff (TeamPlan, ground 2) — "No baseline row shrinks, and deleting an `export` keyword changes zero lines." The triage doc independently calls the src/tools half "Element-neutral". I confirmed the tool-invisibility myself: **0 of the 182 appear in `config/ratchets/knip-baseline.json`** (394 rows; 342 exports / 50 types / 2 files; 386 production-dead / 8 unused — all four of the candidate's baseline counts are correct), so all four PRs change zero baseline rows and zero gate output. The deliverable is 182 keyword deletions with no measurable reduction and no tool signal, across four hosts.

  **4. MATERIAL EVIDENCE ERROR that the candidate uses to dismiss its only real risk.**
  "What we give up: ... no `declaration: true` anywhere outside packages/agent, **which none of these touch**." This is wrong for 82 of the 182. `packages/agent/dist/types/src/` emits `.d.ts` for the repo-root tree (agent, auth, common, latex, model, platform, replacement, shared, tools, transcript, utils are all present), and I matched the 182 against those emitted declarations: **82 are currently emitted as `export type`/`export interface` in the packages/agent declaration build** — `src/agent/core/flows/ModelInvocationNode.ts::ModelInvocationConfig`, `src/agent/workflowScript/sandbox.ts::SandboxOptions`/`SandboxHostBridge`, `src/agent/index/agentRegistry.ts::LoadAgentsOptions`, `src/auth/oauth/pkce.ts::PkcePair`, and 78 more. That is the frozen-but-fenced SDK's emitted type surface (criterion 5 adjacency). It is probably not a _break_ — a round-1 verifier reproduced the shape with the repo's own tsc under `declaration: true, emitDeclarationOnly: true` and got exit 0 — but it means PRs 2, 3, and 4 change the emitted SDK declarations, and the candidate's verification plan (`typecheck`, `lint`, `test`, `check:dead-code-ratchet`) omits both the packages/agent build and `scripts/validate-artifacts.mjs`. The risk section asserts the opposite of the fact.

  **What genuinely survives, and why it is not enough.** The §4 contribution is real. `docs/proposals/2026-08-19-dead-code-gate-blind-spots.md:74-105` does say "mechanism unconfirmed ... treat the twelve as unexplained", and it does ask for "a specimen that is exported, has no test consumer, has no cross-file production consumer, and is still absent from the baseline". `DesktopNavigationPolicyOptions` (`packages/desktop/src/main/desktopNavigationPolicy.ts:33`, used in-file at :38 in the live exported `installDesktopNavigationPolicy`) is such a specimen, and the same-file contrast with `isAllowedExternalUrl` (:13, used in-file at :29, baselined `unused/exports` at knip-baseline.json:1114-1119) is exactly as described — I verified every line number. The `logsPane.ts` counter-side is also right and is stronger than the candidate realizes: `DesktopLogEntry` (:20) and `DesktopLogLevel` (:17) are baselined `production-dead/types` at knip-baseline.json:1208/1214 _despite_ being referenced in-file (`LogViewerState` at :31, `DesktopLogEntryDraft` at :37), which affirmatively **disproves** the competing mechanism a round-1 verifier asserted at proposal:2493 ("knip counts same-file type usage as usage for the `types` category"). So the docs correction is worth making. But it is a one-paragraph edit to a proposal, it is the follow-up write-up of an already-CLOSED issue (#11401, "Record the fourth dead-code-gate blind spot: types reachable only from an exported signature"), and it does not need 182 keyword deletions to land. A docs-only paragraph is not an implementing PR, and it cannot carry a 0-LoC/0-element sweep over the bar.

  **What is not wrong with it.** For the record, so this is not re-litigated: I independently reproduced the census at HEAD (d8c6dfc071) and got **exactly 182**, with the per-area split matching to the unit — desktop 45 (main 27, renderer 16, preload 1, shared 1), src/tools 32, src/shared 29, src/agent 19, src/auth 15, src/common 10, src/test-kernel 10, src/transcript 7, src/utils 7, src/skills 5, src/platform 1, src/housekeeping 1, trace-viewer 1, extension 0. `export *` really does exist in exactly one file (`src/shared/schemas/index.ts`), none of the 182 is on `packages/agent/src/index.ts`'s curated surface, and the 11 dead `*Input` aliases are the right 11. The overlaps with the other refuted round-1 items are false alarms I checked and cleared: the transcript refutation (proposal:2567) is about `StreamLogAppendInput`/`StreamLogDelta`/the DIR constants, none of which is in the 182; the TeamPlan refutation is about `buildTeamOptions`/`teamExecutionFields` (functions, test-consumed), not `TeamPlanStatus`. The census is the most accurate I have checked in this survey. The problem is what it is a census _of_.

  </details>

### `large-files`

- **Collapse the two hand-rolled settings-row clones in LaTeXTab and AIAgentsTab onto the shared settings-row owners**

  <details><summary>Why it was refuted</summary>

  REFUTED under R6 (net-adds elements / relocation rather than deletion), with the ledger inverted on the element axis and roughly 5x optimistic on LoC.

  The duplication itself is real — I confirmed `LaTeXTab.renderConfigRow` (packages/extension/src/settingsView/frontend/tabs/LaTeXTab.ts:848-873) and `AIAgentsTab.renderSelectRow` (packages/extension/src/settingsView/frontend/tabs/AIAgentsTab.ts:120-156) both emit the exact `.settings-row / .settings-row-text / .settings-row-label / .settings-row-help / .settings-row-control` shell that `src/shared/wa/settingsSection.ts:98` and `:147` own. It is not filed, not shipped in round 1, and no dated doc rules it the other way. It dies on the mechanism, not the observation.

  (1) THE COLLAPSE ADDS PUBLIC API ON A SHARED MODULE, NET. The proposal deletes 2 private methods (`renderConfigRow`, `renderSelectRow`) and adds 4 exported/declared elements: `renderSettingsSelectRow` + `SettingsSelectRowOptions` in src/shared/wa/settingsSection.ts, and `renderStateSettingSelectRow` + its options interface in components/shared/stateSettingRows.ts. Net **+2 declarations**, not the claimed -2. On top of that it widens two existing shared interfaces with `trailing` (toggle), `trailing` + `leading` (number), and — see (3) — a control-class hook, i.e. +4 to +6 new interface members on `SettingsToggleRowOptions` (:63) and `SettingsNumberRowOptions` (:120). This is the classic parameterize-the-shared-thing-to-absorb-a-variant move: the shared row's public surface grows by more than the private duplicates shrink.

  (2) NET LoC IS A WASH, NOT -80. I drafted the replacement at prettier width and counted. Deletions: renderConfigRow 27 lines; renderBooleanSetting 875-911 (37) shrinks to ~27, save ~10; renderNumberSetting 945-989 (45) shrinks to ~24, save ~21; renderEnumSetting 997-1047 (51) shrinks to ~40, save ~11; AIAgentsTab renderSelectRow with its doc 115-156 (42) minus a ~4-line call site, save ~38. Total deleted ~107. Additions: the shared select row is **51 lines** measured, not free (I wrote it out: (scratchpad) plus 2-3 side-effect/`readSelectValue` imports, plus ~26 for `renderStateSettingSelectRow` mirroring stateSettingRows.ts:61-75, plus ~12 for the trailing/leading options and their docs. Total added ~~92. **Net ≈ -15 LoC.** Split by half, the toggle/number half is the only real win (~~-49 LoC, -1 element); the select half is net-positive LoC and +4 elements on its own — and you cannot ship the toggle/number half alone, because `renderConfigRow` also serves the 2 enum rows (LaTeXTab.ts:818, :834), so deleting it _requires_ the net-adding select row. The two halves are welded together, and the welded result is a wash.

  (3) TWO UNACKNOWLEDGED VISUAL REGRESSIONS, on a medium-risk UI change with zero test coverage. (a) `renderConfigRow` renders `${opts.statusIcon} ${opts.control} ${opts.reset}` (LaTeXTab.ts:869) — the status icon precedes the control. The proposal says "trailing: statusIcon + reset", which silently moves the pencil/gear icon to the right of the input and select on all 4 non-boolean LaTeX rows. Fixing that needs a `leading` slot the proposal never mentions. (b) The control classes are dropped on the floor: `.setting-enum-select { min-width: 180px }` (LaTeXTab.styles.ts:120) and `.setting-select { min-width: 10rem; max-width: 14rem }` (AIAgentsTab.ts:92) are applied to the `wa-select` at the hand-rolled call sites; the shared row hardcodes only `class="setting-number-input"` and has no class pass-through, so both selects lose their sizing and both CSS rules go dead. That needs yet another option field.

  (4) THE STATED SAFETY NET DOES NOT EXIST. "Confirm src/test-kernel/settings/SettingsStyleContracts.vitest.ts still passes — it is the one test pinned to this row markup" is false. I read all 136 lines: it references `renderSettingsBanner` (:26,:56) and `renderSettingsSectionHeading` (:78) and nothing else; `grep -n 'settings-row|renderSettingsToggleRow|renderSettingsNumberRow|wa-switch|wa-select'` over it returns zero hits. No test pins this markup at all, so "unchanged by construction" is the only guarantee — on a change that, per (3), does not leave the markup unchanged.

  (5) THE "ONE OWNER" END STATE IS NOT REACHED. `rg -c 'class="settings-row"'` returns 8 files, not 3: SubscriptionsTab.ts (5), LaTeXTab.ts (2), settingsSection.ts (2), AIAgentsTab.ts, AgentsTab.ts, ShortcutsTab.ts, AccountTab.ts, SubscriptionSection.ts. Six of those are button/status rows with no shared row helper, and the proposal leaves every one of them hand-rolled. Worse for the select claim specifically: ToolsTab.ts:183-210 is a **third** catalog-backed `wa-select` writing through `postStateSetting` — the candidate's "both tabs filled the gap independently" misses it — and it sits in a different shell (`.setting-block` / `.setting-label`), so the new shared select row would not absorb it either. After the PR the view still has a hand-rolled row shell in 7 files and a third hand-rolled catalog select.

  Prior art worth noting: `renderConfigRow` was introduced by 3e3ac737cb ("refactor: codebase-wide simplification sweep", #9472) — a previous sweep already looked at these rows and chose a local scaffold over widening the shared one. Not a formal ruling, but the candidate does not beat it.

  </details>

### `resources-and-prompts`

- **Retire the VS Code-only AI agent-creator flow; the bundled `creator` tool-use agent already does the same job on all four hosts**

  <details><summary>Why it was refuted</summary>

  REFUTED — the central premise is factually false at HEAD. The candidate rests on "SYSTEM A (universal, all four hosts)", i.e. that `creator.yaml` already delivers agent creation on extension, desktop and CLI. It does not. The wiring that makes `creator.yaml` work is VS Code-only.

  **1. The creator agent's directory wiring is registered exclusively in the VS Code extension host.**
  `creator.yaml` addresses every directory it needs through four template variables (`packages/extension/resources/tool_use_agents/creator.yaml:31,32,33,34,44,68,69,73`). Those variables are filled by `getAgentDirectoryVars()` (`src/agent/prompt/userVars.ts:271-288`), which reads `listExternalRoots()` and — per its own doc comment at `:258-262` — "Reads from the external-roots registry populated at activation … Absent roots render as empty strings".

  I ran the definitive grep (whole repo, excluding `node_modules` and `dist`):

  ```
  grep -rn "registerExternalRoot" --include='*.ts' --include='*.tsx' --include='*.mjs' .
  ```

  Every call site is in one file: `packages/extension/src/frontend/setup.ts:37,43,49,54,85`. That file does `import * as vscode from 'vscode'` at `:3` and its registrar is `registerAgentDirectoryRoots(context: vscode.ExtensionContext)` (`:29`). `src/utils/files/externalRoots.ts:15` states the rule outright: "Registration happens in the VS Code activation layer (see frontend/setup.ts)". Neither `packages/cli/src` nor `packages/desktop/src` contains a single `ExternalRoot`/`externalRoots` reference — I grepped both trees; the only agent-directory hits there are `platform().agentDirectories.*` (`packages/desktop/src/main/index.ts:690,694,696,698,1025`, `packages/cli/src/runtime/initPlatform.ts:266`), which is the platform port, not the tool allowlist.

  Consequence on desktop and CLI today:
  - `BUILTIN_WORKFLOW_DIR`, `BUILTIN_TOOLUSE_DIR`, `CUSTOM_AGENTS_DIR`, `AGENT_DOCS_DIR` all render as `''`. The rendered system prompt tells the model to read `/workflow_schema.md` and to write the new agent to `/<agent_name>.yaml` — filesystem root.
  - Even with a correct absolute path, the file tools reject it. `src/tools/pathResolution.ts:104-125` (`resolveOutsideRoot`) throws `ToolError('Path must stay within the workspace.')` for any out-of-workspace absolute path when there is no matching external root and `TOOL_PATH_PROTECTION_ENABLED` is on — and its default is `true` (`src/shared/schemas/stateSettings.ts:70,1496-1497`).

  So `creator.yaml` being copied into the CLI tarball (`packages/cli/scripts/copy-resources.mjs:14-20`) is bundling, not functioning. Round 1's own verifier note the candidate quotes (survey `:2033`) says only that creator.yaml "IS bundled to the CLI and IS reachable there" — reachable as an agent row, which is what `packages/cli/scripts/validate-tui.mjs:988,1026` exercises. Neither round 1 nor this candidate checked whether the roots it depends on are registered off VS Code. They are not.

  The whole argument therefore inverts. The proposal would delete the only working AI agent-creation path (VS Code), delete the desktop dead-end message and the `mode` field that honestly record the gap, and leave desktop and CLI users pointed at a `creator` agent whose prompt names empty directories and whose writes are refused. That is not "deleting a host-locked path"; it is removing the working half of an asymmetry and keeping the broken half. The real prerequisite work — moving `registerAgentDirectoryRoots` out of the extension activation into a host-neutral composition step so `creator` actually functions on all four hosts — is unfiled and is a strictly larger, additive change than this candidate.

  **2. Two dated rulings decided the other way and the claim does not beat them (rule 4).**
  - `docs/proposals/2026-06-27-prd-runtime-host-decoupling.md:561-564` classifies `agentCreatorCommands` as **Deepen** ("The runtime should own tool-group option projection and selected-option resolution"), and `:1591-1594` plus `:1905-1907` record that deepening as **"Implemented in this branch."** The repo invested in this seam deliberately and recently.
  - `docs/proposals/2026-08-25-agent-sdk-readiness-reverify.md:109-117` — dated the same day as the survey this candidate extends — names `agentCreator` "the one genuine 'logical agent not yet running as one'" and rules that "That boundary stays open **correctly**, because closing it is interactive-UI design work (the `AgentCreatorUI`/approval channel that the public `HostInteractions` deliberately lacks)". The candidate never cites either doc, and its argument (creator.yaml is an equivalent replacement) collapses under §1 above, so it does not beat them.
  - `docs/proposals/2026-07-12-fallback-audit.md:1445` separately adjudicated the agent-creator's template fallback and ruled **"keep validated template"**.

  **3. A required knock-on is missed and points the forbidden direction (rules 3/6).**
  `validateAgentYamlContent` (`src/agent/runtime/agentLoad.ts:37`) has exactly one non-test consumer in the repo: `agentCreatorFlow.ts:404`. I grepped: the only four files touching it are `agentLoad.ts`, `agentCreatorFlow.ts`, `src/test-kernel/agent/runtime/agentLoad.vitest.ts`, `src/test-kernel/agent/AgentCreatorOrchestration.vitest.ts`, and `agentLoad.ts` has no internal caller. `scripts/check-dead-code-ratchet-core.mjs:51-66` runs a production-only knip pass and classifies exactly this shape as `production-dead`. So the PR must either delete `validateAgentYamlContent` and ~50 lines of `agentLoad.vitest.ts` as well, or **widen** `config/ratchets/knip-baseline.json` — the forbidden direction the candidate itself invokes. Neither appears in the seven-step proposal.

  </details>

### `cli-output-parity`

- **Delete four born-dead account-auth copy formatters and the unread `cliCompact` stream-status label style**

  <details><summary>Why it was refuted</summary>

  REFUTED on criterion 3 (WRONG CONSUMER COUNT), fatally, plus criterion 8 for the residual.

  **Part (1) is not dead — it has four live production consumers on a user-facing surface, and the proposal would break the build.**

  `git grep -n "signedInEnabled\|signedInOverrideDisabled" -- ':!*/dist/*' ':!packages/extension/resources/*'` returns, besides the four definition lines in `src/shared/copy/accountAuth.ts`:

  - `packages/cli/src/chat/tui/commands/handlers/loginCommands.ts:73` — `readonly signedInEnabled: (accountLabel: string) => string;` (member of the `SubscriptionAuthCopy` interface)
  - `:74-77` — `readonly signedInOverrideDisabled: (accountLabel: string, target: string) => string;`
  - `:81-83` — `const SUBSCRIPTION_AUTH_COPY: Record<SubscriptionProviderId, SubscriptionAuthCopy> = { chatgpt: CHATGPT_AUTH, grok: GROK_AUTH };`
  - `:103-107` — inside `loginToSubscription(...)`:

  ```ts
  const auth = SUBSCRIPTION_AUTH_COPY[providerId];
  output.appendOutcome(
    update.effective
      ? auth.signedInEnabled(account.label)
      : auth.signedInOverrideDisabled(account.label, update.target),
  );
  ```

  That is the chat-TUI `/login chatgpt` and `/login grok` outcome sentence. Production consumers = 4, not 0. Deleting the members per proposal step 1 fails `npm run typecheck` immediately, because `CHATGPT_AUTH` and `GROK_AUTH` would no longer satisfy `SubscriptionAuthCopy` in the `Record` initializer, and it silently deletes live user-facing copy from the interactive login path.

  **The "born dead 20 days ago" pickaxe inference is invalid.** `git log -S"signedInEnabled" -- src packages` does return exactly one commit, e35d08f2ff (2026-08-06, #9816) — but `git show --stat e35d08f2ff` shows that commit touched **both** `src/shared/copy/accountAuth.ts` (+68) **and** `packages/cli/src/chat/tui/commands/handlers/loginCommands.ts` (84 changed). Definition and consumer landed together, which is exactly why the pickaxe shows one commit. A single `-S` hit proves "added once and never edited since", never "never referenced".

  **The real finding under this lens is the opposite of the one proposed.** `packages/cli/src/commands/_helpers/subscriptionAuthCommand.ts:74-87` (headless `texra login chatgpt`) hand-composes `ACCOUNT_OUTCOME.signedInAs(...)` + a `provider.modelFamily` / "more specific setting overrides the config" clause, while the interactive TUI path emits the per-provider `CHATGPT_AUTH.signedInEnabled` / `signedInOverrideDisabled` sentences. Two renderings of the same outcome, headless vs interactive — a genuine convergence candidate, but the fix is to converge them (probably by deleting the _headless_ hand-composition and reusing the per-provider copy, or vice versa after a copy decision), not to delete the interactive half as "dead".

  **Part (2) alone is real but too thin to stand.** `cliCompact` genuinely has zero production callers — verified: the only `formatStreamStatusLabel` call sites are `packages/cli/src/chat/tui/sessionStatus.ts:57` (`style: 'cli'`), `packages/cli/src/runtime/runProgressRenderer.ts:222` (`style: 'cli'`), `packages/cli/src/chat/tui/panes/WorkflowRunDetails.tsx:98` (default), and three extension progressView sites (default `progressHeader`). Source refs are only `src/test-kernel/shared/SharedStreams.vitest.ts:153,155,157,172` and a doc line. But with its batch partner refuted, the residual is -12 LoC / -2 elements in one file plus four test rows — below the bar for a standalone PR unless folded into another shared-copy sweep.

  Not a duplicate (round1-index.md: no hit; the 49-candidate survey's only `streamStatusDisplay.ts` hit, line 2376, is about un-exporting `StreamStatusLabelStyle` and is unrelated), and not already filed. Those parts of the dedupe held.

  </details>

## 4. Acceptance criteria

Per lane PR:

- `npm run typecheck` clean, `npm run lint` clean, `npm run format` applied.
- Affected vitest suites pass. No new test files unless a deletion earns a regression pin (AGENTS.md testing discipline: default for a refactor PR is zero new tests).
- `npm run check:dead-code-ratchet` passes. Where a deleted export **had** a baseline row, `config/ratchets/knip-baseline.json` shrinks in the same PR; a currently-live export has no row to remove and simply disappears with its consumers. Never add a row: if a deletion orphans some other export into dead code, un-export that symbol rather than baselining it. A baseline that grows is a rejected PR.
- Title uses `refactor:` / `simplify:` / `dedupe:`, which activates the checklist §14 letter-level template: the body carries `## Net elements (R6)` (files, `^[+-]export` symbols, class/interface/enum declarations, net LoC from `git diff --stat origin/main`) and `## Consumer counts (R8)` (grepped caller counts for every deleted public symbol).
- Behavior-preserving unless the candidate explicitly says otherwise. Where a deletion removes a catch or fallback, confirm against checklist §15 that it was not a masking site — a masking site gets fixed loudly, not deleted quietly.

### Two candidates were reverted on review, both the same mistake

`resumeOutcome` (lane `M9-progress`) and the `texra.agent.rounds` read (lane
`M5-shared`) both shipped and were then reverted. In each case the verifier
grepped the repository, found no reader, and concluded the value was dead — but
the consumer was **outside the repo**:

- `resumeOutcome` is forwarded verbatim to the public CLI NDJSON stream by
  `projectCliSessionFact`, and the frozen-wire ruling in
  `2026-08-03-ssot-consolidation-plan.md` puts public-key deletion on a
  0.40-deprecate / 0.41-delete clock. The repo is at 0.40.6.
- `texra.agent.rounds` is absent from every settings catalog, but
  `JsonConfigProvider.get()` consults the workspace and global stores _before_
  the catalog, so the key still overrides the default when a user has it in
  `.texra/config.json`. Its replacement also shipped 2026-08-02, inside the
  three-month compatibility window.

The lesson for the next round: for anything that crosses a **public wire
format, a persisted user file, or a settings key**, an in-repo consumer grep is
not sufficient evidence of deadness. Add that to the verifier kill conditions.

## 5. Risks

- **Verifier corrections are load-bearing.** Most surviving candidates had wrong line numbers, missed importers, or inflated LoC in the original claim. Implementers must read the corrections block, not just the proposal.
- **`knip-baseline.json` is a nine-way merge magnet.** Re-run the ratchet after each rebase instead of hand-resolving.
- **`M9-progress` carries the largest single deletion** (-330 LoC of desktop host-interaction test surface, -260 for the always-true progress-clear flag). Those two are `medium` risk and the desktop one deletes a 405-line suite; verify the extension-side suite genuinely covers the shared machine before deleting, exactly as the verifier claims.
- **Four cross-lane files** listed in §1 will conflict if two lanes land simultaneously without a rebase.
- **Scope discipline.** Several candidates are deliberately batched (three type twins, four `scripts/` residues, three desktop main-process surfaces). Implementers must not expand a batch into a general sweep of its area; §14 R5 bans unrelated churn.
